# 9router parity audit — confirmed findings (2026-09-25)

Produced by an 18-dimension audit (756 agents) comparing OpenProxy against the
9Router baseline at `.tmp/9router` (git 17c4cc76), with every candidate finding
adversarially checked by two independent readers who read BOTH codebases.

Accounting: 359 raw -> 359 unique -> 341 confirmed, 17 refuted, 0 skipped.
Severity below is the ORIGINAL claim severity; the verifier corrected some down
(the synthesis pass reported 32 P0 / 148 P1 / 97 P2 / 65 P3 after correction).

This file is evidence, not a work queue. Beads carry the work.

---

## P1-001 · P0 · 401/403 credential refresh and retry

- **9router**: After executor.execute returns a 401/403, chatCore calls `refreshWithRetry(fn, 3, log)` — a retry loop of `maxRetries` (default 3) with a FIXED `attempt * 1000` ms delay, retrying on ANY thrown error and also on a falsy return. Crucially, on success it performs ONE silent re-`execute()` through the full executor (with its own fallback-URL loop) and only accepts the result `if (retryResult.response.ok)`; if the retry throws it logs a warning and keeps the original 401. The refresh callback MUTATES `credentials.refreshToken` between attempts so rotating-RT providers don't reuse a consumed token.
- **OpenProxy**: `DefaultExecutor::execute` handles 401/403 inline: `if retry == 0 { try_refresh_credentials(...) }`, then one `send_one` retry. `try_refresh_credentials` delegates to `refresh_with_retry(attempt)`, which is `MAX_RETRIES=3` with `BASE_DELAY_MS=500`, `MAX_DELAY_MS=5000` and `jittered_delay(attempt) = BASE * 2^(attempt-1) + rand(0, BASE/2)` — i.e. ~500 ms then ~1 s, versus 9Router's fixed 1000 ms then 2000 ms. It also aborts the whole refresh on the first non-transient error: `let is_transient = !err.contains("40") || err.contains("429") || ...; if !is_transient { return Err(err); }` — a heuristic string match on the error text, so an `invalid_grant` (400) refresh gives up after ONE attempt where 9Router tries three.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's token-refresh backoff is 500 ms/1 s jittered-exponential vs 9Router's fixed 1 s/2 s, and it early-aborts on heuristically-classified permanent errors where 9Router always burns all 3 attempts.
- **Why it matters**: On a transient OAuth blip (a 500/timeout from the token endpoint wrapped in a message that happens to contain "40", or a network error classified non-transient), OpenProxy makes 1 refresh attempt at ~0 ms and gives up → the 401 propagates and the whole combo falls to the next member. 9Router makes 3 attempts over 3 s and recovers in place. Combined with the retry-ordering finding, a client on Claude Code/Codex sees account churn and a failed turn where 9Router would have succeeded on the same account.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:406-425 (`refreshWithRetry(async () => {...}, 3, log)`), open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:435-450 (one silent re-execute, `if (retryResult.response.ok)`), open-sse/services/tokenRefresh.js:257-274 (`for attempt < maxRetries`, `delay = attempt * 1000`, no transient classification). openproxy: src/core/executor/default.rs:2115-2163 (inline 401/403, `if retry == 0`), src/core/executor/default.rs:2334-2404 (try_refresh_credentials), src/oauth/token_refresh.rs:49-51 (MAX_RETRIES=3, BASE_DELAY_MS=500, MAX_DELAY_MS=5000), src/oauth/token_refresh.rs:72-79 (transient heuristic, early return), src/oauth/token_refresh.rs:90-94 (jittered_delay).
- **Parity fix**: Change `refresh_with_retry` to use `attempt * 1000` ms fixed delay and to NOT short-circuit on error class: always attempt all 3 times, swallowing and continuing exactly as `refreshWithRetry` does. Replace the `err.contains("40")` heuristic with 9Router's rule (retry every failure) while keeping `is_unrecoverable_refresh_error` as the only bail-out condition, since 9Router's `isUnrecoverableRefreshError` is applied by callers rather than inside the retry loop.

## P2-001 · P0 · 9router open-sse/handlers/videoProviders/vertex.js:147 + src/sse/handlers/videoGeneration.js:123-125  ⟷  openproxy src/server/api/media.rs:1223, 1245, 1850

- **9router**: handleVideoCreate resolves `vertex/veo-3.1-generate-preview` via getModelInfo (videoGeneration.js:82) to {provider:"vertex", model:"veo-3.1-generate-preview"}, then rewrites the forwarded body before the adapter ever sees it: `if (bodyInfo.parsed && model && bodyInfo.parsed.model !== model) forwardBody = JSON.stringify({ ...bodyInfo.parsed, model })` (videoGeneration.js:123-125). vertex.js:147 then validates the BARE id against /^[A-Za-z0-9._-]+$/, passes, and vertex.js:152 builds `{base}/v1/projects/{p}/locations/{l}/publishers/google/models/veo-3.1-generate-preview:predictLongRunning`. The prefix strip is unconditional — it is not provider-scoped.
- **OpenProxy**: media.rs:1194 resolves the same model correctly to ("vertex", Some("veo-3.1-generate-preview")), but the prefix strip is provider-scoped and explicitly skips vertex: `if canonical_provider != "vertex" { ... obj.insert("model", model_str) }` (media.rs:1223). body["model"] therefore stays "vertex/veo-3.1-generate-preview". media.rs:1245 calls validate_vertex_create_body, which reads that same unstripped value (media.rs:1839-1843) and runs is_safe_vertex_model_id (media.rs:1829-1834) — a predicate that rejects any character outside [A-Za-z0-9._-], so the `/` fails and the handler returns 400 "Invalid Vertex video model id" before any upstream call. The bare form cannot reach the vertex arm either: get_model_info("veo-3.1-generate-preview") falls through to infer_provider_from_model_name (core/model/mod.rs:283) which has no veo prefix rule and returns "openai", so media.rs:1661 misses video_provider_supported, media.rs:1664 rewrites the provider to the xai default, and the request is sent to xAI.
- **Gap**: The provider prefix is stripped from the forwarded `model` for the xAI and OpenRouter arms but not for Vertex, and Vertex's own safety check then rejects the still-prefixed id — so no Vertex video job can ever be created.
- **Why it matters**: The entire Vertex/Veo arm of the video API is dead. Every `POST /v1/videos/generations` with `model: "vertex/<veo-id>"` returns 400 "Invalid Vertex video model id"; the only syntactically-accepted body (`model: "veo-3.1-generate-preview"`, which media.rs:2540's own unit test uses) is silently rerouted to xAI, whose catalog has no such model, producing an upstream 404. Users configuring Vertex for Veo see a 400 for a request 9router accepts, with an error message that names the correct provider and model, making the cause hard to diagnose. The existing test at media.rs:2536 masks the bug because it calls to_vertex_body directly, bypassing the prefix-strip step that actually breaks.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/sse/handlers/videoGeneration.js:123-125 (unconditional strip), open-sse/handlers/videoProviders/vertex.js:147 (validates the bare id), vertex.js:152 (URL built from the bare id). openproxy: src/server/api/media.rs:1223 (`if canonical_provider != "vertex"` — strip skipped), media.rs:1245 + 1839-1850 (validates the unstripped value), media.rs:1829-1834 (is_safe_vertex_model_id rejects '/'), src/core/model/mod.rs:283 + 309-308 (bare veo id infers "openai"), media.rs:1661-1664 (falls back to xai).
- **Parity fix**: Drop the `!= "vertex"` carve-out at media.rs:1223 so the strip is unconditional, exactly as videoGeneration.js:123-125 does it. Read `vertex_model_id` from the already-bare `model` returned by resolve_video_provider_model rather than re-reading body["model"] (media.rs:1250-1256), so the URL model, the validate_vertex_create_body check, and to_vertex_body all agree. Order must be strip-then-validate, matching 9router. Add a handler-level test that drives video_create_handler with `model: "vertex/veo-3.1-generate-preview"` and asserts a predictLongRunning URL is produced, not a 400.

## P3-001 · P0 · 9router open-sse/services/combo.js:176-179 + 134; OpenProxy src/core/combo/mod.rs:283-322 + 409-475

- **9router**: detectRequiredCapabilities scans each of the three request shapes SEPARATELY: `for (const m of trailingUserItems(body.messages)) scanMessage(m)` (combo.js:176), `for (const it of trailingUserItems(body.input)) scanContent(it.content)` (:177), and `const contents = body.contents || body.request?.contents; for (const c of trailingUserItems(contents)) scanContent(c.parts)` (:178-179). scanBlock explicitly reads Gemini parts: `addByMime(b.inlineData?.mimeType || b.fileData?.mimeType)` (combo.js:134). A body of `contents:[{role:"user", parts:[{inlineData:{mimeType:"image/png", data:...}}]}]` therefore yields required = {vision}.
- **OpenProxy**: detect_required_capabilities (mod.rs:283-322) picks the FIRST present array with an or-else chain (messages -> input -> contents -> request.contents, mod.rs:285-290) and then runs scan_message_capabilities on each trailing user item. scan_message_capabilities (mod.rs:409-475) looks at images[], experimental_attachments/attachments, message-level image_url/image/audio_url/audio, and content (array or string) - it never reads a `parts` key. The inlineData/fileData branch inside scan_block (mod.rs:394-403) is therefore unreachable for a real Gemini body, because those blocks live in `parts`, not `content`.
- **Gap**: Gemini-native contents[].parts[].inlineData / fileData blocks are never inspected, so image/audio/PDF requests on the /v1beta and antigravity wire formats report zero required capabilities.
- **Why it matters**: Any Gemini CLI / Antigravity / gemini-cli request carrying an image gets required = {} in OpenProxy. That means (a) reorder_by_capabilities is a no-op so the declared combo order is not capability-filtered, and (b) augment_models_with_capacity_adapter returns early on the required.is_empty() guard (capacity_adapter.rs:179) so the vision/audio pool is never offered. On 9router the same request floats a vision-capable member to the front and can fall back to the adapter pool. The result is a 400 from the first, text-only member.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/services/combo.js:178-179 (scanContent(c.parts)), :134 (addByMime(b.inlineData?.mimeType || b.fileData?.mimeType)). OpenProxy: src/core/combo/mod.rs:285-290 (or-else chain), :409-475 (scan_message_capabilities - no `parts` handling), :458-474 (only `content` is scanned).
- **Parity fix**: Mirror combo.js:176-179 literally: iterate body.messages, body.input, and body.contents (or body.request.contents) independently instead of short-circuiting with or-else, and add a `parts` branch to scan_message_capabilities that calls scan_block on each element of obj["parts"] when present. Also drop the `.filter(role == "user")` narrowing (mod.rs:308) and keep all trailing items so tool/extension turns that carry media are scanned exactly as trailingUserItems does.

## P4-001 · P0 · 9router open-sse/services/combo.js:63-82 (reorderByCapabilities) + open-sse/providers/capabilities.js:453; OpenProxy src/core/combo/mod.rs:538-567 + 480-528

- **9router**: `reorderByCapabilities` (combo.js:63-82) computes each member's tier by calling the full capability resolver: `const caps = getCapabilitiesForModel(provider, model)` (combo.js:72), where provider/model are split on the FIRST slash (combo.js:69-71). `getCapabilitiesForModel` (capabilities.js:453) runs provider-override -> exact MODEL_CAPABILITIES -> ordered PATTERN_CAPABILITIES -> floor, refined by the synced models.dev catalog and the vision-name heuristic. The pattern match runs on the model half, so a gateway-prefixed id like `openrouter/anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.5` still hits `{ pattern: "*claude*", caps: { vision: true, ... } }` (capabilities.js:276) via the `baseModel = model.split("/").pop()` strip. Tier 0 = all hard+soft satisfied; the sort is stable (`a.t - b.t || a.i - b.i`), so members that all satisfy keep declared order.
- **OpenProxy**: `reorder_by_capabilities` (mod.rs:538-567) classifies each member with the hardcoded `model_has_capability(model, cap)` (mod.rs:548). That heuristic (mod.rs:480-528) is a short prefix/name list: vision = starts_with(openai/o1 | openai/o3 | anthropic/claude | google/gemini | vertex/claude | vertex/gemini | aws/claude | gcp/gemini | custom/node-openai) OR contains(vision | -4o | gemini) OR starts_with(oc/mimo); pdf = only the five claude/gemini prefixes; audioInput/videoInput = only oc/mimo*. It tests the WHOLE "<alias>/<model>" string, so a gateway prefix breaks every rule. The accurate resolver DOES exist at src/core/combo/capabilities.rs:723-728 get_capabilities_for_model (same provider/exact/pattern/catalog/refine chain) but the dispatcher never calls it.
- **Gap**: Capability-based fallback reordering in OpenProxy runs a ~10-entry prefix heuristic instead of the capability table, so the order in which combo members are tried is wrong for most real model ids.
- **Why it matters**: Concrete divergence for a combo ["openrouter/anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.5", "openai/gpt-4o"] with an image in the last user turn. 9router: both are tier 0, declared order preserved, Claude is tried first. OpenProxy: model_has_capability("openrouter/anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.5","vision") is false (the string starts with openrouter/ and matches none of the name patterns) so it lands in tier 2, while "openai/gpt-4o" matches -4o and lands in tier 0. OpenProxy silently reorders and dispatches to gpt-4o first, violating the operator's configured priority and burning the wrong account/quota. The same misclassification hits openrouter/anthropic/claude-* for pdf, together/*, fireworks/*, deepseek-v4-flash-vision-exp, qwen3-vl-plus, and every Gemini/Claude model behind OpenRouter, Together, DeepInfra, Novita. It also defeats the capacity adapter: augment_models_with_capacity_adapter (capacity_adapter.rs:175) calls the same heuristic, so it believes the combo already has vision and never prepends the pool model.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/services/combo.js:63-82 (tierOf -> getCapabilitiesForModel at :72); .tmp/9router/open-sse/providers/capabilities.js:453 (getCapabilitiesForModel), :276 (`*claude*` -> vision:true). OpenProxy: src/core/combo/mod.rs:548 (calls model_has_capability), :480-528 (the heuristic), :489 (starts_with anthropic/claude), :500-505 (name patterns); src/core/combo/capabilities.rs:723-728 (the correct resolver, unused here).
- **Parity fix**: Replace the body of reorder_by_capabilities with a per-model capability lookup mirroring combo.js:69-75: split the entry at the first `/` into (provider, model), call crate::core::combo::capabilities::get_capabilities_for_model(provider, model), and read the returned vision / pdf / audio_input / video_input flags. Delete model_has_capability (mod.rs:480-528) and repoint capacity_adapter::model_satisfies (capacity_adapter.rs:167-176) at the same resolver so the adapter gate and the reorder agree. Keep the existing tier buckets and the stable ordering (tier asc, original index asc) so a fully-satisfying combo keeps its declared order.

## P5-001 · P0 · 9router src/sse/handlers/chat.js:143-158; OpenProxy src/server/api/chat.rs:886-913 (ModelRouteKind::Direct)

- **9router**: After the combo branch, handleChat runs the SAME capacity-adapter gate for a single, non-combo model: `const soloAugmented = augmentModelsWithCapacityAdapter([modelStr], requiredCapabilities, settings)` (chat.js:143). If the adapter contributed a pool model (`soloAugmented.length > 1`, chat.js:144) it runs handleComboChat over `[...pool, modelStr]` with `comboName: modelStr` and `comboStrategy: getActiveAdapterStrategy(requiredCapabilities, settings)` (chat.js:147-157), wrapped in withCapacityAdapterStripping. So `model: "openai/gpt-3.5-turbo"` plus an image transparently dispatches to `oc/mimo-v2.5-free` (or the configured vision pool) instead of failing.
- **OpenProxy**: augment_models_with_capacity_adapter is called from exactly one place - the ModelRouteKind::Combo arm (chat.rs:511). The ModelRouteKind::Direct arm (chat.rs:886-913) builds a RequestPlan and calls execute_single_model immediately; it never inspects required_caps, never consults settings.capacity_adapter, and never enters the combo executor. detect_required_capabilities is not even computed on that path.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy is missing the solo (non-combo) capacity-adapter fallback entirely, so a plain provider/model request whose target lacks vision/pdf/audio gets no automatic switch to the configured adapter pool.
- **Why it matters**: A client (Claude Code with a screenshot, Cline, Cursor) sending {"model":"openai/gpt-4o-mini","messages":[...,{"type":"image_url"}...]} works on 9router - the image request is routed to the vision pool model with history stripped - but on OpenProxy the request is dispatched verbatim to a text-only model, which returns 400 or silently loses the image after strip_unsupported_modalities. This is a hard request-flow break for the single-model case, which is the most common client configuration.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/sse/handlers/chat.js:143-158 (soloAugmented -> handleComboChat), :147-157 (comboName: modelStr, getActiveAdapterStrategy). OpenProxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:886-913 (Direct arm, straight to execute_single_model); the only augment_models_with_capacity_adapter call site is src/server/api/chat.rs:511 (Combo arm).
- **Parity fix**: In the ModelRouteKind::Direct arm, before building the plan, port chat.js:143-157: `let required_caps = detect_required_capabilities(&body); let solo = augment_models_with_capacity_adapter(&[model_str.to_string()], &required_caps, &snapshot.settings.capacity_adapter);` and if solo.len() > 1 delegate to execute_combo_strategy_full(&solo, Some(model_str), adapter_strategy, &[], sticky, Some(&required_caps), &snapshot.pricing, capacity_check, ...) with the strip_history_for_context call applied only to the adapter-added entries (same adapter_added guard already used at chat.rs:805-813).

## P6-001 · P0 · 9router src/sse/handlers/chat.js:170-219; OpenProxy src/server/api/chat.rs:818-833

- **9router**: handleSingleModelChat starts with `const modelInfo = await getModelInfo(modelStr)`; if `!modelInfo.provider` it re-enters the FULL combo pipeline (chat.js:170-219): getComboModels, per-combo strategy, detectRequiredCapabilities, augmentModelsWithCapacityAdapter, the fusion branch, and handleComboChat. Because every combo member is dispatched through handleSingleModelChat, a member that is itself a combo name is expanded recursively with its own strategy/sticky/fusion semantics.
- **OpenProxy**: The combo dispatch closure calls get_model_info(&combo_model, &inner_snapshot) (chat.rs:819) but only reads .provider and .model (chat.rs:826-831). A nested combo name yields ResolvedModel { provider: None, route_kind: Combo }, so combo_provider_str becomes the literal "unknown" (chat.rs:826-830) and RequestPlan is built for provider "unknown" with the nested combo name as the model. execute_single_model (chat.rs:1160) has no re-entry into the combo arm, so the request is dispatched to a non-existent provider.
- **Gap**: Combo members that are themselves combo names resolve to provider "unknown" instead of recursing into the nested combo.
- **Why it matters**: Both ModelSelectModals list existing combos as selectable values (9router ModelSelectModal.js fetches /api/combos; OpenProxy web/src/shared/components/ModelSelectModal.tsx:150-153 and :667-683 render the "Combos" section), so building a nested combo is a supported, reachable user action. On 9router it works; on OpenProxy every request to a combo containing a nested combo fails with an unsupported-provider 4xx instead of fanning out.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/sse/handlers/chat.js:170-219 (`if (!modelInfo.provider) { const comboModels = await getComboModels(modelStr); ... }`), with every member dispatched via handleSingleModelChat at :116/:131. OpenProxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:818-833 (closure uses only .provider/.model), :836-857 (no re-dispatch through the Combo arm).
- **Parity fix**: Inside the combo closure, branch on combo_resolved.route_kind: if it is ModelRouteKind::Combo, re-enter the same combo dispatch (factor the arm at chat.rs:493-885 into a reusable dispatch_combo(state, body, combo_name, ...) and call it recursively with a recursion-depth guard), matching chat.js:170-219. Also handle the provider: None case instead of substituting the "unknown" sentinel.

## P7-001 · P0 · 9router src/sse/handlers/chat.js:70-81 · OpenProxy src/server/api/chat.rs:265

- **9router**: `chat.js:70-81` gates every `/v1/*` LLM/media handler on `settings.requireApiKey` (a standalone setting, default `true` at `src/lib/db/repos/settingsRepo.js:27`, independent of `requireLogin` at `:26`). Same gate in `embeddings.js:56`, `tts.js:37`, `stt.js:32`, `imageGeneration.js:40`, `search.js:49`, `fetch.js:52`, `videoGeneration.js:47`, `v1beta/models/[...path]/route.js:182`. Turning the dashboard-login password OFF never opens `/v1/*`: `requireApiKey` stays true, so an unauthenticated call still gets `401 Missing API key`.
- **OpenProxy**: `chat.rs:265` gates on the WRONG variable: `if require_api_key_auth && state.db.snapshot().settings.require_login`. The same wrong gate is repeated at `src/server/api/v1_models.rs:81`, `src/server/api/media.rs:140,464,1183,1506`, `src/server/api/web_fetch.rs:77`, `src/server/api/stt.rs:180`. `Settings` has no `require_api_key` field at all (`src/types/mod.rs:717-786`), and `/v1/*` routes carry no route-level auth (`src/server/api/mod.rs:296-300`).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy reuses the dashboard-password setting `requireLogin` as the `/v1/*` API-key gate, so setting `requireLogin=false` disables auth on the entire LLM gateway — a state 9router cannot reach.
- **Why it matters**: A user who disables the dashboard login (to use a tunnel/CLI without a password) silently exposes `/v1/chat/completions`, `/v1/messages`, `/v1/responses`, `/v1/embeddings`, `/v1/audio/*` and `/v1/models` to anyone who can reach the port — with no API key required. Under 9router the same toggle leaves the gateway key-protected. This is an unauthenticated-billing / quota-abuse exposure on the primary product surface.
- **Evidence**: 9router `.tmp/9router/src/lib/db/repos/settingsRepo.js:26-27`, `.tmp/9router/src/sse/handlers/chat.js:71` vs OpenProxy `src/server/api/chat.rs:265`, `src/types/mod.rs:734`
- **Parity fix**: Add `require_api_key: bool` to `Settings` (default `true`, serde camelCase `requireApiKey`) and to `UpdateSettingsRequest`. Change every `/v1/*` gate listed above from `settings.require_login` to `settings.require_api_key`, keeping the `require_api_key`/`require_login` booleans independent exactly as `settingsRepo.js:26-27` does.

## P8-001 · P0 · 9router src/sse/handlers/videoGeneration.js:110  ⟷  openproxy src/server/api/media.rs:322-328, 334-405

- **9router**: handleVideoCreate gates first and reads the body second: `const authError = await requireValidApiKey(request); if (authError) return authError;` (videoGeneration.js:110) runs before `readForwardableBody` (videoGeneration.js:113). readForwardableBody's non-JSON branch (videoGeneration.js:72-75) returns the exact multipart bytes, but they are still forwarded only through the already-gated path, so both the JSON and the multipart edits/extensions requests require a valid API key when settings.requireApiKey is on.
- **OpenProxy**: video_edits_extensions_proxy splits on Content-Type (media.rs:312). The JSON branch goes to video_create_handler, which does gate (media.rs:1183-1187: `if settings.require_login { require_api_key_with_reload(...) }`). The non-JSON branch goes to video_forward_raw (media.rs:328), whose body opens directly with `let provider = DEFAULT_VIDEO_PROVIDER;` (media.rs:341) and contains no require_login / require_api_key_with_reload call anywhere. No route-level middleware compensates: the video routes sit in the `protected` tier (src/server/api/mod.rs:224-255) which carries no route_layer — the only two route_layers in the file are require_local_only (mod.rs:325) and require_admin (mod.rs:386) on the admin tier, and the app-level layers are only real_ip_middleware and CORS (src/lib.rs:45-46). The poll path does gate correctly (media.rs:1506-1510), so this is create-specific.
- **Gap**: POST /v1/videos/edits and POST /v1/videos/extensions skip the API-key check entirely whenever the request is not application/json, because the multipart branch short-circuits into a handler that has no auth gate and the route tier has no middleware.
- **Why it matters**: An authentication bypass on a billable endpoint. require_login defaults to true (src/types/mod.rs:734), so on a default install anyone who can reach the port can send `curl -X POST -F file=@clip.mp4 http://host/v1/videos/edits` with no Authorization header, and OpenProxy will use the operator's configured xAI credentials to create a paid video job and return its response. 9router rejects the identical request with 401 "Missing API key". Beyond auth, the same short-circuit also drops the account-rotation loop, the 401/403 token refresh, Idempotency-Key forwarding, secret sanitization of the error body, and the connection-id pinning header — so a multipart job is neither account-isolated nor attributable.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/sse/handlers/videoGeneration.js:110 (gate precedes body read), :113 (body read), :72-75 (multipart branch still behind the gate). openproxy: src/server/api/media.rs:322-328 (non-JSON branch), :334-341 (video_forward_raw, no auth call), :1183-1187 (gate present only on the JSON path), :1506-1510 (gate present on the poll path), src/server/api/mod.rs:296-300 + :421 (protected tier has no layer), src/lib.rs:45-46 (only real_ip + CORS).
- **Parity fix**: Hoist the auth check out of video_create_handler into video_edits_extensions_proxy so it runs once for both branches, mirroring videoGeneration.js:110 running before readForwardableBody. Specifically: at the top of video_edits_extensions_proxy (media.rs:306) add the same `if state.db.snapshot().settings.require_login { if let Err(e) = require_api_key_with_reload(&headers, &state.db).await { return auth_error_response(e); } }` block, and drop the now-redundant copy at media.rs:1183-1187. Then route the raw body through the same create pipeline (rotation at CREATE_ROTATION_STATUSES, the 401/403 refresh-and-retry-once, Idempotency-Key, sanitize_video_secrets, connection-id header) rather than the standalone video_forward_raw, so multipart parity is restored in one step.

## P9-001 · P0 · 9router src/sse/handlers/videoGeneration.js:60-76 + :109-189 vs OpenProxy src/server/api/media.rs:301-330 + :334-405

- **9router**: readForwardableBody (videoGeneration.js:60-76) branches ONLY on whether the body is JSON, never on action. A multipart body is forwarded as raw bytes with parsed=null, but then flows through the SAME handleVideoCreate pipeline: requireValidApiKey gate at :110, provider resolution at :116, then the while(true) account loop at :134-189 that calls getProviderCredentials (which filters rate-limited/model-locked accounts via isModelLockActive, auth.js:87), checkAndRefreshToken at :149, and handleVideoProxyCore. Because parsed is null, resolveVideoProvider(null) returns the xai default, so multipart edits/extensions are still authenticated, refreshed and rotated.
- **OpenProxy**: video_edits_extensions_proxy (media.rs:301-330) splits JSON from non-JSON. The JSON branch reuses video_create_handler (auth + rotation). The non-JSON branch calls video_forward_raw (media.rs:334-405), a separate standalone function that: hardcodes `let provider = DEFAULT_VIDEO_PROVIDER;` (media.rs:341) with no provider resolution, never calls require_api_key_with_reload, never calls checkAndRefreshToken, and selects a single connection with `.min_by_key(|c| c.priority.unwrap_or(999))` (media.rs:347) — no exclusion set, no rotation. It also emits x-openproxy-connection-id (media.rs:402) but that header is not what its own poll handler reads first.
- **Gap**: The multipart/non-JSON path for POST /v1/videos/edits and /v1/videos/extensions bypasses the API-key gate, token refresh, and account rotation that 9router applies to the identical request.
- **Why it matters**: A multipart video edit is a billable job that upstream rejects before creation on 401/403/429 — exactly the errors 9router rotates on. With one expired or quota-exhausted xAI account, 9router silently falls to the next account and succeeds; OpenProxy returns the raw upstream 401 to the client with no retry. Worse, the auth gate is skipped entirely, so on a deployment where requireApiKey is enabled this endpoint accepts unauthenticated calls that consume a real provider credential and bill the operator.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/sse/handlers/videoGeneration.js:60-76, :110, :134-189. OpenProxy: src/server/api/media.rs:301-330, :334-405 (esp. :341, :347), route wiring src/server/api/mod.rs:232-247.
- **Parity fix**: Delete video_forward_raw and route the non-JSON branch of video_edits_extensions_proxy into the same account loop video_create_handler uses, matching 9router's single-path design. Concretely: make readForwardableBody's raw-bytes result a first-class input to video_create_handler (accept an optional pre-read body + a provider_override=Some("xai") that replaces only resolve_video_provider_model, per 9router's resolveVideoProvider(null) at videoGeneration.js:79), then run the existing require_api_key_with_reload → select_media_connections → refresh → rotate-on-401/403/429 sequence. Preserve the byte-for-byte body forwarding (never re-encode multipart).

## P10-001 · P0 · 9router src/sse/handlers/videoGeneration.js:97-104 vs OpenProxy src/server/api/media.rs:1443-1478, :1625-1629

- **9router**: withConnectionHeader (videoGeneration.js:97-104) sets the response header `x-9router-connection-id` on every successful create AND every successful poll. Its doc comment at :100-102 states the contract explicitly: "Video jobs are account-bound upstream — clients echo this back as `x-connection-id` on GET polls so the same account is used."
- **OpenProxy**: The create/poll success paths set `x-openproxy-connection-id` instead (media.rs:1443-1447, :1474-1477, :1625-1628, :2272). The poll handler's preferred-connection lookup does accept both spellings — headers.get("x-connection-id").or_else(headers.get("x-openproxy-connection-id")) at media.rs:2343-2344 — but resolve_video_get_provider, which decides WHICH PROVIDER the poll targets, reads only `x-connection-id` (media.rs:1793-1798) and never the openproxy-spelled header.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy renames the create-response connection header to x-openproxy-connection-id, so a 9router-conformant client that echoes the documented x-9router-connection-id value back under the name x-connection-id still works for the connection but is a name-level contract break, and OpenProxy's own resolve_video_get_provider cannot recover the provider from the header it emits.
- **Why it matters**: Multi-account video polling silently degrades. A client that reads x-9router-connection-id from create (per 9router's documented contract) finds nothing in OpenProxy, sends no pin, and the poll falls back to select_media_connection's top-priority xAI account. If the job was created on account #2, the poll hits account #1 and returns a wrong or 404 status for a job that is actually running. This is the exact failure mode the header exists to prevent.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/sse/handlers/videoGeneration.js:97-104 (header set at :102), :203 (client echoes it back as x-connection-id). OpenProxy: src/server/api/media.rs:1477, :1628, :402 emit x-openproxy-connection-id; :1793-1798 resolve_video_get_provider reads only x-connection-id; :2343-2344 select_video_connection accepts both.
- **Parity fix**: Emit `x-9router-connection-id` on video create and poll success responses (media.rs:402, :1446, :1477, :1628, :2272) to preserve the 9router wire contract; keep accepting x-openproxy-connection-id on input as a backwards-compatible alias. Additionally make resolve_video_get_provider (media.rs:1793) fall back to the same .or_else(x-openproxy-connection-id) lookup used by select_video_connection, so the provider resolution and the connection resolution can never disagree.

## P11-001 · P0 · 9router: open-sse/utils/stream.js:110-119; openproxy: src/core/translator/response/openai_to_claude.rs:116-142, openai_responses.rs:1319-1342, openai_to_antigravity.rs:194-217, called from src/server/api/chat.rs:3909-3915

- **9router**: open-sse/utils/stream.js:109-119 — the TransformStream keeps a `buffer` string, splits it on "\n" (keeping the partial tail via `lines.pop()`), and loops over EVERY complete line, calling `translateResponse` once per line. streamHelpers.js:4-34 `parseSSELine` then requires the line to start with 'd' (charCode 100) and slices `line.slice(5)`. So one HTTP chunk containing N SSE frames yields N translated outputs, and a frame split across two chunks is reassembled from the carried-over buffer.
- **OpenProxy**: ResponseTransformFn is `(chunk: &[u8], state) -> Vec<String>` and is called with the RAW network chunk: chat.rs:3909-3915 passes `&chunk` (the `bytes_stream()` item) straight to `registry.translate_response`. Most registered transforms then do `String::from_utf8_lossy(chunk)` and take only the FIRST `data:` payload: openai_to_claude.rs:120-128 `extract_sse_or_json_payload(text.trim())` -> `serde_json::from_str(payload)` -> `Err(_) => return vec![]`. chat_to_responses_streaming (openai_responses.rs:1323-1342) and openai_to_antigravity_streaming (openai_to_antigravity.rs:198-217) are identical. gemini_to_openai.rs:270-277, ollama_to_openai.rs:169-173, kiro_to_claude.rs:28-35 and commandcode_to_openai.rs:340-352 do `serde_json::from_slice(chunk)` on the whole chunk. No cross-call line buffer exists in any of them (only claude_to_openai.rs:397 and responses_to_chat_streaming openai_responses.rs:1368 have one).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy treats one transport chunk as exactly one logical SSE event: any chunk carrying more than one `data:` frame, or a frame split across a chunk boundary, is silently discarded (returns an empty Vec) instead of being split into per-line events and reassembled.
- **Why it matters**: HTTP chunk boundaries are arbitrary and providers coalesce. The most damaging case is the final read of a connection, which routinely contains the terminal frame plus `data: [DONE]`: payload becomes `{...}\n\ndata: [DONE]`, serde_json fails on trailing characters, and the whole chunk is dropped. For a Claude client that loses the finish chunk, so `message_delta` + `message_stop` never arrive and Claude Code / Cursor / Cline hang until their read timeout. For a Codex client it loses `response.completed` and the CLI errors out. A split frame (chunk ends mid-JSON) is lost unconditionally because no state buffer exists.
- **Evidence**: 9router open-sse/utils/stream.js:112-119 + open-sse/utils/streamHelpers.js:21-27 | openproxy src/server/api/chat.rs:3909-3915, src/core/translator/response/openai_to_claude.rs:116-142, src/core/translator/response/openai_responses.rs:1319-1342, src/core/translator/response/openai_to_antigravity.rs:194-217, src/core/translator/response/gemini_to_openai.rs:270-277, src/core/translator/response/ollama_to_openai.rs:169-173
- **Parity fix**: Move SSE de-framing out of each transform and into one shared, stateful layer in front of `translate_response` (mirroring stream.js:110-119): append the raw chunk to a per-stream `String` buffer, split on '\n', keep the tail, and invoke the registered transform once per complete line (one line -> one transform call -> Vec<String>). Keep the existing in-transform buffers (claude_to_openai.rs line_buffer, responses_to_chat buffer) but they must no longer be the only buffering. Also add a `flush_stream` hook that drains the residual buffer at EOF, matching stream.js:411-463.

## P12-001 · P0 · 9router: open-sse/utils/stream.js:135-241 and :385-409; openproxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:3924-3926 and 4169-4175

- **9router**: `buildTransformStream` (streamingHandler.js:36-40) picks `createPassthroughStreamWithLogger` whenever `!needsTranslation(targetFormat, sourceFormat)` — i.e. the OpenAI-client -> OpenAI-provider case. In that mode stream.js:135-241 does a full normalization pass on every `data:` line: `fixInvalidId` (stream.js:144, streamHelpers.js:65-73 — ids equal to "chat"/"completion" or shorter than 8 chars are rewritten to `chatcmpl-<requestId|traceId|Date.now base36>`), injection of `object:"chat.completion.chunk"` and `created` when missing (:149-151), removal of Azure `prompt_filter_results` / `content_filter_results` (:154-165), removal of empty `delta.tool_calls: []` arrays (:172-179), a `hasValuableContent` filter (:181-183), usage merge (:197-200), and usage injection onto the finish chunk (estimated at :204-210, or buffered at :211-215). On flush, stream.js:400-405 emits `data: [DONE]\n\n` if not already sent, with an explicit exemption for `provider === "antigravity" | "gemini" | "vertex"`, and the comment at :395-399 explains this is required so OpenClaw-style clients do not hang until timeout.
- **OpenProxy**: When `plan.needs_translation()` is false, chat.rs:3924-3926 (and the Hyper twin at 4064-4065) yields `sanitize_sse_chunk(&chunk)`. That helper (chat.rs:4169-4175) forwards to `sanitize_sse_body` (src/server/api/sanitization.rs:84-110), which only removes top-level `service_tier` / `system_fingerprint` / `x_*` keys and `usage.usage_breakdown` (sanitization.rs:57-77). It does not touch ids, does not inject `object`/`created`, does not strip Azure content-filter fields, does not strip empty `tool_calls` arrays, does not filter empty chunks, does not inject usage on the finish chunk, and nothing is emitted at EOF, so no `data: [DONE]` is synthesized if the upstream omitted it.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's SSE passthrough is a near-raw byte copy: it performs none of 9Router's passthrough normalization and never guarantees a terminal `data: [DONE]` frame.
- **Why it matters**: Three concrete client breaks. (1) Upstreams that do not emit `data: [DONE]` (Azure AI, several OpenAI-compatible gateways, Ollama-compat shims) leave the client waiting on a socket close; OpenClaw and Cline then hang to timeout and trigger a failover to a second provider, exactly the case the 9router comment at stream.js:395-399 documents. (2) The empty-`tool_calls`-array strip is explicitly a fix for `@ai-sdk/openai-compatible`, which checks `delta.tool_calls != null` — an empty array passes that check and fires a premature `reasoning-end` on every chunk for CodeBuddy CN and similar providers. (3) Providers that echo a short/id-less chunk id break clients that key conversation state on `id`.
- **Evidence**: 9router open-sse/utils/stream.js:144, 149-151, 154-165, 172-179, 197-215, 395-409 (isGeminiFamily at :400) | openproxy src/server/api/chat.rs:3924-3926, 4064-4065, 4169-4175, src/server/api/sanitization.rs:57-77, 84-110
- **Parity fix**: Add a `create_passthrough_stream` transform in OpenProxy that mirrors stream.js:135-241 + 385-409: per-frame JSON parse, `fixInvalidId` equivalent, `object`/`created` injection, `prompt_filter_results`/`content_filter_results` deletion, empty-`tool_calls` deletion, valuable-chunk filter, usage merge, usage injection on the finish chunk; and at stream EOF emit `data: [DONE]\n\n` unless the provider is `antigravity`/`gemini`/`vertex` (plus the same Gemini-family exemption for the translated path). Wire it into chat.rs:3924-3926 / 4064-4065 in place of `sanitize_sse_chunk`, keeping `sanitize_sse_body` as an additional step.

## P13-001 · P0 · 9router: src/sse/handlers/embeddings.js:137-150 (saveRequestUsage on success) vs OpenProxy: src/server/api/media.rs:533-544 (adapter early-return) vs :610-673 (usage block, unreachable)

- **9router**: After `result.success`, handleEmbeddings computes usage = exactEmbeddingUsage(result.usage) (embeddings.js:138) and, when non-null, calls saveRequestUsage({provider, model, connectionId, apiKey, endpoint, tokens, status:'success'}) (embeddings.js:140-148) before returning result.response. This runs for EVERY provider, because handleEmbeddings is the only path — the adapter selection happens inside the core, not before it. exactEmbeddingUsage (embeddings.js:17-24) requires a non-estimated, structurally-consistent usage object (prompt_tokens > 0, completion_tokens === 0, total_tokens === prompt_tokens) and returns null otherwise.
- **OpenProxy**: execute_media_provider calls try_provider_adapter first (media.rs:533) and returns immediately when it yields Some (media.rs:542-543). The embeddings-usage recording block lives further down at media.rs:610-673, i.e. only on the generic forwarder path that is reached when NO adapter matched. Since get_embedding_adapter (src/core/media/embeddings/mod.rs:46-68) returns Some for all 13 registered providers (openai, openrouter, mistral, voyage-ai, fireworks, together, nebius, github, nvidia, jina-ai, vercel-ai-gateway, gemini, google_ai_studio, selfhosted-embedding) plus the openai-compatible-*/custom-embedding-* namespaces, the usage block is dead code for every supported embeddings provider. media.rs:663 is the only track_request call on this route.
- **Gap**: Usage accounting for /v1/embeddings is recorded only on the fallback path that no adapter-backed provider ever reaches, so embeddings token usage is silently dropped for every provider that actually has an embeddings adapter.
- **Why it matters**: Every successful /v1/embeddings call through an adapter-backed provider contributes zero tokens to the usage database. The Usage page, the quota tracker, per-provider cost breakdowns, and any budget guard fed from usage_tracker all under-report embedding spend to zero. Users cannot see or cap embedding cost, and the discrepancy is invisible because the response itself is correct. This is the exact regression the code comment at media.rs:607-609 claims to have ported ("ported from 9router v0.5.45 fix(usage): record exact embedding tokens") — the port landed on the wrong branch.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/sse/handlers/embeddings.js:137-150 (success → saveRequestUsage), :17-24 (exactEmbeddingUsage gate). OpenProxy: src/server/api/media.rs:533-544 (try_provider_adapter → `return resp;` short-circuits), :610-673 (`if route_kind == "embeddings"` usage block, only after that early return), :663 (sole track_request call on the route); src/core/media/embeddings/mod.rs:46-68 (adapter covers all supported providers).
- **Parity fix**: Move the usage-recording block out of execute_media_provider's generic tail and into try_provider_adapter / media_result_to_response so it runs on the adapter path too. Concretely: after `Ok(body)` in media_result_to_response (media.rs:1106), when the route_kind is "embeddings", apply the same gate 9router uses — parse body["usage"], reject when usage.estimated == true, require prompt_tokens (or input_tokens) > 0, completion_tokens (or output_tokens, default 0) == 0, and total_tokens == prompt_tokens — then call state.usage_tracker().track_request(provider, model, Some(&token_usage), Some(&connection.id), Some(&api_key), Some(endpoint), None) with the same TokenUsage shape already built at media.rs:649-660. Thread the provider/model/connection through to media_result_to_response so the call has the identity it needs.

## P14-001 · P0 · 9router: src/sse/handlers/embeddings.js:97-164 (credential + fallback loop) · openproxy: src/server/api/media.rs:518, :713-742 (single-shot connection pick)

- **9router**: `handleEmbeddings` runs a `while (true)` loop: `getProviderCredentials(provider, excludeConnectionIds, model)` (auth.js:98) filters connections by model lock + Antigravity quota + exclusion set, then on a failed core result calls `markAccountUnavailable(...)` (auth.js:153) and, when `shouldFallback`, adds the connection to the exclusion set and `continue`s to the next account (auth.js:155-160). When every account is locked it returns `unavailableResponse(status, msg, retryAfter, retryAfterHuman)` (error.js:116-129) which appends `(reset after …)` to the message and sets a `Retry-After: <seconds>` header. Selection honours per-provider `fallbackStrategy` (fill-first default) and round-robin with `stickyRoundRobinLimit` (auth.js:139-193).
- **OpenProxy**: `execute_media_provider` calls `select_media_connection(&snapshot, provider, model)` once, which is `select_media_connections(...).into_iter().next()` — the lowest-`priority` active connection with credentials, with no model-lock filter, no strategy, and no retry. Any adapter error is returned straight to the client via `media_result_to_response` (media.rs:1104-1112). `grep -i "retry-after" src/server/api/media.rs` returns nothing, so no `Retry-After` header is ever emitted. (The rotation helper `select_media_connections` exists and IS used by the video route at media.rs:1287-1291 — embeddings just never calls it.)
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's `/v1/embeddings` makes exactly one upstream attempt on one arbitrarily-chosen connection, where 9router rotates through every non-locked connection before failing.
- **Why it matters**: Account fallback is a headline OpenProxy feature (AGENTS.md "Account mgmt: credential selection → model-level fallback"). A user with two OpenAI keys where the first is rate-limited or its model is model-locked gets a 200 from 9router and a 429/401 from OpenProxy, and SDK retry logic that honours `Retry-After` has nothing to read. Requests that 9router serves on the second key never reach a second key.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/sse/handlers/embeddings.js:98, :153, :155-160; src/sse/services/auth.js:85-98 (model-lock filter), :139-193 (strategy); open-sse/utils/error.js:116-129 · openproxy: src/server/api/media.rs:518-526, :713-721, :726-742, :1104-1112, :1287-1291
- **Parity fix**: Change `execute_media_provider` to build the full ordered candidate list via `select_media_connections(&snapshot, provider)` (already available and already used by the video path at media.rs:1287) and wrap the `try_provider_adapter` / generic-forward call in a per-connection loop: on a 401/403/429 (or any `shouldFallback` status) call `crate::core::account_fallback::apply_error_state` / `mark_account_unavailable` for that connection id, add it to an exclusion set, and retry with the next candidate. When the candidate list is exhausted, return `unavailableResponse`-equivalent output: `{error:{message:"<msg> (reset after Ns)"}}` plus a `Retry-After: <n>` header.

## P15-001 · P0 · 9router: src/sse/handlers/embeddings.js:97-164 (credential loop) vs OpenProxy: src/server/api/media.rs:518-526 + 713-721 (select_media_connection)

- **9router**: handleEmbeddings runs `while (true)` (embeddings.js:97). Each pass calls getProviderCredentials(provider, excludeConnectionIds, model) (embeddings.js:98), which filters out excluded ids AND model-locked accounts (auth.js:84-96) and rotates via the per-provider fallbackStrategy / round-robin with stickyLimit (auth.js:139-181). On failure it calls markAccountUnavailable (embeddings.js:153) which writes modelLock_<model> + backoffLevel, and if shouldFallback it adds the connectionId to excludeConnectionIds and `continue`s (embeddings.js:155-161). When every account is locked it returns unavailableResponse(status, msg, retryAfter, retryAfterHuman) with a Retry-After header (embeddings.js:101-107); when nothing was ever excluded it returns 400 `No credentials for provider: X` (embeddings.js:108-111).
- **OpenProxy**: execute_media_provider calls select_media_connection ONCE (media.rs:518), which is `select_media_connections(...).into_iter().next()` (media.rs:713-721) — a static sort by `priority` (media.rs:740) of connections that are merely `is_active()` and have a non-empty key (media.rs:730-757). There is no loop, no exclusion set, no model-lock filter, and no markAccountUnavailable anywhere in the media path (grep for mark_account/account_registry/is_account_unavailable/apply_error_state in media.rs and src/core/media/ returns nothing). The single connection is used for the whole request and whatever status it returns is handed straight back to the client.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy pins every /v1/embeddings request to the first priority-ordered account with no fallback loop, no exclusion set, and no account/model cooldown state, so the second and later connections are unreachable.
- **Why it matters**: A user with two connections for the same embedding provider (the normal setup) gets a hard failure whenever the priority-0 key is rate-limited, quota-exhausted, or cooldown-locked, even though a healthy second account exists. 9router transparently fails over; OpenProxy returns 429/401 to the client. This breaks LangChain/LlamaIndex/OpenAI-SDK embedding clients and any vector-DB ingestion job that has more than one key configured. It also means an account that 9router has locked via modelLock_<model> is still selected here, so OpenProxy keeps hammering a known-dead account and keeps returning its error.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/sse/handlers/embeddings.js:97 (while(true)), :98 (getProviderCredentials), :101-107 (unavailableResponse), :108-111 (400 no credentials), :153 (markAccountUnavailable), :155-161 (exclude + continue); src/sse/services/auth.js:84-96 (model-lock filter), :139-181 (round-robin/sticky). OpenProxy: src/server/api/media.rs:518-526 (single select_media_connection), :713-721 (select_media_connection = .into_iter().next()), :726-742 (select_media_connections, priority sort only), :730-757 (filters = is_active + non-empty key only).
- **Parity fix**: Wrap execute_media_provider's embeddings branch in the same exclusion loop 9router uses: build the candidate list once via select_media_connections (already priority-ordered), then loop, maintaining an `exclude_connection_ids: HashSet<String>`; on a retryable status call the existing account_fallback::apply_error_state (src/core/account_fallback/mod.rs:750) to persist the model lock, insert the id into the exclude set, and continue to the next connection. When the candidate list drains, mirror embeddings.js:101-111: emit a 503 with a Retry-After header derived from the earliest model-lock expiry (get_earliest_model_lock_until, mod.rs:630) if any account was ever locked, else 400 `No credentials for provider: {provider}`. Pre-filter candidates through is_account_unavailable (mod.rs:603) / is_model_lock_active (mod.rs:564) so a cooldown account is never picked in the first place.

## P16-001 · P0 · Add Custom Model modal → POST /api/models/custom

- **9router**: AddCustomModelModal renders a "Capabilities" block with a Toggle per CAPACITY_META key (vision, reasoning), seeded from defaultCaps(); handleSave passes `(cleanId, caps)` to onSave (AddCustomModelModal.js:47-57, 74-88). The page forwards it: `handleAddCustomModel(modelId, "llm", providerStorageAlias, caps)` (providers/[id]/page.js:1859-1860), and handleAddCustomModel spreads `...(caps ? { caps } : {})` into the POST body (providers/[id]/page.js:537-545). The route sanitizes and persists it: `sanitizeCaps` keeps only boolean values (api/models/custom/route.js:8-12, 35-36) → `addCustomModel({... ...(cleanCaps ? { caps: cleanCaps } : {})})` (route.js:36) → aliasRepo.js:34-46 stores it, and useModelCaps.js:14-17 indexes it by fullModel/routedModel/model so the picker and combo capability resolution see the override.
- **OpenProxy**: AddCustomModelModal has no Capabilities section at all — the props are `onSave: (modelId: string) => Promise<void>` (AddCustomModelModal.tsx:11) and the JSX goes straight from the Model ID input to the test-result block (AddCustomModelModal.tsx:96-109). ProviderDetailPageClient's onSave drops the second arg entirely: `onSave={async (modelId) => { const ok = await handleAddCustomModel(modelId, "llm", providerStorageAlias); ... }}` (ProviderDetailPageClient.tsx:2043-2045), and handleAddCustomModel posts only `{ providerAlias, id, type }` (ProviderDetailPageClient.tsx:772-778). The backend agrees: `CreateCustomModelRequest { providerAlias, id, type, name }` has no `caps` field (src/server/api/models_custom.rs:36-45) and `CustomModel` (src/types/mod.rs:264-276) only carries caps incidentally through `#[serde(flatten)] extra`, which is never populated on the create path.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's Add Custom Model form has no capability toggles and never sends `caps`, so a user cannot declare a custom model as vision/reasoning-capable.
- **Why it matters**: Capability overrides feed `getCapabilitiesForModel`, `detectRequiredCapabilities` and `reorderByCapabilities` in the combo path. A user who adds a custom vision model (e.g. a self-hosted `qwen2.5-vl`) gets it routed to a non-vision combo member, producing upstream 400s on image content; the exact failure 9router's toggle exists to prevent. It also makes the provider page's ModelRow/ModelSelectModal capability badges permanently empty for custom rows (openproxy's CapacityBadges reads the same `caps` key that is never written).
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/providers/[id]/AddCustomModelModal.js:47-57, 74-88, 110-122; .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/providers/[id]/page.js:537-545, 1859-1860; .tmp/9router/src/app/api/models/custom/route.js:8-12, 35-36; .tmp/9router/src/lib/db/repos/aliasRepo.js:34-46; .tmp/9router/src/shared/hooks/useModelCaps.js:14-17. OpenProxy: web/src/components/providers/AddCustomModelModal.tsx:11, 96-109; web/src/components/providers/ProviderDetailPageClient.tsx:772-778, 2043-2045; src/server/api/models_custom.rs:36-45; src/types/mod.rs:264-276
- **Parity fix**: Add `caps?: Record<string, boolean>` to `CreateCustomModelRequest` (serde camelCase, defaults to None) and to `CustomModel` as an explicit field rather than relying on `flatten`. Re-add the Capabilities Toggle row to AddCustomModelModal.tsx seeded from `Object.fromEntries(Object.keys(CAPACITY_META).map(k => [k, false]))`, change `onSave` to `(modelId: string, caps: Record<string, boolean>) => Promise<void>`, call `onSave(cleanId, caps)` in handleSave, and thread the second arg through ProviderDetailPageClient's `onSave={async (modelId, caps) => handleAddCustomModel(modelId, "llm", providerStorageAlias, caps)}` so the body becomes `{ providerAlias, id, type, caps }`. Mirror 9router's `sanitizeCaps` (drop non-boolean values) on the Rust side before persisting.

## P17-001 · P0 · CLI Tools — OpenClaw configuration

- **9router**: `GET/POST/DELETE /api/cli-tools/openclaw-settings` reads, writes and deletes `~/.openclaw/openclaw.json` — `.tmp/9router/src/app/api/cli-tools/openclaw-settings/route.js`. The response shape the card depends on is `{installed, settings:{models:{providers:{"9router":{baseUrl,...}}, agents:{defaults:{model:{primary}}}}, agents:[...]}` (consumed at `.tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/cli-tools/components/OpenClawToolCard.js:41-45, 80-98`).
- **OpenProxy**: The route does not exist. `src/server/api/cli_tools/openclaw_settings.rs:22` registers only `GET /api/cli-tools/openclaw-config` — a different path with a different read-only shape `{baseUrl, apiKey, defaultModel, availableModels, providers}` (`src/server/api/cli_tools/openclaw_settings.rs:33-39`). There is no POST and no DELETE anywhere. Yet the ported dashboard card still calls the 9router paths: `web/src/components/cli-tools/OpenClawToolCard.tsx:172` (GET), `:203` (POST), `:231` (DELETE), and both status maps point at it (`web/src/components/CLIToolsPageClient.tsx:16`, `web/src/components/cli-tools/ToolDetailClient.tsx:35`).
- **Gap**: The OpenClaw CLI tool card GETs/POSTs/DELETEs `/api/cli-tools/openclaw-settings`, which the OpenProxy backend does not register — the surface was replaced by an unrelated read-only `/api/cli-tools/openclaw-config`.
- **Why it matters**: Every OpenClaw operation in the dashboard 404s: the status card always renders "Not installed", the save button cannot write `~/.openclaw/openclaw.json`, and the reset button cannot delete it. OpenClaw is one of the 18 primary CLI tools this dashboard is required to configure (per AGENTS.md core surfaces).
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/cli-tools/openclaw-settings/route.js:1-50; .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/cli-tools/components/OpenClawToolCard.js:103, 140, 170. OpenProxy: src/server/api/cli_tools/openclaw_settings.rs:22 (route = /api/cli-tools/openclaw-config, GET only); web/src/components/cli-tools/OpenClawToolCard.tsx:172, 203, 231; web/src/components/CLIToolsPageClient.tsx:16; web/src/components/cli-tools/ToolDetailClient.tsx:35.
- **Parity fix**: Add `GET`/`POST`/`DELETE /api/cli-tools/openclaw-settings` to `src/server/api/cli_tools/openclaw_settings.rs` mirroring the 9router handler: read/parse `~/.openclaw/openclaw.json` (tolerating JSONC trailing commas and unparseable-as-empty), detect install via `which openclaw` or the config file's existence, and return `{installed, settings, agents}` where `settings` is the parsed file. Normalise `agents[].model` to a string (`typeof m === "string" ? m : m?.primary ?? ""`, per `.tmp/9router/src/app/api/cli-tools/openclaw-settings/route.js:15-19`) because the card calls `.startsWith()` on it. Keep `/api/cli-tools/openclaw-config` as an additive extra.

## P18-001 · P0 · Combo / solo-capacity-adapter dispatch — stream decision

- **9router**: chatCore.js reads the real Accept header for EVERY dispatch, including combo members: `const acceptHeader = clientRawRequest?.headers?.accept || ""` … `if (clientPrefersJson && !clientPrefersSSE && body.stream !== false && !providerRequiresStreaming) stream = false;`. The combo path threads the raw request down to it: handleChat → handleComboChat({ handleSingleModel: withCapacityAdapterStripping((b, m) => handleSingleModelChat(b, m, clientRawRequest, request, apiKey), …) }) at src/sse/handlers/chat.js:127-140 (combo) and :147-157 (solo capacity adapter), and handleSingleModelChat forwards clientRawRequest into handleChatCore at src/sse/handlers/chat.js:270-295. The comment at chatCore.js:135-137 names the exact motivation: 'This fixes AI SDK compatibility where clients send Accept: application/json'.
- **OpenProxy**: The combo closure hardcodes `None` for accept: `apply_stream_plan(&mut combo_plan, &body, None, client_tool_for_combo, None)` with the in-code comment 'Accept header not available inside combo closure — use body only'. resolve_stream_flags therefore skips its Accept branch entirely for every combo member, and only the direct (ModelRouteKind::Direct) branch at chat.rs:894-900 passes accept_header.as_deref().
- **Gap**: Combo (and solo capacity-adapter) legs never see the client's Accept header, so the AI-SDK JSON-compat rule 9router applies to them is silently dropped in OpenProxy.
- **Why it matters**: Any OpenAI-compatible client that sets `Accept: application/json` without `text/event-stream` and omits `stream` — Vercel AI SDK, LangChain, many agent frameworks — gets a raw `text/event-stream` body back instead of a JSON object when the model is behind a combo or a capacity adapter. The SDK fails to parse the response (or silently yields an empty completion), so the request is broken for every combo-backed model, not just an edge case. For forceStream providers the same omission additionally forces stream=true + SSE→JSON aggregation inside the combo leg where 9router would have gone straight to native non-streaming JSON.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:137-142 (Accept rule), src/sse/handlers/chat.js:127-140 and :147-157 (clientRawRequest threaded into combo members), src/sse/handlers/chat.js:270-295 (passed to handleChatCore). OpenProxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:836-843 (accept = None in combo closure), src/server/api/chat.rs:894-900 (direct branch passes accept_header.as_deref()), src/core/utils/stream_flags.rs:94-102 (the branch that is skipped).
- **Parity fix**: Thread the real Accept header into the combo closure instead of passing None — capture `accept_header` (already in scope at chat.rs:420) into the `move` closure and pass `Some(accept_str)` as the third argument to `apply_stream_plan` at chat.rs:837-843. The existing sibling code at chat.rs:894-900 already does exactly this for the direct branch, so the fix is a one-line change plus moving the borrow into the closure. Leave the separate response-cache guard comment at chat.rs:441-456 alone; it is about the cache, not the stream plan.

## P19-001 · P0 · Cursor API auth header `x-cursor-checksum` (open-sse/utils/cursorChecksum.js → src/core/utils/cursor_checksum.rs)

- **9router**: `generateCursorChecksum` builds the 6-byte timestamp array with `(timestamp >> 40) & 0xFF` and `(timestamp >> 32) & 0xFF` (cursorChecksum.js:48-56). JavaScript's `>>` coerces the operand to int32 AND masks the shift count to 5 bits, so `ts >> 40` is really `ts >> 8` and `ts >> 32` is really `ts >> 0`. For a live timestamp (e.g. 1790262) that yields bytes [81,54,0,27,81,54] → base64 `9MPF4bSH` + machineId. The value is sent as `x-cursor-checksum` on every Cursor request via `buildCursorHeaders` (executors/cursor.js:295).
- **OpenProxy**: `generate_cursor_checksum` uses true u64 shifts on the same value (cursor_checksum.rs:51-58): `((downscaled >> 40) & 0xFF)`, `((downscaled >> 32) & 0xFF)`, which yield [0,0,0,27,81,54] → base64 `paaotuvi` + machineId. The doc comment at cursor_checksum.rs:8-10 explicitly claims it "replicates that exact behaviour" because "the upper 16 bits are always zero" — that reasoning is incorrect: it ignores JS shift-count masking. The value is sent as `x-cursor-checksum` (executor/cursor.rs:1704-1705, built at 2088/2258).
- **Gap**: The two implementations emit a structurally different `x-cursor-checksum` header value for the same instant, because OpenProxy uses mathematical 64-bit shifts where 9router uses JavaScript's int32 + `shift & 31` semantics.
- **Why it matters**: `x-cursor-checksum` is the Cursor API auth-integrity header, produced by an obfuscation ("Jyh") cipher over the current timestamp. The server de-obfuscates it to recover the timestamp and validate recency. OpenProxy's first two ciphertext bytes are wrong, so the server decodes a garbage timestamp. Verified empirically: sampling 3611 timestamps across one hour, 100% of them diverge at byte 0 and byte 1; for ts=1790262 the encoded prefixes are `9MPF4bSH` (9router) vs `paaotuvi` (OpenProxy). Every Cursor request from OpenProxy carries an invalid checksum, breaking the whole Cursor provider path. The existing tests only assert `!checksum.is_empty()` (executor/cursor.rs:3268-3271) and `ends_with(machineId)` (cursor_checksum.rs:166-169), so the byte-level bug is uncovered.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/utils/cursorChecksum.js:48-56 (byte array), :58-63 (Jyh cipher), :117-120 (x-cursor-checksum header), .tmp/9router/open-sse/executors/cursor.js:295 (call site). OpenProxy: src/core/utils/cursor_checksum.rs:51-58 (byte array), :60-64 (cipher), :8-10 (incorrect rationale comment); src/core/executor/cursor.rs:1704-1705 (header), :2088/:2258 (build call). Executed both algorithms on a frozen timestamp to confirm.
- **Parity fix**: In `src/core/utils/cursor_checksum.rs::generate_cursor_checksum`, reproduce JavaScript semantics explicitly: coerce the value to u32 first, then apply the masked shift counts. `let ts32 = downscaled as u32;` then byte0 = `((ts32 >> (40 & 31)) & 0xFF) as u8` (= `>> 8`), byte1 = `((ts32 >> (32 & 31)) & 0xFF) as u8` (= `>> 0`), and keep bytes 2..6 as-is. Update the module doc comment to state the real reason (int32 coercion + 5-bit shift-count mask), and add a regression test with a frozen timestamp asserting the exact 9router output (e.g. ts=1790262 → `9MPF4bSH`).

## P20-001 · P0 · Cursor AgentService error path (agent.v1.AgentService/Run)

- **9router**: `decodeAgentFrames` explicitly SKIPS Connect-RPC trailer frames — `if (!(flags & COMPRESS_FLAG.TRAILER)) onFrame(payload);` (cursor.js:155) — so a trailer-borne `{"error":{...}}` JSON is never dispatched to `onFrame` and the turn ends via the `finally` block's `if (!finished) onEvent({type:"done"})`, yielding a normal 200 completion. The ONLY source of an `error` event is the unsupported-exec-tool branch (cursor.js:582). In non-streaming mode that error returns a structured JSON body with `status: HTTP_STATUS.BAD_REQUEST` (400) and `{error:{message, type:"api_error"}}` (cursor.js:600-614). In streaming mode it emits `sseChunk({error:{message, type:"api_error"}})` + `SSE_DONE` at 200, with no `code` field (cursor.js:639-643).
- **OpenProxy**: `consume_agent_frames` PARSES trailer frames and turns a trailer `{"error":...}` into `AgentEvent::Error(msg, is_rate_limit)` (cursor.rs:599-618), early-returning an SSE-framed error body. `consume_agent_stream` emits that error as `data: {...}\n\n` + `data: [DONE]` **unconditionally, without checking `stream`** (cursor.rs:737-749). `execute_agent` then sets `content_type = if request.stream {"text/event-stream"} else {"application/json"}` and `status = if is_rate_limit {429} else {200}` (cursor.rs:2184-2190). A non-streaming request therefore receives `content-type: application/json` with an SSE-framed body, at 200/429 instead of 400. The `unsupported IDE tool` error (cursor.rs:669) takes the same unconditional-SSE path. OpenProxy also adds a `code` field the JS never emits.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy surfaces the Connect-RPC trailer error that 9router discards, and on any Cursor agent error it returns an SSE-shaped body with a JSON content-type and a 200/429 status where 9router returns a clean 400 JSON for non-streaming requests.
- **Why it matters**: A non-streaming OpenAI-compatible client on the Cursor agent path (any request with `stream` not `true`, plus the unsupported-IDE-tool case) gets a body of `data: {...}\n\ndata: [DONE]\n\n` advertised as `application/json`. `response.json()` throws an unparseable-body error instead of surfacing a structured `api_error`, and the account-fallback layer sees 200/429 rather than 400, so a recoverable IDE-tool refusal is either silently swallowed or mis-routed into rate-limit backoff. Additionally, a trailer-borne `resource_exhausted` that 9router treats as a completed turn now aborts the turn with 429.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/executors/cursor.js:155, 582, 600, 605, 639-643 | openproxy: src/core/executor/cursor.rs:599-618, 669, 737-749, 2184-2190
- **Parity fix**: In `consume_agent_frames` (cursor.rs:599), drop the trailer branch entirely so trailer payloads are never dispatched — mirror cursor.js:155 by returning early on `CONNECT_RPC_FLAG_TRAILER`. Then make `AgentEvent::Error` mode-aware in `consume_agent_stream`: when `stream == false` return a plain JSON `{"error":{"message":…,"type":"api_error"}}` payload and have `execute_agent` map it to HTTP 400; only emit the `data:` SSE frame + `[DONE]` (with no `code` key) when `stream == true`, keeping the overall status at 200. Remove the `is_rate_limit_error_body` 429 remap at cursor.rs:2184-2189 so trailer errors cannot fabricate a rate-limit status.

## P21-001 · P0 · Dashboard shutdown action (Sidebar / Profile)

- **9router**: Three dashboard call sites POST to `/api/version/shutdown`, which is a dashboard-session-gated route (allow-listed in `src/dashboardGuard.js:44`). The handler always returns `{success:true, message:"Shutting down for manual update..."}` then `process.exit(0)` after 500ms — `.tmp/9router/src/app/api/version/shutdown/route.js:10-20`; callers at `.tmp/9router/src/shared/components/Sidebar.js:95`, `.tmp/9router/src/shared/components/HeaderMenu.js:47`, `.tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/profile/page.js:747`.
- **OpenProxy**: `/api/version/shutdown` does not exist at all (no route registered in `src/server/api/mod.rs:421` or any submodule). The OpenProxy dashboard was ported to POST to `/api/shutdown` instead, which is the *different* 9router route: `src/server/api/shutdown.rs:15` registers only `POST /api/shutdown`, gated on `NODE_ENV=production` → 403 and `Authorization: Bearer $SHUTDOWN_SECRET` → 401 `{"success":false,"message":"Unauthorized"}` (`src/server/api/shutdown.rs:18-56`). Callers: `web/src/shared/components/Sidebar.tsx:179`, `web/src/components/ProfilePageClient.tsx:800`.
- **Gap**: The dashboard's Shutdown action targets an endpoint whose auth contract is `Bearer $SHUTDOWN_SECRET` (not set by the dashboard) instead of the session-gated `/api/version/shutdown`, so the modal always 401s and the server never exits.
- **Why it matters**: A user clicking "Shut down" in the Sidebar updater flow or the Profile Security section gets a silent 401 (the call is wrapped in try/catch and its result is never checked) and the dashboard shows a "disconnected" overlay while the process is still running. The intended self-update handoff never happens.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/version/shutdown/route.js:10-20; .tmp/9router/src/dashboardGuard.js:44; .tmp/9router/src/shared/components/Sidebar.js:95; .tmp/9router/src/shared/components/HeaderMenu.js:47; .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/profile/page.js:747. OpenProxy: src/server/api/shutdown.rs:15-56 (only /api/shutdown registered); no /api/version/shutdown anywhere in src/; web/src/shared/components/Sidebar.tsx:179; web/src/components/ProfilePageClient.tsx:800.
- **Parity fix**: Add `POST /api/version/shutdown` in a new `src/server/api/version_shutdown.rs` (merge into the admin-gated router, or the same `remaining` tier with `require_dashboard_or_management_api_key` per handler), returning `{"success":true,"message":"Shutting down for manual update..."}` then signalling `state.signal_shutdown()` after a 500ms `tokio::time::sleep` — mirroring the existing delayed-shutdown pattern already in `src/server/api/shutdown.rs:58-62`. Repoint `web/src/shared/components/Sidebar.tsx:179` and `web/src/components/ProfilePageClient.tsx:800` at `/api/version/shutdown`. Leave `POST /api/shutdown` in place (it is a separate 9router route with the `SHUTDOWN_SECRET` contract).

## P22-001 · P0 · Deep-link / session-gate for all /dashboard/* HTML

- **9router**: `src/dashboardGuard.js:233` intercepts every `pathname.startsWith("/dashboard")` request. It reads `requireLogin` from the DB (`:239-240`), blocks tunnel/tailscale hostnames when `tunnelDashboardAccess !== true` (`:244-251`), then either passes through (`:258`) or issues `NextResponse.redirect(new URL("/login", request.url))` (`:266`, `:270`). The same proxy also redirects `/` -> `/dashboard` (`:274-276`).
- **OpenProxy**: `src/server/dashboard/mod.rs:45-63` `dashboard_fallback` resolves the embedded/disk/proxied asset with no auth check at all. `src/lib.rs:41-47` layers only `real_ip_middleware` and permissive CORS. `is_rust_owned_path` (`:66-73`) reserves only `/api`, `/v1`, `/codex` — `/dashboard/**` is served verbatim to anyone.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy serves the full dashboard HTML shell for any unauthenticated `/dashboard/*` deep link instead of redirecting to `/login`, and it never applies the tunnel/tailscale host gate.
- **Why it matters**: Opening the OpenProxy dashboard over a LAN IP, the Cloudflare tunnel, or Tailscale renders API-key management, tunnel control and shutdown UI to an unauthenticated visitor. The follow-up `/api/*` calls 401, so the page looks broken rather than gated, and the 9router contract (deep link -> `/login` -> `?next`-less re-entry) is lost.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/dashboardGuard.js:233-271, :274-276 | OpenProxy: src/server/dashboard/mod.rs:45-63, :66-73; src/lib.rs:41-47
- **Parity fix**: Add a `dashboard_auth_middleware` between the api router and `server::dashboard::routes()` in `src/lib.rs` that (a) redirects `/` -> `/dashboard`, (b) for any path starting with `/dashboard`, reads `requireLogin` + `tunnelDashboardAccess` from the settings snapshot, redirects to `/login` when a valid `auth_token` session cookie is absent, and (c) redirects to `/login` when the Host matches the configured tunnel/tailscale hostname and `tunnelDashboardAccess` is not true.

## P23-001 · P0 · Embeddings credential selection and account fallback

- **9router**: handleEmbeddings runs a `while (true)` account-selection loop: it calls `getProviderCredentials(provider, excludeConnectionIds, model)` each pass (src/sse/handlers/embeddings.js:97-98), and when `handleEmbeddingsCore` returns `success:false` it calls `markAccountUnavailable(connectionId, status, error, provider, model)` (:153), and on `shouldFallback` adds the id to `excludeConnectionIds` and loops (:157-160). Success calls `clearAccountError` (:133). Only when every account is exhausted does it return 503 with `unavailableResponse(status, msg, retryAfter, retryAfterHuman)` (:106), which also emits a `Retry-After` header (open-sse/utils/error.js:116-131).
- **OpenProxy**: execute_media_handler resolves the connection exactly once: `select_media_connection(&snapshot, provider, model)` (src/server/api/media.rs:518), which is `select_media_connections(snapshot, provider).into_iter().next()` (:713-721) — the first active connection sorted by `priority`, then discarded. There is no loop, no exclusion set, and no call to any account-state/cooldown function anywhere in media.rs. A failure is returned straight to the client.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy picks a single highest-priority connection and returns its error; 9router rotates through accounts and only 503s once all are exhausted.
- **Why it matters**: A user with two OpenAI embedding keys (or one free + one paid tier) gets a hard failure the moment the top-priority key is rate-limited, quota-exhausted, or revoked, instead of a transparent rotation. Worse, because no cooldown is ever written, the same bad key is re-selected on every subsequent request, so the endpoint is permanently down for that provider even after a second key could have served it. This is the single most commonly relied-upon 9router behaviour for multi-account setups.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/sse/handlers/embeddings.js:97, :98, :133, :153, :157, :106 | OpenProxy: src/server/api/media.rs:518, :713-721, :726-742
- **Parity fix**: Port the account loop: make embeddings use `select_media_connections` (plural) and iterate. After each attempt, run the result through the existing `crate::core::account_fallback::check_fallback_error` (src/core/combo/mod.rs:570) and `apply_error_state` (src/core/account_fallback/mod.rs:750) to persist the cooldown exactly as the chat path does, add the connection id to an exclusion set, and retry. When all connections are exhausted, emit 503 with the `Retry-After` header plus the human-readable retry string, matching `unavailableResponse`. On success, clear the account error as 9router does at embeddings.js:133.

## P24-001 · P0 · Executor retry counts and per-URL retry placement

- **9router**: `tryRetry` re-issues against the SAME url via `urlIndex--; continue` and is keyed on `retryAttemptsByUrl[urlIndex]`, so a 502 retries up to 3× on that url BEFORE any fallback url is tried; only after retries are exhausted does `shouldRetry` (429-only) advance `urlIndex`. Network exceptions map to the 502 bucket and get the same 3 retries before falling through to the next url. `shouldRetry(status, urlIndex)` returns true for 429 only when `urlIndex + 1 < getFallbackCount()`.
- **OpenProxy**: The retry loop is `for retry in 0..3 { send_one(...).await?; ... }`. (a) `send_one` is `?`-propagated, so a network/transport error returns immediately — no 502-bucket retry, no fallback-url advance. (b) 502 retries only `if retry < 2 && url == urls.last().unwrap()` — so a 502 on a NON-last url returns immediately with no retry and no advance. (c) 503 mirrors 502. (d) 504 `if retry < 1` = 1 retry vs 9Router's 2. (e) 429/404 advance to the next url only via `break` after the current url's inner loop ends. Also: the whole 429/502/503 arm is dead for every provider, because `fallback_urls` is only ever populated by `with_fallback`, which is `#[allow(dead_code)]` and called from nowhere.
- **Gap**: Network errors and non-final-URL 502/503 responses bypass retries and fallback entirely in OpenProxy, and 9Router's 504 gets 2 retries vs OpenProxy's 1.
- **Why it matters**: A single DNS blip, TCP reset, or TLS handshake failure kills the request in OpenProxy with a 500 where 9Router silently retries three times with a 3 s backoff and then tries the next base URL. Because the 502/503 retry arm is gated on `url == urls.last()`, a provider that ever does get a fallback url configured will not retry a 502/503 on the primary — the exact case the 9Router code was written for. An `ExecutorError::Request` maps to a 500 in chat.rs, so account fallback (`check_fallback_error`) is also skipped, pinning the failure to one connection.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/executors/base.js:107-125 (tryRetry, `urlIndex--; continue`, retryAttemptsByUrl), open-sse/executors/base.js:137 (urlIndex--; continue), open-sse/executors/base.js:163 (tryRetry on BAD_GATEWAY for network errors), open-sse/executors/base.js:83-85 (shouldRetry), open-sse/config/runtimeConfig.js:69-74 (502:3/3000, 503:3/2000, 504:2/3000). openproxy: src/core/executor/default.rs:2093-2096 (`for retry in 0..3` + `?` on send_one), src/core/executor/default.rs:2182 (`if retry < 2 && url == urls.last().unwrap()`), src/core/executor/default.rs:2204 (503 same gate), src/core/executor/default.rs:2218 (504 `retry < 1`), src/core/executor/default.rs:565 (`with_fallback` is `#[allow(dead_code)]`, zero callers), src/core/executor/default.rs:2322 (`urls.extend(self.config.fallback_urls.clone())`).
- **Parity fix**: Rework the loop to mirror base.js: keep a per-url retry counter map; on a `send_one` Err, run the 502 retry bucket (3 attempts × 3 s) against the SAME url, then advance to the next url; on a 502/503 response, retry the same url (3×) regardless of position, only falling through to the next url once the bucket is exhausted; give 504 two retries; advance on 429 only when a further url exists. Separately, wire `fallback_urls` for the providers whose 9router registry declares `baseUrls` (kiro's 3 endpoints, antigravity) so the multi-url path is live rather than dead.

## P25-001 · P0 · GET /v1/models, /v1/models/{kind}

- **9router**: Loads `disabledByAlias = await getDisabledModels()` (:288-293) and defines `isDisabled(alias, modelId)`. Both model-emission branches skip disabled ids: the no-connections branch `if (isDisabled(alias, model.id)) continue;` (:329) and the active-connections branch `if (isDisabled(outputAlias, modelId) || isDisabled(staticAlias, modelId)) continue;` (:483). Result: a model the operator disabled on the Providers page never appears in the advertised list.
- **OpenProxy**: `build_models_list` / `list_models_for_kinds` in `src/server/api/v1_models.rs` never reads `db.extra["disabledModels"]`. The string "disabled" does not occur in the file at all (`grep -c disabled src/server/api/v1_models.rs` → 0). The dead helper `is_model_disabled` / `disabled_models_from_db` exists at `src/server/api/models_disabled.rs:51` and `:43` but has zero callers anywhere in the tree. Every model is emitted regardless of disable state.
- **Gap**: `GET /v1/models` in OpenProxy applies no disabled-model filter, so every model the operator disabled is still advertised to clients.
- **Why it matters**: This is one of the four declared core product surfaces (AGENTS.md: "Providers page — user controls Available Models (disable/enable/custom)"). Disabling a model is the operator's kill-switch for a broken, billing, or deprecated model. Claude Code, Codex, Cline and Cursor all populate their model picker from `GET /v1/models`, so a disabled model keeps reappearing in the user's picker and keeps being selected; the request then dispatches to the account the operator explicitly took out of rotation.
