The dashboard is correct (`ModelSelectModal.tsx:232` fetches `/api/models/disabled` and filters at `:310`, `:375`, `:464`), so the UI shows the model as OFF while the API that clients actually consume still serves it — the two views permanently disagree.
- **Evidence**: 9router: `/Users/tranquangdang21/Projects/openproxy/.tmp/9router/src/app/api/v1/models/route.js:288-293` (load), `:294` (isDisabled def), `:329` (static branch filter), `:483` (connection branch filter). OpenProxy: `/Users/tranquangdang21/Projects/openproxy/src/server/api/v1_models.rs:88-378` (build_models_list, zero disabled handling); dead helper at `src/server/api/models_disabled.rs:43-64`.
- **Parity fix**: In `build_models_list` (`src/server/api/v1_models.rs`), load the disabled map once via `super::models_disabled::disabled_models_from_db(snapshot)` (make the fn `pub(crate)` — it already is) and skip ids in both emission branches, mirroring 9router: in the `active_connections.is_empty()` static branch check `isDisabled(provider_entry.alias, model.id)`, and in the per-connection branch check `isDisabled(output_alias, model_id) || isDisabled(static_alias, model_id)` before `models.push(model_card(...))`. This makes the existing `is_model_disabled` helper live code and restores parity on the provider page ⇄ model picker ⇄ `/v1/models` invariant.

## P26-001 · P0 · GET /v1/models/info — single-model metadata

- **9router**: `GET /v1/models/info?id={alias}/{modelId}&kind={kind}` returns the model object *bare at the top level* — `{id, name, kind, owned_by, endpoint, params, capabilities, options, dimensions, contextWindow, voicesUrl?, searchTypes?/maxResults?/required?}` — `.tmp/9router/src/app/api/v1/models/info/route.js:18-36, 86-110`. Missing `id` → 400 `{error:{message:"Missing required query param: id (e.g. ?id=openai/dall-e-3)", type:"invalid_request_error"}}`; unknown model → 404 `{error:{message:"Model not found: {id}", type:"not_found"}}` (`:87-108`).
- **OpenProxy**: `GET /v1/models/info?model={model}` (note: `model`, not `id`) and the result is wrapped in an envelope: `Json(json!({"object":"model.info","data": info}))` — `src/server/api/v1_models.rs:642-653` and `:723`. The inner object is also a different shape: `{id, provider, model, routeKind, name?, quotaFamily?, strip?, targetFormat?, upstreamModelId?, contextWindow?, capabilities?, kind?, vision?, reasoning?, providerContextWindow?, providerMaxOutput?, tools?}` — `src/server/api/v1_models.rs:671-721`. Missing param → 400 `{error:{message:"model parameter required", type:"invalid_request_error"}}`; unknown model is **not** 404 — it falls through to the no-catalog branch and still returns 200.
- **Gap**: Different query-parameter name (`model` vs `id`), a `{object, data}` wrapper instead of a bare object, a different field set, and a missing-404 that silently returns 200 for unknown ids.
- **Why it matters**: Any OpenAI-compatible client that probes `GET /v1/models/info?id=openai/gpt-4o` gets a 400 in OpenProxy but the real metadata in 9Router. A client that follows the 9Router contract and reads the result at the top level finds only `{object, data}` and sees an undefined model. A client that uses this to validate a model id before calling chat cannot distinguish a typo from a valid id, because the 404 branch was never ported.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/v1/models/info/route.js:18-36 (buildInfo), :86-110 (GET handler, id param, bare-JSON return, 400/404 bodies). OpenProxy: src/server/api/v1_models.rs:648 (`params.get("model")`), :650-653 (400 body), :671-676 (inner object), :723 (envelope), and no 404 branch anywhere in the handler.
- **Parity fix**: In `models_info` (`src/server/api/v1_models.rs:642`), read the query param as `id` first and fall back to `model` (so both the 9router and the existing OpenProxy call sites keep working). Return the info object bare — `Json(info)` — not `{object, data}`. Emit the 9router field set: `owned_by` = the alias, `endpoint` = the `KIND_ENDPOINT[kind]` mapping (`/v1/chat/completions`, `/v1/images/generations`, `/v1/audio/speech`, `/v1/audio/transcriptions`, `/v1/embeddings`, `/v1/search`, `/v1/fetch`), plus `params`/`options`/`dimensions`/`contextWindow`. Add the missing 400 (`"Missing required query param: id (e.g. ?id=openai/dall-e-3)"`) and 404 (`"Model not found: {id}"`, `type:"not_found"`) branches, and keep the `{object, data}` form under an additive `?envelope=1` if any in-tree consumer needs it.

## P27-001 · P0 · Multi-endpoint transports — auth descriptor and static headers

- **9router**: The selected transport carries BOTH its own static headers and its own auth descriptor, and both win: `const headers = { "Content-Type": "application/json", ...(rt ? rt.headers : this.config.headers) }; const desc = rt?.auth || AUTH_DESCRIPTORS[this.provider] || this.resolveAuthDescriptor();` then `applyAuth(headers, desc, credentials)`. For minimax the claude transport is `{ format: "claude", baseUrl: "https://api.minimax.io/anthropic/v1/messages", urlSuffix: "?beta=true", headers: { ...CLAUDE_API_HEADERS }, auth: { combined: true, header: "x-api-key", scheme: "raw" } }`, and CLAUDE_API_HEADERS = { "Anthropic-Version": "2023-06-01", "Anthropic-Beta": "claude-code-20250219,interleaved-thinking-2025-05-14" }. Because desc.combined is true, applyAuth sets exactly `x-api-key: <token>` plus anthropic-version. So a Claude client on minimax/minimax-cn/glm/kimi/deepseek/xiaomi gets x-api-key + Anthropic-Version + Anthropic-Beta on the /anthropic/v1/messages leg.
- **OpenProxy**: RuntimeTransport carries only a base URL: `pub struct RuntimeTransport { #[serde(default)] pub base_url: Option<String> }`. chat.rs sets only that field. Auth is then chosen from the provider id alone, with no reference to which transport was selected: the x-api-key branch is `matches!(provider, "glm" | "kimi" | "agentrouter" | "enally")`, and `else if matches!(provider, "minimax" | "minimax-cn")` inserts `Authorization: Bearer {token}`. The anthropic-version + x-api-key branch is gated on `is_anthropic_compatible` (a provider_node of type anthropic-compatible) or a provider id starting with "anthropic-compatible" — neither is true for a first-class `minimax` connection. Net effect for a Claude client on minimax: Authorization: Bearer, no Anthropic-Version, no Anthropic-Beta. Only xiaomi-tokenplan/xiaomi-mimo have a transport-aware auth branch (default.rs:1737-1752).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's transport record cannot carry an auth descriptor or static headers, so selecting the claude transport of a multi-endpoint provider changes the URL but leaves the request authenticated as if it were the OpenAI transport.
- **Why it matters**: Claude Code, Cline, Zed and Kiro post Claude-format bodies and are routed to `minimax/MiniMax-*`, `minimax-cn/*`, or `deepseek/*`. 9router sends `x-api-key` + `Anthropic-Version: 2023-06-01` + the interleaved-thinking beta flag to the Anthropic-compatible endpoint; OpenProxy sends `Authorization: Bearer` with no Anthropic-Version. Those endpoints reject Bearer-only and/or require Anthropic-Version, answering 401 or 400. The 401 then trips checkFallbackError's status-401 rule and locks the account for the 120s long cooldown, so a combo that worked on 9router silently falls through every member and the user sees 'no active credentials'. The missing Anthropic-Beta also disables interleaved thinking on the claude leg even when the request succeeds.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/executors/default.js:149-155 (rt.headers and rt.auth win), open-sse/executors/default.js:21-37 (applyAuth / combined), open-sse/providers/registry/minimax.js:43-56 (claude transport auth+headers), open-sse/providers/registry/minimax-cn.js:43-56, open-sse/providers/registry/deepseek.js:45-54, open-sse/providers/shared.js:28-31 (CLAUDE_API_HEADERS). OpenProxy: src/types/mod.rs:114-118 (RuntimeTransport = base_url only), src/server/api/chat.rs:1709-1714 (only base_url populated), src/core/executor/default.rs:1681-1684 (is_anthropic_compatible gate), src/core/executor/default.rs:1796-1803 (minimax → Authorization: Bearer), src/core/executor/default.rs:1737-1752 (transport-aware auth exists only for xiaomi).
- **Parity fix**: Extend RuntimeTransport to carry the fields the 9router transport objects carry — `headers: Vec<(String,String)>`, `auth_header: Option<String>`, `auth_scheme: Option<String>` (raw|bearer), `anthropic_version: Option<bool>` — and populate them in `provider_transports()` (src/core/chat/mod.rs:313-425) alongside each `base_url` from the corresponding 9router registry entry. In DefaultExecutor's header builder, mirror default.js:149-155: when `credentials.runtime_transport` is set, apply its headers instead of the provider config headers and use its auth descriptor instead of the provider-id match. Add the same transport-aware branch for `minimax`/`minimax-cn`/`deepseek` that already exists for `xiaomi-tokenplan`/`xiaomi-mimo` (default.rs:1737-1752).

## P28-001 · P0 · Non-SSE upstream response during a streaming request (chatCore streamingHandler)

- **9router**: `handleStreamingResponse` (`open-sse/handlers/chatCore/streamingHandler.js:62-82`) blocks ANY content-type that is neither `text/event-stream` nor `application/json` — an allow-list. It reads the body, extracts `<title>` from an HTML error page, strips all HTML tags, collapses CR/LF, clamps to 160 chars, and returns `{error:{message:"[<status>]: <shortMsg>"}}` at the **upstream's own status** (`providerResponse.status || 502`) with `Content-Type: application/json`. The status is preserved, so a Cloudflare 429 or an upstream 503 surfaces as 429/503 to the client, not as 502.
- **OpenProxy**: `handle_streaming_response` (`src/server/api/chat.rs:3755-3791`) uses a **deny-list narrowed to three types**: the guard fires only when the content-type contains `text/html`, `application/json`, OR `text/plain`. A non-SSE body served as `application/xml`, `application/octet-stream`, `application/x-protobuf`, or any unlisted type is piped straight through as SSE. It also ignores the upstream status entirely and always returns `StatusCode::BAD_GATEWAY` (502). The body snippet is included raw (clamped 500 chars) rather than tag-stripped and clamped to 160.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's non-SSE guard is a 3-type deny-list returning a hardcoded 502, where 9router uses an allow-list that preserves the upstream status code and sanitizes the body.
- **Why it matters**: Two client-visible consequences. (1) Status: a Cloudflare 429 / upstream 503 HTML page reaches the client as 502 in OpenProxy but as 429 / 503 in 9router. Claude Code and Codex back off on 429 and retry on 503; a flattened 502 causes an immediate retry with no backoff, turning a rate-limited upstream into a hot retry loop. (2) Guard coverage: an upstream error page served as `application/xml` (common for some gateway appliances) or any exotic type bypasses the deny-list and is piped into the SSE transform, which is exactly the Next.js "failed to pipe response" crash the allow-list was written to prevent — and in axum it yields a corrupt SSE stream with no terminal `[DONE]`, so the client hangs until the stall timeout. The raw-body echo additionally forwards upstream HTML verbatim into an error field that dashboards may render as HTML, whereas 9router tag-strips it.
- **Evidence**: 9router: `.tmp/9router/open-sse/handlers/chatCore/streamingHandler.js:62-82` (allow-list + `<title>` extraction + `const status = providerResponse.status || 502` at :78). OpenProxy: `src/server/api/chat.rs:3755-3762` (deny-list condition), `:3765-3766` (raw body clamp 500), `:3775-3780` (no tag-stripping), `:3790` (`StatusCode::BAD_GATEWAY` hardcoded).
- **Parity fix**: Rewrite the guard in `src/server/api/chat.rs:3755` to the 9router allow-list: fire when `!ct.is_empty() && !ct.contains("text/event-stream") && !ct.contains("application/json")` (drop the octet-stream/ndjson exemptions and the three-type deny-list). Parse the body for `<title>([^<]+)</title>`, strip `<[^>]*>`, collapse `[\r\n]+` to a space, trim, clamp to 160 chars, and fall back to a generic `Upstream returned non-SSE response ({ct})`. Return `Response::builder().status(status)` using the upstream `status` (falling back to 502) rather than the hardcoded `BAD_GATEWAY`.

## P29-001 · P0 · OAuth connect — Trae / Windsurf / Zed proxy flow

- **9router**: OAuthModal has a dedicated startProxyFlow(providerId): it calls start-proxy to get a dynamic callback port and callbackUrl, calls /authorize with redirect_uri set to that callbackUrl, POSTs /register-session to register the session (Zed also posts its RSA code_verifier in the body so the key never lands in a URL), opens the popup, and polls /poll-status?state=... until the local proxy auto-exchanges. stop-proxy is called on close for codex, xai, trae, windsurf and zed.
- **OpenProxy**: OAuthModal has no proxy-flow branch: startOAuthFlow goes straight to the device-code list and then to GET /authorize with a fixed http://localhost:<port>/callback redirect. stop-proxy is only fired for codex and xai. The backend authorize_oauth_compat has no trae/windsurf/zed arm, so the call returns 500 Unknown provider.
- **Gap**: Trae, Windsurf and Zed fall through to the generic authorize path and fail with HTTP 500 Unknown provider.
- **Why it matters**: These three providers require a dynamic-port local callback server so the browser redirect lands on the running proxy. Without the branch the user gets a hard error on Connect and no way to authenticate, and the session-registration step that lets the proxy match the incoming callback is missing from both the modal and the route table.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/shared/components/OAuthModal.js:182-210 (startProxyFlow: start-proxy then authorize then register-session then popup), :219-222 (PROXY_OAUTH_PROVIDERS dispatch) and :430-438 (stop-proxy for codex/xai/trae/windsurf/zed); backend .tmp/9router/src/app/api/oauth/[provider]/[action]/route.js:139-180 (start-proxy for trae/windsurf/zed) and :303-315 (register-session). OpenProxy: web/src/shared/components/OAuthModal.tsx:195-352 (no proxy branch) and :369-373 / :547-549 (stop-proxy only codex/xai); src/server/api/oauth.rs:3488-3592 (authorize_oauth_compat match with no trae/windsurf/zed arm, fallback at :3591).
- **Parity fix**: Port startProxyFlow into web/src/shared/components/OAuthModal.tsx and dispatch it when the provider is trae, windsurf or zed. On the Rust side add trae/windsurf/zed arms to authorize_oauth_compat, add POST /api/oauth/{provider}/register-session, and extend start-proxy / poll-status / stop-proxy to cover the three providers (Zed deriving native_app_port from the proxy callback URL and registering its RSA code_verifier server-side rather than in the query string).

## P30-001 · P0 · OAuth connect — device-code flow

- **9router**: GET /api/oauth/[provider]/device-code looks the provider up in the OAuth registry and rejects only when providerData.flowType !== "device_code". Every registered device-code provider (github, kiro, kimi, kimi-coding, kilocode, codebuddy-cn, codebuddy-intl, qoder, grok-cli) is served, with a per-provider noPkceDeviceProviders list controlling whether a PKCE challenge is sent. The dashboard OAuthModal device-code list is exactly those providers.
- **OpenProxy**: GET /api/oauth/{provider}/device-code hard-gates on the provider name: `if provider != "kiro" { return 400 {"error":"Provider does not support device code flow"} }`. The OpenProxy OAuthModal still routes github, qwen, kiro, kimi, kimi-coding, kilocode, codebuddy, codebuddy-cn, qoder, grok-cli and kimchi down the device-code branch and calls that GET.
- **Gap**: Every device-code OAuth provider except Kiro is rejected with HTTP 400 by the endpoint the dashboard modal actually calls.
- **Why it matters**: Clicking Connect on GitHub, Kimi, Kilocode, Qoder, Grok CLI, Kimchi, codebuddy-cn/intl or Qwen opens the OAuth modal, immediately fetches /device-code, receives 400, and drops the user into the modal error state. Nine of the twenty-one OAuth providers cannot be connected at all.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/oauth/[provider]/[action]/route.js:238-283 (device-code action, flowType guard + noPkceDeviceProviders) and .tmp/9router/src/shared/components/OAuthModal.js:225-235 (deviceCodeProviders list). OpenProxy: src/server/api/oauth.rs:2918-2928 (the kiro-only guard) and web/src/shared/components/OAuthModal.tsx:195-212 (deviceCodeProviders list, fetch at :212).
- **Parity fix**: Replace the kiro-only guard in start_device_code_compat with the registry-driven equivalent: look the provider up in the OAuth provider table, return 400 only when its flowType is not device_code, and implement the per-provider device_authorization request for GitHub, Kimi, Kilocode, codebuddy, Qoder and Grok CLI. Keep the existing Kiro branch as the kiro specialization and add a noPkceDeviceProviders equivalent so those providers omit the PKCE challenge.

## P31-001 · P0 · OAuth flow — device-code POLL transition

- **9router**: `POST /api/oauth/[provider]/poll` destructures `const { deviceCode, codeVerifier, extraData } = body` (camelCase) and the shipped modal sends exactly `{ deviceCode, codeVerifier, extraData }`. The whole device-code state machine is therefore reachable end-to-end from the dashboard.
- **OpenProxy**: `poll_device_code` parses the body as a raw `serde_json::Value` and reads `body.get("device_code")` (snake_case) → returns 400 `missing_device_code`. The camelCase `deviceCode` key is only honoured on the kiro compatibility branch (`if provider == "kiro" && body.get("deviceCode").is_some()`). The dashboard posts `deviceCode`, so every non-kiro device-code provider can never advance past `pending`.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's poll endpoint requires a snake_case field the dashboard never sends, so the pending→authorized transition is unreachable.
- **Why it matters**: Even with P0-001 fixed, no non-kiro device-code login can complete: every poll returns HTTP 400, the modal surfaces the error and drops to the `error` step. The provider list therefore shows the account as never connected.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/oauth/[provider]/[action]/route.js:487-490 and .tmp/9router/src/shared/components/OAuthModal.js:146-149. OpenProxy: src/server/api/oauth.rs:4902-4912 (`body.get("device_code")`) vs :4892 (kiro-only camelCase branch); web/src/shared/components/OAuthModal.tsx:152-157.
- **Parity fix**: Accept both spellings: replace the raw `Value` lookup with a `#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]` body struct carrying `#[serde(alias = "device_code")] device_code: Option<String>`, `code_verifier`, and `extra_data` — exactly the shape `KiroCompatPollBody` (oauth.rs:483-491) already models — and use it for every provider, not just kiro.

## P32-001 · P0 · OAuth flow — device-code START transition

- **9router**: `GET /api/oauth/[provider]/device-code` checks `providerData.flowType !== "device_code"` and otherwise serves a device code for EVERY device-code provider registered in the OAuth registry: github, kiro, kimi, kimi-coding, kilocode, codebuddy-cn, codebuddy-intl, qoder, grok-cli. Each provider's own `flowType` is `"device_code"` in src/lib/oauth/providers/{github,kimi,kilocode,codebuddy-cn,codebuddy-intl,qoder,grok-cli,kiro}.js.
- **OpenProxy**: `GET /api/oauth/{provider}/device-code` maps to `start_device_code_compat`, whose FIRST statement is `if provider != "kiro" { return 400 "Provider does not support device code flow" }`. The full multi-provider implementation exists (`start_device_code`, gated by `is_device_code_provider`) but is only bound to the *underscore* route `POST /api/oauth/{provider}/device_code`. The dashboard calls the hyphen route.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy refuses the "start device-code login" transition for 8 of the 9 device-code providers that 9router accepts.
- **Why it matters**: Clicking "Connect" for GitHub Copilot, Kimi, Kilocode, Qoder, Grok CLI/Grok Build or CodeBuddy returns an immediate HTTP 400 "Provider does not support device code flow". The provider can never be authenticated from the dashboard — the flow is not degraded, it is unreachable.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/oauth/[provider]/[action]/route.js:238-240 and :262-286; provider registry flowType values in .tmp/9router/src/lib/oauth/providers/github.js / kimi.js / kilocode.js / codebuddy-cn.js / codebuddy-intl.js / qoder.js / grok-cli.js. OpenProxy: src/server/api/oauth.rs:2922-2928 (kiro-only guard), :6603/:6627/:6629 (route table), :689-703 (`is_device_code_provider` full set), :212 in web/src/shared/components/OAuthModal.tsx (dashboard calls `/device-code`).
- **Parity fix**: Drop the `provider != "kiro"` early-return in `start_device_code_compat`; either forward non-kiro providers to the existing `start_device_code` body (converting the GET query into its inputs) or repoint the dashboard at `POST /api/oauth/{provider}/device_code`. Keep the kiro-specific OIDC client-registration path untouched.

## P33-001 · P0 · OpenProxy `src/server/api/chat.rs:265` and every other `/v1` modality gate

- **9router**: Every LLM handler gates on `settings.requireApiKey` — an independent key from the dashboard `requireLogin`. `src/sse/handlers/chat.js:71` `if (settings.requireApiKey) { … "Missing API key" / "Invalid API key" }`; the same predicate at `src/sse/handlers/embeddings.js:56`, `imageGeneration.js:40`, `tts.js:37`, `stt.js:32`, `search.js:49`, `fetch.js:52`, `videoGeneration.js:47`. Toggling `requireLogin` off does not touch LLM key enforcement.
- **OpenProxy**: The chat gate reads the dashboard-login flag: `src/server/api/chat.rs:265` `if require_api_key_auth && state.db.snapshot().settings.require_login { require_api_key_with_reload(...) }`. The identical wrong predicate is repeated at `src/server/api/v1_models.rs:81`, `src/server/api/stt.rs:180`, `src/server/api/media.rs:464` (audio voices), `:1183` (image/video generate), `:1506` (video get), and `src/server/api/web_fetch.rs:77`.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy keys the LLM API-key gate on `settings.requireLogin` (a dashboard-auth flag) instead of the independent `settings.requireApiKey`, coupling two settings that 9router keeps separate.
- **Why it matters**: Turning OFF "Require login" in Profile → Security silently disables API-key enforcement on every `/v1` endpoint — chat, messages, responses, embeddings, images, TTS, STT, video, web fetch and model listing all start accepting anonymous callers. 9router leaves the LLM gate untouched by that toggle. Conversely the only way to enforce keys is to keep the dashboard locked, which is the opposite intent.
- **Evidence**: 9router: `src/sse/handlers/chat.js:71`, `src/sse/handlers/embeddings.js:56`, `src/sse/handlers/tts.js:37`, `src/sse/handlers/stt.js:32`, `src/sse/handlers/search.js:49`, `src/sse/handlers/fetch.js:52`, `src/sse/handlers/videoGeneration.js:47`, `src/sse/handlers/imageGeneration.js:40`. OpenProxy: `src/server/api/chat.rs:265`, `src/server/api/v1_models.rs:81`, `src/server/api/stt.rs:180`, `src/server/api/media.rs:464,1183,1506`, `src/server/api/web_fetch.rs:77`
- **Parity fix**: Introduce the `require_api_key` settings field (see P0-001) and replace `state.db.snapshot().settings.require_login` with `state.db.snapshot().settings.require_api_key` at all seven gate sites, keeping the 9router ordering (parse body first where 9router does, then key check, then model check).

## P34-001 · P0 · OpenProxy `src/types/mod.rs` (Settings), `src/server/api/mod.rs` (UpdateSettingsRequest), `web/src/components/EndpointPageClient.tsx`

- **9router**: `requireApiKey` is a first-class settings key defaulting to `true` (`src/lib/db/repos/settingsRepo.js:27`). `/api/settings` is a generic blob round-trip: GET spreads the whole settings object and PATCH merges the request body into the stored JSON, so any key the dashboard writes persists verbatim (`src/app/api/settings/route.js:33-36`, `:66-68` via `updateSettings` in `src/lib/db/repos/settingsRepo.js:57-68`). The endpoint page reads it back with `setRequireApiKey(data.requireApiKey || false)` (`src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/endpoint/EndpointPageClient.js:212`) and PATCHes `{requireApiKey: value}` (`:244-252`), so the toggle is durable across reloads.
- **OpenProxy**: `Settings` has no `require_api_key` member at all — only `require_login` (`src/types/mod.rs:524`). The PATCH body is deserialized into a fixed allowlist struct `UpdateSettingsRequest` (`src/server/api/mod.rs:2236-2300`) that has no `require_api_key`, and the apply block (`:2427-2600`) writes each field individually, so serde silently discards `requireApiKey`. The page still PATCHes it (`web/src/components/EndpointPageClient.tsx:408-415`) and optimistically sets local state, but `safe_settings_payload_with_db_path` (`src/server/api/mod.rs:700+`) never emits the field, so on reload `data.requireApiKey || false` is `undefined || false` = false (`:365`).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy has no `requireApiKey` setting, so the "Require API key" toggle on the endpoint page is a write-only no-op that silently reverts to OFF on every reload.
- **Why it matters**: A user exposing OpenProxy via Cloudflare tunnel or Tailscale flips "Require API key" ON, the switch renders ON, the security banner disappears — then reloads the page and the switch is OFF again because nothing was stored. They believe the endpoint is protected when the server never received the setting, and the `!requireApiKey` security warning is permanently stuck on.
- **Evidence**: 9router: `src/lib/db/repos/settingsRepo.js:27`, `src/app/api/settings/route.js:33-36`, `src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/endpoint/EndpointPageClient.js:212,244-252`. OpenProxy: `src/types/mod.rs:524`, `src/server/api/mod.rs:2236-2300`, `web/src/components/EndpointPageClient.tsx:365,408-415`
- **Parity fix**: Add `#[serde(default = "default_true", deserialize_with = "deserialize_null_default")] pub require_api_key: bool` to `Settings` (src/types/mod.rs) with `require_api_key: true` in the `Default` impl at :746, add `require_api_key: Option<bool>` to `UpdateSettingsRequest` and a `db.settings.require_api_key = v;` arm in the apply block. No dashboard change needed — the existing PATCH/GET keys will then round-trip.

## P35-001 · P0 · POST /api/provider-nodes (Add OpenAI/Anthropic Compatible form)

- **9router**: POST validates in order: 400 "Name is required" if `!name?.trim()`, 400 "Prefix is required" if `!prefix?.trim()` (provider-nodes/route.js:37-44); then for `openai-compatible` it 400s "Invalid OpenAI compatible API type" unless `apiType ∈ {chat, responses}` (route.js:47-51) and mints a type-tagged id `${OPENAI_COMPATIBLE_PREFIX}${apiType}-${generateId()}` with `prefix: prefix.trim()`, `name: name.trim()` (route.js:52-61); `custom-embedding` strips a trailing slash and a trailing `/embeddings` from baseUrl (route.js:63-78); `anthropic-compatible` strips a trailing slash and a trailing `/messages` (route.js:80-96); any other type 400s "Invalid provider node type" (route.js:98-99). Because the id is minted as `openai-compatible-chat-<id>` / `anthropic-compatible-<id>`, the dashboard's `isOpenAICompatibleProvider(providerId)` (constants/providers.js:84-89) matches, so the created node routes to the compatible-provider detail page.
- **OpenProxy**: `create_provider_node` (src/server/api/provider_nodes.rs:82-125) deserializes `{name, prefix?, apiType?, baseUrl?, type?}` and writes it verbatim: `let id = uuid::Uuid::new_v4().to_string();` then `ProviderNode { id, r#type: req.r#type.unwrap_or("openai-compatible"), name: req.name, prefix: req.prefix, api_type: req.api_type, base_url: req.base_url, ... }` → 201. There is no name-required check, no prefix-required check, no apiType whitelist, no baseUrl sanitization, and no type whitelist. The id is a bare UUID with no `openai-compatible-` / `anthropic-compatible-` / `custom-embedding-` prefix, so `isOpenAICompatibleProvider(providerId)` on the dashboard (web/src/shared/constants/providers.ts:273-277) returns false for a freshly created node.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's compatible-node form submits to a backend that performs none of 9router's five validations and mints unprefixed UUID node ids, so a node created through the form is not recognised as a compatible provider.
- **Why it matters**: Two concrete breakages. (1) A user who submits the form with an empty/whitespace name or prefix gets a 201 and a node that later fails routing, instead of an immediate inline 400. (2) Because the id lacks the `openai-compatible-` prefix, clicking through to `/dashboard/providers/<id>` after creation hits `isCompatible === false`, so the page renders the generic provider layout (no Default Model field, no compatible-models section) for what is in fact a custom endpoint. It also means an `anthropic-compatible` node keeps a `/messages` suffix in `baseUrl` that the runtime will append a second time, producing `…/messages/messages` and a 404 on every request.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/provider-nodes/route.js:37-99; .tmp/9router/src/shared/constants/providers.js:84-89. OpenProxy: src/server/api/provider_nodes.rs:69-79 (request struct), 82-125 (handler, id minting at :92 and verbatim insert at :99-107); web/src/shared/constants/providers.ts:273-277
- **Parity fix**: In `create_provider_node` (src/server/api/provider_nodes.rs), reject `name.trim().is_empty()` with 400 "Name is required" and `prefix` missing/blank with 400 "Prefix is required". Whitelist `type` to `openai-compatible` | `anthropic-compatible` | `custom-embedding` (else 400 "Invalid provider node type"). For `openai-compatible`, require `api_type ∈ {chat, responses}` (else 400 "Invalid OpenAI compatible API type") and mint `id = format!("openai-compatible-{}-{}", api_type, uuid)`; for `anthropic-compatible` mint `anthropic-compatible-<uuid>`; for `custom-embedding` mint `custom-embedding-<uuid>`. Trim `name`/`prefix`/`base_url`, and strip the trailing slash plus a trailing `/messages` for anthropic-compatible and a trailing `/embeddings` for custom-embedding before persisting.

## P36-001 · P0 · POST /api/providers (admin provider creation)

- **9router**: Runs `normalizeProviderId(body.provider)` (:90) — trims, then slugifies `toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/g,"-")`, then resolves a case-insensitive display-name match back to the canonical id (`src/lib/providerNormalization.js:12-23`). Validates the result against a closed set (APIKEY_PROVIDERS ∪ FREE_TIER_PROVIDERS ∪ dual-auth ∪ WEB_COOKIE_PROVIDERS ∪ openai-compatible ∪ anthropic-compatible ∪ custom-embedding) and returns 400 `{error:"Invalid provider"}` on miss (:108-118). Also exempts `ollama-local` from the API-key requirement: `if (!apiKey && provider !== "ollama-local")` → 400 (:119-121).
- **OpenProxy**: `create_provider_api` (`src/server/api/mod.rs:1097`) only checks `provider.trim().is_empty()` → 400 "Provider is required" (:1106-1112). Any non-empty string is accepted and persisted verbatim into `default_conn.provider`. The API-key check at `:1129-1139` is unconditional — `ollama-local` cannot be created keyless. The string "Invalid provider" appears nowhere in the provider-creation path.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy accepts and persists any arbitrary provider string on POST /api/providers and has no `ollama-local` API-key exemption, so typo'd or hostile provider names create permanently undispatchable connections and Ollama cannot be configured.
- **Why it matters**: A connection whose `provider` has no catalog entry and no executor arm is undispatchable: `DefaultExecutor::new` returns `ExecutorError::UnsupportedProvider` and the if/else chain falls through to the generic else. The user sees a successful 201 and a card on the Providers page, then every request to that provider fails at runtime with no indication the name was never valid. Because `/v1/models` (EDGE-001) also does not filter, a bogus provider is not even detectable by listing. The `ollama-local` side is separate and concrete: local Ollama needs no credential, and 9router deliberately permits creating it without one; OpenProxy returns 400 and the user cannot add a local Ollama server through the normal path.
- **Evidence**: 9router: `.tmp/9router/src/app/api/providers/route.js:90` (normalizeProviderId), `:108-118` (closed-set validation → 400), `:119-121` (ollama-local key exemption); normalization logic at `.tmp/9router/src/lib/providerNormalization.js:12-23`. OpenProxy: `src/server/api/mod.rs:1106-1112` (empty-only check), `:1129-1139` (unconditional key requirement), `:1163-1172` (provider persisted verbatim).
- **Parity fix**: Add to `create_provider_api`, after the empty check: (a) slug-normalize `provider` exactly as `normalizeProviderId` does (`provider.to_lowercase().replace(|c: char| !c.is_ascii_alphanumeric(), "-")`, trimming leading/trailing `-`) and resolve display-name aliases against `crate::core::model::catalog::provider_catalog()`; (b) reject with 400 `{"error":"Invalid provider"}` when the normalized id is neither in the catalog, nor an `openai-compatible-*` / `anthropic-compatible-*` / `custom-embedding-*` node, nor a web-cookie provider; (c) relax the key check to `if provider != "ollama-local"` and, for `ollama-local`, normalize `baseUrl` into `providerSpecificData` the way `normalizeProviderSpecificData` does.

## P37-001 · P0 · POST /api/providers/test-batch (engine used)

- **9router**: `testSingleConnection(conn.id)` — the batch route reuses the exact same engine as the single-connection Test button, so each row gets the provider-specific OAuth/API-key probe arm, the connection-proxy pre-test, token refresh, and the `updateProviderConnection({ testStatus, lastError, lastErrorAt, ...newTokens })` write-back. The loop is strictly sequential with a per-connection try/catch.
- **OpenProxy**: `test_provider_batch` calls `test_provider_api(provider, api_key, base_url)` (providers.rs:653) — a separate, minimal prober whose match arms are only `openai`, `anthropic`, `gemini`, `openrouter`, and a `_` fallback that GETs `{baseUrl}/models`. It never touches `test_provider_connection`, never runs the proxy pre-test, never does OAuth, and never writes `test_status`/`last_error`/`last_error_at`. It is additionally wrapped in a 10 s `tokio::time::timeout` (:1097) versus the 15 s used by the single-connection path.
- **Gap**: Even with the payload contract fixed, batch testing runs a completely different code path from single-connection testing and persists nothing.
- **Why it matters**: Batch results would disagree with the per-connection Test button for every OAuth provider, and the provider page's `testStatus` badges / `lastError` tooltips would never update after a batch run — the dashboard would show stale green for a key that just failed, or stale red for one that just passed.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/providers/test-batch/route.js:52 (isActive filter), :88-107 (calls `testSingleConnection`, sequential loop, per-row try/catch), .tmp/9router/src/app/api/providers/[id]/test/testUtils.js:830-896 (the shared engine incl. :893 persistence). OpenProxy: src/server/api/providers.rs:1096-1103 (`test_provider_api` + 10 s timeout), :653-660 (the separate prober's entry), src/server/api/provider_connection_test.rs:145 (the persist that is never reached)
- **Parity fix**: Extract the single-connection engine body of `provider_connection_test::test_provider_connection` into a reusable `pub(crate) async fn test_connection_and_persist(state, id) -> ProviderTestResponse` and call it from `test_provider_batch` inside the same sequential `for id in ids` loop, with a per-connection `match` that turns a panic/transport error into a failed row rather than aborting the batch. Return 9router's row shape (`provider, connectionId, connectionName, authType, valid, latencyMs, error, diagnosis, statusCode, testedAt`) plus `summary: { total, passed, failed }` and `testedAt`. Delete the `test_provider_api` call from the batch path (it may stay for the `openproxy provider test` CLI verb, which is not a parity surface).

## P38-001 · P0 · POST /api/providers/{id}/test — OAuth probe table

- **9router**: `OAUTH_TEST_CONFIG` defines real probes for grok-cli (`GET https://cli-chat-proxy.grok.com/v1/user` with the official CLI header set, `acceptStatuses: [402]`, `softFailMessage`, `refreshable: true`), qoder (`GET https://openapi.qoder.sh/api/v1/userinfo`, `refreshable: false`) and kimchi (`GET KIMCHI_CONFIG.validationUrl` with `User-Agent: kimchi/0.1.40`, `refreshable: false`). Each falls through to `classifyOAuthProbeResult` and persists the verdict.
- **OpenProxy**: `oauth_probe_request` has arms for codex, gemini-cli/antigravity, github, iflow, kilocode and gitlab only; every other provider hits `_ => None` (:1647) and the caller returns `invalid("Provider test not supported")` (:356). `grep` for `grok-cli`, `qoder` and `kimchi` in provider_connection_test.rs returns 0 hits.
- **Gap**: grok-cli, qoder and kimchi OAuth connections always report "Provider test not supported" and are persisted as `testStatus: "error"`.
- **Why it matters**: Three healthy OAuth accounts are permanently shown as failed. "Test Connection One-by-One" reports 0/3 passed, the provider page renders a red badge for accounts that route traffic correctly, and a user chasing a "broken" connection will re-authorize a credential that never needed it.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/providers/[id]/test/testUtils.js:66-76 (qoder), :95-105 (kimchi), :106-128 (grok-cli), :395-442 (generic dispatch). OpenProxy: src/server/api/provider_connection_test.rs:1570-1649 (arm list), :1647 (`_ => None`), :356 (`invalid("Provider test not supported")`); providers confirmed present in web/src/shared/constants/providers.ts:17 (qoder), :107 (grok-cli), :127 (kimchi)
- **Parity fix**: Add the three arms to `oauth_probe_request`. grok-cli: `GET {PROVIDERS["grok-cli"].userUrl}` with `Accept: application/json`, the `x-xai-token-auth: xai-grok-cli` / `x-grok-client-identifier: grok-pager` / `x-grok-client-version` headers from the registry, `Authorization: Bearer {token}`. qoder: `GET https://openapi.qoder.sh/api/v1/userinfo`. kimchi: `GET {KIMCHI validationUrl}` with `Accept: application/json` and `User-Agent: kimchi/0.1.40`. Model the accept/refresh policy as a small per-provider table (`refreshable: bool`, `accept_statuses: &[u16]`, `soft_fail: &[(u16, &str)]`) instead of hardcoding it in one match arm, and make the caller fall back to the same generic classification 9router uses.

## P39-001 · P0 · POST /v1/chat/completions, /v1/messages, /v1/responses, GET /v1/models (LLM API key gate)

- **9router**: Two independent settings. `settings.requireApiKey` (default `true`, `src/lib/db/repos/settingsRepo.js:27`) gates ONLY the LLM routes, checked inside the handler at `src/sse/handlers/chat.js:71-81` — and notably `GET /v1/models` does not check it at all. `settings.requireLogin` (default `true`, `settingsRepo.js:26`) gates the DASHBOARD via `src/dashboardGuard.js`. Turning the dashboard login off does not open `/v1/*`; turning the API-key requirement off does not open the dashboard.
- **OpenProxy**: There is no `requireApiKey` field on `Settings` at all (`src/types/mod.rs:477-540`), and none in `UpdateSettingsRequest` (`src/server/api/mod.rs:2238-2301`). The LLM routes gate on the single `settings.require_login` field instead: `chat_completions_impl` at `src/server/api/chat.rs:265-269`, `list_models_for_kinds` at `src/server/api/v1_models.rs:81-85`, `web_fetch.rs:77-79`, `chat_search.rs:209`. The dashboard still PATCHes `{requireApiKey: value}` (`web/src/components/EndpointPageClient.tsx:413`), which is silently dropped by the unknown-field-tolerant serde struct.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy collapses 9router's two independent settings into one `require_login` and uses it to gate the LLM API, so disabling dashboard login also disables LLM API-key auth, and the dashboard's API-key toggle is a no-op that writes to a field the server does not store.
- **Why it matters**: This is a security-relevant coupling plus a broken control. A user who follows OpenProxy's own dashboard to turn off "Require login" (a normal action when running on a trusted LAN, and the exact action 9router's own Endpoint page offers) simultaneously and unknowingly turns OFF API-key authentication on `/v1/*` — exposing the proxy to any host that can reach the port. Symmetrically, the "Require API key" toggle on the Endpoint page is inert: it PATCHes `requireApiKey`, the field is dropped, the toggle snaps to `false` locally (`if (res.ok) setRequireApiKey(value)` at `:415` — a 200 with the value ignored still looks success), and the user believes auth is on when it is not. The mismatch is also visible on read-back: `setRequireApiKey(data.requireApiKey || false)` at `:365` reads a field the server never returns, so the toggle always renders off.
- **Evidence**: 9router: `.tmp/9router/src/sse/handlers/chat.js:71-81` (requireApiKey gate), `.tmp/9router/src/lib/db/repos/settingsRepo.js:26-27` (two independent defaults), `.tmp/9router/src/dashboardGuard.js` (requireLogin for dashboard), `.tmp/9router/src/app/api/v1/models/route.js:566-580` (no key check). OpenProxy: `src/server/api/chat.rs:265-269` (gates on require_login), `src/server/api/v1_models.rs:81-85`, `src/types/mod.rs:524` (only require_login exists), `src/server/api/mod.rs:2238-2301` (no require_api_key in the PATCH struct), `web/src/components/EndpointPageClient.tsx:365` / `:413-416` (toggle reads/writes a non-existent field).
- **Parity fix**: Add `require_api_key: bool` to `Settings` with `default_true` and `#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]` (default `true`, matching `settingsRepo.js:27`), plus a `require_api_key: Option<bool>` field on `UpdateSettingsRequest` wired through `update_settings_api`. Change the LLM gates to test it: `if require_api_key_auth && state.db.snapshot().settings.require_api_key` at `src/server/api/chat.rs:265`, and the same in `v1_models.rs:81` / `web_fetch.rs:77` / `chat_search.rs:209`. Leave `require_login` governing only `require_dashboard_session`. Then, to match 9router exactly, drop the key check from `list_models_for_kinds` so `GET /v1/models` stays unauthenticated as it is upstream.

## P40-001 · P0 · Provider status machine — backoff-level escalation

- **9router**: `checkFallbackError` returns `newBackoffLevel` ONLY for rules carrying `backoff: true` (text: rate limit / too many requests / quota exceeded / capacity / overloaded; status 429). `markAccountUnavailable` persists `backoffLevel: newBackoffLevel ?? backoffLevel`, so a 400/402/403/404/5xx/transport error leaves the level untouched.
- **OpenProxy**: Both fallback arms pass `decision.new_backoff_level.unwrap_or(current_backoff + 1)`. `check_fallback_error` returns `new_backoff_level: None` for the `Cooldown` (401/402/403/404/text-fixed), `NoMatch` (5xx, transport) and `Permanent` (400) branches — so EVERY failing request increments the level, capped at 15.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy escalates the exponential-backoff level on every error; 9router escalates only on genuine rate-limit classifications.
- **Why it matters**: Five ordinary upstream 500s drive the level to 5; a handful more pin it at the 15 cap. The next genuine 429 then gets `getQuotaCooldown(15) = min(2000 * 2^14, 300000) = 5 minutes` instead of the 9router 2 seconds, so a single rate-limited request takes the account out of rotation for 150x longer.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/services/accountFallback.js:23-49 (newBackoffLevel only under `rule.backoff`) and .tmp/9router/src/sse/services/auth.js:275 (`backoffLevel: newBackoffLevel ?? backoffLevel`). OpenProxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:2888 and :2918 (`unwrap_or(current_backoff + 1)`), src/core/combo/mod.rs:572-602, src/core/config/error_config.rs:199-254, cooldown formula src/core/combo/mod.rs:268-276.
- **Parity fix**: Pass `decision.new_backoff_level.unwrap_or(current_backoff)` at chat.rs:2888 and :2918 so only `ErrorClassification::Backoff` advances the level, matching `newBackoffLevel ?? backoffLevel`.

## P41-001 · P0 · Provider status machine — re-activation reset

- **9router**: `resetHealthStateOnActivation(existing, patch)` runs inside BOTH `createProviderConnection` and `updateProviderConnection`. Whenever a write carries `testStatus: "active"` it force-clears `errorCode`, `rateLimitedUntil`, sets `backoffLevel: 0`, and nulls EVERY `modelLock_*` key on the existing row. This is the only "unlock the account" transition, and it fires on: OAuth re-login (`createProviderConnection({... testStatus:"active"})`), the dashboard "Test" button (`testSingleConnection` → `updateProviderConnection(id, {testStatus:"active",...})`), the API-key validate/save path, and the 401/403 token-refresh path.
- **OpenProxy**: There is no equivalent anywhere. `update_provider` sets `connection.test_status = Some(...)` and nothing else. `persist_test_result` sets only `test_status` / `last_error` / `last_error_at`. The 401/403 refresh write clears `last_error` / `last_error_at` / `error_code` / `backoff_level` but NOT `modelLock_*`, NOT `rate_limited_until`, and NOT `test_status`. `create_imported_oauth_connection` (OAuth re-login) copies tokens + `test_status` and leaves the lock fields intact.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy has no `Unavailable → Active` re-activation transition that clears the model-lock / rate-limit / backoff state.
- **Why it matters**: After a 429 lock, an account stays `testStatus: "unavailable"` with an active `modelLock_<model>` in OpenProxy forever, even after (a) a successful 401-refresh retry, (b) a green "Test" in the dashboard, or (c) a full re-login. `select_connection` keeps excluding it via `is_model_locked`, so the account is permanently unroutable until the original cooldown elapses — 9router recovers immediately.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/lib/db/repos/connectionsRepo.js:15-33 (the reset), :172 (create path), :222 (update path); consumed at .tmp/9router/src/app/api/providers/[id]/test/testUtils.js:865-893, .tmp/9router/src/app/api/oauth/[provider]/[action]/route.js:69 and :473. OpenProxy: src/server/api/admin_items.rs:279-281, src/server/api/provider_connection_test.rs:169-176, src/server/api/chat.rs:2826-2846, src/server/api/oauth.rs:1503-1524. Selection consumer: src/server/api/chat.rs:3002-3007.
- **Parity fix**: Add `pub fn reset_health_state_on_activation(existing: &ProviderConnection, patch_test_status: Option<&str>) -> bool` in `src/core/account_fallback` and call it from every OpenProxy write that sets `test_status = "active"` (admin_items update_provider, provider_connection_test persist_test_result, chat.rs 401/403 refresh, create_imported_oauth_connection): null every `modelLock_*` key in `extra`, set `error_code = None`, `rate_limited_until = None`, `backoff_level = Some(0)`.

## P42-001 · P0 · Providers list — batch test (Test all / oauth / free / apikey / per-provider)

- **9router**: POST /api/providers/test-batch takes {mode, providerId}; mode is validated against provider|oauth|free|apikey|compatible|all (400 otherwise), the connection set is derived server-side from getProviderConnections({isActive:true}) filtered by auth group, and the response is {mode, providerId, results[], summary:{total,passed,failed}, testedAt}. Each result reuses the same testSingleConnection as the single test and carries latencyMs, diagnosis, statusCode and testedAt.
- **OpenProxy**: POST /api/providers/test-batch takes a required providerIds: Vec<String> of connection UUIDs. It never receives mode/providerId, never filters by auth group or isActive, and returns only {results:[{providerId, valid, error, latencyMs}]} with no summary and no testedAt. The dashboard sends {mode, providerId} and reads data.summary without ever checking res.ok.
- **Gap**: The request body does not deserialize, so the entire batch-test surface returns 422 while the client reports No providers connected.
- **Why it matters**: Axum 0.8 answers 422 Unprocessable Entity for the missing providerIds field. The client parses the rejection body as JSON, data.summary is undefined, so the Test all chain silently reports 0/0 and shows No providers connected even when every provider is connected; a single-provider batch test renders the raw axum rejection instead of results. No connection is ever tested, so the per-connection health badges never refresh from a batch run.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/providers/test-batch/route.js:43-82 (mode validation + auth-group filtering + empty-case summary) and :116-126 (response envelope with summary/testedAt). OpenProxy: src/server/api/providers.rs:1040-1042 (TestBatchRequest with provider_ids) and :1069-1124 (handler, no mode handling, Json(TestBatchResponse { results }) at :1123); caller web/src/components/providers/ProvidersPageClient.tsx:436-443 (sends {mode, providerId}) and :455-463 / :488-499 (reads data.summary unguarded).
- **Parity fix**: Change TestBatchRequest to {mode: String, provider_id: Option<String>} and derive the connection set server-side from active connections using the same auth-group helper (free/oauth/apikey/cookie plus the openai-compatible-/anthropic-compatible- prefix test), returning 400 "Invalid mode. Use: provider, oauth, free, apikey, compatible, all" for unknown modes. Return {mode, providerId, results, summary:{total,passed,failed}, testedAt} and route each connection through the shared single-connection test that already persists testStatus/lastError, instead of the api-key-only test_provider_api.

## P43-001 · P0 · Providers page — batch test (3 "Test All" buttons)

- **9router**: POST /api/providers/test-batch reads `{ mode, providerId }`, selects the connection set by auth group (`oauth` / `free` / `apikey` / `compatible` / `all` / `provider`), tests each connection, and returns `{ mode, providerId, results, testedAt, summary: { total, passed, failed } }`. A group with zero connections returns HTTP 200 with `summary: { total: 0, passed: 0, failed: 0 }` (route.js:74-82, 115-126).
- **OpenProxy**: POST /api/providers/test-batch deserializes `TestBatchRequest { provider_ids: Vec<String> }` (camelCase → `providerIds`) and loops over `req.provider_ids` looking up connections by id. It returns `TestBatchResponse { results }` only — no `mode`, no `providerId`, no `summary`. There is no auth-group selection and no zero-connection branch. The dashboard sends `{ mode, providerId }` (ProvidersPageClient.tsx:436-443) and reads `data.summary` (ProvidersPageClient.tsx:490).
- **Gap**: The dashboard POSTs `{mode, providerId}` to a Rust handler that requires `{providerIds: [...]}` and returns a response with no `summary` field, so the entire batch-test loading/results flow is non-functional.
- **Why it matters**: Axum's `Json<T>` extractor rejects the body with 422 because `providerIds` is missing (no `#[serde(default)]` on the struct), so `runBatchTest` throws and every "Test All" click lands in the `catch`: `setTestResults({ error: "Test request failed" })` + `notify.error("Provider test failed")`. The button spins (testingMode set) and then always reports failure — the user can never batch-verify connections. Even if the 422 were fixed, the missing `summary` would leave `data.summary` undefined so the pass/fail/zero-count toasts would never fire and the results view would render an untyped payload.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/providers/test-batch/route.js:44-82 (request destructuring + connection selection), :115-126 (summary in response). OpenProxy: src/server/api/providers.rs:1038-1042 (TestBatchRequest{provider_ids}), :1069-1074 (Json(req) extractor), :1078-1123 (loop over provider_ids), :1044-1048 (TestBatchResponse has only `results`). Frontend: web/src/components/providers/ProvidersPageClient.tsx:436-443 (sends {mode, providerId}), :488-499 (reads data.summary). Route wiring: src/server/api/providers.rs:1172-1173.
- **Parity fix**: Change `TestBatchRequest` to `#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")] pub struct TestBatchRequest { pub mode: String, pub provider_id: Option<String> }`, reject an empty/missing mode with 400 `"mode is required"`, and select connections by auth group exactly as 9router does (`provider`+id, `oauth`, `free`, `apikey`, `compatible`, `all` → all active connections; anything else → 400 `"Invalid mode. Use: provider, oauth, free, apikey, compatible, all"`). Extend `TestBatchResponse` to carry `mode`, `provider_id: Option<String>`, `results`, `tested_at: String`, and `summary: { total: usize, passed: usize, failed: usize }`, and return the zero-connection short-circuit `results: []` + `summary: { total: 0, passed: 0, failed: 0 }` with HTTP 200 so empty sections do not look like failures. Keep the per-connection 10 s timeout already present.

## P44-001 · P0 · SSE inter-chunk stall watchdog

- **9router**: `pipeWithDisconnect` arms a 360 s timer (`STREAM_STALL_TIMEOUT_MS`, open-sse/config/runtimeConfig.js:53) that is re-armed on EVERY upstream byte (`armStall()` inside the `upstreamTap` TransformStream, open-sse/utils/streamHandler.js:227). The handler picks a per-provider override first: `const stallTimeoutMs = PROVIDERS[provider]?.stallTimeoutMs || STREAM_STALL_TIMEOUT_MS;` (open-sse/handlers/chatCore/streamingHandler.js:87) — qoder ships `stallTimeoutMs: 120000` (open-sse/providers/registry/qoder.js:23). Kiro additionally splits TTFT (200 s, `STREAM_FIRST_CHUNK_TIMEOUT_MS`) from post-first-chunk stall (open-sse/executors/kiro.js:559).
- **OpenProxy**: `SSE_STALL_TIMEOUT` is a hardcoded 180 s with no per-provider override: `const SSE_STALL_TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(180);` (src/server/api/chat.rs:110), applied as `tokio::time::timeout(SSE_STALL_TIMEOUT, upstream.try_next())` at src/server/api/chat.rs:3852 (Reqwest branch) and `body.frame()` at src/server/api/chat.rs:4012 (Hyper branch). The correctly-ported `STREAM_STALL_TIMEOUT_MS` (360 s) and `STREAM_FIRST_CHUNK_TIMEOUT_MS` (200 s) constants exist in src/core/config/runtime_config.rs:44,49 but are referenced by NOTHING outside that file (verified by grep) — they are dead constants.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's SSE stall watchdog is 180 s with no per-provider override, vs 9Router's 360 s default plus a qoder-specific 120 s; the ported 360 s/200 s constants are dead code.
- **Why it matters**: Any provider that pauses >180 s mid-stream (extended thinking on an Opus/Sonnet turn, a long tool-use reasoning block, Kiro EventStream frames buffering) is truncated by OpenProxy and the client sees a synthetic `Upstream SSE stream stalled` error frame instead of the completed answer. Claude Code / Codex sessions that legitimately idle 3-6 minutes on a hard reasoning step break deterministically. Qoder, which 9Router gives a SHORTER 120 s budget precisely because its SSE is chatty, instead gets a more generous 180 s from OpenProxy — wrong in the opposite direction for that provider.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/utils/streamHandler.js:192 (default param stallTimeoutMs=STREAM_STALL_TIMEOUT_MS), open-sse/utils/streamHandler.js:227 (armStall per chunk), open-sse/handlers/chatCore/streamingHandler.js:87 (per-provider override), open-sse/providers/registry/qoder.js:23 (stallTimeoutMs:120000), open-sse/config/runtimeConfig.js:53 (360s). openproxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:110 (180s), src/server/api/chat.rs:3852, src/server/api/chat.rs:4012; src/core/config/runtime_config.rs:44,49 (dead constants).
- **Parity fix**: Delete the local `SSE_STALL_TIMEOUT` and use `crate::core::config::runtime_config::STREAM_STALL_TIMEOUT_MS` (360 s) at both call sites. Add a `stall_timeout_ms: Option<u64>` field on `ProviderConfig` populated from the 9router registry (`PROVIDER_CONFIGS` entries gain `stall_timeout_ms` where the JS registry declares `stallTimeoutMs` — qoder 120 000) and resolve `config.stall_timeout_ms.unwrap_or(STREAM_STALL_TIMEOUT_MS)` per request, as 9Router does. Wire `STREAM_FIRST_CHUNK_TIMEOUT_MS` into a separate pre-first-chunk deadline for providers that declare one.

## P45-001 · P0 · Upstream transport timeout (reqwest client pool)

- **9router**: There is NO total request/response timeout on the fetch. `BaseExecutor.execute` arms only a CONNECT timeout via a per-attempt `AbortController` cleared as soon as response headers arrive (`clearTimeout(connectTimer)` right after `await proxyAwareFetch(...)`), and merges it with the client signal: `const mergedSignal = signal ? AbortSignal.any([signal, connectCtrl.signal]) : connectCtrl.signal`. `FETCH_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_MS` defaults to 60_000 (open-sse/config/runtimeConfig.js:59) and is per-provider overridable via `config.timeoutMs` (e.g. qoder 120000, ollama 30000, glm 10000). Once headers land, a long SSE stream may run indefinitely; only the inter-chunk `STREAM_STALL_TIMEOUT_MS` (360s) can end it (open-sse/utils/streamHandler.js:192-211).
- **OpenProxy**: `build_reqwest_client` sets an UNCONDITIONAL total-request timeout: `.connect_timeout(timeout.connect).timeout(timeout.stream)` with `DEFAULT_STREAM_TIMEOUT = 180s`. reqwest's `.timeout()` is the full request/response deadline, so any non-streaming response, forced-SSE-to-JSON aggregation, or dashboard aggregation that takes >180 s wall-clock is aborted with a transport error, and no per-provider `timeoutMs` equivalent exists. Note the Hyper transport (`use_hyper_transport`, used for `/chat/completions` without a proxy) is NOT affected — it only sets `set_connect_timeout` — so this only bites the Reqwest path (Anthropic `/v1/messages`, Gemini, `?beta=true` claude URLs, and anything behind a proxy).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy imposes a hard 180 s whole-request deadline on the Reqwest transport that 9Router does not have, and halves the connect timeout from 60 s to 30 s.
- **Why it matters**: A long non-streaming reasoning call (o200k-context Claude/Gemini request, a slow `/v1/messages` burst, a forced-SSE→JSON collapse) is killed mid-flight at 180 s and surfaces as a generic 500/transport error instead of completing. Agents retrying then burn quota. The connect-timeout halving also fails slow-but-progressing TLS handshakes (common on cold cloud egress) that 9Router tolerates for 60 s.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/executors/base.js:128-135 (connectCtrl + clearTimeout after headers), open-sse/config/runtimeConfig.js:59 (FETCH_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_MS=60s), open-sse/config/runtimeConfig.js:53 (STREAM_STALL_TIMEOUT_MS=360s), open-sse/providers/registry/qoder.js:22 (per-provider timeoutMs:120000). openproxy: src/core/executor/client_pool.rs:19 (DEFAULT_CONNECT_TIMEOUT=30s), src/core/executor/client_pool.rs:20 (DEFAULT_STREAM_TIMEOUT=180s), src/core/executor/client_pool.rs:187-188 (.connect_timeout(...).timeout(...)).
- **Parity fix**: Remove the total `.timeout(timeout.stream)` from `build_reqwest_client` (or set it to `None`) so only the connect deadline is enforced, matching 9Router. If a total cap is wanted for safety, apply it per-request as a header-arrival-only deadline (mirroring 9Router's connectCtrl) rather than a whole-body deadline. Restore `DEFAULT_CONNECT_TIMEOUT` to 60 s and add a per-provider `timeout_ms` field on `ProviderConfig` read from the 9router registry (qoder 120000, ollama 30000, glm/brave/exa/etc. 10000).

## P46-001 · P0 · open-sse/executors/cursor.js transformProtobufToSSE vs src/core/executor/cursor.rs transform_protobuf_to_sse

- **9router**: Cursor tool-call deltas are emitted on the FIRST frame that carries a tool call and incrementally thereafter: a new tool call is assigned `toolCallIndex = toolCalls.length`, pushed to `toolCalls`+`toolCallsMap`, and immediately written to `chunks` (cursor.js:982-1004); subsequent frames for the same id stream the argument delta `tc.function.arguments` (cursor.js:961-979). After the read loop, a finalization pass walks `toolCallsMap` and, for every id not in `finalizedIds`, assigns a fresh index and emits an SSE delta if one was not already emitted — explicitly guarding the case "stream may have ended without isLast=true" (cursor.js:1041-1076).
- **OpenProxy**: Tool-call SSE emission is gated entirely on `is_last_value` (cursor.rs:2810-2838 for an existing id, cursor.rs:2846-2871 for a new id); nothing is written on any non-final frame. The post-loop finalization block at cursor.rs:2936 is `if !tool_calls.is_empty() && !has_content`, but `has_content` was already set to `true` at cursor.rs:2874 on the very first tool-call frame, so that block is unreachable whenever a tool call exists. The finalizer also never assigns an index.
- **Gap**: Tool calls reach the client only if the LAST protobuf frame of the call carries `is_last`; if the stream ends without it, the tool call is silently dropped even though `finish_reason` is still reported as `tool_calls`.
- **Why it matters**: Cursor agent turns that request a tool produce a stream whose terminal chunk says `finish_reason:"tool_calls"` with no `tool_calls` payload at all. Claude Code / Codex / Cline read the tool call list, find it empty, and either surface an empty assistant turn or spin waiting for a call that never arrives. Because the block is also the only path that would emit without `is_last`, any upstream truncation or a protocol revision that drops the flag silently breaks the whole agentic loop for this provider.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/executors/cursor.js:949-951, :961-979, :982-1004, :1041-1076 (finalize "stream may have ended without isLast=true"), :1094 (finish_reason tool_calls). OpenProxy: src/core/executor/cursor.rs:2792-2875 (emission gated on is_last_value), :2874 (has_content = true), :2922-2933 (finalizer builds tool_calls), :2936 (unreachable `&& !has_content` gate), :2953-2957 (finish_reason computed from that list).
- **Parity fix**: Emit the tool-call SSE delta on the first frame that carries a tool call (not only on is_last), stream the per-frame argument delta thereafter, and make the post-loop finalization unconditional over `tool_call_map` (drop the `&& !has_content` term) so ids that never saw `is_last` are still emitted — mirroring cursor.js:982-1004 and :1042-1076.

## P47-001 · P0 · open-sse/executors/cursor.js:906-920 / :750-764 / :257-275 vs src/core/executor/cursor.rs:2762-2774 / :3018-3027 / :2299-2304

- **9router**: When a decoded frame is a JSON error object, `transformProtobufToSSE` (cursor.js:906-920) and `transformProtobufToJSON` (cursor.js:750-764) bail out and `return createErrorResponse(JSON.parse(text))`. `createErrorResponse` (cursor.js:257-275) normalizes the payload to `{error:{message,type,code}}` and returns a real `Response` with HTTP 429 `rate_limit_error` when `error.code === "resource_exhausted"`, otherwise HTTP 400 `api_error`.
- **OpenProxy**: The same frames are inlined as stream content: `transform_protobuf_to_sse` pushes `data: <raw error json>` + `[DONE]` and returns Ok (cursor.rs:2762-2774); `transform_protobuf_to_json` returns the raw body string (cursor.rs:3018-3027). `CursorExecutor::execute` then hardcodes `.status(200)` with content-type text/event-stream or application/json (cursor.rs:2299-2304). The `build_cursor_error_body` normalizer exists (cursor.rs:1890) but is wired only to the non-200 upstream-HTTP branch (cursor.rs:2324-2328), never to the in-stream path.
- **Gap**: A Cursor rate limit delivered inside the protobuf stream is returned to the client as HTTP 200 with the raw upstream error JSON as the payload, instead of 429/400 with a normalized error envelope.
- **Why it matters**: Status 200 means the account-fallback and combo-fallback layers never see a 429, so a rate-limited Cursor account is neither rotated nor quarantined — every subsequent request keeps hammering the same exhausted account. The client additionally receives an unmodelled `{"error":…}` blob where assistant content belongs, which strict clients render as garbage or drop.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/executors/cursor.js:257-275 (createErrorResponse, 429/400), :750-764 (JSON path returns it), :906-920 (SSE path returns it). OpenProxy: src/core/executor/cursor.rs:2762-2774, :3018-3027, :2299-2304 (`.status(200)`); normalizer present but unused on this path at :1890-1926 vs :2324-2328.
- **Parity fix**: Change `transform_protobuf_to_sse`/`transform_protobuf_to_json` to return a Result carrying the upstream status, and in the JSON-error branch call the already-present `build_cursor_error_body` (cursor.rs:1890) to set 429 for `resource_exhausted` / 400 otherwise, propagating that status instead of the hardcoded 200 at cursor.rs:2299.

## P48-001 · P0 · open-sse/executors/cursor.js:982 vs src/core/executor/cursor.rs:2826,2858

- **9router**: Each newly seen tool-call id gets `const toolCallIndex = toolCalls.length` (cursor.js:982) and both the new-call chunk (cursor.js:993) and the continuation chunk (cursor.js:968) carry that per-call `index`, so parallel tool calls are numbered 0, 1, 2, …
- **OpenProxy**: Every emitted tool-call delta hardcodes `"index": 0` — cursor.rs:2826 (existing-id branch) and cursor.rs:2858 (new-id branch). The post-loop finalizer (cursor.rs:2922-2933) does not attach an index either.
- **Gap**: All tool calls in a Cursor response are reported at index 0 regardless of how many there are.
- **Why it matters**: `index` is the field the OpenAI streaming contract uses to route `tool_calls` argument deltas to distinct calls. With every call pinned to 0, an OpenAI-compatible client (Claude Code, Codex, Cline) concatenates the argument fragments of N parallel tool calls into a single tool call, producing malformed JSON arguments and a wrong tool name. Cursor's agent mode emits parallel tool calls routinely, so this corrupts every multi-tool turn.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/executors/cursor.js:968 (`index: existing.index`), :982 (`const toolCallIndex = toolCalls.length;`), :993 (`index: toolCallIndex`). OpenProxy: src/core/executor/cursor.rs:2826 and :2858 (`"index": 0`).
- **Parity fix**: Track a monotonic counter in `transform_protobuf_to_sse`: assign `index = tool_calls.len()` the first time an id is seen, store it on the map entry, and emit that stored index in both the new-call and continuation deltas — exactly as cursor.js:982-1004 does.

## P49-001 · P0 · open-sse/rtk/autodetect.js:30 vs src/core/rtk/autodetect.rs:54-58 (+ Cargo.toml:162)

- **9router**: autoDetectFilter peeks the head with a CHAR-indexed slice: `const head = text.length > DETECT_WINDOW ? text.slice(0, DETECT_WINDOW) : text;` (open-sse/rtk/autodetect.js:30). JS string indexing is UTF-16 code-unit based and can never split a character, so this can never throw. The whole rtk/ tree is contractually fail-open ('any error returns null and leaves the body untouched — never throw out of them', open-sse/AGENTS.md; open-sse/rtk/index.js:83-86 wraps compressMessages in try/catch).
- **OpenProxy**: auto_detect_filter peeks the head with a BYTE index and no char-boundary guard: `let head = if text.len() > DETECT_WINDOW { &text[..DETECT_WINDOW] } else { text };` (src/core/rtk/autodetect.rs:54-58). The only catch_unwind in the RTK path wraps filter application (src/core/rtk/apply_filter.rs:19), not autodetect; compress_tool_text_owned calls auto_detect_filter directly (src/core/rtk/mod.rs:815). Cargo.toml:162 sets `panic = "abort"` in [profile.release] and there is no tower-http CatchPanicLayer in src/.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy slices a &str at a hard byte offset without a floor_char_boundary check, so a multi-byte character straddling byte 1024 panics, and with panic="abort" that panic terminates the whole proxy process.
- **Why it matters**: Any tool_result blob of 1025 bytes..10 MiB whose byte 1024 falls inside a multi-byte character — i.e. essentially any CJK or emoji content (the product explicitly targets Vietnamese/Japanese/Chinese users) — aborts the OpenProxy process mid-request, dropping the response and killing every other in-flight request. 9Router degrades to "no compression" for the same input.
- **Evidence**: 9router open-sse/rtk/autodetect.js:30 (char slice, panic-free) and open-sse/rtk/index.js:84 (try/catch fail-open); OpenProxy src/core/rtk/autodetect.rs:54-58 (byte slice), src/core/rtk/apply_filter.rs:19 (catch_unwind does not cover autodetect), Cargo.toml:162 (panic = "abort")
- **Parity fix**: In auto_detect_filter, compute the window as a char boundary before slicing, e.g. `let mut end = DETECT_WINDOW.min(text.len()); while end > 0 && !text.is_char_boundary(end) { end -= 1; } let head = &text[..end];`, matching 9router's panic-free char-indexed window. Additionally keep the whole compress_messages pass inside a catch_unwind at the call site so the fail-open contract survives a future panic (note: with panic="abort" catch_unwind cannot help, so the boundary fix is mandatory).

## P50-001 · P0 · open-sse/rtk/headroom.js:267-279 vs src/core/rtk/headroom.rs:448-487

- **9router**: For `format === "claude"` the body is round-tripped through 9Router's own translators so structure survives: `const oai = claudeToOpenAIRequest(model, body, false)` then proxy then `const claudeBody = openaiToClaudeRequest(model, { ...oai, messages: data.messages }, false)` then `body.messages = claudeBody.messages; body.system = claudeBody.system` (open-sse/rtk/headroom.js:267-279). tool_use, tool_result, thinking and image blocks are translated out and translated back.
- **OpenProxy**: compress_claude_body flattens every message to a bare `{role, content}` pair, keeping ONLY `.text` strings joined with a newline, then overwrites the Claude body with whatever the proxy returned: `Value::Array(blocks).iter().filter_map(|b| b.get("text")...).join("\n")` (src/core/rtk/headroom.rs:448-463) and `body["messages"] = Value::Array(compressed.clone())` (src/core/rtk/headroom.rs:476-477). The OpenAI-shaped proxy output is never translated back to Claude.
- **Gap**: With Headroom enabled on a Claude-native body, OpenProxy replaces the entire typed content-block array with flattened text, destroying tool_use / tool_result / thinking / image blocks; 9Router round-trips through its translators and preserves them.
- **Why it matters**: Any Claude Code / Cline / OpenClaw session routed to a Claude-format provider (anthropic or any anthropic-compatible) with headroom_enabled=true loses every tool result in the request. The model can no longer see what its tools returned and cannot emit a valid follow-up tool call; providers that validate block structure also 400. The identical request works in 9Router.
- **Evidence**: 9router open-sse/rtk/headroom.js:267-279; OpenProxy src/core/rtk/headroom.rs:448-463 (flatten) and src/core/rtk/headroom.rs:476-477 (untranslated write-back)
- **Parity fix**: Replace the flatten/unflatten in compress_claude_body with the existing registry translators, mirroring headroom.js: claude_to_openai_request(model, body) then call_compress then openai_to_claude_request(model, {...oai, messages: data.messages}), writing back body["messages"] and body["system"] only when the translator produced them.

## P51-001 · P0 · open-sse/shared/qoder/cosy.js:47-56 vs src/core/executor/qoder.rs:1330-1341

- **9router**: `aesEncryptCbcBase64` pads once with `pkcs7Pad(plaintext, 16)` and then sets `cipher.setAutoPadding(false)` (cosy.js:47-56), so the emitted ciphertext is exactly `len + (16 - len % 16)` bytes — the same length a stock PKCS#7 padder would produce.
- **OpenProxy**: `aes_cbc_encrypt_base64` pads the plaintext manually into `padded` (qoder.rs:1330-1335) and then calls `encrypt_padded_mut::<Pkcs7>(&mut padded, padded_len)` (qoder.rs:1337-1339), which pads the already-aligned buffer a second time and appends a full extra 16-byte block.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's `info` ciphertext is always exactly 16 bytes longer than 9router's for the same plaintext and key (verified empirically: 11-byte plaintext → 32 bytes vs 16; 16-byte plaintext → 48 vs 32).
- **Why it matters**: `info` is the AES-encrypted user identity carried inside the COSY `Authorization` payload that every signed Qoder request must present. A standard single-pass PKCS#7 unpad on the server strips only the outer 16-byte block, leaving the user-info JSON followed by a redundant padding block of control bytes — the identity parse fails, so the COSY signature is rejected. This fires on every Qoder inference request, image upload and model-list call.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/shared/qoder/cosy.js:47-56 (`pkcs7Pad` + `setAutoPadding(false)`). OpenProxy: src/core/executor/qoder.rs:1330-1339. Confirmed by compiling the exact cbc/aes call sequence from qoder.rs:1330-1339 against cbc 0.1 / aes 0.8 (same versions as Cargo.toml:115-116).
- **Parity fix**: Drop the manual padding block and call `encrypt_padded_mut::<Pkcs7>(&mut buf, plaintext.len())` directly (the cipher crate's Pkcs7 padder already produces the 9router byte layout), or keep the manual pad and switch to `encrypt_padded_mut::<NoPadding>`.

## P52-001 · P0 · open-sse/shared/zedAuth.js:227-238 + src/lib/oauth/providers/zed.js:39-59 vs src/core/executor/zed.rs:264-284 + src/server/api/oauth.rs:6917-6932

- **9router**: `postExchange` fetches `/client/users/me` and calls `resolveZedOrganizationId`, then `mapTokens` persists BOTH `organizationId` and `systemId` into `providerSpecificData` (providers/zed.js:39-59). `resolveZedOrganizationId` reads `psd.organizationId || psd.defaultOrganizationId`, then `userInfo.default_organization_id || userInfo.defaultOrganizationId`, then `organizations.find(is_personal) || organizations[0]` and returns `org.id` (zedAuth.js:227-238).
- **OpenProxy**: The Zed callback handler persists only `userId` and `authMethod` (oauth.rs:6917-6932) — no `organizationId`, no `systemId`. The executor therefore re-probes `/client/users/me` on every request and reads only `data.pointer("/organization/id")`, `data.get("organizationId")`, `data.get("organization_id")` (zed.rs:275-281) — a field set disjoint from the one 9router reads.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy never persists `organizationId`/`systemId` and its `/client/users/me` fallback looks in three fields that 9router never reads, so a freshly logged-in Zed account resolves no organization.
- **Why it matters**: With `organization_id` empty, `zed_auth::fetch_llm_token` returns `Err("No Zed organization selected")` (zed_auth.rs:191-193) before any completion is attempted, so chat.rs turns it into a 500 for every Zed request. The provider is unusable until a user hand-edits `organizationId` into the connection. `x-zed-system-id` is likewise never sent (zed.rs:270-272).
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/shared/zedAuth.js:227-238 (field names), src/lib/oauth/providers/zed.js:39-59 (persists organizationId + systemId). OpenProxy: src/server/api/oauth.rs:6917-6932 (psd = userId + authMethod only), src/core/executor/zed.rs:275-281 (wrong field set), src/oauth/zed_auth.rs:191-193 (hard failure).
- **Parity fix**: Call `fetchZedAuthenticatedUser` in the OAuth callback, resolve the org with the 9router precedence (`default_organization_id`/`defaultOrganizationId`, then `organizations.find(is_personal) || organizations[0] → id`), and persist `organizationId` + `systemId` into the connection's `provider_specific_data`; then align the executor's probe field set to the same names.

## P53-001 · P0 · openproxy: src/core/translator/request/openai_responses.rs:630-636 (chat_to_openai_responses_request) + src/core/executor/codex.rs:405-409 (transform_request_body) | 9router: open-sse/translator/request/openai-responses.js:350 + :260-269, and open-sse/executors/codex.js:428

- **9router**: extractInstructionsText (openai-responses.js:260-269) explicitly handles the array form: `if (Array.isArray(content)) return content.map(c => typeof c?.text === "string" ? c.text : ...).filter(Boolean).join("\n")`. It is called at :350 for every system/developer message, so an array-content system prompt becomes the real instructions string. The executor's trigger (codex.js:428) is `if (!body.instructions || body.instructions.trim() === "")` — non-empty text is kept, so the client's own prompt reaches Codex verbatim.
- **OpenProxy**: The system branch at openai_responses.rs:630-635 only does `msg.get("content").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("")`. For an array-valued `content` (the standard OpenAI array-of-parts system shape, and the shape Claude Code emits for system prompts) `as_str()` returns None, so `instructions` is set to the empty string "". Critically, `has_system` is still set to `true` at :636, so the `if !has_system { result["instructions"] = "" }` reset at :736-738 does not fire — the empty value survives. The executor then hits codex.rs:405-409, `.filter(|s| !s.is_empty())` evaluates false, and `unwrap_or(Self::DEFAULT_CODEX_INSTRUCTIONS)` substitutes the full 11,692-byte default Codex agent prompt.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy drops array-shaped system/developer prompt content to an empty string and then silently replaces the client's system prompt with the 11,692-byte default Codex agent instructions, whereas 9Router extracts the array text parts and preserves the client's actual instructions.
- **Why it matters**: Any OpenAI-compatible client that sends a system prompt as an array of content parts — the documented and widely used OpenAI shape, and what Claude Code emits before translation — loses its entire system prompt on the Codex path. The upstream model instead receives OpenProxy's built-in Codex CLI agent prompt ("You are Codex, based on GPT-5... prefer using `rg`... NEVER use destructive commands... Final answer structure and style guidelines"). This is silent, produces no error, and materially changes model behavior: persona, tool-use policy, sandboxing assumptions, and output formatting all come from the wrong source. It affects every request routed to a codex model where the client used array system content, and it is exactly the class of request a coding CLI sends.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/translator/request/openai-responses.js:260-269 (extractInstructionsText handles Array), :350 (call site), :424-425 (empty-instructions fallback only when NO system message exists); open-sse/executors/codex.js:428 (`.trim() === ""` trigger). openproxy: src/core/translator/request/openai_responses.rs:628-637 (as_str-only extraction, has_system=true set regardless), :736-738 (reset guarded by has_system), src/core/executor/codex.rs:405-409 (`!s.is_empty()` + unwrap_or(DEFAULT)). Verified by executing both implementations: JS extractInstructionsText([{type:'text',text:'MY SYSTEM PROMPT'}]) => "MY SYSTEM PROMPT"; the Rust `as_str()` path yields None => "".
- **Parity fix**: Port extractInstructionsText to openai_responses.rs and use it at the system branch (openai_responses.rs:630-635), replacing the as_str-only extraction. It must handle: string content returned as-is; array content mapped by taking each part's `text` (falling back to `content` when it is a string), dropping non-string parts via filter(Boolean), and joining with "\n"; any other shape (object, number, null) returning "". Keep the `has_system = true` assignment and the `if !has_system` reset at :736-738 exactly as-is — that guard is already correct and matches openai-responses.js:424-425. With that in place the executor's existing codex.rs:405-409 check will then correctly keep the non-empty client instructions and skip the default, so no executor change is needed for this case.

## P54-001 · P0 · src/core/executor/client_pool.rs:19-20, 186-191; src/server/api/chat.rs:110

- **9router**: `STREAM_STALL_TIMEOUT_MS = 360 * 1000` (runtimeConfig.js:53) is an INTER-CHUNK timer: `pipeWithDisconnect` re-arms it on every upstream byte (`armStall()` in the tap, streamHandler.js:239) and per-provider overrides win (`PROVIDERS[provider]?.stallTimeoutMs || STREAM_STALL_TIMEOUT_MS`, streamingHandler.js:87). There is no total-request cap, so a stream may run indefinitely as long as a chunk lands within 360s.
