First-chunk budget is a separate 200s (runtimeConfig.js:56) and connect is 60s (runtimeConfig.js:59).
- **OpenProxy**: `ClientTimeout::default()` sets `stream: DEFAULT_STREAM_TIMEOUT = Duration::from_secs(180)` and `build_reqwest_client` applies it as `reqwest::Client::builder().timeout(timeout.stream)` (client_pool.rs:191). In reqwest 0.12 `.timeout()` is the TOTAL request deadline — from connect until the response body completes — so it fires on a long-but-healthy stream. The separately declared inter-chunk timer is `SSE_STALL_TIMEOUT = 180s` (chat.rs:110), and the ported 360s/200s constants (runtime_config.rs:44, 49) are never referenced anywhere.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy caps the whole SSE lifetime at 180s wall-clock and halves the inter-chunk stall budget to 180s, with no per-provider override.
- **Why it matters**: An agentic turn (Claude Code / Codex tool loop) that legitimately streams for four minutes with a chunk every few seconds is cut off mid-answer in OpenProxy and never even reaches the stall detector; 9router runs it to completion. `grep` for `STREAM_FIRST_CHUNK_TIMEOUT_MS` in OpenProxy returns only its definition — a slow-prefill model that 9router tolerates for 200s is killed at 180s.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/config/runtimeConfig.js:53, 56, 59; .tmp/9router/open-sse/utils/streamHandler.js:192-253; .tmp/9router/open-sse/handlers/chatCore/streamingHandler.js:87. OpenProxy: src/core/executor/client_pool.rs:19-20, 186-191; src/server/api/chat.rs:110, 3852; src/core/config/runtime_config.rs:42-49
- **Parity fix**: Remove `.timeout(timeout.stream)` from `build_reqwest_client` (keep `.connect_timeout`) so no total deadline applies, raise `SSE_STALL_TIMEOUT` to `STREAM_STALL_TIMEOUT_MS` (360s) and actually use it, and add a separate first-chunk timer of `STREAM_FIRST_CHUNK_TIMEOUT_MS` (200s). Add a per-provider `stall_timeout_ms` on `ProviderExecutorConfig` mirroring `PROVIDERS[provider].stallTimeoutMs`.

## P55-001 · P0 · src/core/executor/default.rs:2094-2096, 2286-2306, 751-757 → src/server/api/chat.rs:2642

- **9router**: `BaseExecutor.execute` wraps `proxyAwareFetch` in a try/catch (base.js:164-180). A network/transport exception is retried via `tryRetry(urlIndex, 502, …)` using `DEFAULT_RETRY_CONFIG[502] = {attempts:3, delayMs:3000}` (runtimeConfig.js:78-83) — 3 retries at 3s — then falls through the fallback-URL list, and finally `throw error` (base.js:179). chatCore.js:394-402 then converts it: `createErrorResult(502, formatProviderError(...))` → HTTP 502 with `[502]: <message> (cause: ECONNREFUSED)`.
- **OpenProxy**: `send_one` maps the transport failure straight to `ExecutorError::Request(reqwest::Error)` / `ExecutorError::Hyper(...)` (default.rs:2295, 2305), and the call site at default.rs:2094-2096 uses `?`, so the failure escapes `execute` on the FIRST attempt with no retry and no fallback-URL attempt. `into_combo_attempt_error` maps it through the `other` arm to `status: 500, message: "Execution failed: Request(reqwest::Error { kind: Request, url: … })"`.
- **Gap**: DNS failures, connection refused, TLS errors and client timeouts get zero retries and surface as HTTP 500 with a Rust `Debug` blob, instead of 3×3s retries, fallback-URL rotation and HTTP 502.
- **Why it matters**: A single transient DNS blip or a proxy that drops one connection kills the whole user turn in OpenProxy where 9router would ride it out. 500 also tells the client the proxy is broken (non-retryable) instead of 502 (gateway, retryable), and the `Debug` blob leaks the internal URL/error structure into `error.message`.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/executors/base.js:164-183; .tmp/9router/open-sse/config/runtimeConfig.js:78-83; .tmp/9router/open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:394-402, 398; .tmp/9router/open-sse/utils/error.js:139-147. OpenProxy: src/core/executor/default.rs:2094-2096, 2286-2306, 751-757; src/server/api/chat.rs:2642
- **Parity fix**: Replace the `?` at default.rs:2096 with a catch that mirrors base.js:164-180: on `ExecutorError::Request`/`Hyper`, sleep 3s and retry up to 3 times against the same URL, then advance to the next fallback URL, and only then return a `ComboAttemptError { status: 502, message: format!("[502]: {e} (cause: {code})") }`.

## P56-001 · P0 · src/core/executor/default.rs:2250-2268, 2130-2164, 2272-2274 → src/server/api/chat.rs:2642 / 736-758

- **9router**: `BaseExecutor.execute` returns `{ response, url, headers, transformedBody }` for EVERY status it does not retry (base.js:163). The 401/403 refresh + non-ok parsing lives in chatCore.js:406-450 and 453-474: `parseUpstreamError` yields the real `statusCode` (401/403/400/402/…), `formatProviderError` prefixes `[<status>]: `, and `createErrorResult` returns a Response with that status. `handleSingleModelChat` then calls `markAccountUnavailable(..., status, ...)` (2-min model lock) and loops to the NEXT account (chat.js:325-335).
- **OpenProxy**: `DefaultExecutor::execute` branches on status itself. 401/403 does one inline credential refresh and then `break` (default.rs:2162-2163) → the url loop exhausts → `Err(ExecutorError::MaxRetriesExhausted("all retries and fallback URLs exhausted"))` (default.rs:2272). 400/402/405/409/422/500/501/504 and every other unlisted status become `Err(ExecutorError::UpstreamStatus(status, "upstream returned {code} for URL {url}: {body}"))` (default.rs:2256). `into_combo_attempt_error` maps `MaxRetriesExhausted`/`UpstreamStatus`-other to `status: 500, message: "Execution failed: …"` (default.rs:751-757), and the `?` at chat.rs:2642 returns straight out of `forward_with_provider_fallback` — never reaching the `!status.is_success()` block at chat.rs:2750.
- **Gap**: Every DefaultExecutor-backed provider (openai, openrouter, deepseek, groq, together, nvidia, all `openai-compatible-*` / `anthropic-compatible-*` nodes, …) answers 401/403/400/402/5xx to the client as HTTP 500 and never enters the account-fallback loop.
- **Why it matters**: Clients see a server error instead of an auth/quota error, so they will not prompt for re-auth and SDK auto-retry policies key off the wrong class. Because the loop is skipped, no `mark_connection_unavailable` runs and no account is excluded: a revoked key on account #1 is retried forever instead of failing over to account #2 — the single most important fallback behaviour in the router.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/executors/base.js:163; .tmp/9router/open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:406-450, 453-474; .tmp/9router/src/sse/handlers/chat.js:325-335. OpenProxy: src/core/executor/default.rs:2130-2164, 2250-2268, 2272-2274, 736-758; src/server/api/chat.rs:2640-2642, 2750-2773
- **Parity fix**: Make `DefaultExecutor::execute` status-agnostic like `BaseExecutor.execute`: return `Ok(ExecutionResponse { response, url, headers, transformed_body, transport })` for every non-retried status (keep the existing 502/503/429/504 retry loops and the 401/403 single inline refresh), and delete the `ExecutorError::UpstreamStatus` / `MaxRetriesExhausted` construction at default.rs:2256 and 2272. The status-specific handling already exists downstream at chat.rs:2750-2790.

## P57-001 · P0 · src/server/api/chat.rs:4694-4712 → 2763-2773 → 4836-4849

- **9router**: `parseUpstreamError` extracts the message and returns `{statusCode, message, resetsAtMs}` (error.js:58-89). chatCore.js:468 wraps it in `formatProviderError(new Error(message), provider, model, statusCode)` (error.js:139-147) → `"[<status>]: <message>"`, and `createErrorResult` (error.js:98-106) always builds `{"error":{"message","type","code"}}` via `buildErrorBody`/`errorResponse` (error.js:9-38).
- **OpenProxy**: `extract_error_message_and_retry_after_with_body` keeps the whole raw upstream body (`upstream_body: Some(text.into_bytes())`, chat.rs:4708-4712) and `attempt_error_response` returns it byte-for-byte: `(status, Body::from(body_bytes)).into_response()` (chat.rs:4839-4841). No `[status]: ` prefix, no `error.type`, no `error.code`, and whatever dialect the provider used (Anthropic `{"type":"error","error":{…}}`, Gemini `{"error":{"code","status"}}`, plain text, HTML) is what the client receives.
- **Gap**: Non-2xx provider responses are streamed back to the client in the provider's native error shape instead of 9router's OpenAI envelope with the `[<status>]: ` message prefix.
- **Why it matters**: OpenAI-compatible clients (Cline, Roo, Continue, OpenWebUI) read `error.code` / `error.type` to decide whether to re-auth, switch model, or retry; those fields are absent. Claude Code / Codex show the raw provider blob. Anything that scrapes the `[{code}]: ` prefix — including 9router's own dashboard `getConnectionErrorTag` message sniffer — stops matching.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/utils/error.js:9-38, 58-89, 98-106, 139-147; .tmp/9router/open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:455, 468, 474. OpenProxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:4694-4712, 2751-2773, 4836-4849
- **Parity fix**: Drop `upstream_body` from `ComboAttemptError` and build the client response the way `createErrorResult` does: `let msg = format!("[{status}]: {message}", status = error.status, message = error.message);` then `build_error_body(error.status, Some(&msg))`. Keep the raw body only for the request-detail/usage log, not for the wire.

## P58-001 · P0 · src/server/auth/mod.rs:46-54 (secret resolution) vs src/server/api/auth.rs:149-190 (token mint + cookie)

- **9router**: loadJwtSecret() resolves the signing key with a 3-tier fallback: `process.env.JWT_SECRET` → read `<DATA_DIR>/jwt-secret` from disk → generate a random 32-byte hex, write it to `<DATA_DIR>/jwt-secret` with mode 0o600, and return it (src/lib/auth/dashboardSession.js:12-22). The file is written once and re-read on every subsequent process start, so the secret is STABLE across server restarts. The resulting token is a 24h HS256 JWT (`.setExpirationTime("24h")`, :37) set as an `auth_token` httpOnly/lax cookie with `maxAge: SESSION_MAX_AGE_SEC = 24*60*60` (:10, :63-70). A dashboard login therefore survives any number of server restarts until the 24h token TTL elapses.
- **OpenProxy**: The signing key is `static JWT_SECRET: Lazy<String> = Lazy::new(...)` which returns `JWT_SECRET` env if set, else a fresh `rand::thread_rng()` 32-byte hex generated IN MEMORY (src/server/auth/mod.rs:46-54). The source comment states the consequence explicitly: "This means the secret changes on every server restart, invalidating all existing sessions." There is no file write anywhere in src/server/auth/ (grep for fs::write/File::create returns nothing). The token TTL itself is correct: `expires_at = now_secs() + 86400` with a 86400s cookie (src/server/api/auth.rs:149, :190), matching 9router's 24h. Only the key persistence differs.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy regenerates the dashboard JWT signing key in memory on every process start, so every dashboard session cookie is invalidated by any server restart, while 9router persists the key to `<DATA_DIR>/jwt-secret` and sessions survive restarts for the full 24h TTL.
- **Why it matters**: This is the core dashboard-auth persistence contract. A user who logs in, then has the binary restarted (upgrade, `./scripts/dev.sh build && restart`, crash-restart, systemd/Docker restart, or a host reboot) is silently bounced to /login even though their 24h cookie has barely expired. Because the cookie value is unchanged and still unexpired, the browser re-sends it and the server rejects it as an invalid signature — there is no "session expired" affordance, so the failure looks like a broken app rather than a re-login. The stated mitigation (set JWT_SECRET) is not discoverable from the UI and is not surfaced by any doctor/init output, so the default deployment — which is the entire target audience — logs everyone out on every deploy. It also means the OpenProxy and 9router `DATA_DIR` backups are not interchangeable for auth state.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/lib/auth/dashboardSession.js:12-22 (loadJwtSecret, readFileSync/writeFileSync of `<DATA_DIR>/jwt-secret`), :10 (SESSION_MAX_AGE_SEC=86400), :37 (setExpirationTime("24h")), :63-70 (cookie maxAge). OpenProxy: src/server/auth/mod.rs:46-54 (Lazy in-memory random, no disk write), src/server/api/auth.rs:149 (expires_at = now_secs() + 86400), :190 (build_auth_cookie(&token, 86400, ...)).
- **Parity fix**: Add a `load_jwt_secret(data_dir: &Path) -> String` in src/server/auth/mod.rs that mirrors 9router exactly: (1) return `JWT_SECRET` if set and non-empty; (2) try to read `<data_dir>/jwt-secret` and trim; (3) on miss, generate 32 random bytes hex, `fs::write` the file with owner-only permissions (0o600 on unix), and return it. Replace the `Lazy::new(...)` random fallback with a call into this loader, threading `state.db.data_dir` (already available on AppState, used at src/main.rs:432) into the initializer. Keep the `JWT_SECRET` env override first so existing deployments that set it are unaffected. This makes a restart a no-op for live sessions while preserving the 24h TTL already correct in auth.rs.

## P59-001 · P1 · /dashboard/media-providers/webSearch and /webFetch redirect semantics

- **9router**: `src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/media-providers/[kind]/page.js:149-153` calls `router.replace("/dashboard/media-providers/web")` — a client-side history REPLACE with no full reload. Pressing Back from `/web` goes to whatever the user was on before the kind page; it never returns to `webSearch`.
- **OpenProxy**: `web/src/components/MediaProvidersKindPageClient.tsx:160-164` calls `window.location.href = "/dashboard/media-providers/web"` — a full document load that PUSHES a new history entry.
- **Gap**: The redirect uses `location.href` instead of `router.replace`, so it both reloads the page and leaves the source URL in the history stack.
- **Why it matters**: Back from `/dashboard/media-providers/web` returns to `/dashboard/media-providers/webSearch`, which immediately redirects forward again — a back-button trap where the user cannot leave the page. The extra full reload also flashes the kind page before redirecting.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/media-providers/[kind]/page.js:149-153 | OpenProxy: web/src/components/MediaProvidersKindPageClient.tsx:160-164
- **Parity fix**: Replace the assignment with `window.history.replaceState({}, "", "/dashboard/media-providers/web"); window.location.reload();` (or, once a client router exists, `router.replace(...)`) so the `webSearch`/`webFetch` entry is removed from history exactly as 9router does.

## P60-001 · P1 · 404 semantics for unknown / dashboard paths

- **9router**: `src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/cli-tools/[toolId]/page.js:8` does `if (!CLI_TOOLS[toolId]) notFound()`. `src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/media-providers/[kind]/page.js:179` does `if (!kindConfig) return notFound()`. Any unmatched path renders Next's 404 page with a 404 status.
- **OpenProxy**: `src/server/dashboard/mod.rs:114-121` falls back to `lookup_embedded("dashboard.html")` and then `index.html` for any extension-less miss. `web/src/pages/dashboard.astro` is that shell, and it renders `EndpointPageClient`. `web/src/components/MediaProvidersKindPageClient.tsx:191-200` additionally returns an inline "Kind Not Found" card with HTTP 200. Concrete example: 9router has a `devin` CLI tool, so `/dashboard/cli-tools/devin` resolves; OpenProxy's `getStaticPaths` (`web/src/pages/dashboard/cli-tools/[toolId]/index.astro:7-11`) emits no `devin.html` and no `cli-tools/_dynamic.html`, so the request falls to `dashboard.html` and renders the Endpoint page.
- **Gap**: Every unmatched or unbuilt dashboard path returns HTTP 200 with the Endpoint page instead of a 404.
- **Why it matters**: Typoed URLs, stale bookmarks and 9router deep links that OpenProxy does not implement all render a plausible-looking but wrong screen. The user has no signal that the page does not exist, and the sidebar/header show the Endpoint context rather than a not-found state.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/cli-tools/[toolId]/page.js:8; .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/media-providers/[kind]/page.js:179 | OpenProxy: src/server/dashboard/mod.rs:114-121; web/src/pages/dashboard.astro:6-9; web/src/components/MediaProvidersKindPageClient.tsx:191-200; web/src/pages/dashboard/cli-tools/[toolId]/index.astro:7-11
- **Parity fix**: Drop the `dashboard.html` SPA-shell fallback for paths that begin with `/dashboard/` in `serve_embedded`/`serve_from_disk` and return `404 Not Found` instead, so unbuilt dashboard routes behave like Next's `notFound()`. Keep the `dashboard.html` fallback only for genuinely unknown top-level prefixes if desired.

## P61-001 · P1 · 9router open-sse/handlers/videoCore.js:181-184  ⟷  openproxy src/server/api/media.rs:903-917

- **9router**: videoCore.js:181-184: on any non-ok upstream, `const message = sanitizeSecrets(bodyText || "HTTP " + upstream.status, credentials); return createErrorResult(upstream.status, "[${provider}] " + message.slice(0, 2000));`. createErrorResult (utils/error.js:98-106) calls errorResponse (error.js:30-38), which emits buildErrorBody (error.js:9-22) — the OpenAI envelope `{error:{message,type,code}}` with type/code resolved from ERROR_TYPES (config/errorConfig.js:2-14, e.g. 429 → rate_limit_error/rate_limit_exceeded, 401 → authentication_error/invalid_api_key). The response carries only Content-Type and Access-Control-Allow-Origin; no upstream header is forwarded.
- **OpenProxy**: proxy_video_response media.rs:903-917: `if status.is_success() { return proxy_upstream_response(...) }` and otherwise reads the body, runs sanitize_video_secrets over the RAW text, and re-emits it verbatim at the upstream status, copying every non-hop-by-hop upstream header (media.rs:912-916). There is no `[provider] ` prefix, no 2000-character truncation, and no build_error_body wrapping. OpenProxy's json_error_response helper does build the same envelope (media.rs:1114-1121 → core/utils/error.rs:11-45, byte-identical shape to buildErrorBody), but it is only used for locally-generated failures, never for the upstream passthrough.
- **Gap**: When the upstream video provider returns a non-2xx, OpenProxy relays the provider's own error body and headers verbatim instead of wrapping it in the `{error:{message,type,code}}` envelope with a `[provider] ` prefix and 2000-char cap that 9router produces.
- **Why it matters**: Every OpenAI-compatible client branches on `body.error.message`, `body.error.type` and `body.error.code`. xAI and OpenRouter emit their own shapes (`{"error":"..."}`, `{"code":...,"message":...}`), so on any upstream video failure a Claude Code / Cursor / custom client sees undefined where 9router guarantees populated fields, and error classification by `type === "rate_limit_error"` silently misses real 429s — which also defeats client-side backoff. The lost 2000-char truncation additionally lets a multi-megabyte upstream HTML error page reach the client. The leaked upstream headers (cf-ray, x-request-id, and notably Retry-After on a 429 that 9router replaces) further diverge from the 9router contract, compounding P1-005.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/handlers/videoCore.js:181-184 (createErrorResult with prefix + slice), open-sse/utils/error.js:98-106 → :30-38 → :9-22 (envelope), open-sse/config/errorConfig.js:2-14 (type/code table). openproxy: src/server/api/media.rs:903-917 (raw re-emit + header copy), media.rs:909 (sanitize only), media.rs:1114-1121 and src/core/utils/error.rs:11-45 (the correct envelope exists but is not used on this path).
- **Parity fix**: In proxy_video_response, replace the non-success branch with the 9router shape: keep the upstream status, take the sanitized body text (falling back to "HTTP {status}" when empty), slice(0, 2000), and emit build_error_body(status, Some(&format!("[{}] {}", provider, truncated))) with Content-Type: application/json and Access-Control-Allow-Origin: * only — dropping the upstream header copy at media.rs:912-916. `provider` is already available at every call site (media.rs:1461, 1471, 1624, 2252) and must be threaded into the helper.

## P62-001 · P1 · 9router open-sse/handlers/videoCore.js:182-183 vs OpenProxy src/server/api/media.rs:897-918

- **9router**: On a non-ok upstream, videoCore.js:182-183 sanitizes the body and caps it: `const message = sanitizeSecrets(bodyText || \`HTTP ${upstream.status}\`, credentials); return createErrorResult(upstream.status, \`[${provider}] ${message.slice(0, 2000)}\`)`. The client therefore always receives a bounded (≤2000 char), provider-prefixed message.
- **OpenProxy**: proxy_video_response (media.rs:897-918) reads the non-2xx body in full, sanitizes secrets, and re-emits it with no length cap and no provider prefix — the entire upstream error payload is proxied verbatim apart from redaction.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy does not truncate upstream video error bodies to 2000 characters and does not prefix them with the provider name.
- **Why it matters**: Error payload size is unbounded. A misbehaving or hostile upstream (or a proxy returning a multi-megabyte HTML error page) is relayed whole into the client response, which can blow up client memory and HTTP response limits, and the client loses the [provider] disambiguation that lets it tell which account failed when several are configured.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/handlers/videoCore.js:182-183. OpenProxy: src/server/api/media.rs:906-917 (sanitize_video_secrets applied at :909 with no truncation).
- **Parity fix**: In proxy_video_response, cap the sanitized non-2xx body to 2000 characters (0..=2000 on char boundaries) before emitting, matching videoCore.js:183. Note the 9router prefix `[${provider}] ` is added to the error MESSAGE; since OpenProxy proxies the upstream body rather than re-wrapping it, cap the length and leave the body shape as-is to avoid changing the response contract — the length cap is the behaviourally significant half.

## P63-001 · P1 · 9router open-sse/handlers/videoCore.js:9, :47-53, :99 vs OpenProxy src/server/api/media.rs:1183-1191, :1506-1510, :1377-1391

- **9router**: handleVideoProxyCore wraps every upstream fetch in combineSignals(signal, timeoutMs) (videoCore.js:99, :47-53) with timeoutMs defaulting to VIDEO_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS = Number(process.env.VIDEO_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS || 120000) (videoCore.js:9). A hung upstream therefore aborts at 120s and returns a clean 408 REQUEST_TIMEOUT: `video ${method} aborted: ...` (videoCore.js:142-144).
- **OpenProxy**: video_create_handler and video_get_handler_with_query issue `client.post(&url)...send().await` and `client.get(&url)...send().await` (media.rs:1377-1391, :1560-1570) with no per-request deadline of their own — they inherit only the pooled client's connect/read timeout. The abort branch at media.rs:1385-1390 maps any error to BAD_GATEWAY with the text "{provider} video POST aborted: {e}", so a genuine timeout and a connection refusal are indistinguishable and both report 502 rather than 408.
- **Gap**: Video upstream requests have no 120-second deadline and no 408 timeout response; they inherit an unspecified client-pool timeout and report every transport failure as 502.
- **Why it matters**: A stalled xAI/OpenRouter/Vertex upstream leaves the video create request hanging until the pooled client's own timeout, which is not the 120s contract 9router advertises and is overridable per-deployment via VIDEO_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS. Clients that implement a 408-with-retry backoff on video create never see a 408 from OpenProxy, and the operator loses the env-tunable deadline that makes 9router's video path operable in production.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/handlers/videoCore.js:9 (120000ms), :47-53 (combineSignals), :99, :142-144 (408). OpenProxy: src/server/api/media.rs:1377-1391 (create), :1560-1570 (poll), :1386-1389 (502 on abort).
- **Parity fix**: Wrap the video create and poll sends in tokio::time::timeout using a VIDEO_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS constant (default 120_000, overridable from env to match 9router's process.env.VIDEO_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS), selecting on the client's request against the caller's cancellation. Map a deadline elapse to 408 REQUEST_TIMEOUT with the message "[{provider}] video {method} aborted: ...", and reserve 502 BAD_GATEWAY for genuine connect/send failures, matching videoCore.js:141-147.

## P64-001 · P1 · 9router open-sse/services/combo.js:300-361 (no pre-gate); OpenProxy src/core/combo/mod.rs:852-860 + 868-886

- **9router**: There is no provider-level pre-gate. Every configured member is attempted in order; a degraded or unavailable provider is discovered INSIDE handleSingleModelChat, which returns 404 `No active credentials for provider: X` (chat.js:244-247) or a 503 unavailableResponse (chat.js:237-243). handleComboChat records that status via `if (!lastStatus) lastStatus = result.status` (combo.js:353) and keeps going, so the FINAL response's status is the FIRST failing member's status.
- **OpenProxy**: Before rotation, execute_combo_strategy_full builds a skip set and extends it with `models.iter().filter(|m| crate::core::health::is_model_degraded(m))` (mod.rs:852-860). is_model_degraded (health/registry.rs:140-147) resolves the alias to a provider and returns true when EVERY health record for that provider is inside its degrade window (5 minutes on 5xx, health/probe.rs). Affected members are removed from active (mod.rs:862-866) and never dispatched. If every member is skipped, the dispatcher returns 400 `All combo members are disabled` or 503 `All combo members are currently quarantined or degraded after recent failures` (mod.rs:868-886). This applies to Fallback too, not just RoundRobin.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy removes health-degraded members from the dispatch order for every strategy, so a Fallback combo silently skips a member the operator configured first and changes the final status/message when all members are degraded.
- **Why it matters**: Five minutes after a single upstream 5xx burst on a provider, a Fallback combo stops trying that provider at all. The first request in the window loses a working member; if the degrade window is stale and the provider has recovered, the request still skips it. When every member is degraded the client gets 503/400 with an OpenProxy-specific message instead of 9router's 404 `No active credentials for provider: X`, so client-side retry classification and status-code-driven logic diverge.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/services/combo.js:300-361 (no pre-gate; all members attempted), :353 (first status retained), src/sse/handlers/chat.js:244-247 (404 no-active-credentials). OpenProxy: src/core/combo/mod.rs:852-860 (health skip), :862-866 (active filter), :868-886 (400/503 short-circuit); src/core/health/registry.rs:140-147 (is_model_degraded).
- **Parity fix**: Restrict the health pre-gate to advisory use (for example reordering only) or drop it from execute_combo_strategy_full. The 9router-equivalent behaviour is to attempt the member and let handleSingleModelChat / execute_single_model surface 404/503 through the normal error path, which already consults per-connection degradedUntil via filter_available_accounts (chat.rs:3029). If the gate is kept, emit the 9router 404 `No active credentials for provider: X` for the all-degraded case rather than the OpenProxy-specific strings.

## P65-001 · P1 · 9router open-sse/services/combo.js:300-361; OpenProxy src/core/combo/mod.rs:925-942

- **9router**: handleComboChat iterates rotatedModels and ALWAYS calls handleSingleModel for every member (combo.js:300-305). There is no capacity pre-filter. A member whose provider is fully rate-limited still runs handleSingleModelChat, which returns unavailableResponse(503, "[provider/model] ...", retryAfter, retryAfterHuman) (chat.js:237-243) - that 503 is consumed by the loop (checkFallbackError -> shouldFallback true) and the loop moves on, and if it is the last member the 503 with a Retry-After header is what the client receives.
- **OpenProxy**: When strategy == RoundRobin and order.len() > 1, the dispatcher filters the order down to members whose capacity_check returns ModelCapacity::Available (mod.rs:926-930). model_capacity (chat.rs:2990-3016) reports Busy whenever the provider has no active/credentialed/non-rate-limited/non-locked connection, or all of them are at MAX_IN_FLIGHT_PER_ACCOUNT. If the filtered list is empty the dispatcher returns 503 "All combo providers are at max in-flight capacity" with earliest_retry_after: None (mod.rs:932-939) WITHOUT attempting a single member and without a Retry-After header.
- **Gap**: In RoundRobin mode OpenProxy silently drops saturated members from the rotation and can 503 without trying anything, instead of attempting them and surfacing the provider's own 503 plus Retry-After.
- **Why it matters**: The final HTTP status, body message, and presence of Retry-After all differ for the same saturation condition. 9router returns 503 with a Retry-After header computed from the earliest account reset, which is what Claude Code and Codex use to back off correctly; OpenProxy returns 503 with no Retry-After, so clients either hammer immediately or treat it as permanent. In the partial case, a member that would have succeeded after the inner per-account fallback loop (which in 9router retries the next account and can still land a free slot) is never tried at all.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/services/combo.js:300-305 (unconditional `await handleSingleModel(body, modelStr)` per member), src/sse/handlers/chat.js:237-243 (unavailableResponse with Retry-After). OpenProxy: src/core/combo/mod.rs:925-942 (capacity filter + 503 short-circuit), src/server/api/chat.rs:2990-3016 (model_capacity -> Busy).
- **Parity fix**: Either drop the RoundRobin capacity pre-filter so the sequential iterator runs the same members 9router would, or make it advisory: keep the available list for ordering but fall through to the full order when it is empty instead of returning 503. If the 503 is retained, populate earliest_retry_after from the earliest rateLimitedUntil across the Busy members' connections (9router getEarliestRateLimitedUntil, accountFallback.js:72-83) so attempt_error_response emits Retry-After the way unavailableResponse does.

## P66-001 · P1 · 9router open-sse/services/model.js:20-22, 111-113, 126-142; OpenProxy src/core/model/mod.rs:266-288, 309-344

- **9router**: getModelInfoCore resolves a bare (slash-free) name through three steps: user aliases, then BUILTIN_MODEL_ALIASES - `{"grok-build": "gcli/grok-build"}` (model.js:20-22), applied at model.js:113 - then inferProviderFromModelName with MODEL_PREFIX_PROVIDERS = [/^claude-/->anthropic, /^gemini-/->gemini, /^gpt-/->openai, /^o[134]/->openai, /^deepseek-/->openrouter], default "openai" (model.js:126-142). So `model: "grok-build"` routes to gcli/grok-build, and `model: "grok-4"` / "command-r-plus" / "mistral-large" / "jamba-1.5-large" all route to openai.
- **OpenProxy**: get_model_info (model.rs:266-288) has NO builtin-alias table: it tries db.model_aliases, then falls straight to infer_provider_from_model_name (model.rs:283). OpenProxy's inference table (model.rs:309-344) additionally maps mistral-* / open-mistral-* / mistralai-* / codestral-* / ministral-* / mixtral-* -> mistral, command-* / command-r* -> cohere, grok-* -> xai, and jamba-* -> ai21, with openai only as the final fallback.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy drops 9router's built-in grok-build alias and adds four provider-inference rules that 9router does not have, so a bare model name routes to a different provider.
- **Why it matters**: `model: "grok-build"` is the model id the grok-build CLI tool config ships (gcli/grok-build in the default preset, and 9router's own constant). On 9router it resolves to the Grok CLI provider; on OpenProxy it resolves to xai/grok-build. More broadly any client that sends a bare, un-prefixed model id (grok-4, command-r-plus, mistral-large) is sent to a different vendor on OpenProxy than on 9router - a silent routing change that produces a 401/404 from the wrong upstream or, worse, a billable request against an account the user did not intend.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/services/model.js:20-22 (BUILTIN_MODEL_ALIASES), :111-113 (alias || builtin fallback), :126-142 (MODEL_PREFIX_PROVIDERS). OpenProxy: src/core/model/mod.rs:275-288 (alias then infer, no builtin table), :322-336 (the extra mistral/cohere/xai/ai21 rules), :337-343 (openai fallback).
- **Parity fix**: Port the builtin table: add a BUILTIN_MODEL_ALIASES list of (&str, &str) pairs containing ("grok-build", "gcli/grok-build") in src/core/model/mod.rs and consult it in get_model_info between resolve_model_alias_from_map(&alias_name, &db.model_aliases) and the infer_provider_from_model_name fallback, exactly as model.js:112-113 orders them. Separately, prune the mistral / command / grok / jamba arms from infer_provider_from_model_name so bare names that 9router sends to openai keep going there.

## P67-001 · P1 · 9router src/app/api/provider-nodes/[id]/route.js:64-77 · OpenProxy src/server/api/provider_nodes.rs:163-185

- **9router**: After `updateProviderNode`, the handler fetches every connection whose `provider` equals the node id and calls `updateProviderConnection` for each, writing `providerSpecificData: { ...existing, prefix, apiType, baseUrl, nodeName: updated.name }` (`provider-nodes/[id]/route.js:64-77`). The node edit is therefore a fan-out write across the `providerNodes` → `providerConnections` relation.
- **OpenProxy**: `update_provider_node` (`src/server/api/provider_nodes.rs:163-185`) mutates only the `provider_nodes` entry inside the single `db.update` closure. No connection is touched. `ProviderNode` has no relational link on the connection side other than the string `connection.provider == node.id`.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's provider-node PUT does not propagate the node's `prefix` / `apiType` / `baseUrl` / `name` down to the connections that reference it, while 9router fans the write out to every member connection.
- **Why it matters**: Connections created through 9router's `POST /api/providers` snapshot the node's `prefix`/`baseUrl` into `providerSpecificData` (`9router src/app/api/providers/route.js:132-140`). Editing the node's base URL in OpenProxy leaves every existing connection pointing at the old URL, so the request keeps hitting the previous upstream while the node editor shows the new value. A user migrating a self-hosted OpenAI-compatible endpoint to a new host sees no effect until they re-enter the key on every connection.
- **Evidence**: 9router `.tmp/9router/src/app/api/provider-nodes/[id]/route.js:64-77`, `.tmp/9router/src/app/api/providers/route.js:132-140` vs OpenProxy `src/server/api/provider_nodes.rs:163-185`
- **Parity fix**: Inside the same `db.update` closure (or a follow-up one), iterate `db.provider_connections` where `connection.provider == id` and set `provider_specific_data` entries `prefix`, `apiType` (only for `openai-compatible`), `baseUrl` and `nodeName` to the node's current values, merging rather than replacing.

## P68-001 · P1 · 9router src/app/api/provider-nodes/[id]/route.js:83-99 · OpenProxy src/server/api/provider_nodes.rs:239-248

- **9router**: Node deletion runs `deleteProviderConnectionsByProvider(id)` then `deleteProviderNode(id)` (`provider-nodes/[id]/route.js:88-93`). Custom models live in `kv` under scope `customModels` keyed by `providerAlias|id|type` (`repos/aliasRepo.js:23-25`) and are NOT touched — they survive as orphans and reattach if a node with the same id is recreated. Disabled models (`kv` scope `disabledModels`) also survive.
- **OpenProxy**: `delete_provider_node` (`src/server/api/provider_nodes.rs:239-248`) removes the node, its connections, AND its custom models: `db.custom_models.retain(|m| m.provider_alias.as_str() != id.as_str())`.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy cascades custom-model deletion when a provider node is removed; 9router preserves them.
- **Why it matters**: Deleting and recreating an openai-compatible/anthropic-compatible/custom-embedding node in 9router restores the user's custom model list; in OpenProxy that list is permanently gone with no confirmation and no undo. Per AGENTS.md, provider configuration is user data that must survive; here it does not survive a node delete/recreate cycle.
- **Evidence**: 9router `.tmp/9router/src/app/api/provider-nodes/[id]/route.js:83-99`, `.tmp/9router/src/lib/db/repos/aliasRepo.js:23-25` vs OpenProxy `src/server/api/provider_nodes.rs:239-248`
- **Parity fix**: Remove the `db.custom_models.retain(...)` line from `delete_provider_node` so only the node and its connections cascade, matching 9router. The connections cascade itself is correct and should stay.

## P69-001 · P1 · 9router src/app/globals.css:5 + src/store/themeStore.js:47  vs  openproxy web/src/shared/components/styles/global.css:15 + web/tailwind.config.js + web/src/store/themeStore.ts:55

- **9router**: Tailwind v4 (CSS-first, no tailwind.config file; postcss.config.mjs uses @tailwindcss/postcss) registers class-based dark mode with the v4 directive `@custom-variant dark (&:where(.dark, .dark *));` at src/app/globals.css:5. themeStore.js:47 does `root.classList.add("dark")`. Every `dark:` utility therefore compiles to a `:where(.dark, .dark *)` scoped rule that follows the in-app theme toggle.
- **OpenProxy**: Tailwind v3.4.17 via @astrojs/tailwind ^6 (web/package.json:44-46). web/tailwind.config.js has NO `darkMode` key, so v3 falls back to `darkMode: 'media'`. The v4-only at-rule `@custom-variant dark (&:where(.dark, .dark *));` is copied verbatim at global.css:15 but is unknown to v3 and silently dropped, registering nothing. themeStore.ts:55 still toggles `.dark` on <html>. Verified in the shipped bundle: all 74 compiled `dark:` rules sit inside a single `@media(prefers-color-scheme:dark){...}` block and 0 are scoped to `.dark`; there is no `:is(.dark` output. Source uses `dark:` in 452 places across 64 .tsx files (9router: 458 across 69 files).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's `dark:` utilities are driven by the OS colour-scheme media query instead of the `.dark` class the theme toggle actually sets, so the dashboard renders a hybrid light/dark UI whenever the OS setting and the in-app setting disagree.
- **Why it matters**: The OpenProxy palette is CSS-variable driven, so bg-canvas/text-ink DO follow the toggle, but the 452 hard-coded `dark:` utilities do not. A user on a light-OS machine who toggles the dashboard to dark gets dark surfaces with light-mode leftovers: the Sidebar Remote/9English row hover `dark:hover:bg-white/5` is near-invisible on a dark surface, the HeaderMenu popover `dark:border-white/10` border is wrong, and `dark:text-brand-300`, `dark:text-green-400`, `dark:bg-gray-900/95` (Modal) and `dark:hover:bg-white/5` on every menu item all stay in light mode. The inverse (dark OS, light app) breaks identically. 9router is fully consistent with the toggle.
- **Evidence**: 9R: src/app/globals.css:5; src/store/themeStore.js:47; no tailwind.config.* exists in the 9router root. OP: web/src/shared/components/styles/global.css:15; web/tailwind.config.js (no darkMode key); web/src/store/themeStore.ts:55; built bundle web/dist/_astro/dashboard.DmzKc7D6.css has 74/74 `dark\:` rules inside @media(prefers-color-scheme:dark) and 0 outside.
- **Parity fix**: Add `darkMode: "class"` to web/tailwind.config.js so v3 scopes `dark:` variants to the `.dark` ancestor that themeStore.ts already toggles. Then delete the inert `@custom-variant dark` line from global.css:15 and re-verify in the rebuilt CSS that `dark\:` rules appear as `.dark .dark\:...` rather than inside `@media(prefers-color-scheme:dark)`. Do NOT migrate to v4 in the same change; `darkMode: 'class'` alone restores 9router behaviour with no class churn.

## P70-001 · P1 · 9router src/lib/db/index.js:102-110 · OpenProxy src/db/sqlite/import.rs:29-42

- **9router**: `importDb` (`src/lib/db/index.js:96-110`) wipes only configuration tables: `settings`, `providerConnections`, `providerNodes`, `proxyPools`, `apiKeys`, `combos`, and `kv WHERE scope IN ('modelAliases','customModels','mitmAlias','pricing')`. `usageHistory`, `usageDaily` and `requestDetails` are never touched by an import.
- **OpenProxy**: `import_all` (`src/db/sqlite/import.rs:29-42`) DELETEs from all eleven tables including `usageHistory`, `usageDaily` and `requestDetails` before re-inserting — but the payload it is fed (`export_all`, `src/db/sqlite/export.rs:222-234`) contains NO usage keys, so the usage data is destroyed and never restored. `Db::replace_app_db` (`src/db/mod.rs:389-399`) routes the backup-restore path through `import_db` (`src/server/api/db_backups.rs:283`).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's database import/restore deletes the entire usage history, daily rollups, and request-detail log, while 9router's import leaves all three intact.
- **Why it matters**: Restoring a config backup (Profile → Database → import, or `db-backups` restore) in OpenProxy permanently deletes all usage, quota and request-log rows, so the Usage page, the Quota tracker and the cost figures all reset to zero with no warning. In 9router the same operation preserves them.
- **Evidence**: 9router `.tmp/9router/src/lib/db/index.js:102-110` vs OpenProxy `src/db/sqlite/import.rs:29-42`, `src/db/mod.rs:389-399`, `src/db/sqlite/export.rs:222-234`
- **Parity fix**: Drop `usageHistory`, `usageDaily` and `requestDetails` from the `tables` wipe list in `import_all` (`src/db/sqlite/import.rs:29-42`), leaving only the config tables 9router's `importDb` clears. `import_usage_impl` already owns the usage tables for the explicit `usage.json` import path.

## P71-001 · P1 · 9router src/lib/db/migrate.js:79-109 · OpenProxy src/db/sqlite/migrations.rs:47-94

- **9router**: Boot runs `runVersionedMigrations` (`migrate.js:57-76`), which for each pending version executes `m.up(adapter)` and only then stamps `schemaVersion`; afterwards `syncSchemaFromTables` (`migrate.js:79-109`) iterates the declarative `TABLES` map and `ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN`s any missing declared column (stripping PK/UNIQUE per SQLite's ADD COLUMN rules), then re-creates indexes. Any column added to `TABLES` in any release is therefore applied to existing databases automatically.
- **OpenProxy**: `SqliteDb::init` (`src/db/sqlite/mod.rs:86-100`) only runs `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` (new tables are covered) and `migrations::apply_pending_migrations` (`src/db/sqlite/migrations.rs:47-66`), which hardcodes exactly one column addition — `add_api_keys_budget_column` (`:70-94`) — then unconditionally stamps `SCHEMA_VERSION` (`:61-64`) with no version-gated DDL body. There is no generic column-diff pass; grep for `ADD COLUMN` in `src/` returns only the single `apiKeys.monthly_budget_usd` site.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy has no generic additive-column migration, so columns added to `TABLES_SQL` after a database was created are never applied to that database, and `_meta.schema_version` is bumped to 2 without running any 1→2 DDL.
- **Why it matters**: Every runtime read of `combos` selects the `data` column (`src/db/sqlite/repo/combo_repo.rs:12,20`; `src/db/sqlite/export.rs:173`; `src/db/sqlite/import.rs:164`) and `usage_repo::get_history` selects `bytesBefore/bytesAfter/bytesSaved/imagePrompts` (`src/db/sqlite/repo/usage_repo.rs:11-16,26-30`). On a pre-existing database missing either set of columns those queries fail with `no such column`, which at startup (snapshot hydration in `src/db/mod.rs:148-152`) aborts boot, and at runtime 500s the combos page and the usage dashboard. `SCHEMA_VERSION` already reads `2` (`src/db/sqlite/schema.rs:13`), so the missing ALTER will never be retried.
- **Evidence**: 9router `.tmp/9router/src/lib/db/migrate.js:57-76,79-109` vs OpenProxy `src/db/sqlite/migrations.rs:47-94`, `src/db/sqlite/mod.rs:86-100`, `src/db/sqlite/schema.rs:13`
- **Parity fix**: Port `syncSchemaFromTables`: on every `init`, `PRAGMA table_info(<t>)` each table in `TABLES_SQL`'s declared column set and `ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN` any missing one (skipping PK/UNIQUE-bearing defs), then stamp the version. Alternatively make `apply_pending_migrations` run real `1→2` DDL before `set_schema_version(SCHEMA_VERSION)`.

## P72-001 · P1 · 9router src/lib/db/repos/aliasRepo.js:23-25 · OpenProxy src/db/sqlite/patch.rs:246-251

- **9router**: `customKey(providerAlias, id, type)` returns `` `${providerAlias}|${id}|${type}` `` (`aliasRepo.js:23-25`), and both `addCustomModel` (`:34-51`) and `deleteCustomModel` (`:53-55`) address the row by that composite key. Two providers may each define a custom model with the same bare `id`; the rows are distinct.
- **OpenProxy**: `custom_models_map` (`src/db/sqlite/patch.rs:246-251`) keys every custom model by `m.id` alone: `.map(|m| (m.id.clone(), …))`. The kv primary key is `(scope, key)` (`src/db/sqlite/schema.rs:133-138`), so the diff's `kv_repo::set` upsert (`src/db/sqlite/repo/kv_repo.rs:19-26`) overwrites the previous row when two providers share an `id`. `import.rs:180-192` uses the same bare-`id` key, so the import path is at least internally consistent. The in-memory `create_custom_model` guard (`src/server/api/models_custom.rs:97-101,122-128`) DOES compare provider+id+type, so both entries enter the Vec and the loss happens at the SQLite write.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy stores custom models in the `kv` table under a bare `id` key instead of 9router's `providerAlias|id|type` composite key, so identical model ids across providers collide on the primary key.
- **Why it matters**: Adding a custom model `gpt-4o` under `openai` and then under `anthropic` leaves only one row on disk. The in-memory list shows both until restart, then `export_all`/`kv_scope_to_array` (`src/db/sqlite/export.rs:289-300`) reads back a single row and one custom model silently disappears from the provider page's Available Models and from `ModelSelectModal`. AGENTS.md names the provider page model list as a surface that must survive rebuilds.
- **Evidence**: 9router `.tmp/9router/src/lib/db/repos/aliasRepo.js:23-25,34-55` vs OpenProxy `src/db/sqlite/patch.rs:246-251`, `src/db/sqlite/repo/kv_repo.rs:19-26`, `src/db/sqlite/schema.rs:133-138`
- **Parity fix**: Change `custom_models_map` to key on `format!("{}|{}|{}", m.provider_alias, m.id, m.r#type)` to match `aliasRepo.js:23-25`, and apply the same key in `import.rs:180-192` and wherever `deleteCustomModel` computes its row key. `getCustomModels` reads by scope only, so the read path needs no change.

## P73-001 · P1 · 9router src/lib/db/repos/connectionsRepo.js:112-122 · OpenProxy src/server/api/mod.rs:1170, src/server/api/admin_items.rs:262-264,397-403

- **9router**: `reorderInTx` (`connectionsRepo.js:112-122`) renumbers every connection of a provider to a dense `1..N` sequence, sorting by `(priority asc, updatedAt desc)`, and is invoked on create (`:207`), on update when `priority` is present (`:225`) and on delete (`:238`). When `priority` is omitted on create it defaults to `max(existing)+1` (`:183-186`), so a newly added connection deterministically lands at one end of the list.
- **OpenProxy**: No re-normalization exists. `create_provider_api` assigns `priority = Some(req.priority.unwrap_or(1))` (`src/server/api/mod.rs:1170`), `update_provider` assigns the raw value (`src/server/api/admin_items.rs:262-264`), and `delete_provider` just retains (`:397-403`); `connection_repo::update`/`delete` (`src/db/sqlite/repo/connection_repo.rs:159-176`) do no renumbering. Reads sort by `connection.priority.unwrap_or(999)` with a stable sort (`src/db/mod.rs:239`).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy never re-normalizes `providerConnections.priority` on create, update, or delete, so priorities collide and the dashboard's move-up/move-down reorder does not persist.
- **Why it matters**: Both dashboards reorder by PUTting `{priority: index}` to the two swapped rows (`web/src/components/ProviderDetailPageClient.tsx:918-946` ↔ `9router .../providers/[id]/page.js:880-896`). With OpenProxy's default all-at-1 priorities the swap produces a tie, the stable sort keeps the original order, and the re-sync at `:947` snaps the rows back — the arrow buttons appear to work (optimistic swap) and then revert. Under 9router the same click renumbers and the row actually moves. Connection order also drives account fallback selection, so a no-op reorder means the user's intended primary/secondary account order is not honoured.
- **Evidence**: 9router `.tmp/9router/src/lib/db/repos/connectionsRepo.js:112-122,183-186,207,225,238` vs OpenProxy `src/server/api/mod.rs:1170`, `src/server/api/admin_items.rs:262-264,397-403`, `src/db/sqlite/repo/connection_repo.rs:159-176`, `src/db/mod.rs:239`
- **Parity fix**: Add a `reorder_connections(conns: &mut Vec<ProviderConnection>, provider: &str)` helper mirroring `connectionsRepo.js:112-122` (sort by `(priority.unwrap_or(0), updatedAt desc)`, then renumber `i+1`) and call it at the end of the create, update (whenever `priority` changed) and delete mutations in `create_provider_api` / `update_provider` / `delete_provider`. Change the create default from `unwrap_or(1)` to `max(existing)+1` per `connectionsRepo.js:183-186`.

## P74-001 · P1 · 9router src/lib/db/repos/connectionsRepo.js:133-168 · OpenProxy src/server/api/oauth.rs:1490-1524

- **9router**: `createProviderConnection` dedupes before inserting, but only on a *matched identity*: for Codex it requires both rows to expose the same `providerSpecificData.chatgptAccountId` (`:144-147`); for other providers it requires matching `username` when either side has one, treating a one-sided username as a distinct identity (`:150-164`). The comment at `:139-143` states the reason explicitly — refresh tokens are rotated single-use, so collapsing a second login onto an existing bare-email row "overwrites the first account's token pair and makes it look invalid after adding a second account". When no match is found, a NEW row is inserted and `reorderInTx` runs.
- **OpenProxy**: `create_imported_oauth_connection` (`src/server/api/oauth.rs:1490-1524`) finds an existing row with `candidate.provider == provider && candidate.auth_type == "oauth" && candidate.email.as_deref() == Some(email)` (`:1505-1512`) — bare email only — then overwrites `access_token`, `refresh_token`, `expires_at`, `id_token`, `token_type`, `scope`, `test_status` and the whole `provider_specific_data` map. No `chatgptAccountId` / `username` comparison.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy collapses new OAuth logins onto an existing row by bare email alone, overwriting the stored token pair, while 9router requires a matching workspace/username identity before merging.
- **Why it matters**: A user with two accounts under the same email address (e.g. two ChatGPT workspaces on Codex) ends up with a single connection whose refresh token belongs to the second account. The first account is destroyed and the second silently takes its slot, so per-account quota tracking and account fallback across those two logins are impossible — precisely the failure 9router's comment documents. The wholesale `provider_specific_data` replacement additionally drops any user-set `proxyPoolId` / `connectionProxyUrl` on that connection on re-login.
- **Evidence**: 9router `.tmp/9router/src/lib/db/repos/connectionsRepo.js:133-168` vs OpenProxy `src/server/api/oauth.rs:1490-1524`
- **Parity fix**: Port the identity predicate from `connectionsRepo.js:144-164`: require `providerSpecificData.chatgptAccountId` equality when both sides have one (mandatory for Codex), otherwise require `providerSpecificData.username` equality and treat a one-sided username as a distinct identity. When no match is found, insert a new row. Also merge `provider_specific_data` key-by-key instead of replacing it wholesale, so proxy settings survive re-auth.

## P75-001 · P1 · 9router src/lib/db/repos/settingsRepo.js:20-25 · OpenProxy src/types/mod.rs:782

- **9router**: `DEFAULT_SETTINGS.capacityAdapter` seeds two pools ENABLED with empty model lists: `settingsRepo.js:20-25` → `{ vision: {enabled:true, roundRobin:false, models:[]}, pdf: {enabled:false,...}, audioInput: {enabled:true,...}, videoInput: {enabled:false,...} }`. `getCapacityAdapterConfig` (`open-sse/services/capacityAdapter.js:35-40`) then substitutes `DEFAULT_FALLBACK_MODEL = "oc/mimo-v2.5-free"` for an enabled-but-empty pool, so on a fresh install vision and audioInput requests are routed to that free model.
- **OpenProxy**: `Settings::default().capacity_adapter = json!({})` (`src/types/mod.rs:782`). `get_capacity_adapter_config` (`src/core/combo/capacity_adapter.rs:88-91`) does `settings.get(cap).map(normalize_cap_entry).unwrap_or_else(CapEntry::disabled)`, so every capability resolves to disabled. `PATCH /api/settings {capacityAdapter}` (`src/server/api/mod.rs:2602-2603`) can turn pools on, but nothing seeds the 9router defaults.
- **Gap**: The persisted default for the `capacityAdapter` settings key is `{}` instead of 9router's vision/audioInput-enabled object, so the capacity-adapter fallback pools are off by default.
- **Why it matters**: On a fresh install, a request carrying an image (`vision`) or audio input to a combo whose members are all text-only SUCCEEDS under 9router (routed to `oc/mimo-v2.5-free`) but FAILS under OpenProxy with no capable candidate. This is a routing difference on multimodal requests, not a cosmetic one.
- **Evidence**: 9router `.tmp/9router/src/lib/db/repos/settingsRepo.js:20-25`, `.tmp/9router/open-sse/services/capacityAdapter.js:16,35-40` vs OpenProxy `src/types/mod.rs:782`, `src/core/combo/capacity_adapter.rs:88-91`
- **Parity fix**: Change the `Settings::default()` initializer to `capacity_adapter: json!({ "vision": {"enabled": true, "roundRobin": false, "models": []}, "pdf": {"enabled": false, "roundRobin": false, "models": []}, "audioInput": {"enabled": true, "roundRobin": false, "models": []}, "videoInput": {"enabled": false, "roundRobin": false, "models": []} })`, keeping `CAPABILITY_KEYS` order (vision, pdf, audioInput, videoInput) as in `capacityAdapter.js:13`.

## P76-001 · P1 · 9router src/lib/db/repos/settingsRepo.js:27 · OpenProxy src/server/api/mod.rs:2238-2301 (UpdateSettingsRequest)

- **9router**: `DEFAULT_SETTINGS.requireApiKey = true` (`settingsRepo.js:27`). `updateSettings` (`settingsRepo.js:96-108`) does a raw read-merge-write of the settings JSON blob (`next = { ...current, ...updates }`), so ANY key the dashboard PATCHes is persisted verbatim and returned by `getSettings()` (which merges `DEFAULT_SETTINGS` at `:92-99`).
- **OpenProxy**: `Settings` (`src/types/mod.rs:477-786`) has no `require_api_key` field, and `UpdateSettingsRequest` (`src/server/api/mod.rs:2237-2301`) has no `require_api_key` field and no `#[serde(flatten)]` catch-all — serde silently drops the unknown key and the PATCH returns 200 with unchanged settings. `safe_settings_payload_with_db_path` (`src/server/api/mod.rs:700-703`) therefore never emits `requireApiKey`.
- **Gap**: `requireApiKey` is not a persisted setting in OpenProxy, so the dashboard's "Require API key" switch is a write-only no-op that silently reverts on every reload.
- **Why it matters**: `web/src/components/EndpointPageClient.tsx:409-418` PATCHes `{requireApiKey: value}` and optimistically sets local state whenever `res.ok` (always true). The toggle flips, then resets to `false` on the next `GET /api/settings` (`EndpointPageClient.tsx:365` reads `data.requireApiKey || false`). The UI also gates several security banners on this value (`:838`, `:1054`, `:1160`, `:1166`), so the page tells the user their gateway is open when it is not, and vice-versa.
- **Evidence**: 9router `.tmp/9router/src/lib/db/repos/settingsRepo.js:27,96-108` vs OpenProxy `src/server/api/mod.rs:2237-2301`, `src/types/mod.rs:477-786`, `web/src/components/EndpointPageClient.tsx:365,409-418`
- **Parity fix**: Declare `require_api_key` on both `Settings` (with `#[serde(default = "default_true")]` so existing rows inherit the 9router default) and `UpdateSettingsRequest`, and set `db.settings.require_api_key = v` in `update_settings_api`. Pair with DB-001 so the gate reads this field.

## P77-001 · P1 · 9router src/lib/db/repos/settingsRepo.js:96-108 · OpenProxy src/server/api/mod.rs:2302-2606 (update_settings_api)

- **9router**: `updateSettings` performs a raw JSON read-merge-write into the `settings.data` blob (`settingsRepo.js:101-107`), so the settings table is schema-less at the write boundary: any key a caller sends is stored, whether or not the Rust/JS reader knows it.
- **OpenProxy**: `UpdateSettingsRequest` is a closed struct with no `#[serde(flatten)] extra: Map` and no `deny_unknown_fields=false` catch-all, and `update_settings_api` (`src/server/api/mod.rs:2302-2606`) copies only declared fields. Two dashboard settings that OpenProxy's own readers depend on are undeclared and therefore dropped: `quotaVisibility` (read at `web/src/components/usage/ProviderLimits/index.tsx:295`, PATCHed at `:295` with no server counterpart anywhere in `src/`) and `ccFilterNaming` (read at `src/server/api/chat.rs:371-375` from `settings.extra["ccFilterNaming"]`, PATCHed at `web/src/components/cli-tools/ClaudeToolCard.tsx:179`).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's typed settings PATCH silently discards any setting key it does not declare, whereas 9router persists the raw JSON merge verbatim.
- **Why it matters**: `ccFilterNaming` can never become true: the OpenProxy bypass handler always passes `false` to `detect_bypass` (`chat.rs:375-377`), so Claude Code's `isNewTopic`/topic-extraction requests are never intercepted the way 9router intercepts them (`chat.js:90` → `bypassHandler.js:60`). `quotaVisibility` never persists, so hidden quota rows reappear on every reload. Both are user-visible toggles that appear to work and silently do nothing.
- **Evidence**: 9router `.tmp/9router/src/lib/db/repos/settingsRepo.js:101-107` vs OpenProxy `src/server/api/mod.rs:2237-2301`, `src/server/api/chat.rs:371-375`, `web/src/components/cli-tools/ClaudeToolCard.tsx:179`, `web/src/components/usage/ProviderLimits/index.tsx:295`
- **Parity fix**: Add `#[serde(flatten)] pub extra: serde_json::Map<String, Value>` to `UpdateSettingsRequest` and merge the unrecognised keys into `db.settings.extra` after the typed copies — mirroring `mergeProviderSpecificData` semantics. At minimum declare `cc_filter_naming` and `quota_visibility` explicitly so the existing readers get fed.

## P78-001 · P1 · 9router src/lib/db/schema.js:89-99 (combos) · OpenProxy src/db/sqlite/repo/combo_repo.rs:26-50

- **9router**: The `combos` table has no `data`/`extra` column at all (`schema.js:89-99`); `createCombo`/`updateCombo` (`repos/combosRepo.js:35-67`) persist only `name`, `kind`, `models`, `updatedAt`, and 9router has no per-combo `disabledModels` concept.
- **OpenProxy**: `Combo.disabled_models` is a typed serde field (`src/types/mod.rs:406`, serialized as `disabledModels`). `combo_repo::create`/`update` write `serde_json::to_string(&c.extra)` into `combos.data` (`src/db/sqlite/repo/combo_repo.rs:28,38`) and `row_to_combo` (`:70`) rebuilds `extra` from that blob with `..Default::default()` for everything else. Because `disabled_models` is a declared field it never lands in `extra`, so it is never written. `PUT /api/combos/{id}` does set it in memory (`src/server/api/admin_items.rs:491-493`) and the dispatcher reads it (`src/core/combo/mod.rs:740`), so it works until restart. Note `import.rs:227-243` (`combo_data_json`) is inconsistent with the repo layer — it copies every non-canonical top-level key, so `disabledModels` survives a DB import but not a normal restart.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's `Combo.disabled_models` field has no persistence column: the combo repo serializes only `extra`, so muted combo members are lost on every restart even though the API accepts and the dispatcher honours the field.
- **Why it matters**: A user who mutes a known-bad combo member via `PUT /api/combos/{id} {"disabledModels":[…]}` sees it respected until the next restart, after which the member is dispatched again — a routing change that reappears without any user action. `kind`, `strategy`, `isActive` and `extra` persist correctly because they do live in `extra`, which makes the `disabledModels` loss specifically surprising.
- **Evidence**: 9router `.tmp/9router/src/lib/db/schema.js:89-99`, `.tmp/9router/src/lib/db/repos/combosRepo.js:35-67` vs OpenProxy `src/db/sqlite/repo/combo_repo.rs:26-50,57-82`, `src/types/mod.rs:406`, `src/server/api/admin_items.rs:491-493`
- **Parity fix**: In `combo_repo::create`/`update`, build the `data` blob from a full `serde_json::to_value(&combo)` minus the canonical columns (i.e. reuse the logic already present in `import.rs::combo_data_json`) instead of `c.extra`, and in `row_to_combo` deserialise the blob into a `Combo` rather than into `extra` alone — keeping `extra` as the `#[serde(flatten)]` remainder.

## P79-001 · P1 · 9router src/sse/handlers/chat.js:99-101; OpenProxy src/core/combo/mod.rs:169-184 + src/types/mod.rs:358-363

- **9router**: `const comboSpecificStrategy = comboStrategies[modelStr]?.fallbackStrategy; const comboStrategy = comboSpecificStrategy || settings.comboStrategy || "fallback";` (chat.js:100-101). The || chain tests the VALUE, so a comboStrategies[name] entry that exists but carries no fallbackStrategy (e.g. only judgeModel) falls through to the global settings.comboStrategy. The identical chain is repeated at chat.js:176-177 and in fetch.js:96, search.js:76, tts.js:51, imageGeneration.js:53.
- **OpenProxy**: strategy_for_combo tests the MAP ENTRY, not the value: `if let Some(entry) = snapshot.settings.combo_strategies.get(combo_name) { entry.strategy_name() }` (mod.rs:170-171), and ComboStrategyEntry::strategy_name() returns `c.fallback_strategy.as_deref().unwrap_or("fallback")` for the object form (types/mod.rs:361). So an entry present without fallbackStrategy resolves to Fallback and the global settings.combo_strategy is never consulted.
- **Gap**: An existing-but-strategy-less comboStrategies[name] entry masks the global comboStrategy and forces Fallback, instead of falling through to the global default.
- **Why it matters**: With the global comboStrategy set to round-robin (the documented Profile setting), a combo whose settings entry carries only judgeModel or fusionTuning - both writable via PATCH /api/settings {"comboStrategies":{...}} and producible by the media-combo round-robin toggle that writes `updated[combo.name] = { fallbackStrategy: "round-robin" }` and later deletes it - silently loses round-robin rotation. Every request keeps hammering the first member. The operator sees a global round-robin setting that is not applied to that combo.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/sse/handlers/chat.js:100-101 (`comboSpecificStrategy || settings.comboStrategy || "fallback"`). OpenProxy: src/core/combo/mod.rs:170-171 (`if let Some(entry) = ...get(combo_name) { entry.strategy_name() }`), src/types/mod.rs:361 (`fallback_strategy.as_deref().unwrap_or("fallback")`).
- **Parity fix**: Change strategy_for_combo to mirror the JS value-chain: resolve the per-combo fallback_strategy, and only fall back to snapshot.settings.combo_strategy when that resolved string is None or empty - i.e. `let specific = entry.and_then(|e| e.fallback_strategy.as_deref()).filter(|s| !s.is_empty());` then `specific.unwrap_or(&snapshot.settings.combo_strategy)`. Keep combo.extra["strategy"] as the next-priority source per the current precedence, but only when the per-combo entry supplied no value.

## P80-001 · P1 · 9router src/sse/handlers/search.js:73-86, tts.js:48-61, imageGeneration.js:50-63; OpenProxy src/server/api/chat_search.rs:233-248, src/server/api/media.rs:62-74 and 124-130

- **9router**: Combo names are expanded on four non-chat endpoints, each with the same strategy/sticky resolution as chat: search.js:73-86 (getComboModelsFromData -> handleComboChat with comboName: providerInput), tts.js:48-61 (getComboModels(modelStr) -> handleComboChat), imageGeneration.js:50-63 (same). A client that sets model or provider to a combo name on /v1/search, /v1/audio/speech, or /v1/images/generations gets the full fallback / round-robin / fusion treatment.
- **OpenProxy**: Combo expansion exists on exactly two surfaces: chat.rs:496 (/v1/chat/completions, /v1/messages, /v1/responses, /v1beta) and web_fetch.rs:115 (/v1/web/fetch). /v1/search resolves the model through resolve_search_provider(model_str) with a bare provider field fallback and never consults snapshot.combos (chat_search.rs:240-248). /v1/audio/speech (media.rs:62-74) and /v1/images/generations (media.rs:124-130) go straight into generic_media_handler; src/core/media/ contains no reference to combos at all.
- **Gap**: /v1/search, /v1/audio/speech, and /v1/images/generations never expand a combo name, so a combo configured as a search, TTS, or image model or provider is treated as a literal provider id.
- **Why it matters**: A user who creates a combo named my-search and points a search-capable CLI or the media-providers dashboard at it gets a provider-not-found failure on OpenProxy, while the same configuration works on 9router. The /v1/models listing still advertises the combo (or would, if kind-matching passed), so the config looks valid and the failure only appears at call time.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/sse/handlers/search.js:73-86, src/sse/handlers/tts.js:48-61, src/sse/handlers/imageGeneration.js:50-63. OpenProxy: src/server/api/chat_search.rs:240-248 (no combo branch), src/server/api/media.rs:62-74 (audio_speech), :124-130 (images_generations); `grep -rn combo src/core/media/` returns nothing.
- **Parity fix**: Add the same combo branch used in web_fetch.rs:114-141 to chat_search.rs before provider resolution, and to the TTS and image entry points in media.rs. For search/TTS/image use execute_combo_strategy(&combo_models, Some(&name), strategy_for_combo(&snapshot, &name), ...) and return the first successful member; gate TTS/image on the combo's kind (llm/tts/image) the way combo_matches_kinds does so an LLM-only combo is not accepted by /v1/audio/speech.

## P81-001 · P1 · 9router src/sse/handlers/videoGeneration.js:135,149,171,177 + src/sse/services/auth.js:110-134  ⟷  openproxy src/server/api/media.rs:726-742, 1302

- **9router**: The create path maintains account-fallback state on every call. getProviderCredentials (videoGeneration.js:135) filters candidates through isModelLockActive (auth.js:85-98) and, when every account is locked, returns `{allRateLimited:true, retryAfter, retryAfterHuman, lastError, lastErrorCode}` (auth.js:110-134). checkAndRefreshToken (videoGeneration.js:149) proactively refreshes a token expiring inside the 5-minute lead (tokenRefresh.js:224; oauthCredentialManager.js:45-50). On failure markAccountUnavailable (videoGeneration.js:177, auth.js:239) records the cooldown and per-model lock; on success clearAccountError (videoGeneration.js:171) clears it. When all accounts are rate-limited the handler returns unavailableResponse (videoGeneration.js:141), which sets a Retry-After header (utils/error.js:116-129).
- **OpenProxy**: select_media_connections (media.rs:726-742) filters only on `connection.provider == provider && connection.is_active() && connection_has_credentials(...)`, sorted by priority. It never consults the AccountRegistry, and `media.rs` contains no reference to `state.account_registry` at all — contrast src/server/api/chat.rs:1535 and src/server/api/web_fetch.rs:223, which both do. The create loop (media.rs:1302) therefore iterates a fixed, never-mutated list: there is no markAccountUnavailable equivalent, no clearAccountError equivalent, no allRateLimited branch, and no Retry-After emission anywhere in the file (grep for account_registry / mark / retry_after in media.rs returns zero hits).
- **Gap**: The video create path keeps no account-cooldown, model-lock or rate-limit state — it neither records failures nor skips accounts that are already known to be down, and it never returns a 429 with Retry-After.
- **Why it matters**: A video account that just returned 401 or 429 stays at the head of the priority list and is re-selected first on the next request, so every call re-hits the known-dead account, burns the 401/403 refresh path, and still fails — while 9router has already locked it out and moved to the next account, and once all accounts are locked answers 429 + Retry-After so the client can back off instead of hot-looping. Because nothing is recorded, the Providers dashboard never shows lastError/errorCode for a video failure, so the user has no signal that re-auth is needed. On the poll side the same gap means a job pinned to a cooled account is not honoured, compounding P1-004.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/sse/handlers/videoGeneration.js:135 (getProviderCredentials), :141 + open-sse/utils/error.js:116-129 (unavailableResponse / Retry-After), :149 (checkAndRefreshToken), :171 (clearAccountError), :177 (markAccountUnavailable), src/sse/services/auth.js:85-98 + :110-134 (model-lock filter, allRateLimited). openproxy: src/server/api/media.rs:726-742 (priority-only filter), :1302-1480 (fixed iteration, no state mutation), :1114-1121 (json_error_response, the only error builder, never emits Retry-After); absence confirmed against src/server/api/chat.rs:1535 and src/server/api/web_fetch.rs:223.
- **Parity fix**: Filter the candidate list through the AccountRegistry the way chat.rs:1535 already does: drop connections whose model lock is active or whose unavailable-until is in the future, and when the surviving list is empty return the earliest lock expiry as 429 with a Retry-After header plus the `[provider/model]` message, matching unavailableResponse (error.js:116-129). After each attempt call apply_error_state on failure (9router's markAccountUnavailable semantics, restricted to 401/403/429 per CREATE_ROTATION_STATUSES) and reset_account_state on success. Add the pre-emptive checkAndRefreshToken equivalent before the upstream call so a token inside the 5-minute TOKEN_EXPIRY_BUFFER_MS is refreshed before it is spent, not after a 401.

## P82-001 · P1 · 9router src/sse/handlers/videoGeneration.js:44-53 vs OpenProxy src/server/api/media.rs:1183-1187, :1506-1510

- **9router**: requireValidApiKey (videoGeneration.js:44-53) gates on `settings.requireApiKey` — a dedicated flag, default true (src/lib/db/repos/settingsRepo.js:27) — and is invoked for BOTH create and poll (videoGeneration.js:110, :199). It is independent of the separate `requireLogin` dashboard flag (settingsRepo.js:26).
- **OpenProxy**: Both video_create_handler (media.rs:1183-1187) and video_get_handler_with_query (media.rs:1506-1510) gate on `settings.require_login`, which OpenProxy uses as the single combined flag (src/types/mod.rs:524, default true). There is no `requireApiKey` field in the OpenProxy Settings struct at all (grep for require_api_key/requireApiKey in src/types/mod.rs returns nothing).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy gates the video endpoints on require_login instead of 9router's requireApiKey, so the two independent 9router settings are collapsed into one and the video-specific flag does not exist.
- **Why it matters**: An operator who turns OFF requireApiKey in 9router (to let a local CLI reach /v1 without a key) but leaves requireLogin on gets a 401 from OpenProxy on every video call, and vice-versa an operator who disables the dashboard login requirement unintentionally opens the video endpoints. The two settings are separately toggled in the 9router dashboard, so this is a user-visible, silently-divergent auth posture on a billable endpoint.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/sse/handlers/videoGeneration.js:44-53 (uses settings.requireApiKey at :47), :110, :199; flag defined at src/lib/db/repos/settingsRepo.js:27. OpenProxy: src/server/api/media.rs:1183, :1506 (uses settings.require_login); field defined at src/types/mod.rs:524; no requireApiKey equivalent exists.
- **Parity fix**: Add a `require_api_key: bool` (default true, camelCase `requireApiKey` on the wire) field to the OpenProxy Settings struct in src/types/mod.rs, migrate it alongside require_login, and switch the two video gates (media.rs:1183, :1506) to it — preserving 9router's semantics where requireLogin and requireApiKey are independent. Apply the same field to the other media handlers that currently read require_login (media.rs:140, :464) for consistency with 9router's stt.js:32 / tts.js:37 / embeddings.js:56 / imageGeneration.js:40, all of which check settings.requireApiKey.

## P83-001 · P1 · 9router src/sse/handlers/videoGeneration.js:97-104  ⟷  openproxy src/server/api/media.rs:1477, 1628

- **9router**: withConnectionHeader (videoGeneration.js:97-104) sets `headers.set("x-9router-connection-id", String(connectionId))` on the create response (videoGeneration.js:173) and on the poll response (videoGeneration.js:231). Clients are expected to echo it back as `x-connection-id`, which is read at videoGeneration.js:127 (create, passed to getProviderCredentials as preferredConnectionId) and videoGeneration.js:203 (poll, passed to resolveGetProvider). The write name and the read name differ on purpose: the read side accepts the generic `x-connection-id`, the write side uses the product-branded spelling.
- **OpenProxy**: All five emission sites write `x-openproxy-connection-id`: media.rs:402 (raw multipart forward), :1446 (post-refresh create retry), :1477 (create), :1628 (poll), :2272 (vertex response). The read side at media.rs:1794 and media.rs:2343-2344 accepts `x-connection-id` and additionally `x-openproxy-connection-id`, so OpenProxy is a strict superset on input — but it never emits the `x-9router-connection-id` name a 9router client is written to read (grep for "9router-connection" across src/ and web/ returns zero hits).
- **Gap**: The response header that pins a video job to the account that created it is named `x-openproxy-connection-id` in OpenProxy versus `x-9router-connection-id` in 9router, so a 9router-shaped client reads undefined and cannot pin the poll.
- **Why it matters**: Video jobs are account-bound upstream. A client written against 9router reads `x-9router-connection-id`, gets undefined against OpenProxy, and omits `x-connection-id` on the poll — so OpenProxy falls back to priority order (media.rb:2361 → select_media_connections) and, on a multi-account xAI or Vertex setup, polls the wrong account, getting a job-not-found 404 for a job that is genuinely still rendering. The reverse direction also breaks: a client that learned to echo `x-openproxy-connection-id` has that header ignored by 9router, which reads only `x-connection-id` (videoGeneration.js:127, 203). The `video_get` response re-emits the same branded name, so the value never becomes usable across the two implementations.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/sse/handlers/videoGeneration.js:102 (writes x-9router-connection-id), :173 and :231 (applied to create/poll responses), :127 and :203 (reads x-connection-id). openproxy: src/server/api/media.rs:1477 and :1628 (writes x-openproxy-connection-id), :402, :1446, :2272 (same on the other paths), :1794 and :2343-2344 (reads x-connection-id plus the openproxy spelling).
- **Parity fix**: Emit both names on every video create and poll response — insert `x-9router-connection-id` alongside `x-openproxy-connection-id` at media.rs:402, 1446, 1477, 1628 and 2272. Emitting the 9router name is the parity requirement; keeping the openproxy name preserves compatibility for any client already written against this build. Keep the read side as-is, since accepting both spellings is a harmless superset. A shared `with_connection_header(conn_id, &mut response)` helper mirroring videoGeneration.js:97-104 will keep the five sites from drifting apart.

## P84-001 · P1 · 9router: open-sse/config/runtimeConfig.js:53 + open-sse/providers/registry/qoder.js:23 + open-sse/handlers/chatCore/streamingHandler.js:87; openproxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:110

- **9router**: `STREAM_STALL_TIMEOUT_MS = envMs("STREAM_STALL_TIMEOUT_MS", 360 * 1000)` (runtimeConfig.js:53). streamingHandler.js:87 resolves `const stallTimeoutMs = PROVIDERS[provider]?.stallTimeoutMs || STREAM_STALL_TIMEOUT_MS;` and qoder overrides it to 120000 (registry/qoder.js:23). The watchdog is re-armed on every raw upstream byte (streamHandler.js:239), independent of transform output.
- **OpenProxy**: `const SSE_STALL_TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(180);` (chat.rs:110), applied unconditionally at chat.rs:3852 and 4012 via `tokio::time::timeout(SSE_STALL_TIMEOUT, upstream.try_next())`. There is no per-provider override and no env knob.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy hard-codes a 180 s stall timeout for every provider; 9Router uses 360 s by default and 120 s for Qoder.
- **Why it matters**: OpenProxy aborts a healthy-but-slow reasoning stream 180 s into any upstream silence where 9Router would keep waiting. Long Claude extended-thinking / Kiro prefill runs that emit no bytes for 3-6 minutes are cut off mid-answer. Conversely for Qoder the direction reverses and OpenProxy hangs 60 s longer than 9Router on a genuinely dead connection, tying up an account slot (MAX_IN_FLIGHT_PER_ACCOUNT) and delaying provider fallback.
- **Evidence**: 9router open-sse/config/runtimeConfig.js:53, open-sse/providers/registry/qoder.js:23, open-sse/handlers/chatCore/streamingHandler.js:87, open-sse/utils/streamHandler.js:202-210 | openproxy src/server/api/chat.rs:110, 3852, 4012
- **Parity fix**: Make the timeout a runtime-config constant defaulting to 360 s and read a per-provider override before the stream is built: `let stall = crate::core::config::runtime_config::STREAM_STALL_TIMEOUT; if provider == "qoder" { stall = Duration::from_millis(120_000); }` at chat.rs:3852/4012, and honour an env override equivalent to 9router's `envMs("STREAM_STALL_TIMEOUT_MS", ...)`.

## P85-001 · P1 · 9router: open-sse/handlers/chatCore/sseToJsonHandler.js:228-230, 261-269, 275-283; openproxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:3442-3507 (esp. 3503) and src/core/media/responses/stream_to_json.rs:445-530

- **9router**: `handleForcedSSEToJson` branches on the CLIENT format after collapsing the SSE. If `sourceFormat === FORMATS.OPENAI_RESPONSES` it returns the Responses object verbatim (:228-230). If `sourceFormat` is `ANTIGRAVITY`/`GEMINI`/`GEMINI_CLI` it returns a Gemini envelope `{response:{candidates:[{content:{role:"model",parts:[{text}]}}],usageMetadata:{promptTokenCount,candidatesTokenCount,totalTokenCount},modelVersion,responseId}}` (:261-269). Otherwise it returns a `chat.completion` with a folded `usage` block and `prompt_tokens_details` (:275-283). Tool calls are lifted from Responses `function_call` output items for all three shapes (:250-259).
- **OpenProxy**: `proxy_sse_to_json_response` (chat.rs:3442-3507) calls `sse_stream_to_json(&body_bytes, Some(model))`, which unconditionally returns a `chat.completion` (stream_to_json.rs:522-530), then sanitizes and returns it. There is no branch on `plan.source_format`; chat.rs:3503 is literally `let _ = plan; // reserved for future format-specific collapse`.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's forced-SSE->JSON collapse always emits a `chat.completion`, with no Responses-object branch and no Gemini `candidates` envelope branch.
- **Why it matters**: Two clients break. (1) Codex CLI / any Responses-API client sending `stream:false` against a forceStream provider (codex, openai, commandcode, codebuddy-*, grok-cli, zed, opencode, api-airforce) receives `object:"chat.completion"` where it expects a `response` object and fails schema validation. (2) Gemini CLI / `/v1beta` clients against the same providers receive an OpenAI envelope instead of `candidates[]`, so the SDK finds no content and renders an empty reply.
