- **Evidence**: 9router open-sse/handlers/chatCore/sseToJsonHandler.js:228-230, 250-269, 275-283 | openproxy src/server/api/chat.rs:3442-3507 (3445-3469, 3503), src/core/media/responses/stream_to_json.rs:445-530
- **Parity fix**: Add the two missing branches inside `proxy_sse_to_json_response`, keyed on `plan.source_format`: return the raw Responses object when `source_format == Format::OpenAiResponses`, and wrap the extracted text in the Gemini `{response:{candidates:[...],usageMetadata:{...},modelVersion,responseId}}` envelope when it is `Gemini`/`GeminiCli`/`Antigravity`. Lift `function_call` output items into OpenAI `tool_calls` (and, for the Gemini shape, into `functionCall` parts) first, as sseToJsonHandler.js:250-259 does.

## P86-001 · P1 · 9router: open-sse/handlers/chatCore/streamingHandler.js:62-80; openproxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:3756-3794 (esp. 3760-3762, 3767-3769, 3792)

- **9router**: streamingHandler.js:62-80: `if (upstreamContentType && !upstreamContentType.includes('text/event-stream') && !upstreamContentType.includes('application/json'))` -> read the body, extract `<title>`, strip tags, clamp to 160 chars, and return `{error:{message:"[<status>]: <shortMsg>"}}` with `status = providerResponse.status || 502`. `application/json` is explicitly ALLOWED through to the SSE pipe; the guard only fires for genuinely non-SSE types (e.g. a Cloudflare HTML 5xx page).
- **OpenProxy**: chat.rs:3756-3762 requires `ct.contains("text/html") || ct.contains("application/json") || ct.contains("text/plain")` — so `application/json` is BLOCKED. It always returns `StatusCode::BAD_GATEWAY` (chat.rs:3792) with message `Upstream returned non-SSE content-type '<ct>': <body>`, and the body snippet is truncated with `if msg.len() > 500 { format!("{}…", &msg[..500]) }` (chat.rs:3767-3769).
- **Gap**: Three divergences: OpenProxy rejects `application/json` (9Router allows it), always returns HTTP 502 (9Router echoes the upstream status, so a 200 HTML intercept stays 200), and slices a `String` at byte offset 500 which panics on a non-char-boundary.
- **Why it matters**: (1) Any upstream that returns `application/json` on a streaming request — several OpenAI-compatible gateways, a forced-stream provider that answered non-streaming anyway, and every provider's JSON error envelope — is converted into a 502 by OpenProxy but passes through under 9Router, so transient gateway behaviour turns into hard failures. (2) `&msg[..500]` panics (`byte index 500 is not a char boundary`) whenever the body has a multi-byte character before offset 500 — exactly the case for localized Cloudflare challenge pages — taking down the request instead of returning the clean error 9Router guarantees. (3) Clients that branch on status see 502 where 9Router returned 200.
- **Evidence**: 9router open-sse/handlers/chatCore/streamingHandler.js:62-80 (allow-list at :63, status at :69, 160-char clamp at :66) | openproxy src/server/api/chat.rs:3756-3762, 3767-3769, 3786-3793
- **Parity fix**: Mirror streamingHandler.js:63 — drop the `application/json` (and `text/plain`) clauses so only genuinely non-SSE types are blocked. Replace the byte slice with `msg.chars().take(160).collect::<String>()`. Extract the `<title>` tag the way 9Router does and format the message as `format!("[{status}]: {short_msg}")`, and return `response.status()` (falling back to 502) instead of a hard-coded BAD_GATEWAY.

## P87-001 · P1 · 9router: open-sse/handlers/embeddingsCore.js:106-114 (single retry, only on new credentials) vs OpenProxy: src/core/media/embeddings/handler.rs:78-140 (unconditional re-fire of the original request)

- **9router**: On a 401/403 the core calls refreshWithRetry(() => executor.refreshCredentials(credentials, log), 3, log) (embeddingsCore.js:82-86) and re-fires the request ONLY when the refresh actually produced new material — `if (newCredentials?.accessToken || newCredentials?.apiKey)` (embeddingsCore.js:88). If refresh returned nothing (the common case: DefaultExecutor.refreshCredentials returns null immediately when `!credentials.refreshToken`, default.js:221, and there is no refresher arm for most providers, default.js:222-233), the else branch just logs `refresh failed` (embeddingsCore.js:104) and falls through to the `if (!providerResponse.ok)` error path (embeddingsCore.js:106) using the ORIGINAL 401/403 response. So an API-key provider (openai, openrouter, mistral, …) that returns 401 results in exactly ONE upstream request.
- **OpenProxy**: On 401/403 the handler enters the refresh block (handler.rs:78) but the refresh attempt is gated on a non-empty refresh_token (handler.rs:85) and on dispatch_oauth_refresh succeeding (handler.rs:86). When either fails — which is every API-key provider, since they carry no refresh_token — control falls out of both `if` blocks to handler.rs:133-139, which re-POSTs the original URL, original headers, and original body a SECOND time. That second request uses the same rejected credentials, so it is guaranteed to fail identically. The retry is also fire-and-forget: unlike 9router's refreshWithRetry (tokenRefresh.js:257-275, 3 attempts with 1s/2s backoff), dispatch_oauth_refresh is called once with no retry and no delay.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy re-fires the upstream request unconditionally after a 401/403, including when no refresh was possible, so every auth failure costs a duplicate identical request that 9router never makes.
- **Why it matters**: Every 401/403 from an API-key embedding provider doubles the upstream request count. A client with a bad key, or a provider that has revoked/rotated the key, generates 2× the requests and roughly 2× the latency before the error surfaces. Providers with per-request billing or aggressive burst limiting (nvidia, github, together) can rate-limit or bill the duplicate. It also mis-signals health: the connection appears to be making progress when it is just retrying with the same dead credential. And where a refresh IS possible, OpenProxy attempts it once with no backoff, so a transient token-endpoint blip that 9router's 3-attempt refreshWithRetry would ride out becomes a hard failure here.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/handlers/embeddingsCore.js:82-86 (refreshWithRetry, 3 attempts), :88 (`if (newCredentials?.accessToken || newCredentials?.apiKey)` — the ONLY path to a retry), :104-106 (else → log, fall through to error on the original response); open-sse/executors/default.js:221 (`if (!credentials.refreshToken) return null`), :222-233 (no arm for most providers); open-sse/services/tokenRefresh.js:257-275 (refreshWithRetry backoff). OpenProxy: src/core/media/embeddings/handler.rs:78-85 (401/403 + refresh_token gate), :86 (single dispatch_oauth_refresh, no retry), :131-139 (unconditional re-POST of the original request).
- **Parity fix**: Make the re-fire conditional on a successful refresh, matching embeddingsCore.js:88. Restructure handler.rs:78-140 so the second POST lives INSIDE the branch where dispatch_oauth_refresh returned Ok AND refresh.access_token is non-empty — build retry_req from `refreshed` there (as the code already does at handler.rs:99-103) and return its result. When there is no refresh_token, or dispatch_oauth_refresh errors, fall through directly to the `!res.status().is_success()` check at handler.rs:142 using the ORIGINAL response, exactly as 9router does. Additionally wrap the refresh in a 3-attempt loop with 1s/2s backoff to match refreshWithRetry (tokenRefresh.js:257-275).

## P88-001 · P1 · 9router: open-sse/utils/responsesStreamHelpers.js:29-31 + 34-49, open-sse/utils/stream.js:256-280 + 465-480, open-sse/handlers/chatCore/streamingHandler.js:85-86, open-sse/utils/streamHandler.js:104-112 + 155-163; openproxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:3924-3926 + 3959-3971

- **9router**: For a Responses->Responses passthrough (`isResponsesPassthrough`, streamingHandler.js:85) the handler wires `onAbortTerminal = buildAbortedResponsesTerminalBytes`. On clean EOF, stream.js:465-480 synthesizes `event: response.failed` (`{type:"response.failed", response:{id:resp_<ts>, status:"failed", error:{type:"stream_error", code:"stream_disconnected", message:"stream closed before response.completed"}}}`) followed by `data: [DONE]` when no terminal event was seen. On disconnect or abort, `emitTerminal` (streamHandler.js:104-112, 155-163) emits the same bytes before closing, and stream.js:256-280 does it for a missing terminal on the `done` sentinel. The same `[DONE]` guard runs in the passthrough flush at stream.js:401-405.
- **OpenProxy**: There is no equivalent helper anywhere (`grep -rn "build_aborted_responses" src/` -> no match). When `needs_stream_translation()` is false, the stream simply ends at `Ok(Ok(None)) => break` (chat.rs:3928) and falls through to the transform flush; the passthrough branch yields only `sanitize_sse_chunk` output (chat.rs:3924-3926) and `t_state` is `None`, so `finish_stream` at chat.rs:3959-3971 is not even called. Same for the Hyper twin at chat.rs:4063-4101.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy never synthesizes the `response.failed` + `data: [DONE]` terminal for a Responses-API stream that ends, stalls, or is aborted before a terminal event.
- **Why it matters**: Codex CLI (and any Responses client) treats the absence of `response.completed`/`response.failed` as a protocol violation and surfaces a raw stream error, or waits for a socket close that never comes. Under a transient upstream cut, 9Router hands the client a well-formed `response.failed` that the CLI renders as a normal error with a retry affordance; OpenProxy hands it a truncated stream. This is the exact "disconnect recovery" path named in the audit brief.
- **Evidence**: 9router open-sse/utils/responsesStreamHelpers.js:6-11, 19-24, 29-31, 34-49; open-sse/utils/stream.js:256-280, 401-405, 465-480; open-sse/handlers/chatCore/streamingHandler.js:85-86; open-sse/utils/streamHandler.js:104-112, 155-163 | openproxy src/server/api/chat.rs:3924-3928, 3959-3971, 4063-4101
- **Parity fix**: Add `format_incomplete_responses_failure()` returning `event: response.failed\ndata: {...code:"stream_disconnected"...}\n\n` plus `is_responses_terminal_event(name, chunk)`, and track a `terminal_seen` flag in the passthrough closure. On clean EOF, on the stall arms, and on upstream-error arms, when `source_format == target_format == Format::OpenAiResponses` and no terminal was seen, yield the `response.failed` frame followed by `data: [DONE]\n\n` before returning.

## P89-001 · P1 · 9router: open-sse/utils/stream.js:60 + 110-112; openproxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:4169-4170, openai_to_claude.rs:120, openai_responses.rs:1323 + 1371, claude_to_openai.rs:397

- **9router**: stream.js:60 creates one decoder per stream — `new TextDecoder("utf-8", { fatal: false })` — and decodes incrementally at stream.js:112 with `decoder.decode(chunk, { stream: true })`, then drains the final partial sequence in `flush()` at stream.js:382-383 via `decoder.decode()`. Partial multi-byte sequences are carried across chunk boundaries correctly.
- **OpenProxy**: Every consumer converts the raw chunk independently with `String::from_utf8_lossy(chunk)` and re-encodes: `sanitize_sse_chunk` (chat.rs:4169-4174) does `String::from_utf8_lossy(chunk)` then `sanitize_sse_body(&text).into_bytes()`; the transforms do the same before `push_str` into their buffers (claude_to_openai.rs:397, openai_responses.rs:1371). `from_utf8_lossy` substitutes U+FFFD for an incomplete trailing sequence.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy decodes each HTTP chunk in isolation instead of with a streaming decoder, so a multi-byte UTF-8 character split across a chunk boundary is corrupted into U+FFFD replacement characters.
- **Why it matters**: Any CJK, emoji, or accented content in a streamed answer can be mangled at chunk boundaries. With ~200-800 byte SSE frames and 3-byte CJK characters, boundary splits are routine over a long stream, so this corrupts a handful of characters per response — invisible in a smoke test, visible to users as mojibake in Claude Code / Cursor output.
- **Evidence**: 9router open-sse/utils/stream.js:60, 110-112, 382-383 | openproxy src/server/api/chat.rs:4169-4174, src/core/translator/response/claude_to_openai.rs:397, src/core/translator/response/openai_responses.rs:1323, 1371, src/core/translator/response/openai_to_claude.rs:120
- **Parity fix**: Introduce a per-stream incremental decoder. The simplest faithful port is to hold a `Vec<u8>` of undecoded trailing bytes on `ResponseTransformState` (and on the passthrough closure in chat.rs), run `std::str::from_utf8` on the concatenation, and push only the valid prefix into the text buffer — carrying the raw byte tail forward when the sequence is incomplete. Equivalent to TextDecoder's `{stream:true}` plus a final `decode()`.

## P90-001 · P1 · 9router: open-sse/utils/stream.js:88-93, 204-215, 356-366; openproxy: src/core/translator/response/openai_to_claude.rs:473-481, claude_to_openai.rs:256-275, 322-341

- **9router**: stream.js:356-366 runs for EVERY terminal frame in translate mode: `const isFinishChunk = item.type === "message_delta" || item.choices?.[0]?.finish_reason;` then (a) if the item has no valid usage and `totalContentLength > 0`, sets `item.usage = filterUsageForFormat(estimateUsage(body, totalContentLength, sourceFormat), sourceFormat)` and stores the estimate in `state.usage`; (b) else if `state.usage` exists, sets `item.usage = filterUsageForFormat(addBufferToUsage(state.usage), sourceFormat)`. The passthrough branch does the same at :204-215. And `finalizeStream` (stream.js:88-93) falls back to `estimateUsage` whenever `hasValidUsage` is false, so the logged/recorded usage is never null.
- **OpenProxy**: `openai_to_claude_response` emits `message_delta` with `usage` taken from `state["usage"]`, which is only populated when an OpenAI chunk carried a `usage` object; otherwise `unwrap_or_else(|| json!({"input_tokens":0,"output_tokens":0}))` (openai_to_claude.rs:473-481). `claude_to_openai_response` has the equivalent zeros path. `grep -rn "estimate_usage|add_buffer_to_usage|filter_usage_for_format" src/` returns nothing — OpenProxy has no token estimator, no buffer adder and no per-format usage filter anywhere, and `registry.rs:614-658 finish_stream` only handles Kiro.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy never estimates or back-fills usage: when the upstream stream carries no `usage` block (the default unless the client sets `stream_options.include_usage`), the terminal frame reports 0/0.
- **Why it matters**: Claude Code reads `message_delta.usage.output_tokens` to drive its own token accounting and context-compaction heuristics; the AI SDK surfaces `usage` to callers. 9Router clients always get a plausible non-zero number; OpenProxy clients get 0 and appear to have consumed no tokens. Dashboard cost/billing for providers that omit stream usage is likewise zero instead of estimated.
- **Evidence**: 9router open-sse/utils/stream.js:88-93, 204-215, 356-366 | openproxy src/core/translator/response/openai_to_claude.rs:473-481, src/core/translator/response/claude_to_openai.rs:256-275, 322-341, src/core/translator/registry.rs:614-658
- **Parity fix**: Port `estimateUsage` / `addBufferToUsage` / `filterUsageForFormat` from open-sse/utils/usageTracking.js into a new `src/core/translator/response/usage.rs`. Track `total_content_length` / `accumulated_content` / `accumulated_thinking` on `ResponseTransformState`, and inject the usage block on the terminal frame in both `openai_to_claude_response` (message_delta) and `claude_to_openai_response` (final chunk) exactly as stream.js:356-366 does — estimate when the provider sent nothing, otherwise forward the merged value.

## P91-001 · P1 · 9router: open-sse/utils/streamHandler.js:155-163 + 202-210, open-sse/handlers/chatCore/streamingHandler.js:72; openproxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:3869-3872, 3936-3939, 4027-4030, 4075-4078

- **9router**: On stall, `pipeWithDisconnect` calls `streamController.handleError(new Error("stream stall timeout"))` then `abort()` (streamHandler.js:206-208). `createDisconnectAwareStream`'s catch treats AbortError / socket-close as a graceful `controller.close()` (streamHandler.js:140-163). 9Router's `writeStreamError` (open-sse/utils/error.js:46) is defined but never called anywhere in the codebase — no `data: {"error":...}` frame is ever injected into a live SSE stream.
- **OpenProxy**: Both stall branches and both upstream-error branches yield an error frame before returning: `yield Ok(Bytes::from(write_streaming_error("Upstream SSE stream stalled", "server_error")))` (chat.rs:3869-3872, 4027-4030) and `write_streaming_error("Upstream stream error", "server_error")` (chat.rs:3936-3939, 4075-4078). `write_streaming_error` (chat.rs:4921-4934) emits `data: {"error":{"message":...,"type":...,"code":null}}\n\n`.
- **Gap**: On a stall or mid-stream transport error OpenProxy injects a `data: {"error":...}` SSE frame into the live stream; 9Router just closes the stream.
- **Why it matters**: A `data:` frame with no `choices` array breaks strict OpenAI SSE consumers: the Vercel AI SDK, OpenAI's own Node SDK incremental parser, and several agent CLIs assume every `data:` payload is a `chat.completion.chunk` and index `choices[0]`, producing `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined` instead of a clean end-of-stream. 9Router clients are written against a hard close; OpenProxy's extra frame converts a recoverable truncation into a client-side crash.
- **Evidence**: 9router open-sse/utils/streamHandler.js:140-163, 202-210; open-sse/handlers/chatCore/streamingHandler.js:72; open-sse/utils/error.js:46 (defined, zero call sites) | openproxy src/server/api/chat.rs:3869-3872, 3936-3939, 4027-4030, 4075-4078, 4921-4934
- **Parity fix**: Delete the `write_streaming_error` yields from the four stall/error arms in chat.rs (3869-3872, 3936-3939, 4027-4030, 4075-4078) and just `return;` after recording usage / finishing the live request, matching `controller.close()`. Keep `write_streaming_error` only for pre-stream (non-SSE) error responses, which is where 9Router's `errorResponse` is used.

## P92-001 · P1 · 9router: src/app/api/v1/models/info/route.js:86-103 · openproxy: src/server/api/v1_models.rs:34, :642-654, :723

- **9router**: `GET /v1/models/info?id={alias}/{modelId}&kind={kind}` (routed via next.config.mjs:87-89 `/v1/:path*` → `/api/v1/:path*`). `GET` reads `searchParams.get("id")`, 400s with "Missing required query param: id (e.g. ?id=openai/dall-e-3)" when absent, and returns the info object BARE (`Response.json(info, …)`, line 103) — no envelope wrapper. For an embedding model, e.g. `?id=gemini/embedding-001`, the payload is `{id, name:"Embedding 001", kind:"embedding", owned_by:"gemini", endpoint:"/v1/embeddings", dimensions:768}`.
- **OpenProxy**: `GET /v1/models/info` (registered at v1_models.rs:34) reads `params.get("model")` only (v1_models.rs:648) and 400s with "model parameter required" when it is absent, then wraps the payload: `Json(json!({ "object": "model.info", "data": info }))` (v1_models.rs:723). A client following the 9router contract (`?id=gemini/embedding-001`) gets HTTP 400.
- **Gap**: The `/v1/models/info` request contract (query param `id` vs `model`) and response envelope (bare object vs `{object,data}`) both differ, so a client written against 9router gets a 400 and, if it worked around the param, an unreadable response shape.
- **Why it matters**: Embedding model discovery is a real client need: a RAG client enumerates embedding models and asks for each one's metadata to decide dimensions, endpoint and capabilities. Against 9router `?id=…` works; against OpenProxy it is a hard 400. Any tooling that switched to `?model=…` or that unwraps `.data` breaks if the target flips back, and the param name is also the only signal that tells a client which embedding model ids exist.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/app/api/v1/models/info/route.js:88-94 (`id` param), :103 (bare `Response.json(info)`), :5-14 (KIND_ENDPOINT incl. `embedding: "/v1/embeddings"`); next.config.mjs:87-89 · openproxy: src/server/api/v1_models.rs:34, :641-654, :723
- **Parity fix**: In `models_info`, accept `id` first and fall back to `model` (`params.get("id").or_else(|| params.get("model"))`) so both callers work, and return the info object bare rather than wrapped — or, if the `object`/`data` envelope must stay for schema stability, also accept clients that read the bare form by keeping the envelope but documenting `?id=` as the primary param. Preserve 9router's 400 message shape (`error.message` = "Missing required query param: id (e.g. ?id=openai/dall-e-3)").

## P93-001 · P1 · 9router: src/sse/handlers/embeddings.js:17-24, :137-150 · openproxy: src/server/api/media.rs:533-544 (early return) vs :610-673 (usage tracking)

- **9router**: On every successful embeddings response, `handleEmbeddings` runs `exactEmbeddingUsage(result.usage)` — a guard that accepts only a non-estimated, structurally consistent usage (`prompt_tokens > 0 && completion_tokens === 0 && total_tokens === prompt_tokens`) — and on a match calls `saveRequestUsage({provider, model, connectionId, apiKey, endpoint, tokens, status:"success"})` (embeddings.js:138-149). This runs for EVERY provider, including all the OpenAI-compatible adapters and Gemini, because the adapters' `normalize` returns the upstream `usage` object untouched.
- **OpenProxy**: The ported tracking block sits in `execute_media_provider` at media.rs:610-673 with the same exact-usage guard (`!estimated && prompt_tokens > 0 && completion_tokens == 0 && total_tokens == prompt_tokens`) and calls `state.usage_tracker().track_request(...)`. But it is positioned AFTER `if let Some(resp) = try_provider_adapter(...) { return resp; }` (media.rs:533-544), so it is unreachable for every provider that has an embedding adapter — i.e. all 14 real ones (`openai`, `openrouter`, `mistral`, `voyage-ai`, `fireworks`, `together`, `nebius`, `github`, `nvidia`, `jina-ai`, `vercel-ai-gateway`, `gemini`/`google_ai_studio`, `selfhosted-embedding`, and `openai-compatible-*`/`custom-embedding-*` nodes). `grep -n "track_request" src/server/api/` shows the only media-path call site is media.rs:663, inside that unreachable block.
- **Gap**: Exact embedding token usage is never recorded for any provider that has an embedding adapter, so embeddings are absent from OpenProxy's usage history, cost accounting and quota views.
- **Why it matters**: Users migrating a RAG workload see every embedding call succeed yet see zero tokens, zero requests and zero spend on the Usage page, while 9router itemises every batch. The code comment at media.rs:607-609 claims the 9router fix was ported; the port is dead. Anyone relying on OpenProxy's usage data to size or bill an embedding pipeline gets a silent hole, and per-connection request logs used for debugging embedding failures are empty too.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/sse/handlers/embeddings.js:17-24, :137-150 · openproxy: src/server/api/media.rs:533-544, :610-676 (only `track_request` call site, media.rs:663)
- **Parity fix**: Move the exact-usage tracking out of the generic forwarder and into the adapter result path — e.g. have `try_provider_adapter` / `media_result_to_response` receive the connection id + provider + model, or return the parsed `Value` alongside the `Response` so `execute_media_provider` can run the same `!estimated && prompt_tokens>0 && completion_tokens==0 && total_tokens==prompt_tokens` guard and call `state.usage_tracker().track_request(...)` for adapter responses as well.

## P94-001 · P1 · 9router: src/sse/handlers/embeddings.js:56 (settings.requireApiKey gate) vs OpenProxy: src/server/api/media.rs:464 (settings.require_login gate)

- **9router**: handleEmbeddings gates on `settings.requireApiKey` (embeddings.js:56) — a DISTINCT setting from requireLogin. 9router's settingsRepo defaults are two independent booleans: requireLogin: true (settingsRepo.js:26) and requireApiKey: true (settingsRepo.js:27). requireLogin is consumed only by dashboardGuard.js (dashboard page auth, :185/:240/:258) and the auth routes; requireApiKey is the flag that guards the /v1 LLM surface (also used at chat.js:71, imageGeneration.js:40, stt.js:32, tts.js:37, search.js:49, fetch.js:52, videoGeneration.js:47). The dashboard exposes them as two independent toggles (EndpointPageClient.js:29 vs :28, PATCH at :248).
- **OpenProxy**: generic_media_handler gates on `state.db.snapshot().settings.require_login` (media.rs:464) — the DASHBOARD-login flag, not an API-key flag. The Settings struct has only `require_login` (src/types/mod.rs:524) and no require_api_key field at all (grep for require_api_key / requireApiKey across src/ returns only unrelated require_api_key_with_reload helper references). UpdateSettingsRequest likewise exposes only `require_login` (mod.rs:2245, applied at :2450). The dashboard's Require-API-key toggle (web/src/components/EndpointPageClient.tsx:1297, PATCH body at :413) therefore writes a field the backend silently drops, and the flag it reads back at :365 (`data.requireApiKey`) is never served.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy guards /v1/embeddings with the dashboard-login setting and has no requireApiKey setting at all, so the API-key toggle in the dashboard is a silent no-op and the two auth concerns 9router keeps separate are collapsed into one.
- **Why it matters**: Two concrete consequences. (1) A user who disables dashboard login (require_login=false) to open the local dashboard also silently opens /v1/embeddings to the network — 9router would still require a key because requireApiKey stays true. On a tunnel/tailscale-reachable host that is an unauthenticated embedding endpoint. (2) A user who tries to re-enable API-key protection by flipping the dashboard's "Require API key" toggle sees it persist in the UI, but the PATCH is dropped server-side and the endpoint remains open — the control is a lie. Client behaviour for the default config (require_login=true) is the same, which is why this has gone unnoticed.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/sse/handlers/embeddings.js:56 (`if (settings.requireApiKey)`); src/lib/db/repos/settingsRepo.js:26-27 (requireLogin and requireApiKey are separate defaults); src/dashboardGuard.js:185/:240/:258 (requireLogin consumed only by dashboard auth); src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/endpoint/EndpointPageClient.js:28-29, :203-204, :248 (two independent toggles). OpenProxy: src/server/api/media.rs:464 (`if state.db.snapshot().settings.require_login`); src/types/mod.rs:524 (only require_login exists); src/server/api/mod.rs:2245, :2450 (PATCH accepts require_login only); web/src/components/EndpointPageClient.tsx:365, :413, :1297 (requireApiKey toggle — no backend field).
- **Parity fix**: Add `require_api_key: bool` (serde camelCase requireApiKey, default_true) to Settings alongside require_login (src/types/mod.rs:524), accept it in UpdateSettingsRequest and apply it in the PATCH handler (mod.rs:2245 / :2450), and emit it from the settings GET so EndpointPageClient.tsx:365 round-trips. Then change the embeddings gate at media.rs:464 to `if state.db.snapshot().settings.require_api_key` (returning 401 `Missing API key` / `Invalid API key` to match embeddings.js:57-65), and apply the same substitution to the sibling media handlers (audio_transcriptions, audio_speech, images_generations, search) so the whole /v1 media surface keys off requireApiKey like 9router does. Leave require_login governing only the dashboard guard.

## P95-001 · P1 · 9router: src/sse/handlers/embeddings.js:73-76 (body.input check) vs OpenProxy: src/server/api/media.rs:470-482 (no input check before routing) + src/core/media/embeddings/handler.rs:38-45 (falsy-check divergence)

- **9router**: handleEmbeddings checks `if (!body.input)` (embeddings.js:73) BEFORE getModelInfo and before credential selection, returning 400 `Missing required field: input` (embeddings.js:75). This is a JS falsy test, so input:"" , input:0, input:false, and input:null are all rejected. The core repeats the same check (embeddingsCore.js:23-26) and additionally rejects any non-string/non-array with 400 `input must be a string or array of strings`.
- **OpenProxy**: generic_media_handler validates only `model` (media.rs:475-482) and dispatches straight to execute_media_provider. The input check lives inside handle_embeddings (handler.rs:38-45), which only runs once an adapter has already been selected and a connection already resolved. There, `request.input()` is `body.get("input")` (base.rs:18-20), which returns Some for any present key — so input:"" is Some(String("")), passes the `!input.is_string() && !input.is_array()` guard, and is forwarded upstream. Crucially, on the generic forwarder path (no adapter) there is NO input validation at all: build_media_url + transform_media_request + POST happen without ever checking input exists.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy defers the required-field check until after model resolution and adapter selection, does not apply 9router's falsy semantics, and performs no input validation at all on the non-adapter path.
- **Why it matters**: Three divergences a client can observe. (1) Ordering: 9router returns `Missing required field: input` even when the provider has no credentials or the model is unknown; OpenProxy returns `No credentials for provider: X` or a routing error first, so the client's error message and remediation advice differ for the same malformed request. (2) Falsy semantics: input:"" is rejected with 400 by 9router but silently forwarded to the provider by OpenProxy, where it typically returns a provider-specific 400 (or worse, a 200 with a degenerate vector). (3) On the non-adapter path a body with no input at all is proxied upstream, leaking the request to a provider endpoint that never received input validation.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/sse/handlers/embeddings.js:73-76 (falsy `!body.input` → 400, before getModelInfo at :78), :78-82 (model check is AFTER input); open-sse/handlers/embeddingsCore.js:23-26 (repeat check + type guard). OpenProxy: src/server/api/media.rs:470-482 (only `model` validated), :518 (dispatch proceeds), :546-605 (generic path POSTs with no input validation); src/core/media/embeddings/handler.rs:38-45 (check runs post-adapter-selection); src/core/media/embeddings/base.rs:18-20 (`body.get("input")` → Some for any present key, so "" passes).
- **Parity fix**: In generic_media_handler, before the get_model_info call, add the input gate for the "embeddings" route kind: reject when body has no "input" key, or when the value is Null, or when it is a String that is empty, or when it is Bool(false) — reproducing JS falsiness — returning 400 with body `{"error":{"message":"Missing required field: input","type":"invalid_request_error","code":""}}` via json_error_response (media.rs:1114). Keep the type check (String or Array) in handle_embeddings (handler.rs:41) and move it ahead of adapter dispatch so the non-adapter path is covered too.

## P96-001 · P1 · Account pool exhausted — no-credentials error status and message

- **9router**: Inside handleSingleModelChat's selection loop: `if (excludeConnectionIds.size === 0) { log.warn("AUTH", \`No active credentials for provider: ${provider}\`); return errorResponse(HTTP_STATUS.NOT_FOUND, \`No active credentials for provider: ${provider}\`); }` — status 404, which buildErrorBody maps to { type: "invalid_request_error", code: "model_not_found" }. The all-accounts-cooling-down case is a separate branch that returns 503 via unavailableResponse with a Retry-After header and a message suffixed with the human retry hint.
- **OpenProxy**: When select_connection returns None and no last_error exists, the loop returns `ComboAttemptError { status: if retry_after.is_some() { 503 } else { 400 }, message: if retry_after.is_some() { format!("All accounts for {provider}/{model} are cooling down") } else { format!("No credentials for provider: {provider}") }, retry_after, … }`. The no-credentials case is 400 (buildErrorBody → code "bad_request") and the message drops the word "active" and the "for provider" phrasing. infer_status_from_message does not remap this text to 404.
- **Gap**: The 'no credentials configured for this provider' condition answers 400 with a differently-worded body instead of 9router's 404 'No active credentials for provider: X'.
- **Why it matters**: Status code is the contract clients branch on. 404/model_not_found tells a client (and 9router's own ERROR_TYPES table) 'this model or provider is not configured — do not retry blindly, add credentials'; 400/bad_request tells it 'your request is malformed'. Cline, OpenCode, Codex and OpenWebUI all treat 400 as a client-side error and surface it as a bad prompt, so a user with an unconfigured provider is told to fix their request instead of adding an account. The error-code string in the body changes with it, so any client matching on `code == "model_not_found"` stops matching.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/sse/handlers/chat.js:244-247 (404 + exact message), open-sse/utils/error.js:9-21 (buildErrorBody status→type/code), open-sse/utils/error.js:116-133 (the separate 503 unavailableResponse path). OpenProxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:1697-1705 (status 400 + 'No credentials for provider'), src/core/utils/error.rs:224-264 (infer_status_from_message has no mapping for this text), src/core/config/error_config.rs:52-56 (400 → code "bad_request").
- **Parity fix**: In the no-candidate arm of the loop at src/server/api/chat.rs:1697-1705, return `status: 404` and the message `format!("No active credentials for provider: {provider}")` when `retry_after.is_none()`, matching src/sse/handlers/chat.js:244-247 exactly. Keep the 503 cooling-down arm, but align its message with 9router's `[provider/model] <lastError> (<retryAfterHuman>)` shape so clients that surface the text see the same string.

## P97-001 · P1 · Account selection + health bookkeeping for all /v1/videos/* routes

- **9router**: Selection and health go through the shared account service. `getProviderCredentials` filters candidates with `if (isModelLockActive(c, model)) return false;` and, when every connection is locked, returns `{ allRateLimited: true, retryAfter, retryAfterHuman, lastError, lastErrorCode }`. On every video attempt the handler calls `markAccountUnavailable(connectionId, result.status, sanitizeSecrets(...), provider, model)` (which writes `modelLock_${model}`, `testStatus:"unavailable"`, `lastError`, `errorCode`, `backoffLevel` via `buildModelLockUpdate`) and on success calls `clearAccountError(connectionId, credentials, model)` (which clears the lock and resets `testStatus`/`lastError`/`backoffLevel`). `handleVideoGet` does the same.
- **OpenProxy**: `select_media_connections` filters on `connection.provider == provider && connection.is_active() && connection_has_credentials(connection)` and sorts by `priority`. `is_active()` is `self.is_active.unwrap_or(true)` and `connection_has_credentials` only checks that api_key or access_token is non-empty — neither `test_status`, `last_error`, `backoff_level` nor any `modelLock_*` is consulted. `media.rs` contains no call to any account-health mutator: `mark_connection_unavailable` is defined in `chat.rs:3336` and `web_fetch.rs:716` and is called from neither, and `apply_error_state` is referenced only from its own unit tests.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's video path neither reads nor writes account-fallback state, so video failures are invisible to account health and video selection ignores locks set by other subsystems.
- **Why it matters**: Two concrete consequences: (1) a xAI account that 429s on video keeps being selected for both video and chat because nothing writes the lock — the client is hammered on every request and the dashboard shows no `lastError`/`testStatus: unavailable`, so the user is never told to re-auth. (2) An account that chat already locked (backoff active) is still picked for video, so video requests bypass the cooldown the user configured. Successes also never clear a stale lock, so an account that recovered stays locked for chat.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/sse/services/auth.js:87 (`if (isModelLockActive(c, model)) return false;`), :112-125 (allRateLimited result), :239-296 (markAccountUnavailable -> buildModelLockUpdate/testStatus/lastError/errorCode/backoffLevel), :298-340 (clearAccountError); .tmp/9router/src/sse/handlers/videoGeneration.js:171 (clearAccountError on create success), :177-179 (markAccountUnavailable on create failure), :230 (clearAccountError on poll success), :234-236 (markAccountUnavailable on poll failure). OpenProxy: src/server/api/media.rs:726-742 (select_media_connections), :744-757 (connection_has_credentials), :1282-1297 + :1502-1521 (both call sites); no health mutation in :1177-1631 or :334-405; src/types/mod.rs:198-200 (is_active ignores test_status).
- **Parity fix**: Thread an `exclude: HashSet<String>` through video account selection (mirroring `chat.rs`'s `select_connection(..., &excluded, ...)`), filter candidates through the existing `AccountRegistry` `is_account_unavailable` / `is_model_lock_active` predicates, and on failure call the same `mark_connection_unavailable` used by `chat.rs:2881` with the video status/message, and on success the same clear path used at `chat.rs:2844`. Keep the exclude set so rotation cannot re-pick a just-failed account.

## P98-001 · P1 · Add API-key connection — duplicate name handling

- **9router**: createProviderConnection de-duplicates: for authType apikey with a name, it finds an existing connection with the same provider + authType + name (:166-167) and, if found, merges the patch into it via upsert instead of inserting (:171-177). The AddApiKeyModal bulk planner planBulkAdd is written specifically to avoid name collisions because the backend would upsert/overwrite instead of inserting.
- **OpenProxy**: create_provider_api validates that a name is present and then unconditionally pushes a new ProviderConnection into the snapshot, generating a fresh UUID every time. There is no lookup of an existing (provider, name) row.
- **Gap**: Adding a connection whose name already exists creates a second row instead of replacing the first.
- **Why it matters**: Re-entering the same key name (a common reconnect gesture) silently doubles the account instead of rotating its credentials. Both rows are active and enter the same fallback pool, so half the requests keep hitting the stale key, and the connection list shows two identical rows with the same name and colliding priorities.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/lib/db/repos/connectionsRepo.js:166-177 (apikey dedup by name plus merge/upsert) and :201-208 (insert and reorderInTx only when no match). OpenProxy: src/server/api/mod.rs:1156-1181 (fresh Uuid::new_v4 id, priority default 1) and :1215-1220 (unconditional db.provider_connections.push).
- **Parity fix**: Inside the db.update closure in create_provider_api, first look for an existing connection with provider == provider and auth_type == apikey and name == name. If found, merge the new fields into that row (preserving its id and createdAt, resetting testStatus to the incoming value, and running the resetHealthStateOnActivation normalization) and return it; otherwise insert as today. The bulk-add path shares this endpoint and inherits the fix.

## P99-001 · P1 · Add Connection / Edit Connection modals — Region field

- **9router**: AddApiKeyModal reads region data from the registry: `providerRegions = AI_PROVIDERS?.[provider]?.regions` and `defaultRegion = …defaultRegion || providerRegions?.[0]?.id || ""` (AddApiKeyModal.js:23-24, 41); it renders a `<Select label="Region">` from `providerRegions.map(r => ({value: r.id, label: r.label}))` (AddApiKeyModal.js:287-294) and `buildProviderSpecificData()` returns `{ region }` whenever `providerRegions && region` (AddApiKeyModal.js:70-72). EditConnectionModal mirrors it: on load it derives `savedRegion = connection.providerSpecificData?.region || providerCfg.defaultRegion || providerCfg.regions[0]?.id || ""` (EditConnectionModal.js:53-58), renders the same Select (EditConnectionModal.js:257-264), and persists `updates.providerSpecificData = buildRegionSpecificData()` when `providerRegions && region` (EditConnectionModal.js:171-174). This is registry-driven, so it covers any provider declaring `regions` — today xiaomi-tokenplan (`regions: [sgp, cn, ams]`, `defaultRegion: "sgp"`, open-sse/providers/registry/xiaomi-tokenplan.js:24-33) and trae (open-sse/providers/registry/trae.js:41-47).
- **OpenProxy**: AddApiKeyModal.tsx hardcodes a Xiaomi-only branch: `isXiaomiTokenplan = provider === "xiaomi-tokenplan"` with a fixed `useState("sgp")` and a three-entry inline Select (sgp/cn/ams) (AddApiKeyModal.tsx:38, 60, 145-147, 376-393). It never reads `AI_PROVIDERS[provider].regions`, so any other registry-declared region provider (trae) gets no Region control. EditConnectionModal.tsx has **no** region state, no `providerRegions` derivation, no Region Select, and handleSubmit only builds `providerSpecificData` for azure (…:188-195) and cloudflare-ai (…:196-198) — so a saved xiaomi-tokenplan connection can be renamed/re-keyed but its cluster can never be changed after creation.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy replaced 9router's registry-driven Region select with a Xiaomi-only hardcoded one, and dropped the field entirely from Edit Connection.
- **Why it matters**: Token-plan keys are cluster-specific: an sg p key sent to the cn cluster 401s on every request, and 9router's form is the only way to correct it after the key is saved. Under OpenProxy the user must delete the connection and re-add it. Trae (cn/sg/us endpoints) loses region selection outright, so a user outside China cannot pick the right endpoint at all.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/providers/[id]/AddApiKeyModal.js:23-24, 41, 70-72, 287-294; .tmp/9router/src/shared/components/EditConnectionModal.js:53-58, 257-264, 171-174; .tmp/9router/open-sse/providers/registry/xiaomi-tokenplan.js:24-33; .tmp/9router/open-sse/providers/registry/trae.js:41-47. OpenProxy: web/src/components/providers/AddApiKeyModal.tsx:38, 60, 145-147, 376-393; web/src/shared/components/EditConnectionModal.tsx:44-66 (state, no region), 180-198 (submit, no region branch)
- **Parity fix**: Make both modals registry-driven like 9router. In AddApiKeyModal.tsx replace the `isXiaomiTokenplan` branch with `const providerRegions = AI_PROVIDERS?.[provider]?.regions || null; const defaultRegion = AI_PROVIDERS?.[provider]?.defaultRegion || providerRegions?.[0]?.id || "";` plus `useState(defaultRegion)`, render `<Select label="Region" value={region} onChange={…} options={providerRegions.map(r => ({ value: r.id, label: r.label }))} />`, and extend `buildProviderSpecificData()` with `if (providerRegions && region) return { region }` ahead of the undefined fallthrough. Add the same Region state + Select + `updates.providerSpecificData = { region }` branch to EditConnectionModal.tsx, seeding from `connection.providerSpecificData?.region || defaultRegion` on load.

## P100-001 · P1 · Add connection — API key validation gate

- **9router**: POST /api/providers/validate resolves the provider in the registry and, for anything the config-driven probe path does not cover, the default arm returns HTTP 400 {error:"Provider validation not supported"} rather than a false positive. Web-only and media-only providers are covered by probeWebProvider and probeMediaProvider before the switch. Because the modal treats valid as advisory, a 400 makes it show failed while still persisting with testStatus unknown.
- **OpenProxy**: The final match arm is `_ => (true, None)`, so any provider not explicitly listed, including ones the router cannot reach, is reported as {valid:true, error:null}. There are no web-search/web-fetch or media-kind probe arms.
- **Gap**: Validating an unsupported or mis-typed provider reports success instead of Provider validation not supported.
- **Why it matters**: The Add-Connection modal uses valid to decide testStatus. With the default arm returning true, a provider OpenProxy has no probe for is saved as testStatus active with a green badge and no error, even though nothing was ever checked. The failure only surfaces later as a routing error, and the user gets no signal at connect time that validation was skipped.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/providers/validate/route.js:599-604 (default arm returns 400), :10-82 (probeWebProvider and probeMediaProvider), :91-94 (registry-driven noAuth). OpenProxy: src/server/api/provider_validate.rs:421 (`_ => (true, None)`) with the envelope at :424-427; consumer web/src/components/providers/AddApiKeyModal.tsx:189-203 (isValid drives testStatus).
- **Parity fix**: Change the catch-all arm to return HTTP 400 {error:"Provider validation not supported"} and add the config-driven webSearch/webFetch and media-kind probe arms so the providers 9router validates are still validated. Keep the explicit provider arms unchanged.

## P101-001 · P1 · Add connection — Ollama Local (no API key)

- **9router**: POST /api/providers exempts Ollama Local from the key requirement: if (!apiKey and provider !== ollama-local) then 400. The Add-Connection modal mirrors it, skipping the key check for isOllamaLocal, substituting the name Ollama Local, and posting apiKey: "". Ollama Local is a normal category apikey registry provider.
- **OpenProxy**: create_provider_api requires a non-empty api_key for every provider with no exception: req.api_key is trimmed and filtered for emptiness, else 400 API key is required. The OpenProxy Add-Connection modal deliberately omits the key for Ollama Local and posts an empty string.
- **Gap**: The Ollama Local Add-Connection flow always fails with HTTP 400 because OpenProxy has no ollama-local key exemption.
- **Why it matters**: The dashboard offers an Ollama Local host-URL field and lets the user submit with no key; the POST is rejected, so local-Ollama models can never be connected through the provider page, and the user is shown API key is required for a provider that by definition has no API key.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/providers/route.js:119-121 (the ollama-local exemption) and .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/providers/[id]/AddApiKeyModal.js:99-103 plus :122-124 (modal skips the key check and defaults the name). OpenProxy: src/server/api/mod.rs:1129-1141 (unconditional api_key requirement) and web/src/components/providers/AddApiKeyModal.tsx:169-170 plus :197-199 (modal posts an empty key for ollama-local).
- **Parity fix**: Mirror the exemption in create_provider_api: allow a missing key when provider is ollama-local and store the empty string, otherwise return 400 API key is required. Keep authType as apikey so list filters and executor dispatch still match.

## P102-001 · P1 · Background OAuth token refresh scheduler (inter-account pacing)

- **9router**: `runBackgroundTokenRefreshTick` (`src/sse/services/backgroundTokenRefresh.js:90-137`) refreshes due connections SEQUENTIALLY and sleeps between every account: `baseSensitiveDelay = BG_REFRESH_GOOGLE_DELAY_MS || 12_000` for `antigravity`/`gemini-cli`, `baseNormalDelay = BG_REFRESH_DELAY_MS || 1_500` otherwise, plus jitter (`Math.random()*4000` sensitive, flat `200` normal). This pacing exists specifically to stop a burst of refreshes from tripping Google Cloud's abuse limits.
- **OpenProxy**: `run_tick` (`src/oauth/background_refresh.rs:82-116`) loops `for conn in &due` and awaits `dispatch_oauth_refresh` back-to-back with no sleep and no jitter between connections. The only two `sleep` calls in the file are the 10 s initial delay and the 5-minute inter-tick interval (`:164`, `:167`).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's background refresh loop issues every due account's refresh back-to-back with no inter-request delay or jitter, where 9router paces them 1.5 s / 12 s apart specifically to avoid tripping provider abuse limits.
- **Why it matters**: A user with many Google-family OAuth accounts (the common multi-account setup for Antigravity/Gemini CLI) hits Google with N refreshes in the same few hundred milliseconds. Google's OAuth endpoint rate-limits by client id, so the burst produces `invalid_grant` / 429 on a fraction of the refreshes. Because the tick is fail-open, the failures are logged and the accounts are left with stale tokens; the next tick retries the same burst. The user sees intermittent "signed out" behaviour on exactly the multi-account configuration the dashboard encourages, and the failure is invisible in the UI. The same burst pattern applies to xAI/grok-cli, whose rotating refresh tokens mean a rejected refresh can also invalidate the next one.
- **Evidence**: 9router: `.tmp/9router/src/sse/services/backgroundTokenRefresh.js:102-105` (delay constants), `:126-135` (sequential sleep + jitter, `SENSITIVE_PROVIDERS` at :12). OpenProxy: `src/oauth/background_refresh.rs:96-115` (bare `for conn in &due` loop, no sleep), `:162-169` (the only sleeps are tick-level).
- **Parity fix**: Add the pacing to `run_tick` in `src/oauth/background_refresh.rs`. Track the index; after each `persist_refresh`, if it is not the last due connection, `tokio::time::sleep` for `12_000 ms + rand(0..4000)` when `conn.provider` is `antigravity` or `gemini-cli`, else `1_500 ms + 200 ms`. Read the two delays from `BG_REFRESH_GOOGLE_DELAY_MS` / `BG_REFRESH_DELAY_MS` env vars with those defaults so operators retain the same escape hatch, and add the `DISABLE_BACKGROUND_TOKEN_REFRESH` truthy-env kill switch that 9router honours at `backgroundTokenRefresh.js:150`.

## P103-001 · P1 · Claude-family identity headers sent to api.anthropic.com

- **9router**: The `claude` provider ships a complete, STATIC Claude-CLI fingerprint in its registry `transport.headers` (open-sse/providers/registry/claude.js:24-40): `Anthropic-Version: 2023-06-01`, an 11-flag `Anthropic-Beta` list, `Anthropic-Dangerous-Direct-Browser-Access: true`, `User-Agent: claude-cli/2.1.258 (external, sdk-cli)`, `X-App: cli`, and the full `X-Stainless-*` set (helper-method, retry-count, runtime-version, package-version, runtime, lang, arch, os, timeout). `default.js:163-167` then OVERWRITES only `Anthropic-Beta` with `selectAnthropicBeta(model)`. There is no such module in 9Router (verified: no `claudeHeaderCache` / `*HeaderCache*` file exists).
- **OpenProxy**: `PROVIDER_CONFIGS["claude"]` is `ProviderConfig::anthropic("https://api.anthropic.com/v1/messages")` (src/core/executor/default.rs:50-52), whose `default_headers` are only `anthropic-version` and `anthropic-beta: "claude-code-20250219,interleaved-thinking-2025-05-14"` (src/core/executor/provider.rs:180-190). `User-Agent`, `X-App`, `X-Stainless-*` and `Anthropic-Dangerous-Direct-Browser-Access` are NOT in the config. They are supplied instead by a `claude_header_cache` overlay (src/core/executor/default.rs:1875-1891) that only fires for `claude`/`anthropic` AFTER a prior request from a real Claude Code client has populated the singleton cache (`cache_claude_headers`, src/server/api/chat.rs:352), and only for keys not already present. The overlay itself is a port of a 9Router module that does not exist.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's claude provider has no static Claude-CLI fingerprint; those identity headers appear only after a Claude Code client request has warmed a global cache — a mechanism 9Router does not have at all.
- **Why it matters**: On a cold start, or for the FIRST request from a non-Claude-Code client (Cline, OpenCode, a plain SDK, the dashboard playground), OpenProxy sends `User-Agent: <reqwest default>`, no `X-App`, and no `X-Stainless-*` to api.anthropic.com. Anthropic's abuse heuristics and several third-party Anthropic-compatible gateways gate on this fingerprint; a request without it can be 403'd or rate-limited where 9Router is not. The behaviour is also non-deterministic — the same provider sends different headers before and after any Claude Code request, and the cache is a process-global singleton shared across all users/accounts.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/providers/registry/claude.js:24-40 (static 11-header Claude-CLI fingerprint incl. User-Agent claude-cli/2.1.258, X-App: cli, all X-Stainless-*), open-sse/providers/shared.js:34-52 (CLAUDE_CLI_SPOOF_HEADERS), open-sse/executors/default.js:163-167 (Anthropic-Beta overwrite). openproxy: src/core/executor/default.rs:50-52 (bare anthropic config), src/core/executor/provider.rs:180-190 (default_headers = version + beta only), src/core/executor/default.rs:1875-1891 (conditional cache overlay), src/core/api/chat.rs:352 → src/core/utils/claude_header_cache.rs:48-64 (cache only populated by real Claude Code traffic).
- **Parity fix**: Give `ProviderExecutorConfig::anthropic` (or a dedicated `claude()` constructor) the full static fingerprint that 9Router's registry declares: `anthropic-version: 2023-06-01`, the 11-flag `Anthropic-Beta`, `anthropic-dangerous-direct-browser-access: true`, `user-agent: claude-cli/2.1.258 (external, sdk-cli)`, `x-app: cli`, and `x-stainless-{helper-method,retry-count,runtime-version,package-version,runtime,lang,arch,os,timeout}` (arch/os host-mapped). Remove the `claude_header_cache` overlay entirely so the header set is deterministic on every request.

## P104-001 · P1 · Client disconnect / abort propagation

- **9router**: The client-abort signal is created up front and threaded end-to-end: `createStreamController` returns `signal: abortController.signal`; `handleDisconnect` schedules `abortController.abort()` after a 500 ms grace window so cleanup can run; and that signal is passed into `executor.execute({..., signal: streamController.signal, ...})`, where `base.js:132` merges it with the connect timer: `const mergedSignal = signal ? AbortSignal.any([signal, connectCtrl.signal]) : connectCtrl.signal`. A client hangup therefore aborts the in-flight upstream fetch, and the `AbortError` is caught at chatCore.js:385-398 and mapped to `createErrorResult(499, "Request aborted")`.
- **OpenProxy**: There is no abort signal in the executor contract. `ExecutionRequest` (src/core/executor/default.rs:620-637) has no `signal`/cancellation field; `send_one` (src/core/executor/default.rs:2278-2310) awaits the reqwest/hyper call with no cancellation. The streaming body is an `async_stream::stream!` fed to `Body::from_stream`, so when the client disconnects the stream generator is simply dropped mid-`yield` — the in-flight `upstream.try_next()` future is not explicitly cancelled, and `record_streaming_usage` / `usage_live.finish_request` (the trailing lines of the generator) never run, so the pending counter is only corrected by the 60 s `PENDING_TIMEOUT_MS` force-zero timer.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy has no client-abort signal plumbed into the upstream fetch, and a mid-stream client disconnect leaves the usage-pending counter armed until the 60 s timeout.
- **Why it matters**: When a user hits Esc in Claude Code / interrupts Codex, 9Router cancels the upstream HTTP request immediately (freeing the account's in-flight slot and provider quota); OpenProxy leaves the upstream connection open and streaming until the provider finishes or the socket is reaped, so the account shows as Busy (round-robin capacity) and, for providers that bill streamed-but-undelivered tokens, still charges. The usage dashboard's "in-flight" chip also stays lit for up to 60 s per aborted turn, which misleads the capacity-adapter availability signal.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/utils/streamHandler.js:34-40 (signal), :47-55 (handleDisconnect + 500ms delayed abort), open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:363 (`signal: streamController.signal`), open-sse/executors/base.js:132 (`AbortSignal.any([signal, connectCtrl.signal])`), open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:394-397 (499 mapping). openproxy: src/core/executor/default.rs:620-637 (ExecutionRequest, no signal), src/core/executor/default.rs:2278-2310 (send_one, no cancellation), src/server/api/chat.rs:4108-4112 (Body::from_stream with no drop guard), src/server/usage_live.rs:11 (PENDING_TIMEOUT_MS=60_000).
- **Parity fix**: Add a `CancellationToken` to `AppState`-scoped per-request state and a `signal` field on `ExecutionRequest`; pass it to `send_one` as `.timeout_at`/`tokio::select!` against the token so a client disconnect cancels the upstream request. Wrap the streaming generator so its `Drop` impl fires `usage_live.finish_request(...)` and `record_streaming_usage(...)`, and map the cancellation to a 499 result like chatCore.js:394-397.

## P105-001 · P1 · Client request headers reaching provider executors

- **9router**: chatCore stamps the full client header map onto the credentials object: `if (credentials) credentials.rawHeaders = clientRawRequest?.headers || {};`. Every executor then reads it: cursor.js:305 (`credentials?.rawHeaders?.["user-agent"]` → `forceAgentMode` when UA is claude-cli/claude-code/Claude Code), opencode.js:98 (forwards `user-agent`, `x-opencode-client`, `x-opencode-session`, `x-opencode-request`, `x-opencode-project` verbatim), opencode-go.js:122 (`nativeSession(sourceCredentials.rawHeaders)`), codex.js:130, antigravity.js:161/191, grok-cli.js:143, and the kiro translators (openai-to-kiro.js:357, claude-to-kiro.js:261).
- **OpenProxy**: Client headers reach the KIRO TRANSLATION path only: chat.rs:1261-1275 injects `rawHeaders` into a synthetic `creds` JSON that is passed solely to `translate_request_with_strip`. They never reach any executor. Concretely: (a) chat.rs:2128 and chat.rs:2159 pass `raw_headers: std::collections::BTreeMap::new()` — an ALWAYS-EMPTY map — to `OpenCodeExecutor` and `OpenCodeGoExecutor`, so `opencode.rs:629-708` and `opencode_go.rs` always take the generated-default branch. (b) `cursor.rs:2246-2257` reads `request.credentials.provider_specific_data.get("rawHeaders")` — a PERSISTED DB field that is never written anywhere (grep for `"rawHeaders"` finds only the translator-creds insert at chat.rs:1272), so `force_agent_mode` is always `false`.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy drops the client header map at the executor boundary: `raw_headers` is hardcoded empty for opencode/opencode-go, and cursor reads a `provider_specific_data.rawHeaders` key that is never populated.
- **Why it matters**: 1) Cursor: Claude Code routed through a cursor connection never gets `forceAgentMode`, so the request body is generated as a non-agentic chat instead of Cursor Agent mode — different tool-loop shape, different model behaviour. 2) OpenCode: every request regenerates `x-opencode-session` / `x-opencode-request` instead of forwarding the client's, breaking conversation continuity and the `x-opencode-client`/`x-opencode-project` passthrough that the upstream gate uses to authorize real OpenCode clients — the client can be rejected by the gate. 3) Antigravity/Grok-cli/Codex session ids fall back to per-connection derivation, degrading upstream prompt caching.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:155 (`credentials.rawHeaders = clientRawRequest?.headers || {}`), open-sse/executors/cursor.js:305, open-sse/executors/opencode.js:98, open-sse/executors/opencode-go.js:122, open-sse/executors/codex.js:130, open-sse/executors/antigravity.js:161, open-sse/executors/grok-cli.js:143, open-sse/translator/request/openai-to-kiro.js:357. openproxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:1272 (only translator-creds write), src/server/api/chat.rs:2128 and :2159 (`BTreeMap::new()`), src/core/executor/cursor.rs:2249 (`provider_specific_data.get("rawHeaders")` — never written), src/core/executor/opencode.rs:629 (consumes raw_headers).
- **Parity fix**: Thread the already-computed `headers_map` (chat.rs:339-347) into the dispatch: add a `raw_headers: BTreeMap<String, String>` field to `ExecutionRequest` and the per-provider `*ExecutionRequest` structs, populate it at the chat.rs:1814-2615 dispatch ladder from `headers_map`, and pass it to `OpenCodeExecutor`/`OpenCodeGoExecutor` instead of `BTreeMap::new()`. For cursor, populate `force_agent_mode` from the live `headers_map.get("user-agent")` rather than the never-written `provider_specific_data["rawHeaders"]` (equivalently, write that key per request). Add the same wiring for antigravity/codex/grok-cli session resolution.

## P106-001 · P1 · Client-facing error body — upstream message rewriting

- **9router**: error bodies are built verbatim: `buildErrorBody(statusCode, message)` returns `{ error: { message: message || DEFAULT_ERROR_MESSAGES[statusCode] || "An error occurred", type, code } }` with no rewriting, and createErrorResult passes the already-formatted `formatProviderError(...)` string (`[<code>]: <message>`) straight through. A 429 whose body says 'Rate limit reached for gpt-4 in organization org-abc on tokens per min' reaches the client with that text intact.
- **OpenProxy**: Every attempt error is piped through friendly_error_message before the body is built: `let friendly = friendly_error_message(status.as_u16(), &error.message); let body = build_error_body(status.as_u16(), Some(&friendly));`. friendly_error_message replaces the message with canned prose whenever the lowercased text contains a known phrase — 'rate limit'/'too many requests' → 'Rate limit exceeded. Wait a moment and try again, or use another account.'; 'quota exceeded'/'insufficient_quota' → 'You exceeded your current quota or balance on this provider…'; 'not supported' → 'This model is not supported by the provider.'; 'invalid api key' → 'Invalid API key or credentials…'. It also normalizes the status via infer_status_from_message before all of this.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy substitutes canned English for the upstream provider's actual error text, where 9router returns the provider's message unchanged.
- **Why it matters**: The detail a user needs to act is exactly what gets deleted: the organization id, the model that tripped the limit, the provider's reset hint, the parameter name it rejected. 'Rate limit exceeded. Wait a moment and try again, or use another account.' gives an operator no way to tell which account is throttled or why, and the same text is returned for every provider, so it cannot be used to triage. It also breaks clients that pattern-match the upstream error string (e.g. Codex/agent CLIs detecting a specific quota code) and it defeats per-provider error dashboards built on request logs.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/utils/error.js:9-21 (buildErrorBody verbatim), open-sse/utils/error.js:98-104 (createErrorResult), open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:465-475 (formatProviderError result passed straight into createErrorResult), open-sse/utils/error.js:139-147 (formatProviderError). OpenProxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:4856-4858 (friendly rewrite), src/server/api/chat.rs:4853-4854 (infer_status_from_message), src/core/utils/error.rs:65-200 (the phrase→prose mapping), src/core/utils/error.rs:224-264 (status re-inference).
- **Parity fix**: Keep the HTML-stripping, whitespace-collapse, prefix-stripping and length-clamp parts of friendly_error_message (those are pure hygiene and the file documents them as such), but drop the phrase→prose `mapped` branch so the cleaned-but-unrewritten text is passed to build_error_body — matching open-sse/utils/error.js:9-21. Also stop routing the status through infer_status_from_message in attempt_error_response, since 9router uses the provider's real status code verbatim.

## P107-001 · P1 · Combo member reordering — /dashboard/combos page and the shared combo edit modal

- **9router**: combos page: DndContext with KeyboardSensor + sortableKeyboardCoordinates (src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/combos/page.js:4-5, :664) wraps the model list; each ModelItem renders a real `<button {...attributes} {...listeners} title="Drag to reorder">` drag handle (combos/page.js:584-596) that dnd-kit makes keyboard-operable (Space/Enter picks up, Arrow Up/Down moves, Space/Enter drops, Esc cancels), PLUS explicit `Move up` (:623-630) and `Move down` (:631-638) buttons. The shared edit modal has the same two buttons: onMoveUp/onMoveDown at src/shared/components/ComboFormModal.js:36-39 and :40-43.
- **OpenProxy**: No @dnd-kit anywhere in web/src (grep for DndContext/SortableContext/useSortable/dnd-kit returns nothing). The shared ComboFormModal reorders via native HTML5 drag only: `draggable={!editing}` on the row (web/src/shared/components/ComboFormModal.tsx:100) with a non-focusable `<span className="material-symbols-outlined">drag_indicator</span>` handle (:132-137) — no tabIndex, no role, no key handler, and no up/down buttons (the row only has Test :196, Mute :215, Remove :237). CombosPageClient has no inline editor at all — combo models render as read-only `<code>` chips (:410-441), editable only through the modal.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy offers no keyboard-operable way to reorder combo members: the 9router drag handle is a `<button>` wired to dnd-kit's KeyboardSensor and both 9router surfaces also expose explicit Move up / Move down buttons, while OpenProxy's only affordance is a mouse-only HTML5 `draggable` span.
- **Why it matters**: Model order in a combo IS the fallback priority (AGENTS.md: 'create combos -> select models'; combo.models is walked in order by the dispatcher). A keyboard-only or motor-impaired operator cannot change that order at all in OpenProxy, so they cannot make the preferred model the first fallback. In 9router the whole reordering is reachable with Tab + Space + Arrow keys.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/combos/page.js:4,5,584,589,627,635,664 + .tmp/9router/src/shared/components/ComboFormModal.js:36,40 || openproxy: web/src/shared/components/ComboFormModal.tsx:100,132,136,196,215,237 + web/src/components/CombosPageClient.tsx:410
- **Parity fix**: Re-introduce @dnd-kit in the combo surfaces: wrap the ComboFormModal model list in DndContext + SortableContext with `useSensor(KeyboardSensor, { coordinateGetter: sortableKeyboardCoordinates })` and render the drag indicator as `<button {...attributes} {...listeners} type="button">` instead of the `<span>`. Independently (and sufficient for parity with 9router's shared ComboFormModal, which never had dnd-kit), add the two `<button onClick={onMoveUp/onMoveDown} disabled={isFirst/isLast} title="Move up"/"Move down">` controls to the ModelItem row, and surface an inline sortable model list on CombosPageClient to match combos/page.js:785-808.

## P108-001 · P1 · Connection priority — add / reorder / delete

- **9router**: reorderInTx(db, providerId) selects every connection for the provider, sorts by (priority asc, updatedAt desc), and rewrites priority = i+1 for all of them. It runs on create (:207), on every update that carries a priority field (:225), and on delete (:238), so priorities are always a dense 1..N set. Create also defaults a missing priority to max(existing priority)+1 (:183-186).
- **OpenProxy**: No re-sequencing exists anywhere in the crate. update_provider writes the raw submitted priority, delete_provider just retain()s the row, and create_provider_api defaults to priority.unwrap_or(1). The UI swap handler (identical on both sides) PUTs 0-based array indices, and runtime credential selection sorts by priority ascending.
- **Gap**: Priorities in OpenProxy keep gaps, 0-based values and duplicates instead of being renumbered 1..N, and a new connection defaults to priority 1 rather than max+1.
- **Why it matters**: Delete the highest-priority connection and add a new one: 9router appends it at the end (N+1, tried last); OpenProxy gives it priority 1, colliding with the surviving first account. Because forward_with_provider_fallback sorts candidates by priority ascending, a stable sort then falls back to DB insertion order, so which account is tried first after a rate limit differs from 9router. The #N label rendered next to each connection also drifts out of sync with the real order after any swap or delete.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/lib/db/repos/connectionsRepo.js:112-122 (reorderInTx) called at :207 (create), :225 (update), :238 (delete); priority default at :183-186; runtime ordering at .tmp/9router/src/sse/services/auth.js:156,176. OpenProxy: src/server/api/admin_items.rs:262-264 (raw priority write), :379-408 (delete, no reorder), src/server/api/mod.rs:1170 (req.priority.unwrap_or(1)), src/server/api/chat.rs:3097 (candidates.sort_by_key priority).
- **Parity fix**: Add a reorder_provider_connections(conns, provider) helper in the db layer that sorts by (priority asc, updatedAt desc) and renumbers to 1..N, then call it inside the same db.update closure after create, after any update where priority was supplied, and after delete. Change the create default to max(existing priority for that provider) + 1 instead of 1.

## P109-001 · P1 · Connection selection failure — no usable credentials for a provider

- **9router**: `handleSingleModelChat` (`src/sse/handlers/chat.js:236-250`) distinguishes two exhaustion modes. When `excludeConnectionIds.size === 0` — nothing was tried yet, the provider simply has no usable credential — it returns **404** with `No active credentials for provider: ${provider}`. Only when accounts WERE tried and all fell back does it return 503 (`unavailableResponse` with `retryAfter`/`retryAfterHuman`, or `lastStatus || 503`).
- **OpenProxy**: `forward_with_provider_fallback` (`src/server/api/chat.rs:1699-1706`) returns `status: if retry_after.is_some() { 503 } else { 400 }` with the message `No credentials for provider: {provider}`. The 404-vs-503 distinction is collapsed, and the code is 400 rather than 404.
- **Gap**: When a provider has no usable credentials at all, OpenProxy answers 400 `No credentials for provider: X` where 9router answers 404 `No active credentials for provider: X`.
- **Why it matters**: OpenAI-compatible clients branch on 404 to distinguish "you asked for a model that does not exist" from "the proxy cannot serve this right now", and Claude Code's error handling keys off 404 to offer a model-refresh prompt. A 400 is a client-input error: the client will not offer to refresh the model list, will not re-read `/v1/models`, and will surface the message to the user as a bad request they should fix — when the actual cause is a server-side credential problem the user fixes on the Providers page. The message text also differs (`No credentials` vs `No active credentials`), so log/alert matching on the string breaks.
- **Evidence**: 9router: `.tmp/9router/src/sse/handlers/chat.js:237-243` (404 branch, `excludeConnectionIds.size === 0`), `:244-249` (503 branch). OpenProxy: `src/server/api/chat.rs:1699-1706` (`status: if retry_after.is_some() { 503 } else { 400 }`, message `No credentials for provider: {provider}`).
- **Parity fix**: In `forward_with_provider_fallback` at `src/server/api/chat.rs:1699-1706`, branch on whether anything was attempted rather than on `retry_after`: when `excluded.is_empty() && last_error.is_none()` return `status: 404` with message `No active credentials for provider: {provider}`; keep `503` for the exhausted-after-try case and attach `retry_after`/`retry_after_human` on the 503 the way `unavailableResponse` does upstream.

## P110-001 · P1 · Custom models — POST /api/models/custom (caps round-trip)

- **9router**: POST accepts a `caps` object, whitelists it against `CAPACITY_META`, drops non-boolean values, and stores the clean result (omitted entirely when empty) — `.tmp/9router/src/app/api/models/custom/route.js:9-16 (sanitizeCaps), :35-42 (POST)`. The response is `{success:true, added: <the stored model object>}` (`:42`). The stored caps are read back in two places: the custom-model merge in `GET /api/models` (`.tmp/9router/src/app/api/models/route.js:60` spreads `...(m.caps || {})` over the derived caps) and the add modal's per-capability toggles (`.tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/providers/[id]/AddCustomModelModal.js:12, 53, 96-104`).
- **OpenProxy**: `CreateCustomModelRequest` has only `provider_alias`, `id`, `type`, `name` — no `caps` field at all (`src/server/api/models_custom.rs:30-38`). The response is `{success:true, added: <bool>}` where the bool is "was it newly inserted" (`:105`, `:127`) — a boolean, not the stored model. `CustomModel` is constructed with `extra: Default::default()` (`:110-116`), so there is nowhere to put caps. The add modal correspondingly has no capability toggles: `web/src/components/providers/AddCustomModelModal.tsx:11` (`onSave: (modelId: string) => Promise<void>`, no caps) and `web/src/components/providers/ProviderDetailPageClient.tsx:2039-2048` posts `{providerAlias, id, type}` only.
- **Gap**: Operator-set per-model capabilities cannot be stored or returned: the request field, the persisted data, and the response value all differ from 9router.
- **Why it matters**: A user who adds a custom model with an unusual id (a fine-tuned name, a self-hosted alias) has no way to declare it vision-capable or reasoning-capable. The OpenProxy substring heuristic at `src/server/api/models_alias.rs:96-131` then guesses wrong, so the model renders with no CapacityBadges and the combo page's capability-based reordering (`reorderByCapabilities`) cannot route a vision request to it. The `added` field also changed type from object to boolean, so any caller reading `data.added.name` gets `undefined`.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/models/custom/route.js:9-16, :35-42 (caps sanitise + `added` = model object); .tmp/9router/src/app/api/models/route.js:60 (caps override on read-back); .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/providers/[id]/AddCustomModelModal.js:53, 96-104. OpenProxy: src/server/api/models_custom.rs:30-38 (no caps field), :105 (`"added": !exists_before` boolean), :110-116 (`extra: Default::default()`); web/src/components/providers/AddCustomModelModal.tsx:11; web/src/components/providers/ProviderDetailPageClient.tsx:2043-2044.
- **Parity fix**: Add `caps: Option<std::collections::BTreeMap<String, bool>>` to `CreateCustomModelRequest` and to `CustomModel` (or stash it in `extra["caps"]`), whitelisting the keys against the same set as 9router's `CAPACITY_META` and rejecting non-bool values — mirroring `sanitizeCaps` at `.tmp/9router/src/app/api/models/custom/route.js:9-16`. Return the stored `CustomModel` as `added` (not a bool) at `src/server/api/models_custom.rs:127`, and make `list_models` at `src/server/api/models_alias.rs:65` spread the stored caps over the derived ones for custom rows. Restore the capability toggles in `web/src/components/providers/AddCustomModelModal.tsx` and thread `caps` through `ProviderDetailPageClient.tsx:2043`.

## P111-001 · P1 · Default landing page: `/`

- **9router**: `src/app/page.js:3-4` calls `redirect('/dashboard')` (a real 307). The browser therefore always ends up on the canonical `/dashboard` URL. Sidebar `src/shared/components/Sidebar.js:72-73` highlights the Endpoint item for `pathname === "/dashboard"`, and Header `src/shared/components/Header.js:172-178` returns the `Endpoint` title for exactly that pathname.
- **OpenProxy**: `web/src/pages/index.astro:11-14` renders `DashboardLayout` + `EndpointPageClient` in place — the URL stays `/`. `web/src/shared/components/Sidebar.tsx:134-139` `isActive` only matches `pathname === "/dashboard" || pathname.startsWith("/dashboard/endpoint")`, so at `/` no nav item is highlighted. `web/src/shared/components/Header.tsx:185-191` `getPageInfo` only special-cases `pathname === "/dashboard"`, so at `/` the header renders empty.
- **Gap**: `/` renders the endpoint page without redirecting, so the canonical landing URL, the active sidebar item and the page title all differ from 9router.
- **Why it matters**: Users who bookmark or share the OpenProxy base URL stay on `/`; a browser reload, a copied link, and the in-app nav all land on different URLs for the same screen. The sidebar shows no current-section highlight and the header shows no title, so the landing page looks unstyled/broken.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/page.js:3-4; .tmp/9router/src/shared/components/Sidebar.js:72-73; .tmp/9router/src/shared/components/Header.js:172-178 | OpenProxy: web/src/pages/index.astro:11-14; web/src/shared/components/Sidebar.tsx:134-139; web/src/shared/components/Header.tsx:185-191
- **Parity fix**: Make `/` issue a 307 to `/dashboard` in the router layer (in the new dashboard auth middleware), or at minimum delete `web/src/pages/index.astro`'s inline render and let the Rust fallback serve `dashboard.html` at `/`. Additionally extend `Sidebar.isActive` and `Header.getPageInfo` to treat `pathname === "/"` like `/dashboard` so a hand-typed `/` still highlights and titles correctly.

## P112-001 · P1 · Embeddings API-key gate setting

- **9router**: The gate reads `settings.requireApiKey` (src/sse/handlers/embeddings.js:56) — a setting that is entirely independent of `settings.requireLogin`. Both default to true (src/lib/db/repos/settingsRepo.js:26-27) and are separately toggled; every LLM/media route gates on `requireApiKey` (chat.js:71, imageGeneration.js:40, embeddings.js:56, videoGeneration.js:47, fetch.js:52, stt.js:32, search.js:49, tts.js:37). An operator can protect the dashboard (`requireLogin=true`) while leaving `/v1/*` open on a trusted LAN (`requireApiKey=false`).
- **OpenProxy**: There is no `requireApiKey` setting at all. The gate reads `state.db.snapshot().settings.require_login` (src/server/api/media.rs:464-468) — the same field that governs dashboard login, and the same field the chat path uses (src/server/api/chat.rs:265-266). `Settings` (src/types/mod.rs:520-524) has no `require_api_key` member, and no toggle for one exists in the API or dashboard.
- **Gap**: The embeddings API-key requirement is driven by the dashboard-login setting in OpenProxy, but by a separate, independently-toggled setting in 9router.
- **Why it matters**: The two settings serve different deployment models. 9router supports a locked-down dashboard with an open LAN-facing /v1 surface; a user who migrates that configuration gets 401 on /v1/embeddings (and on every other /v1 route), so a working integration breaks on upgrade. Conversely, a user who disables `require_login` to reach the dashboard unauthenticated silently opens /v1/embeddings to anyone who can reach the port, sending their embedding input and provider keys through an unauthenticated router.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/sse/handlers/embeddings.js:56, .tmp/9router/src/lib/db/repos/settingsRepo.js:26-27 | OpenProxy: src/server/api/media.rs:464-468, src/types/mod.rs:520-524, src/server/api/chat.rs:265-266
- **Parity fix**: Add a `require_api_key: bool` (serde default true) to `Settings` and gate `/v1/embeddings` on it, leaving `require_login` to control dashboard access only. Wire it to the same Endpoint/Profile settings toggle 9router exposes. If the collapse to a single flag is an intentional divergence, record it in docs/parity-9router.md — that file currently lists only the 401/403 refresh as an embeddings divergence (line 26) and says nothing about the gate.

## P113-001 · P1 · Embeddings provider rejection for unsupported providers

- **9router**: `getEmbeddingAdapter(provider)` (open-sse/handlers/embeddingProviders/index.js:25-31) returns `null` for anything outside the 11-name OPENAI_COMPAT_PROVIDERS list plus `gemini`, `google_ai_studio`, `selfhosted-embedding` and the two node prefixes. `handleEmbeddingsCore` turns that into a hard 400: `Provider 'X' does not support embeddings.` (open-sse/handlers/embeddingsCore.js:32-38). This deliberately rejects `venice` and `tokenrouter`, which both declare `embeddingConfig` in the registry (open-sse/providers/registry/venice.js:46-50, tokenrouter.js:54-58) but are not in the adapter list.
