- **OpenProxy**: `get_embedding_adapter` returns `None` for the same set (src/core/media/embeddings/mod.rs:46-68), but `None` is treated as "no adapter, use the generic forwarder" rather than as an error: `try_provider_adapter` propagates the `None` (src/server/api/media.rs:1101, `media_result_to_response(result?)`), so `execute_media_provider` falls through to `build_media_url` (:546) and actually POSTs the request. The URL is derived from the connection's chat base URL, so for `venice` — whose `PROVIDER_CONFIGS` base_url is `https://api.venice.ai/api/v1/chat/completions` (src/core/executor/default.rs:240-242) — the request goes to `https://api.venice.ai/api/v1/chat/completions/embeddings`. For a provider with neither a baseUrl nor a config entry, `get_provider_base_url` guesses `https://api.<provider>.com/v1` (:840).
- **Gap**: 9router returns 400 for an unsupported embedding provider; OpenProxy silently proxies the request to a derived (usually wrong) URL.
- **Why it matters**: Two concrete consequences. First, a user who configures a Venice or TokenRouter embedding key gets a confusing 404/405 from a malformed URL instead of 9router's clear `Provider 'venice' does not support embeddings.` — the failure is attributed to the provider rather than to the unsupported configuration. Second, for a mistyped or unknown provider id with no baseUrl, OpenProxy ships the user's input text and Bearer API key to a guessed third-party host (`https://api.<typo>.com/v1/embeddings`) where 9router would have refused locally. That is the exact data-leak class 9router's own selfhostedEmbedding adapter was written to prevent (open-sse/handlers/embeddingProviders/selfhostedEmbedding.js:1-18).
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/handlers/embeddingsCore.js:32-38, open-sse/handlers/embeddingProviders/index.js:7-31, open-sse/handlers/embeddingProviders/selfhostedEmbedding.js:1-18 | OpenProxy: src/core/media/embeddings/mod.rs:46-68, src/server/api/media.rs:1101, :546, :790-800, :840, src/core/executor/default.rs:240-242
- **Parity fix**: Distinguish "no adapter because the provider is not an embedding provider" from "no adapter configured" at the `embeddings` route kind. When `route_kind == "embeddings"` and `embeddings::dispatch` returns `None`, return 400 with the message `Provider '{provider}' does not support embeddings.` instead of falling through to `build_media_url`. That both restores parity and closes the guessed-host leak, since the generic forwarder is never reached for embeddings.

## P114-001 · P1 · Embeddings upstream-error HTTP status

- **9router**: On a non-ok upstream, `parseUpstreamError(providerResponse)` returns `statusCode: response.status` verbatim (open-sse/utils/error.js:86) and `handleEmbeddingsCore` uses it unchanged: `createErrorResult(statusCode, errMsg)` (open-sse/handlers/embeddingsCore.js:113-116). `formatProviderError` only reformats the message string (open-sse/utils/error.js:139-147); it never alters the status.
- **OpenProxy**: `handle_embeddings` captures the upstream status faithfully (src/core/media/embeddings/handler.rs:142-143), but `media_result_to_response` routes it through `json_error_response` (src/server/api/media.rs:1107-1109), which calls `infer_status_from_message(status.as_u16(), message)` (src/core/utils/error.rs:224-265). That function overrides the status by substring-matching the upstream error text: `invalid api key`/`invalid credentials`/`unauthorized` to 401 (:247-251), `insufficient`/`quota`/`balance` to 403 (:229-240), `rate limit`/`too many requests` to 429 (:226-227), `not supported` to 406, `model not found` to 404.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy re-derives the HTTP status from the upstream error text instead of passing the upstream status through.
- **Why it matters**: OpenAI-compatible clients branch on the status code: 401 triggers an API-key prompt or credential refresh, 429 triggers exponential backoff and retry, 403 is surfaced as a permission/quota error. A provider returning 400 `{"error":{"message":"Incorrect API key provided"}}` — which several embedding providers do — makes 9router return 400 and OpenProxy return 401, so the client asks the user for a new key for a non-auth problem. An upstream 500 whose body merely mentions "quota" becomes a 403 and a 500 mentioning "rate limit" becomes a 429, suppressing retries during a genuine outage. The error taxonomy also changes: the body flips from `invalid_request_error` to `authentication_error`/`rate_limit_error` (open-sse/config/errorConfig.js:3-16), which strict clients parse.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/handlers/embeddingsCore.js:113-116, open-sse/utils/error.js:86, open-sse/utils/error.js:139-147 | OpenProxy: src/server/api/media.rs:1107-1109, :1114-1121, src/core/utils/error.rs:224-265
- **Parity fix**: Pass the upstream status through unchanged for the embeddings adapter path. `EmbeddingsHandlerError::Http(u16, String)` already carries the true status; `media_result_to_response` should emit `build_error_body(err.status(), Some(&message))` directly with that status instead of funnelling through `json_error_response`/`infer_status_from_message`. Keep `friendly_error_message` for HTML-title extraction if desired, but drop the status inference for this route.

## P115-001 · P1 · Embeddings usage/token accounting

- **9router**: On every successful embeddings call the handler runs `exactEmbeddingUsage(result.usage)` and, when the upstream usage is non-estimated and structurally consistent (prompt_tokens > 0, completion_tokens === 0, total_tokens === prompt_tokens), calls `saveRequestUsage({ provider, model, connectionId, apiKey, endpoint, tokens, status: "success" })` (src/sse/handlers/embeddings.js:137-151). The guard is exactEmbeddingUsage at :17-24, which requires `!raw.estimated` and `completionTokens === 0`.
- **OpenProxy**: Usage recording is unreachable for the supported path. The per-provider adapter is tried first and short-circuits the function: `if let Some(resp) = try_provider_adapter(...).await { return resp; }` (src/server/api/media.rs:533-544). The `if route_kind == "embeddings" && response.status().is_success()` usage-recording block lives further down at src/server/api/media.rs:610-676 — i.e. AFTER that early return, in the generic fallback branch. `try_provider_adapter` only returns `None` when `embeddings::dispatch` returns `None`, which `get_embedding_adapter` does solely for providers outside the adapter list (src/core/media/embeddings/mod.rs:66).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy records exact embedding tokens only for providers that 9router rejects with 400, and records nothing for every provider the adapter actually supports.
- **Why it matters**: Zero token and cost accounting for all real embedding traffic. The provider page, the usage/quota dashboards, and per-key spend limits all read this data, so embedding spend is invisible and unbudgetable. The inversion is exact: the 13 providers OpenProxy supports produce no usage rows, and the unsupported providers it accidentally forwards do.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/sse/handlers/embeddings.js:17-24, :137-151 | OpenProxy: src/server/api/media.rs:533-544 (early return) vs :610-676 (usage block), src/core/media/embeddings/mod.rs:66
- **Parity fix**: Hoist the usage-recording block out of the generic branch and run it on the adapter path too, or (cleaner) have `handle_embeddings` return the normalized body's `usage` alongside the body and record it in `execute_media_provider` before the early return. Reuse the exact same guard as OpenProxy's own generic path (`!estimated && prompt_tokens > 0 && completion_tokens == 0 && total_tokens == prompt_tokens`) so the two paths cannot drift, then call `state.usage_tracker().track_request(...)` (src/server/api/media.rs:661-672).

## P116-001 · P1 · Endpoint page — Tailscale connect / auth-wait state

- **9router**: 9router does NOT auto-open a popup (browsers block popups opened after async work). It stores the URL in `tsAuthUrl` plus a human label in `tsAuthLabel` via `requestUserAuth(url, label)`, and the `(tsLoading || tsConnecting)` render branch shows an inline `Button` reading `{tsAuthLabel || "Open"}` (`"Open Login Page"` for `data.needsLogin`, `"Open Funnel Settings"` for funnel) that calls `window.open` from a real user click. Progress text is `Login required — click "Open Login Page" to continue` / `Click "Open Funnel Settings" to enable Funnel...`. The `finally` block always runs `clearUserAuth()` so the label/URL are cleared when the wait ends.
- **OpenProxy**: OpenProxy has no `requestUserAuth` / `tsAuthLabel` at all. `handleConnectTailscale(preOpenedTab)` is invoked from a "Connect" button that pre-opens a blank tab, and when the backend returns `needsLogin`/`funnelNotEnabled` it navigates that tab or calls `window.open` itself, setting `tsProgress("Waiting for login...")` / `"Enable Funnel in browser, waiting..."`. The `(tsLoading || tsConnecting)` render branch (line 1112) has NO auth button — only the progress line and a Stop button. `setTsAuthUrl` is set but never surfaced while loading. The `finally` block uses `if (!tsAuthUrl) setTsProgress("")`, so the progress string is never cleared.
- **Gap**: During the Tailscale login/funnel wait the user sees only a spinner with no way to (re)open the auth page, and the progress text is never cleared on completion — 9router shows a labelled inline button and always clears its auth state.
- **Why it matters**: The pre-opened tab is opened from a `window.open("")` in the Connect click handler, but browsers routinely block or discard a tab that later navigates to a cross-origin auth page, and the flow explicitly re-opens it from `pollFunnelEnable` long after the user gesture. With no inline button in the loading branch, a blocked popup leaves the user staring at "Waiting for login..." for the full 40 × 3000 ms = 120 s poll budget before "Login timed out." — with no recovery affordance. Because `setTsProgress("")` is gated on `!tsAuthUrl` and `tsAuthUrl` is always set by this path, the stale progress string also persists into later renders, so a subsequent manual connect shows a wrong status.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/endpoint/EndpointPageClient.js:53 (tsAuthLabel state), :486-490 (requestUserAuth), :516-517 (needsLogin → requestUserAuth + prompt), :566-567 (pollFunnelEnable → requestUserAuth + prompt), :872-880 (inline Button in the tsLoading branch), :882 (clearUserAuth in finally), :556-563 (finally block). OpenProxy: web/src/components/EndpointPageClient.tsx:688-689 (preOpenedTab param), :714-719 (needsLogin → window.open + "Waiting for login..."), :773-779 (pollFunnelEnable → window.open + "Enable Funnel in browser, waiting..."), :1112-1125 (tsLoading branch has no auth button), :766-771 (finally gates setTsProgress on !tsAuthUrl), :1573-1582 (Connect button pre-opens a blank tab).
- **Parity fix**: Add `const [tsAuthLabel, setTsAuthLabel] = useState("")` and a `requestUserAuth(url, label)` / `clearUserAuth()` pair, then in the `needsLogin` branch call `requestUserAuth(data.authUrl, "Open Login Page")` with progress `Login required — click "Open Login Page" to continue` (no `window.open`), and in `pollFunnelEnable` call `requestUserAuth(enableUrl, "Open Funnel Settings")` with progress `Click "Open Funnel Settings" to enable Funnel...`. Add `{tsAuthUrl && (<Button size="sm" icon="open_in_new" onClick={() => window.open(tsAuthUrl, "tailscale_auth", "width=600,height=700,noopener,noreferrer")}>{tsAuthLabel || "Open"}</Button>)}` inside the `(tsLoading || tsConnecting)` branch, and restore the unconditional `setTsProgress(""); clearUserAuth();` in the `finally` block. The pre-opened-tab optimization may be kept as a best-effort but must not be the only path to the auth page.

## P117-001 · P1 · GET /api/models — dashboard capability feed

- **9router**: Each entry carries `fullModel`, `routedModel` (= `${providerAlias}/${model}`), `alias`, and a five-key `caps` block `{vision, search, reasoning, contextWindow, maxOutput}` from `getCapabilitiesForModel`, with custom models appended and their stored `caps` spread over the derived ones — `.tmp/9router/src/app/api/models/route.js:22-64`.
- **OpenProxy**: Each entry carries `fullModel`, `alias`, `name`, `kind`, and a two-key `caps` block `{vision, reasoning}` only — `src/server/api/models_alias.rs:88-145`. The caps derivation is a hand-rolled substring test (`id_lower.contains("vision") || contains("vl") || contains("reason") || contains("thinking") || contains("o1"/"o3"/"o4")`, `:96-131`) rather than 9router's `capabilities.js` table.
- **Gap**: `caps` is missing `search`, `contextWindow` and `maxOutput`; `routedModel` is missing entirely; and the derivation is a substring heuristic rather than 9router's pattern table.
- **Why it matters**: `useModelCaps` indexes `byFull[m.routedModel] = m.caps` in 9router (`.tmp/9router/src/shared/hooks/useModelCaps.js:16`) — the OpenProxy hook has no `routedModel` line, so any caller passing a routed (alias-qualified) key that differs from `fullModel` misses the cache. The missing `contextWindow`/`maxOutput` are the same fields the 9router GrokBuild card reads via `getCaps(model)?.contextWindow` (`.tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/cli-tools/components/GrokBuildToolCard.js:78, 173, 183`) to write the correct subagent context window; OpenProxy's `GrokBuildToolCard.tsx` has no `getCaps` call at all. The `search` key drives the search-capability badge, which can never light on the OpenProxy side.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/models/route.js:22-37 (routedModel + 5-key caps), :44-64 (custom models with `...(m.caps || {})` override); .tmp/9router/src/shared/hooks/useModelCaps.js:16, :48-55 (fallback shape with contextWindow/maxOutput); .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/cli-tools/components/GrokBuildToolCard.js:78. OpenProxy: src/server/api/models_alias.rs:88-145 (caps = {vision, reasoning} only, no routedModel); web/src/shared/hooks/useModelCaps.ts:118-136 (no routedModel indexing, no contextWindow fallback).
- **Parity fix**: In `list_models` (`src/server/api/models_alias.rs:65`), replace the substring block at `:96-131` with a call to the Rust equivalent of `getCapabilitiesForModel` (the repo already has `core::combo::capabilities::get_capabilities_for_model` at `src/core/combo/capabilities.rs:724`) and emit the full five-key object `{vision, search, reasoning, contextWindow, maxOutput}`. Add `"routedModel": full_model` to the emitted JSON. Then add `routedModel?: string` to `ModelsApiEntry` and the `full[m.routedModel] = caps` line to `web/src/shared/hooks/useModelCaps.ts`, and restore `getCaps` usage in `web/src/components/cli-tools/GrokBuildToolCard.tsx` so the subagent context window is written from real data.

## P118-001 · P1 · GET /api/tunnel/status — intent vs. actual state

- **9router**: `getTunnelStatus()` returns `enabled: settingsEnabled && running` **and** `settingsEnabled` as a separate key, so the dashboard can distinguish "the user asked for this and the watchdog is restarting it" from "the user turned it off" — `.tmp/9router/src/lib/tunnel/cloudflare/manager.js:144-149`. Tailscale does the same and adds `loggedIn` — `.tmp/9router/src/lib/tunnel/tailscale/manager.js:123-128`. The dashboard reads `settingsEnabled ?? enabled` for exactly this reason — `.tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/endpoint/EndpointPageClient.js` and `ToolDetailClient.js:91-93` equivalents.
- **OpenProxy**: `tunnel_status` emits only `{enabled, tunnelUrl, shortId, publicUrl, running}` for cloudflare and `{enabled, tunnelUrl, running}` for tailscale — `src/server/api/tunnel.rs:130-146`. `settingsEnabled` is computed inline (`settings.tunnel_enabled && matches!(tunnel.provider…, "cloudflare")`, `:132`) and folded into `enabled`, so the two concepts are collapsed. Tailscale has no `loggedIn`. The dashboard falls back to the collapsed value: `web/src/components/EndpointPageClient.tsx:222, 235, 375, 383` all read `data.tunnel?.settingsEnabled ?? data.tunnel?.enabled`, and `web/src/components/cli-tools/ToolDetailClient.tsx:91-93` reads `data.tunnel?.enabled || data.tunnel?.settingsEnabled`.
- **Gap**: `settingsEnabled` and `loggedIn` are never emitted, so the dashboard's `?? enabled` fallback always resolves to the actual-run state — the user's on/off intent is not representable in the response.
- **Why it matters**: OpenProxy's own dashboard comment states the reason: "Trust user intent (settingsEnabled): UI stays 'enabled' while watchdog restarts process" (`web/src/components/EndpointPageClient.tsx:216`). Because the key is always absent, the `??` fallback fires and the toggle snaps back to off every time the tunnel process is mid-restart. A user toggling Tailscale on during a watchdog restart sees the switch flip off, and the Tailscale card cannot tell "logged out" from "daemon not running" because `loggedIn` is missing, so it will not re-prompt a `tailscale login` for a device that was removed server-side.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/lib/tunnel/cloudflare/manager.js:134, 141-149 (settingsEnabled separate from enabled); .tmp/9router/src/lib/tunnel/tailscale/manager.js:117, 120, 123-128 (settingsEnabled + loggedIn). OpenProxy: src/server/api/tunnel.rs:132 (settingsEnabled folded into enabled), :138-142 (tailscale block lacks loggedIn); web/src/components/EndpointPageClient.tsx:216 (comment), :222, :235, :375, :383; web/src/components/cli-tools/ToolDetailClient.tsx:91-93.
- **Parity fix**: In `tunnel_status` (`src/server/api/tunnel.rs:118`), emit `"settingsEnabled": settings.tunnel_enabled` for cloudflare (`:131-137`) and `"settingsEnabled": settings.tailscale_enabled` plus `"loggedIn": <probe>` for tailscale (`:138-142`), keeping `enabled` as the conjoined `settingsEnabled && running` value. This is purely additive — every existing reader that uses `data.tunnel?.enabled` keeps working, and the `?? settingsEnabled` readers in `EndpointPageClient.tsx` start resolving as intended.

## P119-001 · P1 · GET /v1 — root discovery endpoint

- **9router**: `next.config.mjs:88-90` rewrites `/v1` → `/api/v1`, and `src/app/api/v1/route.js:1` is `export { GET, OPTIONS } from "./models/route"`. So `GET /v1` returns the *same* `{object:"list", data:[…]}` model catalog as `GET /v1/models` (`.tmp/9router/src/app/api/v1/models/route.js:571-579`).
- **OpenProxy**: `GET /v1` returns a static endpoint index instead: `{version:"v1", endpoints:["/v1/chat/completions", …]}` — `src/server/api/mod.rs:82` (route) and `:424-446` (`v1_root` handler). There is no `/api/v1` route at all.
- **Gap**: `GET /v1` returns a hand-maintained endpoint-index JSON in OpenProxy but the full routable model list in 9Router.
- **Why it matters**: The index is a hardcoded list that is already stale (it names `/v1/web/fetch` and `/v1/usage` but not `/v1/audio/voices`, `/v1/rerank`, `/v1/moderations`, `/v1/videos/*`, `/v1/models/image`, or `/v1/search`-adjacent media routes that OpenProxy does register), and it is missing the 7 trailing-slash-free OpenProxy-only routes entirely. A client that discovers capability by hitting the version root gets an incomplete answer, whereas 9router's answer is the real dispatchable catalog.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/next.config.mjs:88-90 (rewrite /v1 → /api/v1); .tmp/9router/src/app/api/v1/route.js:1 (`export { GET, OPTIONS } from "./models/route"`); .tmp/9router/src/app/api/v1/models/route.js:571-579. OpenProxy: src/server/api/mod.rs:82 (`/v1` → `v1_root`), :424-446 (static endpoint list).
- **Parity fix**: Change `v1_root` in `src/server/api/mod.rs:424` to delegate to `v1_models::list_default_models` (or register `/v1` with a thin handler that forwards to it), so `GET /v1` returns `{object:"list", data:[…]}` exactly like `GET /v1/models`. If the endpoint index has independent value, move it to an additive `GET /api/version` field or a new `/v1/index` rather than changing the parity surface.

## P120-001 · P1 · GET /v1/models — cross-instance recursion guard

- **9router**: Defines `INTERNAL_MODELS_FETCH_HEADER = "x-9r-internal-models-fetch"` (:144). When it fetches another instance's `/models` it sets that header to `"1"` (:210). `GET` reads it back (`skipDynamicFetch = request.headers.get(...) === "1"`, :574) and threads it into `buildModelsList` (:575), which suppresses the recursive compatible-provider fetch at `:388` (`if (isCompatibleProvider && rawModelIds.length === 0 && !skipDynamicFetch)`). Two instances pointed at each other therefore terminate after one hop.
- **OpenProxy**: No such header or guard exists — `INTERNAL_MODELS_FETCH` / `x-9r-internal` appear nowhere in `v1_models.rs` or `provider_models.rs`. `list_default_models` (:41-43) always calls `list_models_for_kinds` unconditionally, which reaches `fetch_discovered_model_ids` (`src/server/api/provider_models.rs:282-305`) → `fetch_openai_compatible_models` (:810-819) → `fetch_openai_style_models_with_bearer` (:832), which fetches `{baseUrl}/models` with no marker header.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's `/v1/models` has no cross-instance recursion marker, so two instances configured as each other's OpenAI-compatible base URL recurse without bound.
- **Why it matters**: Setting up "9router in front of OpenProxy" or a failover pair is a normal multi-instance deployment, and pointing instance A's compatible node at instance B's `/v1` is the obvious way to do it. Without the marker, A's `/v1/models` fetches B's, B's fetches A's, and each request fans into an unbounded loop that never returns — every model-list call from a client hangs until its own timeout, and the process accumulates one in-flight outbound request per hop, exhausting sockets. The failure is invisible in logs because each individual hop succeeds. 9router's header breaks exactly this; OpenProxy reproduces the vulnerable half of the design (the compatible-provider fetch) without the guard.
- **Evidence**: 9router: `.tmp/9router/src/app/api/v1/models/route.js:144` (header const), `:210` (header sent on the outbound fetch), `:574-575` (header read + threaded), `:388` (recursion suppressed). OpenProxy: `src/server/api/v1_models.rs:41-43` (no guard), `:88-378` (`build_models_list` reaches the fetch unconditionally), `src/server/api/provider_models.rs:282-305` (`fetch_discovered_model_ids`), `:810-819` (outbound fetch, no marker header).
- **Parity fix**: Add `const INTERNAL_MODELS_FETCH_HEADER: &str = "x-9r-internal-models-fetch";` and thread a `skip_dynamic_fetch: bool` from the handler into `build_models_list`. Set the header to `"1"` on the outbound `{baseUrl}/models` requests in `fetch_openai_style_models_with_bearer` / the anthropic-compatible fetcher, read it off the incoming `HeaderMap` in `list_default_models` / `list_models_by_kind`, and skip the `fetch_discovered_model_ids` call at `src/server/api/v1_models.rs:~240` when it is set.

## P121-001 · P1 · GET /v1/models/{provider}/{model} — single-model lookup

- **9router**: `/v1/models/[...model]` is a catch-all. With a single segment it filters by kind; with two segments it joins them and does an exact `id` lookup against the same `buildModelsList([LLM_KIND])` catalog, returning the matched model card bare on success, or 404 `{error:{message:"The model '{id}' does not exist or you do not have access to it.", type:"invalid_request_error", code:"model_not_found"}}` — `.tmp/9router/src/app/api/v1/models/[...model]/route.js:40-77`.
- **OpenProxy**: Only `GET /v1/models/{kind}` is registered — `src/server/api/v1_models.rs:27-35` (and the same three routes nested under `/v1/v1/models`, `src/server/api/mod.rs:90-105`). Axum's `{kind}` matches exactly one segment, so `GET /v1/models/openai/gpt-4o` lands in `list_models_by_kind`, whose `_` arm returns 404 `{error:{message:"Unknown model kind: openai/gpt-4o. Supported: image, tts, stt, embedding, image-to-text, web", type:"invalid_request_error"}}` — `src/server/api/v1_models.rs:45-64`. No single-model lookup path exists.
- **Gap**: `GET /v1/models/{provider}/{model}` returns 404 "Unknown model kind" instead of the model card, and genuinely unknown ids get the wrong error type and message.
- **Why it matters**: This is the standard OpenAI single-model-retrieval call that SDKs (OpenAI Python/Node `models.retrieve`) issue. In 9Router it returns the card; in OpenProxy it always 404s with a message that names a *kind* vocabulary the caller never used, so the failure looks like a client bug rather than an unimplemented route. A 200-then-404 probing client cannot validate ids at all.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/v1/models/[...model]/route.js:40-42 (catch-all param), :58-77 (join, exact-id lookup, bare card, 404 body). OpenProxy: src/server/api/v1_models.rs:31-33 (only /v1/models and /v1/models/{kind} and /v1/models/info registered); :45-64 (the `_` arm's 404 message); src/server/api/mod.rs:90-105 (same three under /v1/v1/models).
- **Parity fix**: Add `.route("/v1/models/{provider}/{model}", get(get_model_by_id))` (axum 0.7+ wildcard `{*rest}` or a two-segment pattern) to `v1_models::routes()` and to the `/v1/v1/models` nest. The handler should call `build_models_list(&state, &snapshot, &[LLM_KIND])`, `find` on `candidate.id == format!("{provider}/{model}")`, and return the matched `ModelCard` bare — falling back to the 9router 404 body `{error:{message:"The model '{id}' does not exist or you do not have access to it.", type:"invalid_request_error", code:"model_not_found"}}`. Register it *after* the existing single-segment `/v1/models/{kind}` so kind filtering still wins for `image`/`tts`/`stt`/`embedding`/`image-to-text`/`web`.

## P122-001 · P1 · In-app navigation model (client router vs full page load)

- **9router**: Next.js App Router: `next/link` is imported in 13 files and rendered as 29 `<Link>` elements (e.g. `src/shared/components/Sidebar.js:162`, `:210`, `:225`, `:242`, `:270`, `:326`; `src/shared/components/Header.js:258`; `cli-tools/components/MitmLinkCard.js:12`), plus 11 `router.push` call sites. Navigation is client-side: React state, scroll position and the loaded JS chunks are preserved, and the server is not re-entered.
- **OpenProxy**: There is no client router. All 29 nav links are raw anchors — `web/src/shared/components/Sidebar.tsx:200` (logo), `:242` (navItems), `:289`/`:304` (media kinds + web), `:322` (systemItems), `:347` (debugItems), `:377` (footerItems); `web/src/shared/components/Header.tsx:275` (breadcrumbs); `web/src/components/cli-tools/MitmLinkCard.tsx:22`; `web/src/components/MediaProvidersKindPageClient.tsx:48`. No `view-transitions`, no `astro:after-swap`, no link interception anywhere in `web/src`.
- **Gap**: Every sidebar/header/breadcrumb click performs a full document reload instead of a client-side transition.
- **Why it matters**: Each navigation tears down and re-hydrates the whole React tree, discards all page state (open modals, expanded sections, scroll offset, in-flight form input) and re-issues every page's fetches. On the core providers/combos flow this turns a single-click context switch into a visible full reload; browser back/forward also re-executes the server round-trip rather than restoring the prior view.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/shared/components/Sidebar.js:162,210,225,242,270,326; .tmp/9router/src/shared/components/Header.js:258 (13 `next/link` imports, 29 `<Link>`, 11 `router.push`) | OpenProxy: web/src/shared/components/Sidebar.tsx:200,242,289,304,322,347,377; web/src/shared/components/Header.tsx:275 (29 `<a href>`, zero client-router hooks)
- **Parity fix**: Either adopt Astro's `<ClientRouter />` (view-transitions router) in the root `Layout.astro` with matching `transition:*` directives, or introduce a small client-side nav component that `history.pushState` + swaps the page island and re-mounts `Header`/`Sidebar` on `popstate`, replacing every `<a href="/dashboard/...">` in the shell with it.

## P123-001 · P1 · ModelSelectModal / Provider detail page — capability badges (`useModelCaps`)

- **9router**: `useModelCaps` keeps a module-level `cache` and `inflight` promise so a single `/api/models` fetch is shared by every consumer, AND registers `window.addEventListener("customModelChanged", invalidate)` where `invalidate` nulls the cache and re-fetches. So after any add/delete of a custom model — which dispatches `customModelChanged` — the capability maps are refreshed and the `CapacityBadges` (vision / search / reasoning / contextWindow / maxOutput) re-render without a page reload.
- **OpenProxy**: `useModelCaps` has no module cache, no in-flight dedup, and no event listener at all (`grep -c addEventListener` on the file returns 0). Every consumer instance runs its own `fetch("/api/models")` in a `useEffect(..., [])` and, when the effect re-runs, the caps are read once and frozen. Meanwhile 8 sites still `dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent("customModelChanged"))` (ProviderDetailPageClient.tsx:553, 610, 782, 802; ModelsCard.tsx:180, 188, 203, 213) — but the only listener left is `SttExampleCard.tsx:58`, which is unrelated to caps.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's `useModelCaps` dropped both the shared cache/in-flight dedup and the `customModelChanged` invalidation listener, so capability badges are fetched N times and never refresh after a custom-model change.
- **Why it matters**: Two distinct loading defects. (1) Stale data: a user who adds a custom model with declared caps sees the vision/reasoning badges stay absent (falling back to the client-side `inferCaps` glob heuristic, which cannot know provider-specific capabilities) until a full page reload — 9router updates them live. (2) Request amplification: `useModelCaps` has 5 consumers (ModelSelectModal, CombosPageClient ×2 sites, ProviderDetailPageClient, ModelsCard) and each mounts its own unguarded fetch, so opening the combos page or a provider page issues several redundant `/api/models` round-trips per navigation where 9router issues exactly one.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/shared/hooks/useModelCaps.js:7-8 (module `cache` + `inflight`), :22-38 (loadModelCaps dedup), :71-80 (customModelChanged listener + invalidate), consumers at shared/components/ModelSelectModal.js, app/(dashboard)/dashboard/combos/page.js, app/(dashboard)/dashboard/providers/[id]/page.js. OpenProxy: web/src/shared/hooks/useModelCaps.ts:96-130 (per-instance fetch, no cache, no listener, no dedup, deps `[]`); dispatchers at web/src/components/providers/ProviderDetailPageClient.tsx:553,610,782,802 and web/src/components/providers/ModelsCard.tsx:180,188,203,213; sole unrelated listener at web/src/components/media-providers/SttExampleCard.tsx:58. Badge consumers: ProviderDetailPageClient.tsx:1302,1331 and CombosPageClient.tsx:435,666.
- **Parity fix**: Restore the 9router module-level cache shape in web/src/shared/hooks/useModelCaps.ts: `let cache: {byFull, byId} | null = null; let inflight: Promise<...> | null = null;` with `loadModelCaps()` returning `cache` when present, `inflight` when a request is in flight, and clearing `inflight` in `.finally()`. Seed `useState` from `cache?.byFull` so an already-warm cache paints synchronously. Then add `window.addEventListener("customModelChanged", invalidate)` in the existing effect, where `invalidate` sets `cache = null` and re-runs `loadModelCaps().then(sync)`, and remove the listener on cleanup. Keep the existing `inferCaps` heuristic as the per-key fallback so custom models still get pattern-derived caps between refetches.

## P124-001 · P1 · ModelSelectModal — combo visibility under capacity-adapter filter

- **9router**: `filteredCombos` returns `[]` when *either* a kind or a capability filter is active: `if (kindFilter || capFilter) return [];` (ModelSelectModal.js:427). The comment states the reason: combos are LLM-only by design, and a capacity-adapter pick must be a concrete provider model. `capFilter` is a first-class prop (ModelSelectModal.js:81) and the only caller passes it from the combos page capacity-adapter card: `capFilter={cap.key}` (combos/page.js:547).
- **OpenProxy**: `filteredCombos` only checks the kind filter: `if (kindFilter) return [];` (ModelSelectModal.tsx:499), with deps `[combos, searchQuery, kindFilter]` (:502) — `capFilter` is not consulted even though it is a declared prop (ModelSelectModal.tsx:75, 97) and is honoured for models at ModelSelectModal.tsx:520-521. Its own caller passes it identically: `capFilter={cap.key}` (CombosPageClient.tsx:718).
- **Gap**: 9router hides the Combos group whenever a capacity adapter's `capFilter` is active; OpenProxy only hides it for `kindFilter`, so combos leak into the vision/audio capacity-adapter picker.
- **Why it matters**: The capacity-adapter pools are exactly the place where picking a chat combo is wrong: an adapter slot is filled with a model that must accept image (or audio) input, and a combo is an ordered chat fallback chain, not a vision model. Letting the user add `my-combo` to a vision adapter pool produces an adapter entry that cannot serve an image request, and the combo's first member — possibly a text-only model — gets selected for it.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/shared/components/ModelSelectModal.js:81 (prop), 427 (guard), and .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/combos/page.js:547 (caller). OpenProxy: web/src/shared/components/ModelSelectModal.tsx:75, 97 (prop), 499-502 (guard), 520-521 (capFilter honoured for models only), and web/src/components/CombosPageClient.tsx:718 (caller)
- **Parity fix**: Change the guard at ModelSelectModal.tsx:499 to `if (kindFilter || capFilter) return [];` and add `capFilter` to the dependency array at :502, so the memo re-evaluates when a capacity adapter is selected. This matches ModelSelectModal.js:427 and restores the 9router invariant that combos never appear under a capability filter.

## P125-001 · P1 · OAuth connect modal — proxy-OAuth and paste-token modes

- **9router**: OAuthModal has three flows. (1) Proxy OAuth: `PROXY_OAUTH_PROVIDERS = new Set(["trae", "windsurf", "zed"])` (OAuthModal.js:10) and `startOAuthFlow` short-circuits into `startProxyFlow(provider)` for them (OAuthModal.js:221-224); the manual-paste fallback for a blocked popup posts `{ code: input, state: authData?.state }` to `/api/oauth/${provider}/exchange` (OAuthModal.js:599-608). (2) Paste-token: `PASTE_TOKEN_PROVIDERS = { trae, windsurf }` with per-provider instructions, placeholder, `ideName`, and `ideOptional` (OAuthModal.js:14-31); the modal renders a browser-vs-paste-token toggle (OAuthModal.js:686-702), probes `/api/oauth/${provider}/ide-status` on open to warn when the IDE is missing (OAuthModal.js:418-423), and shows a token Input gated by `disabled={!pasteToken}` (OAuthModal.js:730-752). (3) On close it stops the local proxy for all five proxy providers (OAuthModal.js:429-441).
- **OpenProxy**: OAuthModal.tsx has none of it. There is no `PROXY_OAUTH_PROVIDERS` set, no `PASTE_TOKEN_PROVIDERS` map, no `authMode`/`pasteToken`/`ideStatus` state, and no `/ide-status` probe. `startOAuthFlow` always takes the generic popup path, and the only manual branch is `handleManualSubmit`, which posts to `/api/oauth/${provider}/exchange` with a callback URL (OAuthModal.tsx:619). The manual-paste block is also gated only on `!isDeviceCode` (OAuthModal.tsx:568) rather than 9router's `!isDeviceCode && !PROXY_OAUTH_PROVIDERS.has(provider)` (OAuthModal.js:760), and the close handler only stops the proxy for codex and xai (OAuthModal.tsx:366-370). The backend routes exist and are registered (src/server/api/oauth.rs:6606, 6614), and trae/windsurf/zed are registered as OAuth providers (web/src/shared/constants/providers.ts:123-125), so only the UI is missing.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's OAuth modal omits 9router's proxy-OAuth and paste-token modes, so trae, windsurf and zed cannot complete sign-in through the dashboard.
- **Why it matters**: trae and windsurf are the two providers whose auth is a *token copy-out of a desktop IDE* rather than a redirect; without the paste-token path a user with no local IDE traffic gets a browser flow that never returns, and the modal sits on "Waiting for popup authorization…" forever. zed needs proxy OAuth because its flow uses a fixed-port native-app callback. The user-visible result is an unusable connect button for three registered providers.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/shared/components/OAuthModal.js:10, 14-31, 221-224, 418-423, 429-441, 599-608, 686-702, 730-752, 760. OpenProxy: web/src/shared/components/OAuthModal.tsx:59-68 (state, no authMode/pasteToken/ideStatus), 189-216 (startOAuthFlow, no proxy branch), 355-370 (open effect, only codex/xai stop-proxy), 568 (render gate), 619-625 (manual submit); src/server/api/oauth.rs:6606, 6614 (routes exist); web/src/shared/constants/providers.ts:123-125 (providers registered)
- **Parity fix**: Port the three pieces verbatim: add `const PROXY_OAUTH_PROVIDERS = new Set(["trae", "windsurf", "zed"])` and the `PASTE_TOKEN_PROVIDERS` map (trae + windsurf entries) to OAuthModal.tsx; add `authMode`/`pasteToken`/`ideStatus` state; in the open effect, probe `/api/oauth/${provider}/ide-status` for paste-token providers and extend the close branch to stop-proxy for trae/windsurf/zed; in `startOAuthFlow`, short-circuit `if (PROXY_OAUTH_PROVIDERS.has(provider) && authMode === "browser") return startProxyFlow(provider)`; in `handleManualSubmit`, add the `if (PROXY_OAUTH_PROVIDERS.has(provider) && input)` branch that POSTs `{ code: input, state: authData?.state }` to `/api/oauth/${provider}/exchange`; and gate the generic manual-paste block on `!isDeviceCode && !PROXY_OAUTH_PROVIDERS.has(provider)` so the two UIs do not render simultaneously.

## P126-001 · P1 · OAuth connect — second account under the same email

- **9router**: createProviderConnection collapses an OAuth login onto an existing row only when it is genuinely the same identity. For codex it requires both rows to expose the same chatgptAccountId; otherwise it matches on chatgptAccountId when present, and otherwise requires (email + username) with explicit guards that reject one-sided matches. A second Codex workspace account, or a second cross-IdP login sharing an email, therefore creates a new row instead of overwriting the first account's rotated refresh token.
- **OpenProxy**: create_imported_oauth_connection matches an existing row on provider == provider and auth_type == oauth and email == email only, and overwrites its access/refresh/id tokens, expiresAt, displayName and providerSpecificData in place. There is no chatgptAccountId or username discrimination.
- **Gap**: Two distinct OAuth accounts that share an email collapse into one row and the first account's tokens are destroyed.
- **Why it matters**: This is the exact failure 9router's codex comment warns about: refresh tokens are single-use and rotated, so overwriting the first account's token pair makes it look invalid as soon as a second grant is added. In OpenProxy the first Codex, Kimi or Grok account silently disappears from the account list and stops being usable, with no error surfaced.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/lib/db/repos/connectionsRepo.js:133-165 (codex chatgptAccountId match, workspace match, email+username match with asymmetric-mismatch guards). OpenProxy: src/server/api/oauth.rs:1503-1524 (match on provider + auth_type oauth + email, then overwrite access_token/refresh_token/id_token/provider_specific_data at :1510-1520).
- **Parity fix**: Port the identity-matching rules: match on (provider, authType oauth, email) AND, when both sides expose providerSpecificData.chatgptAccountId, equality of that id; otherwise require equality of providerSpecificData.username when either side has one, treating a one-sided match as a distinct identity. Only merge when the identity test passes.

## P127-001 · P1 · OAuth flow — raw-JWT exchange transition

- **9router**: `POST /api/oauth/[provider]/exchange` has an explicit terminal branch: if `code` starts with `"eyJ"` and contains a `.`, it decodes the JWT (both the nested `https://api.openai.com/auth` claims and top-level `account_id` / `plan_type` / `email`) and stores a connection with `authType: "access_token"`, `providerSpecificData: { authMethod: "access_token", chatgptAccountId, chatgptPlanType }` and `testStatus: "active"`. No token endpoint is contacted.
- **OpenProxy**: `exchange_oauth_compat` has no such branch. It validates `code`/`redirectUri`/`code_verifier` and dispatches straight to the provider's token endpoint, which rejects a JWT with a 4xx/5xx, surfaced as HTTP 500 `Token exchange failed: …`.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy does not permit the "pasted raw access token → active connection" transition that 9router supports at the same endpoint.
- **Why it matters**: A user who pastes a ChatGPT/Copilot JWT into the manual-input step of the Codex modal (blocked popup, remote dashboard) gets a 500 and no connection in OpenProxy; the identical paste creates a working account in 9router.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/oauth/[provider]/[action]/route.js:414-455. OpenProxy: src/server/api/oauth.rs:4426-4520 (no JWT branch; `decode_jwt_claims` exists at :1568 but is only used for Codex id_token inspection).
- **Parity fix**: Insert, before the required-fields check in `exchange_oauth_compat`, the 9router branch: if `code.starts_with("eyJ") && code.contains('.')`, decode with `decode_jwt_claims` (+ `extract_codex_account_info`), build a `ProviderConnection { auth_type: "access_token", test_status: Some("active"), .. }` and route it through `create_imported_oauth_connection`.

## P128-001 · P1 · OpenProxy `src/core/rtk/mod.rs:134-147,209-211,524-528`

- **9router**: `open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:278-280`: `if (tokenSaverEnabled && cavemanEnabled && cavemanLevel) { injectCaveman(translatedBody, finalFormat, cavemanLevel); xf.push(...) }`. The only conditions are the settings toggle, the level string, and the per-request `x-9router-token-saver: off` opt-out. There is no request-size or context-pressure condition anywhere in the caveman path.
- **OpenProxy**: `src/server/api/chat.rs:1390-1392` calls `apply_request_preprocessing`, which at `src/core/rtk/mod.rs:134-147` wraps the injection in `if should_auto_apply_caveman(body, model)`. That predicate (`:209-211`) is `estimate_context_tokens(body) >= caveman_trigger_threshold(model)`, and the threshold (`:524-528`) is `infer_context_window(model) / 8` clamped to `[MIN_TRIGGER_TOKENS=512, MAX_TRIGGER_TOKENS=2048]` (`:23-24`). For any Claude/Gemini model the window is 200k, so the threshold saturates at 2048 tokens.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy only injects the Caveman prompt when the estimated context exceeds ~2048 tokens; 9router injects on every request once Caveman is enabled.
- **Why it matters**: A user sets Caveman to "Full" and the toggle reads ON, but every normal coding turn (a few hundred to ~2000 tokens) gets no Caveman instruction at all and the model answers in its default verbose style. The divergence is silent — there is no log line, and the only requests that change behaviour are the long-context ones the user was not thinking about.
- **Evidence**: 9router: `open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:278-280`, `open-sse/rtk/caveman.js:7`. OpenProxy: `src/core/rtk/mod.rs:134-147`, `:209-211`, `:524-528`, `:23-24`, `src/server/api/chat.rs:1390-1392`
- **Parity fix**: Drop the `should_auto_apply_caveman` gate from `apply_request_preprocessing` (`src/core/rtk/mod.rs:139-147`) and call `inject_caveman_prompt` unconditionally when `settings.caveman_enabled`, matching `chatCore.js:278`. Keep `normalize_caveman_level` for the level string and keep the `token_saver_enabled` guard applied by the caller.

## P129-001 · P1 · OpenProxy `src/core/rtk/mod.rs:213-227`, `src/core/translator/ponytail.rs:131-146`, `src/core/rtk/system_inject.rs:16-22`

- **9router**: `open-sse/rtk/systemInject.js:11` `injectSystemPrompt(body, format, prompt)` first checks `isKiroBody(body) || format === FORMATS.KIRO` (`:17`) and routes to `injectKiroSystem` (`:273`), which appends the prompt to the first `conversationState.history[].userInputMessage.content` (falling back to `currentMessage.userInputMessage`). Both `open-sse/rtk/caveman.js:7` and `open-sse/rtk/ponytail.js:7` are thin wrappers over it, so Kiro models get the prompt.
- **OpenProxy**: Both live injectors dispatch on body shape only: `src/core/rtk/mod.rs:213-227` checks `fields.contains_key("system")` → Claude, `is_gemini_shape` → Gemini, else `inject_openai_shape`, which (`:230-244`) only matches `instructions`, `messages`, and `input` and returns `false` when none exist. `src/core/translator/ponytail.rs:131-166` is identical. A Kiro body is `{conversationState: {...}}` — it has none of those keys, so both return `false`. `src/core/rtk/system_inject.rs:16-22` also has no `"kiro"` match arm.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy has no Kiro branch in the system-prompt injector, so caveman, ponytail and the global system-prompt override are all no-ops on `kr/` models.
- **Why it matters**: Ponytail is documented (and implemented) as having no auto-trigger and always applying when enabled; on Kiro it silently never applies, so a user who enabled "lazy senior dev" gets verbose, over-engineered code back from Kiro with no indication that the setting was ignored. 9router explicitly built the Kiro branch to fix exactly this class of silent no-op.
- **Evidence**: 9router: `open-sse/rtk/systemInject.js:11,17,62,273`, `open-sse/rtk/caveman.js:7`, `open-sse/rtk/ponytail.js:7`. OpenProxy: `src/core/rtk/mod.rs:213-227,230-244`, `src/core/translator/ponytail.rs:131-166`, `src/core/rtk/system_inject.rs:16-22`
- **Parity fix**: Port `isKiroBody` + `injectKiroSystem` from `open-sse/rtk/systemInject.js:62,273`: in both `inject_caveman_prompt` (`src/core/rtk/mod.rs:213`) and `inject_ponytail_prompt` (`src/core/translator/ponytail.rs:131`), test for a `conversationState` with `history[]`/`currentMessage.userInputMessage` before the shape dispatch, and append the prompt (with the `\n\n` separator and the same exact-segment dedup) to that user message's `content` string. Add a `"kiro"` arm to `inject_system_prompt` in `src/core/rtk/system_inject.rs:16`.

## P130-001 · P1 · OpenProxy `src/server/api/guard.rs:145-165` + `src/server/api/mod.rs:303-322`

- **9router**: `src/dashboardGuard.js:68-84` lists `LOCAL_ONLY_PATHS` = `/api/cli-tools/cowork-settings`, `/api/cli-tools/antigravity-mitm`, `/api/mcp/`, `/api/tunnel/tailscale-install`, `/api/tunnel/tailscale-enable`, `/api/tunnel/tailscale-disable`, `/api/tunnel/tailscale-check`, `/api/tunnel/enable`, `/api/tunnel/disable`, `/api/oauth/cursor/auto-import`, `/api/oauth/kiro/auto-import`, `/api/auth/reset-password`, `/api/headroom/start`, `/api/headroom/stop`, `/api/headroom/proxy`. These are checked first (`:206-210`) and require `canAccessLocalOnlyRoute` (`:161-166`): a valid `x-9r-cli-token` **or** `isLocalRequest(request) && isAuthenticated(request)`, else `403 {"error":"Local only: CLI token required"}`.
- **OpenProxy**: `require_local_only` exists (`src/server/api/guard.rs:145-165`, 403 `{"error":"Localhost only"}`) but is layered onto exactly one router: `/api/headroom/*` at `src/server/api/mod.rs:303-322`. `/api/mcp/{plugin}/sse` and `/api/mcp/{plugin}/message` are guarded only by `require_admin` (`src/server/api/mcp.rs:39-42`), and every `/api/tunnel/*` route by `require_dashboard_or_management_api_key` (`src/server/api/tunnel.rs:45,95,119,176`). `require_dashboard_session` returns `Ok` unconditionally when `settings.require_login` is false (`src/server/auth/mod.rs:207-216`). The `LOCALLY_ONLY_PATHS` const at `src/server/api/guard.rs:48-62` is annotated `#[allow(dead_code)]` — documentation only, never enforced.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy applies the loopback restriction to `/api/headroom/*` only, while 9router applies it to 15 process-spawning / host-secret / exposure routes including `/api/mcp/*` and every `/api/tunnel/*` action.
- **Why it matters**: With `requireLogin` disabled — a supported, documented setting in both products — a remote caller reaching a tunnel- or Tailscale-exposed OpenProxy can hit `/api/mcp/{plugin}/sse` and make the server spawn a registered child process, and can hit `/api/tunnel/enable` to re-expose the box or `/api/tunnel/tailscale-install` to install a system package. 9router returns 403 for all of these from any non-loopback address.
- **Evidence**: 9router: `src/dashboardGuard.js:68-84`, `:161-166`, `:206-210`. OpenProxy: `src/server/api/guard.rs:48-62` (dead const), `:145-165`, `src/server/api/mod.rs:303-322`, `src/server/api/mcp.rs:39-42`, `src/server/api/tunnel.rs:45,95,119,176`, `src/server/auth/mod.rs:207-216`
- **Parity fix**: Move `LOCALLY_ONLY_PATHS` out of `#[allow(dead_code)]` into real enforcement: add a `local_only_paths` router in `src/server/api/mod.rs` carrying `require_local_only` over `/api/mcp/{plugin}/*`, `/api/tunnel/enable`, `/api/tunnel/disable`, `/api/tunnel/tailscale-{install,enable,disable,check}`, `/api/cli-tools/cowork-settings`, `/api/cli-tools/antigravity-mitm` and `/api/oauth/{cursor,kiro}/auto-import`, and merge it ahead of the unguarded/remaining tiers. Also emit the 9router message text `"Local only: CLI token required"` so the two error bodies match.

## P131-001 · P1 · OpenProxy `src/server/api/mod.rs` (`UpdateSettingsRequest`) vs `web/src/components/cli-tools/ClaudeToolCard.tsx:179`

- **9router**: `src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/cli-tools/components/ClaudeToolCard.js:137` PATCHes `/api/settings` with `{ccFilterNaming: value}`. Because the settings PATCH is a generic JSON merge (`src/app/api/settings/route.js:66-68` → `updateSettings`), the key persists. The chat path reads it at `src/sse/handlers/chat.js:90` `handleBypassRequest(body, modelStr, userAgent, !!settings.ccFilterNaming)`, which intercepts Claude Code's `isNewTopic` title-extraction calls before they reach a provider.
- **OpenProxy**: `web/src/components/cli-tools/ClaudeToolCard.tsx:179` PATCHes `{ccFilterNaming: value}` through `patchSettings`. `UpdateSettingsRequest` (`src/server/api/mod.rs:2236-2300`) has no `cc_filter_naming` field and the apply block (`:2427-2600`) has no write arm, so serde drops it. The consumer reads `snapshot.settings.extra.get("ccFilterNaming")` at `src/server/api/chat.rs:371-376` — a map that nothing ever writes. The card re-reads `data.ccFilterNaming` on mount (`:172`), which is always undefined, so the checkbox always renders unchecked.
- **Gap**: `ccFilterNaming` is a live toggle on the Claude CLI-tool card in both products, but OpenProxy's fixed-allowlist settings PATCH silently discards it, so the bypass it controls is permanently disabled.
- **Why it matters**: The user ticks the Claude Code naming-request filter, the checkbox flips on, and after any reload it is off again. Meanwhile every Claude Code `isNewTopic` topic-extraction request is forwarded to a paid provider and billed/rate-limited, which is precisely the cost that setting exists to avoid. The failure is invisible because the checkbox gives no error.
- **Evidence**: 9router: `src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/cli-tools/components/ClaudeToolCard.js:137`, `src/app/api/settings/route.js:66-68`, `src/sse/handlers/chat.js:90`. OpenProxy: `web/src/components/cli-tools/ClaudeToolCard.tsx:172,179`, `src/server/api/mod.rs:2236-2300`, `src/server/api/chat.rs:371-376`
- **Parity fix**: Add `cc_filter_naming: Option<bool>` to `UpdateSettingsRequest` and, in the apply block, `if let Some(v) = req.cc_filter_naming { db.settings.extra.insert("ccFilterNaming".into(), v.into()); }` — matching the existing `provider_thinking` / `claude_auto_ping` extra-map pattern at `src/server/api/mod.rs:2590-2600` so the existing read at `chat.rs:371` works unchanged.

## P132-001 · P1 · OpenProxy `src/server/api/v1_models.rs:75-86`

- **9router**: `src/app/api/v1/models/route.js:571-585` — the `GET` handler goes straight to `buildModelsList` with no `requireApiKey` check and returns `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`. Model discovery is always anonymous, even with `requireApiKey: true`.
- **OpenProxy**: `list_models_for_kinds` (reached by `/v1/models`, `/v1/models/{kind}`, `/v1/models/info` — `src/server/api/v1_models.rs:27-35`) gates on `snapshot.settings.require_login` at `:81` and returns 401 `authentication_error` otherwise. Since `require_login` defaults to `true` (`src/types/mod.rs:524` + `:734`), a default OpenProxy install rejects keyless `GET /v1/models`.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy gates `GET /v1/models` behind the API key; 9router never gates model listing at all.
- **Why it matters**: Claude Code, Cline, opencode and other OpenAI-compatible clients frequently probe `/v1/models` with a plain GET before they have configured (or choose to send) a key. On a default OpenProxy install that probe 401s and the client cannot populate its model list; the same client works against 9router.
- **Evidence**: 9router: `src/app/api/v1/models/route.js:571-585`. OpenProxy: `src/server/api/v1_models.rs:27-35,75-86`
- **Parity fix**: Delete the `require_login`/`require_api_key_with_reload` block at `src/server/api/v1_models.rs:81-85` and return the model list with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, matching `src/app/api/v1/models/route.js`.

## P133-001 · P1 · OpenProxy `web/src/components/usage/ProviderLimits/index.tsx:295` vs `src/server/api/mod.rs` (`UpdateSettingsRequest`)

- **9router**: `src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/usage/components/ProviderLimits/index.js:582` PATCHes `/api/settings` with `{quotaVisibility: nextVisibility}`; the generic merge persists it, and the page re-reads it from the same settings blob at `:549` (`setQuotaVisibility(s?.quotaVisibility || {})`). The helpers at `ProviderLimits/utils.js:329-345` filter rows by that map.
- **OpenProxy**: `web/src/components/usage/ProviderLimits/index.tsx:295` calls `useSettingsStore.getState().patchSettings({ quotaVisibility: nextVisibility })`. `UpdateSettingsRequest` (`src/server/api/mod.rs:2236-2300`) has no `quota_visibility` field and the apply block has no arm, so the value is dropped. The page keeps only in-memory state (`index.tsx:177`) and the helpers at `web/src/components/usage/ProviderLimits/utils.ts:271-297` read a `quotaVisibility` argument that is always `{}` after a reload.
- **Gap**: `quotaVisibility` is dropped by OpenProxy's settings PATCH, so per-provider hidden quota rows never persist.
- **Why it matters**: A user hides noisy quota rows in the Quota Tracker; they reappear on every page reload or server restart. 9router keeps the preference indefinitely.
- **Evidence**: 9router: `src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/usage/components/ProviderLimits/index.js:549,582`, `ProviderLimits/utils.js:329-345`. OpenProxy: `web/src/components/usage/ProviderLimits/index.tsx:177,295`, `src/server/api/mod.rs:2236-2300`, `web/src/components/usage/ProviderLimits/utils.ts:271-297`
- **Parity fix**: Add `quota_visibility: Option<Value>` to `UpdateSettingsRequest` and write it to `db.settings.extra` under the key `"quotaVisibility"` so the camelCase name the dashboard reads survives the round-trip.

## P134-001 · P1 · POST /api/providers/validate — anthropic-compatible nodes

- **9router**: Strips a trailing `/messages` from the node baseUrl and POSTs to `${normalizedBase}/v1/messages` (the `/v1` segment is appended explicitly). Validity is `res.status !== 401 && res.status !== 403`.
- **OpenProxy**: Strips the trailing `/messages` but then builds `format!("{}/messages", base_url)` — no `/v1` — and classifies with `code == 401 || code == 403 → invalid; else if status.is_success() || (400..500).contains(&code) && code != 429 → valid (with a body-string auth sniff)`. A 404 therefore lands in the 4xx arm and is reported valid.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy probes `/messages` instead of `/v1/messages` and then treats the resulting 404 as proof the key works.
- **Why it matters**: A user adding an Anthropic-compatible node whose baseUrl is e.g. `https://gateway.internal` is shown "valid" the moment they click validate, because the wrong path 404s and 404 is inside the permissive 4xx band. The node is saved and only fails later on real traffic. 9router would have caught it.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/providers/validate/route.js:148-178 (`${normalizedBase}/v1/messages`, validity at :177). OpenProxy: src/server/api/provider_validate.rs:359-367 (`{}/messages`), :404-412 (4xx-except-429 counts as valid)
- **Parity fix**: Build the probe URL as `format!("{}/v1/messages", base_url)` after the `/messages` strip, and use 9router's criterion — `if code == 401 || code == 403 { invalid } else { valid }` — dropping the 4xx-with-429-exception band and the body-string sniff.

## P135-001 · P1 · POST /api/providers/{id}/test — API-key probe table

- **9router**: `testApiKeyConnection` has explicit arms for vercel-ai-gateway (`https://ai-gateway.vercel.sh/v1/models`), alims-intl (`https://dashscope-intl.aliyuncs.com/compatible-mode/v1/chat/completions`, grouped with alicode/alicode-intl), fal-ai (`Authorization: Key`, `?limit=1`, `!== 401 && !== 403`), xiaomi-mimo (`https://api.xiaomimimo.com/v1/models`), blackbox (`{baseUrl}/models`), qoder PAT (POST `https://openapi.qoder.sh/api/v1/jobToken/exchange` with `Cosy-Version: 1.0.1` / `Cosy-ClientType: 5`, prefixing `pt-` when absent), llm7 (`{baseUrl|https://api.llm7.io/v1}/models`) and kimchi (the shared validationUrl with `User-Agent: kimchi/0.1.40`).
- **OpenProxy**: The `match connection.provider.as_str()` in `test_api_key_connection` covers 42 providers and ends with `_ => invalid("Provider test not supported")` (:763). None of vercel-ai-gateway, alims-intl, fal-ai, xiaomi-mimo, blackbox, qoder, llm7 or kimchi appear anywhere in provider_connection_test.rs (0 grep hits for each), even though all eight are registered in web/src/shared/constants/providers.ts and therefore have a reachable Test button.
- **Gap**: Eight API-key providers that 9router probes have no arm in OpenProxy and fall through to the unsupported branch.
- **Why it matters**: Clicking Test on any of these eight always reports "Provider test not supported" and writes `testStatus: "error"` + `lastError`, which the provider page and the batch grid render as a red badge. The user is told the key is broken when the key was never sent anywhere. qoder is doubly affected: it is dual-auth, so its OAuth path fails via CT-003 and its PAT path fails here.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/providers/[id]/test/testUtils.js:553-556 (vercel-ai-gateway), :612-628 (alims-intl), :712-716 (fal-ai), :763-770 (xiaomi-mimo), :771-777 (blackbox), :778-797 (qoder PAT), :798-804 (llm7), :805-818 (kimchi), :819-820 (the shared `default` arm OpenProxy mirrors). OpenProxy: src/server/api/provider_connection_test.rs:413-764 (arm list), :763 (the `_` arm); provider presence in web/src/shared/constants/providers.ts (vercel-ai-gateway, xiaomi-mimo, fal-ai, blackbox, alims-intl, llm7, qoder:17, kimchi:127)
- **Parity fix**: Add the eight arms, reusing the existing helpers: `simple_get_bearer_test` for vercel-ai-gateway / xiaomi-mimo / llm7 / blackbox, `openai_chat_status_test` for alims-intl, a `simple_get_key_test` variant for fal-ai's `Authorization: Key` prefix with the `!== 401 && !== 403` criterion, and two bespoke branches for qoder's PAT exchange and kimchi's validationUrl probe. Add a table-driven regression test that asserts no id in a "must support" list falls through to the `_` arm.

## P136-001 · P1 · POST /api/providers/{id}/test — anthropic-compatible providers

- **9router**: Strips a trailing `/messages`, builds `${modelsBase}/v1/messages`, and POSTs `{ model: connection.defaultModel || "claude-3-haiku-20240307", max_tokens: 1, messages: [{ role: "user", content: "test" }] }` with `x-api-key`, `anthropic-version: 2023-06-01`, `content-type` and `Authorization: Bearer`. Validity is `res.status !== 401 && res.status !== 403` — the comment states "400/529 still confirms key accepted; only 401/403 = bad key".
- **OpenProxy**: `normalize_anthropic_models_url` strips `/messages` and appends `/models`, and the request is a GET with the same three auth headers and no body. The verdict comes from `compatible_result`, which sets `valid: response.status().is_success()` (2xx only).
- **Gap**: Different method, different path (GET /models instead of POST /v1/messages) and a much stricter success criterion (2xx instead of "not 401/403").
- **Why it matters**: Many self-hosted Anthropic-compatible gateways (LiteLLM proxies, Bedrock/Vertex shims) do not expose `GET /models` and return 404 or 405, so the connection is marked broken even though `/v1/messages` works. Conversely, a gateway that answers `GET /models` with 200 while rejecting `/v1/messages` reports healthy. The 400/529 tolerance 9router deliberately added is lost, so gateways that reject the `max_tokens: 1` probe body are false negatives.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/providers/[id]/test/testUtils.js:489-516 (POST `${base}/v1/messages`, validity at :512). OpenProxy: src/server/api/provider_connection_test.rs:388-411 (GET), :1891-1897 (`normalize_anthropic_models_url` → `/models`), :1988-2005 (`compatible_result` → `is_success()`)
- **Parity fix**: Rewrite the anthropic-compatible branch to POST to `${base.trim_end_matches('/')}/v1/messages` with the `max_tokens: 1` body and the `defaultModel || "claude-3-haiku-20240307"` model, then classify with `valid = status != 401 && status != 403` (add a `compatible_anthropic_result` helper rather than reusing `compatible_result`, which the OpenAI-compatible branch correctly keeps at strict 2xx to match testUtils.js:483).

## P137-001 · P1 · POST /api/providers/{id}/test — codex 400 acceptance

- **9router**: codex declares `acceptStatuses: [400]` with the comment "400 (bad request) means auth succeeded; only 401/403 means token is bad". `classifyOAuthProbeResult` computes `const accepted = res.ok || config.acceptStatuses.includes(status)` — a codex 400 is accepted unconditionally, with no body inspection.
- **OpenProxy**: `status.is_success() || matches!(provider, "codex" if status == 400 && (body_text.contains("input") || body_text.contains("validation") || body_text.contains("must")))`. The body is read and the 400 is only accepted when it contains one of three English substrings.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy adds a body-content predicate to codex's 400 acceptance that 9router does not have.
- **Why it matters**: Any codex 400 whose message differs (localized text, a schema error naming a different field, a proxy/WAF page with a JSON body) is reported as "API returned 400" and flips the connection to `testStatus: "error"` even though the token is fine. This is the single most-used OAuth connection, so the false negative is highly visible.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/providers/[id]/test/testUtils.js:32-36 (`acceptStatuses: [400]`), :137-159 (`classifyOAuthProbeResult`, acceptance at :140). OpenProxy: src/server/api/provider_connection_test.rs:326-334
- **Parity fix**: Change the guard to `status.is_success() || (connection.provider == "codex" && status.as_u16() == 400)`. If the body-text heuristic is wanted as a fallback, keep it only for providers that declare it, not for codex.

## P138-001 · P1 · POST /api/providers/{id}/test — codex probe identity headers

- **9router**: Probe headers are `Content-Type: application/json`, `originator: codex_cli_rs`, and `User-Agent: codex_cli_rs/${CODEX_CLI_VERSION}` where the version comes from the registry (`CODEX_CLI_VERSION = "0.154.0"`, open-sse/providers/registry/codex.js:5) and the same pair is declared in the codex transport headers (registry/codex.js:43-45). No `session_id` header is sent on the probe.
- **OpenProxy**: Probe headers are `Content-Type: application/json`, `originator: codex_cli_rs`, an extra `session_id: default`, and `User-Agent: codex-cli/1.0.18 (macOS; arm64)`.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy sends a different CLI identity User-Agent and an extra `session_id` header on the codex probe.
- **Why it matters**: chatgpt.com's codex backend gates on the CLI identity. A User-Agent the backend does not recognise can return 403 instead of the deliberately-provoked 400, and CT-005's gate would then turn that into "Access denied" / a red connection. The extra `session_id` makes the probe a different request shape than 9router's.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/providers/[id]/test/testUtils.js:31 (`User-Agent: codex_cli_rs/${CODEX_CLI_VERSION}`), .tmp/9router/open-sse/providers/registry/codex.js:5 and :43-45. OpenProxy: src/server/api/provider_connection_test.rs:1580-1586
- **Parity fix**: Set `User-Agent: codex_cli_rs/0.154.0` (hoist the version into a `CODEX_CLI_VERSION` const next to the other client-id/URL consts at provider_connection_test.rs:27-40) and drop the `session_id: default` header from the probe.

## P139-001 · P1 · POST /api/providers/{id}/test — gemini-cli / antigravity probe target

- **9router**: Before the generic probe, `testOAuthConnection` special-cases these two providers and calls `probeCloudCodeAssistAccess`, which POSTs `{ metadata: { ideType, platform, pluginType: "GEMINI" } }` to `https://cloudcode-pa.googleapis.com/v1internal:loadCodeAssist` with a provider-specific User-Agent (`google-api-nodejs-client/9.15.1 gemini-cli/0.34.0` / `... vscode-antigravity/1.107.0`). The `userinfo` URL in `OAUTH_TEST_CONFIG` is dead code for these providers. A 401 there triggers a refresh-and-retry.
- **OpenProxy**: `oauth_probe_request` returns `GET https://www.googleapis.com/oauth2/v1/userinfo?alt=json` with only `Authorization: Bearer {token}`. No Cloud Code Assist probe, no 401-refresh-retry for these two providers.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy validates the Google OAuth token against Google's identity endpoint instead of the Cloud Code Assist service these providers actually call.
- **Why it matters**: The two checks prove different things. A Google account that is not provisioned for Gemini CLI / Antigravity (Code Assist returns 403) reports "active" in OpenProxy but "error" in 9router; a Google token whose Code Assist grant was revoked is still reported active. The user gets a green tick and then the first real chat request 403s.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/providers/[id]/test/testUtils.js:353-368 (dispatch to the Cloud Code Assist probe), :172-217 (`probeCloudCodeAssistAccess` + body/UA), :357-365 (401 → refresh → retry). OpenProxy: src/server/api/provider_connection_test.rs:1595-1603
- **Parity fix**: Add a `"gemini-cli" | "antigravity"` branch to `test_oauth_connection` ahead of the generic probe that POSTs the same metadata body to `CLOUD_CODE_ASSIST_TEST_URL`, with the per-provider User-Agent, and reuses the existing 401-refresh-retry shape already implemented for cline (provider_connection_test.rs:275-312). Keep the userinfo URL only for the generic path.

## P140-001 · P1 · POST /api/providers/{id}/test — soft-success persistence

- **9router**: `classifyOAuthProbeResult` distinguishes a third outcome: `accepted = res.ok || acceptStatuses.includes(status)`, and when the status is in `acceptStatuses` but has a `softFailMessage` entry it returns `{ valid: true, error: <message>, soft: true }`. `testSingleConnection` then computes `const softWarning = result.valid && (result.warning || result.error)` and persists `testStatus: "active"`, `lastError: softWarning`, `lastErrorAt: now` — i.e. the connection stays green but carries a warning. grok-cli uses this for HTTP 402 ("Connected, but Grok Build credits are exhausted").
- **OpenProxy**: `persist_test_result` writes `last_error = None` and `last_error_at = None` whenever `result.valid` is true, and `ConnectionTestResult` has no soft/warning field. There is no code path that can produce `valid: true` with a non-null `error`.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy has no soft-success channel: any warning attached to a valid result is silently dropped.
- **Why it matters**: The provider page has no way to show "connected but out of credits". Once the grok-cli probe from CT-003 lands, an out-of-credits Grok Build account would either be reported dead (forcing a pointless re-auth) or, if the 402 is naively accepted, reported clean with no indication that every request will fail on billing.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/providers/[id]/test/testUtils.js:122-128 (grok-cli 402 config), :147-156 (soft classification), :860-872 (softWarning persistence). OpenProxy: src/server/api/provider_connection_test.rs:169-176 (`persist_test_result` clears last_error on valid)
- **Parity fix**: Add `soft_error: Option<String>` to `ConnectionTestResult` and a `soft_fail` table on the probe config. In `persist_test_result`, use 9router's exact rule: `let soft = result.valid && (result.soft_error.is_some() || result.error.is_some()); last_error = if result.valid { soft_error.clone().or(error.clone()) } else { error.clone() }; last_error_at = if result.valid { soft.then(|| now) } else { Some(now) };` while keeping `test_status = "active"` whenever `valid`.

## P141-001 · P1 · POST /api/providers/{id}/test — tokenExists short-circuit

- **9router**: `OAUTH_TEST_CONFIG["codebuddy-cn"] = { tokenExists: true }`; `testOAuthConnection` returns `{ valid: true }` immediately when `config.tokenExists` is set, because the provider has no cheap identity endpoint.
- **OpenProxy**: `if connection.provider == "cursor" || connection.provider == "codebuddy"` — the id is `codebuddy`, but the real provider ids are `codebuddy-cn` and `codebuddy-intl` (web/src/shared/constants/providers.ts:129-130), so the comparison never matches.
- **Gap**: The tokenExists short-circuit is keyed on a provider id that does not exist in the registry.
- **Why it matters**: Both CodeBuddy connections fall through to `oauth_probe_request` → `None` → "Provider test not supported" → `testStatus: "error"`, identical to CT-003 but from a different root cause (a typo rather than a missing arm), so it will survive a naive "add the missing arms" fix.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/providers/[id]/test/testUtils.js:94 (`"codebuddy-cn": { tokenExists: true }`), :327-329 (the tokenExists branch). OpenProxy: src/server/api/provider_connection_test.rs:266 (`connection.provider == "codebuddy"`), web/src/shared/constants/providers.ts:129-130 (actual ids)
- **Parity fix**: Replace the string comparison with `matches!(connection.provider.as_str(), "cursor" | "codebuddy-cn" | "codebuddy-intl")`. Add a regression test that constructs a `ProviderConnection { provider: "codebuddy-cn", access_token: Some("x".into()), .. }` and asserts `valid == true`.

## P142-001 · P1 · POST /v1/videos/* and GET /v1/videos/{id} — all-accounts-unavailable response

- **9router**: When `getProviderCredentials` returns `allRateLimited`, the handler computes `status = lastStatus || Number(credentials.lastErrorCode) || HTTP_STATUS.SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE` and returns `unavailableResponse(status, "[provider/model] <error>", credentials.retryAfter, credentials.retryAfterHuman)`. `unavailableResponse` emits `Retry-After: <max(ceil((retryAfter-now)/1000),1)>` and a body of `{error:{message:"<msg> (<human>)"}}` with the upstream status (401/403/429) or 503. A genuinely-empty credential set returns 400 `No credentials for provider: X`.
- **OpenProxy**: `if connections.is_empty() { return json_error_response(StatusCode::BAD_REQUEST, &format!("No credentials for provider: {provider}")) }`. There is no `Retry-After` header, no `retryAfterHuman` text, and no distinction between "you have no accounts" and "every account is currently in cooldown". `video_get_handler_with_query` returns the same 400 via `select_video_connection`'s `ok_or_else`.
- **Gap**: When no account is currently usable, OpenProxy returns a flat 400 `No credentials for provider: X` with no `Retry-After`, where 9Router returns the real upstream status (or 503) plus a `Retry-After` header and a human-readable cooldown hint.
- **Why it matters**: A client (or the 9router CLI one-shot flow) that respects `Retry-After` will correctly pause and resume; against OpenProxy it sees a 400, which SDKs treat as a permanent bad request and do not retry, so a transient all-accounts-cooldown moment surfaces to the user as an unrecoverable error. The 400 also mislabels a capacity problem as a configuration problem.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/sse/handlers/videoGeneration.js:137-146 (allRateLimited -> unavailableResponse at :141); .tmp/9router/open-sse/utils/error.js:116-133 (unavailableResponse sets Retry-After and status). OpenProxy: src/server/api/media.rs:1292-1297 (create: 400 "No credentials for provider: {provider}"), :2361-2366 (poll: same 400).
- **Parity fix**: Add a `select_video_connections_with_locks` that returns `(available, all_rate_limited: Option<(retry_after, retry_after_human, last_error, last_error_code)>)`, mirroring `auth.js:112-125`. When the second is populated, return the upstream/503 status with a `Retry-After` header and the `"<msg> (<human>)"` body, exactly as `unavailableResponse` does; keep the 400 `No credentials for provider: X` only for the genuinely-empty case.

## P143-001 · P1 · POST /v1/videos/edits and /v1/videos/extensions — non-JSON (raw passthrough) path

- **9router**: `handleVideoCreate` is uniform for JSON and multipart. For every attempt it reads `x-connection-id` as `preferredConnectionId`, calls `getProviderCredentials(provider, excludeConnectionIds, model, { preferredConnectionId })` inside a `while (true)` loop, calls `checkAndRefreshToken` before the POST, and after a failure calls `markAccountUnavailable` and `continue`s the loop (adding the connection to `excludeConnectionIds`) when `shouldFallback && CREATE_ROTATION_STATUSES.has(result.status)`. `handleVideoProxyCore` additionally does the 401/403 refresh-and-single-retry for the raw body too.
- **OpenProxy**: `video_forward_raw` picks ONE connection with `.filter(|c| c.provider == provider && c.is_active()).min_by_key(|c| c.priority.unwrap_or(999))` — it never reads `x-connection-id`, never calls a token pre-refresh, and issues exactly one `client.post(&url)` whose `Err` arm returns 502. There is no retry-status set, no account rotation, and no 401/403 refresh path in this function at all.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's raw multipart edits/extensions path has no `x-connection-id` pin, no account rotation on 401/403/429, and no 401/403 token-refresh retry, all of which 9Router applies uniformly to the same requests.
- **Why it matters**: A multipart edit/extension sent to an OAuth xAI account whose access token has expired fails with an unrecoverable 401 in OpenProxy, where 9Router refreshes the token and retries. With two xAI accounts where the highest-priority one is rate-limited, OpenProxy returns the 429 to the caller instead of rotating to the healthy account. Because video jobs are billable, callers cannot safely retry the create themselves, so the request is lost rather than merely slowed.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/sse/handlers/videoGeneration.js:127 (preferredConnectionId = request.headers.get("x-connection-id")); :134-147 (credential loop + allRateLimited branch); :149 (checkAndRefreshToken); :151-168 (handleVideoProxyCore with rawBody); :177-186 (markAccountUnavailable + rotate on CREATE_ROTATION_STATUSES); :38-42 (CREATE_ROTATION_STATUSES = {401,403,429}); .tmp/9router/open-sse/handlers/videoCore.js:150-177 (401/403 refresh + one retry). OpenProxy: src/server/api/media.rs:334-405 — selection at :343-348, single POST at :369-375, network-error arm at :392-397; no pin/rotation/refresh anywhere in the function.
- **Parity fix**: Fold `video_forward_raw` into the same rotation loop used by `video_create_handler`: read `x-connection-id` for the pin, iterate `select_media_connections(&snapshot, provider)` with an exclude set, add `[401,403,429]` as the rotation set, and on a 401/403 with a `refresh_token` call `refresh_media_connection` + persist + re-fire the identical raw bytes once. Keep the never-retry-on-network-error rule for the raw path too.

## P144-001 · P1 · POST /v1/videos/generations (and /v1/video/generations, /v1/v1/...)

- **9router**: All three create actions share one handler. `readForwardableBody` reads `content-type`; when it does NOT include `application/json` it does `const buf = Buffer.from(await request.arrayBuffer())` and returns `{raw: buf, parsed: null, contentType}` — the exact bytes plus the original Content-Type. `handleVideoCreate` then resolves the provider from the (absent) parsed body and forwards the Buffer untouched, so a multipart POST to `/v1/videos/generations` is proxied byte-for-byte to `{xaiVideoBase}/generations`.
- **OpenProxy**: `video_generations` takes `body: Result<Json<Value>, JsonRejection>`. `let Json(mut body) = match body_result { Ok(b)=>b, Err(_)=> return json_error_response(StatusCode::BAD_REQUEST, "Invalid JSON body") }`. axum's `Json` rejects any non-`application/json` body, so a multipart POST to `/v1/videos/generations` returns 400 `{"error":{"message":"Invalid JSON body",...}}` and never reaches the upstream. Only `/v1/videos/edits` and `/v1/videos/extensions` were given the raw-body split (`video_edits_extensions_proxy`); `/v1/videos/generations` was not.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's `/v1/videos/generations` is JSON-only and 400s any multipart body, while 9Router forwards multipart bodies byte-for-byte on that same route.
- **Why it matters**: Image-to-video / file-upload clients that POST `multipart/form-data` to `/v1/videos/generations` get a hard 400 and can never create a job. Because edits/extensions accept multipart but generations does not, the same client works on two routes and fails on the third, which is a request-flow break rather than a cosmetic difference.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/v1/videos/generations/route.js:14-16 (POST -> handleVideoCreate(request,"generations")); .tmp/9router/src/sse/handlers/videoGeneration.js:60-76 (readForwardableBody: JSON parse OR Buffer.from(await request.arrayBuffer())); :109-118; :122-125 (forwardBody = bodyInfo.raw untouched for non-JSON). OpenProxy: src/server/api/media.rs:271-277 (pub async fn video_generations -> body: Result<Json<Value>, JsonRejection>); :1189-1192 (Err(_) => 400 "Invalid JSON body"); src/server/api/mod.rs:224-230 (route wiring). Contrast: the raw path exists only for edits/extensions at src/server/api/media.rs:301-330.
- **Parity fix**: Route `/v1/videos/generations` (and the `/v1/v1/*`, `/v1/video/*` aliases) through `video_edits_extensions_proxy(state, headers, request, "generations")` instead of `video_generations`, or generalise that helper to sniff Content-Type itself and call it from all three create handlers. That helper already preserves the exact bytes for non-JSON and only re-serializes the JSON path when the provider prefix was stripped, which is exactly 9router's `readForwardableBody` semantics.

## P145-001 · P1 · Per-request OAuth token refresh (pre-dispatch)

- **9router**: Every request that selects credentials calls `checkAndRefreshToken(provider, credentials)` at `src/sse/handlers/chat.js:253` BEFORE dispatch. `checkAndRefreshToken` (`src/sse/services/tokenRefresh.js:224-273`) proactively refreshes when `_shouldRefreshCredentials` is true (expiry within the provider lead), persists via `updateProviderCredentials`, merges the new expiry, and fires a non-blocking `_refreshProjectId`. It also has a dedicated arm for the GitHub `copilotToken`/`copilotTokenExpiresAt` pair. So a token that is 3 minutes from expiry is refreshed before the request goes out, not after it 401s.
