- **OpenProxy**: No per-request pre-dispatch refresh exists. `forward_with_provider_fallback` (`src/server/api/chat.rs:1520+`) selects a connection and dispatches immediately; the ONLY refresh on this path is the reactive 401/403 arm at `:2806-2854`. `ProviderExecutor::needs_refresh` exists (`src/core/executor/provider.rs:299`, `:618`) but is never called from `chat.rs` or `default.rs` (grep for `.needs_refresh(` in both returns nothing), and the GitHub `copilotToken` expiry has no equivalent arm. Proactive coverage is delegated entirely to the background scheduler.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy has no per-request pre-dispatch token refresh; it relies solely on the reactive 401/403 arm plus a 5-minute background tick, where 9router refreshes proactively on every credential selection.
- **Why it matters**: Every request whose token expires inside the request window costs a full wasted round trip: dispatch → upstream 401 → refresh → re-dispatch on a loop iteration that had already called `mark_connection_unavailable` at `chat.rs:2780`. For a user whose Claude/Codex token sits near expiry, this doubles latency on that request and marks a healthy account errored in the DB, which can then cooldown-lock it. The gap is worst on a server that has been running for a while: the background tick's 30-minute lead bounds staleness to 30 minutes, but a token whose `expires_at` is rewritten to a shorter life (or a connection imported with a stale expiry) can be dispatched expired and pay the full penalty on every request until the next tick corrects it.
- **Evidence**: 9router: `.tmp/9router/src/sse/handlers/chat.js:253` (`checkAndRefreshToken` call), `.tmp/9router/src/sse/services/tokenRefresh.js:224-273` (proactive body + GitHub copilot arm). OpenProxy: `src/server/api/chat.rs:1520-1530` (loop entry, no pre-refresh), `:2806-2854` (reactive-only refresh); unused trait method at `src/core/executor/provider.rs:299` and `:618`.
- **Parity fix**: Inside the dispatch loop in `forward_with_provider_fallback`, immediately after a connection is selected and before the executor runs, gate a proactive refresh on the same predicate 9router uses: for OAuth connections with a `refresh_token`, if `crate::oauth::token_refresh::should_refresh_credentials(provider, &connection.expires_at, connection.last_refresh_at.as_deref(), true, lead_ms)` then call `dispatch_oauth_refresh`, persist the merged token/expires_at/refresh_token, and `continue` to re-select from the fresh snapshot (the `refreshed_this_request` set already prevents a refresh loop). Add a second arm for `provider == "github"` that refreshes `providerSpecificData.copilotToken` when `copilotTokenExpiresAt * 1000 - now < TOKEN_EXPIRY_BUFFER_MS`, matching `tokenRefresh.js:273+`.

## P146-001 · P1 · Provider node validation — custom embedding

- **9router**: The custom-embedding branch issues POST {base}/embeddings with {model: modelId, input: "ping"} and, on success, parses the response to report dimensions (the length of data[0].embedding). A 401/403 is reported as API key unauthorized; any other failure includes the upstream body truncated to 200 chars.
- **OpenProxy**: The custom-embedding branch calls the generic test_url(embed_url, api_key, Some("embedding"), model_id), and test_url always issues client.get(url), a GET with no request body. The dimensions field is hardcoded to None with a comment that it would need the response body.
- **Gap**: Custom-embedding nodes are validated with GET /embeddings instead of POST, and the embedding dimension count is never returned.
- **Why it matters**: OpenAI-compatible embedding endpoints only implement POST /embeddings; a GET returns 405, so every custom-embedding node validates as invalid and the user cannot create the node. When a lenient endpoint does answer the GET, the caller still gets dimensions null, so the dashboard cannot show the vector size it shows in 9router.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/provider-nodes/validate/route.js:79-105 (POST /embeddings plus dims from data.data[0].embedding.length). OpenProxy: src/server/api/providers.rs:233-235 (the test_url call) and :830-876 (test_url builds client.get at :850); dims hardcoded None at :238.
- **Parity fix**: Add a dedicated embedding probe that POSTs {model, input:"ping"} to {base}/embeddings with the bearer header, returns valid:true with the parsed dimensions on 2xx, API key unauthorized on 401/403, and a status-plus-body message otherwise. Leave test_url as the GET probe for the /models path.

## P147-001 · P1 · Provider status machine — 400 cooldown duration

- **9router**: `ERROR_RULES` has no 400 status rule. A 400 whose text matches none of the eight text needles falls through to the default `return { shouldFallback: true, cooldownMs: TRANSIENT_COOLDOWN_MS }` = 30 000 ms.
- **OpenProxy**: `classify_error` adds a post-rule `Permanent` branch for statuses 400/401/402/403, and `check_fallback_error` maps `Permanent` to `LONG_COOLDOWN` = 120 s.
- **Gap**: An unmatched 400 locks the account for 120 s in OpenProxy and 30 s in 9router — 4x longer.
- **Why it matters**: `cache_control budget exceeded` and other client-shaped 400s (the exact case the OpenProxy comment cites) take the account out of rotation four times longer, so a subsequent valid request on the same provider is routed to a different account (or 503s when it is the only one).
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/config/errorConfig.js:59-79 (no 400 rule) + .tmp/9router/open-sse/services/accountFallback.js:49. OpenProxy: src/core/config/error_config.rs:248-254 (Permanent branch), src/core/combo/mod.rs:26 (`LONG_COOLDOWN = 120s`) and :597-601.
- **Parity fix**: Either drop 400 from the `Permanent` match at error_config.rs:251 (leaving it to `NoMatch` → `TRANSIENT_COOLDOWN_MS`, 30 s), or return a distinct `Permanent(30s)` variant. Note that 401/402/403 are already matched by explicit `ERROR_RULES` entries before the `Permanent` branch, so removing 400 from it changes nothing else.

## P148-001 · P1 · Provider status machine — 404 handling

- **9router**: There is no 404 special case. `markAccountUnavailable` is called once; `checkFallbackError(404, …)` matches the `{ status: 404, cooldownMs: COOLDOWN.long }` rule → 120 000 ms, writes the lock once, leaves `backoffLevel` unchanged, and the caller then excludes the connection and loops.
- **OpenProxy**: A dedicated 404 arm writes a 300 s lock, then execution FALLS THROUGH (no `continue`) to the generic `if decision.should_fallback` arm, which calls `mark_connection_unavailable` a second time for the same connection+model, overwriting the just-written 300 s lock with 120 s and passing `current_backoff + 1` as the backoff level.
- **Gap**: The 300 s model-lock branch is dead code that is immediately overwritten, and a plain 404 escalates the backoff level.
- **Why it matters**: Every 404 performs two DB writes and two `consecutive_errors` increments instead of one, the 5-minute intent is replaced by 2 minutes, and the connection's backoff level climbs from a client-side model error — feeding the runaway escalation described in P0-004.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/sse/services/auth.js:239-277 (single write) + .tmp/9router/open-sse/config/errorConfig.js:76 (`{ status: 404, cooldownMs: COOLDOWN.long }`). OpenProxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:2778-2789 (300 s arm, no `continue`) and :2878-2892 (second write, `unwrap_or(current_backoff + 1)` at :2888).
- **Parity fix**: Delete the standalone 404 arm at chat.rs:2778-2789 and let the single generic write handle 404 (it already resolves to 120 s via ERROR_RULES), matching 9router's one-write path.

## P149-001 · P1 · Provider status machine — OAuth probe coverage

- **9router**: `OAUTH_TEST_CONFIG` probes every OAuth provider, including grok-cli (`/v1/user`, with `acceptStatuses: [402]` and a `softFailMessage` so an out-of-credits 402 is a SOFT success: `valid: true`, `testStatus: "active"`, `lastError` carries the warning), qoder (`/api/v1/userinfo`, deliberately `refreshable: false` and no `checkExpiry` so revoked tokens surface), and codebuddy-cn / cursor via the `tokenExists` shortcut. `classifyOAuthProbeResult` is the shared classifier.
- **OpenProxy**: `oauth_probe_request` matches only codex, gemini-cli, antigravity, github, iflow, kilocode, gitlab. `is_check_expiry_provider` covers claude/qwen/kiro/kimi and the `tokenExists` shortcut only covers `cursor` and the literal string `codebuddy` (not `codebuddy-cn`). grok-cli, qoder, codebuddy-cn and codebuddy-intl therefore fall to `None => invalid("Provider test not supported")` → `valid: false` → `testStatus: "error"`.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy marks healthy grok-cli / qoder / codebuddy-cn / codebuddy-intl accounts as `error`; 9router never leaves them in that state.
- **Why it matters**: Clicking "Test" on a working Grok CLI or Qoder account turns its badge red with the message "Provider test not supported", and an out-of-credits Grok Build account (a 402, credentials still good) is reported as broken instead of active-with-warning. There is no soft-success/warning channel at all — `persist_test_result` collapses every valid result to `last_error = None`.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/providers/[id]/test/testUtils.js:63-72 (qoder), :94 (`codebuddy-cn: { tokenExists: true }`), :107-124 (grok-cli + acceptStatuses/softFailMessage), :132-156 (`classifyOAuthProbeResult`), :861-872 (soft-warning persist). OpenProxy: src/server/api/provider_connection_test.rs:1570-1649 (`oauth_probe_request`), :266 (`cursor`/`codebuddy`), :1935-1940 (`is_check_expiry_provider`), :356/:763 (`invalid("Provider test not supported")`), :148-176 (`persist_test_result`, no warning field).
- **Parity fix**: Port `OAUTH_TEST_CONFIG` + `classifyOAuthProbeResult`: add the grok-cli `/v1/user` probe with `acceptStatuses:[402]`, the qoder `/api/v1/userinfo` probe, and `tokenExists` for cursor/codebuddy-cn/codebuddy-intl; add a `warning: Option<String>` to `ConnectionTestResult` and, on a soft success, write `test_status = "active"` with `last_error = Some(warning)` (and `last_error_at = now`), matching 9router's `softWarning` branch.

## P150-001 · P1 · Provider status machine — partial success

- **9router**: `clearAccountError` computes `remainingActiveLocks` and only resets `testStatus` / `lastError` / `errorCode` / `backoffLevel` when there are none. If a different model is still locked, the connection deliberately STAYS `unavailable` with its error text so the operator can see the live cooldown.
- **OpenProxy**: `clear_connection_error_for_model` unconditionally clears `last_error`, `last_error_at`, `error_code`, `backoff_level`, `consecutive_errors` and `test_status`, and then calls `retain_lock_after_success` — which correctly preserves the still-active locks of other models, but the error state is wiped regardless.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy permits the `Unavailable → healthy` transition even when other model locks are still active; 9router does not.
- **Why it matters**: After a 429 on model B, a success on model A turns the badge green and removes the error text in OpenProxy while model B remains locked and unroutable. The dashboard now hides a live cooldown that 9router would still surface.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/sse/services/auth.js:317-333. OpenProxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:3413-3437 with `retain_lock_after_success` at :3383-3410.
- **Parity fix**: In `clear_connection_error_for_model`, first compute whether any `modelLock_*` key survives `retain_lock_after_success`; only when the retained set is empty set `test_status`/`last_error`/`last_error_at`/`error_code`/`backoff_level` to the cleared values, exactly as `remainingActiveLocks.length === 0` does in 9router.

## P151-001 · P1 · Provider status machine — success terminal state

- **9router**: `clearAccountError` ends with `if (remainingActiveLocks.length === 0) Object.assign(clearObj, { testStatus: "active", lastError: null, errorCode: null, lastErrorAt: null, backoffLevel: 0 })`. The success terminal state is the literal string `"active"`.
- **OpenProxy**: `clear_connection_error_for_model` writes `connection.test_status = None`. (The sibling `web_fetch.rs` version does the same.) The success terminal state is "field absent", which the UI renders as `Unknown`.
- **Gap**: The success transition lands on a different state: 9router `active`, OpenProxy `None`.
- **Why it matters**: The providers grid counts only `testStatus === "active" || "success"` as connected and uses that count for both the "N Connected" badge and the `sortByPriority` ordering. A working account drops out of both the badge count and the sort after its first successful request. The per-connection badge also flips from a green `active` to a grey `Unknown`.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/sse/services/auth.js:326-334. OpenProxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:3434, src/server/api/web_fetch.rs:781. Consumers: web/src/components/providers/ProvidersPageClient.tsx:294-297 + :512-513 + :554-555, web/src/components/providers/ConnectionRow.tsx:198-214.
- **Parity fix**: Change `connection.test_status = None;` to `connection.test_status = Some("active".to_string());` in both `src/server/api/chat.rs:3434` and `src/server/api/web_fetch.rs:781`, keeping the selective lock retention.

## P152-001 · P1 · Quota page — account status filter + pagination (ProviderLimits)

- **9router**: The accounts toolbar has a native `<select aria-label="Filter accounts by status">` (ProviderLimits/index.js:923-938) bound to `accountFilter` (:150) and posted as `accountStatus` (:182). Below the table there is a full pagination block: `aria-label="Accounts per page"` select (:1339), a free-text `aria-label="Custom accounts per page"` input whose `onKeyDown` (:1366-1377) and `onBlur` both parse/clamp the value and reset to page 1, plus `aria-label="Previous accounts page"` (:1404) and `aria-label="Next accounts page"` (:1423).
- **OpenProxy**: ACCOUNT_FILTER_OPTIONS is exported from web/src/components/usage/ProviderLimits/utils.ts:13 but is never imported or rendered; there is no `accountFilter` state, no status `<select>`, and no pagination block at all — the component renders every connection in one unbounded list. The only filter is the provider dropdown (`aria-haspopup="menu"` at ProviderLimits/index.tsx:868) and the Codex quota-sort select (:939).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's quota page dropped the keyboard-reachable 'Filter accounts by status' select, the page-size select, the custom page-size input with Enter-to-apply, and the prev/next page buttons that 9router renders.
- **Why it matters**: With many provider connections the OpenProxy quota list is an unbounded scroll with no way to narrow to active/inactive accounts and no way to page. Keyboard users lose three native, arrow-key-operable controls (two `<select>`s plus the number input's Enter/blur commit) that 9router exposes, and the page-size affordance that keeps the rendered list bounded.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/usage/components/ProviderLimits/index.js:150,182,923,933,1339,1366,1379,1404,1423 || openproxy: web/src/components/usage/ProviderLimits/index.tsx (no accountFilter/pagination render) + web/src/components/usage/ProviderLimits/utils.ts:13 (dead export)
- **Parity fix**: Port the toolbar select verbatim: add `const [accountFilter, setAccountFilter] = useState("all")`, include `accountStatus: accountFilter` in the fetch params, and render `<select value={accountFilter} onChange={...} aria-label="Filter accounts by status">{ACCOUNT_FILTER_OPTIONS.map(...)}</select>`. Then port the pagination footer (ACCOUNT_PAGE_SIZE_OPTIONS select with `aria-label="Accounts per page"`, the custom-size input with the same onKeyDown/onBlur Enter-clamp logic and `aria-label="Custom accounts per page"`, and the prev/next buttons with their aria-labels), wired to page/pageSize state via the already-present `paginate()` helper in utils.ts.

## P153-001 · P1 · Re-enable a cooled-down connection (reconnect after 429 / key rotation)

- **9router**: Every write goes through resetHealthStateOnActivation(existing, patch). When patch.testStatus === "active" it forces testStatus active, nulls lastError/lastErrorAt (or takes the caller's value), nulls errorCode and rateLimitedUntil, resets backoffLevel to 0, and nulls every modelLock_ key on the existing row. It is applied on both createProviderConnection (:172) and updateProviderConnection (:222).
- **OpenProxy**: update_provider writes test_status, last_error and last_error_at verbatim and never touches rate_limited_until, backoff_level, error_code or any modelLock_ entry. EditConnectionModal sends {testStatus:"active", lastError:null, lastErrorAt:null} after a successful re-validation, which clears the visible error but nothing else.
- **Gap**: Setting a connection back to testStatus active no longer clears the rate-limit cooldown, the backoff level, or the per-model locks that actually gate routing.
- **Why it matters**: After a 429, apply_error_state sets rate_limited_until (up to 30 min) and backoff_level, and is_account_unavailable still returns true on that field. The user pastes a fresh working key, validation succeeds, the badge flips to active, but the account is still excluded from rotation and the cooldown chip keeps ticking until the original cooldown expires naturally. In 9router the same edit immediately returns the account to service.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/lib/db/repos/connectionsRepo.js:15-33 (resetHealthStateOnActivation, including rateLimitedUntil: null, backoffLevel: 0, and nulling every modelLock_ key), applied at :172 and :222. OpenProxy: src/server/api/admin_items.rs:279-287 (test_status/last_error/last_error_at written as-is), src/core/account_fallback/mod.rs:603-609 (is_account_unavailable still reads rate_limited_until), :750-775 (apply_error_state sets rate_limited_until and backoff_level).
- **Parity fix**: Replicate resetHealthStateOnActivation inside the admin_items.rs update closure: when the incoming testStatus is active, also set last_error = None, last_error_at = None, error_code = None, rate_limited_until = None, backoff_level = Some(0), and remove every extra key starting with modelLock_. Apply the same normalization in the OAuth connection-upsert path.

## P154-001 · P1 · Response header naming and poll-pinning for /v1/videos/*

- **9router**: `withConnectionHeader` sets `headers.set("x-9router-connection-id", String(connectionId))` on the create response and on the poll response. `skills/9router-video/SKILL.md` documents this as the client contract: the create response carries `x-9router-connection-id: <id>` and "Jobs are account-bound upstream: poll with the same connection that created the job (`x-connection-id` header, value from the create response's `x-9router-connection-id`)". Poll reads only `x-connection-id`.
- **OpenProxy**: Every video response inserts `x-openproxy-connection-id` (media.rs:402, 1446, 1477, 1628, 2272). Poll-time selection accepts `x-connection-id` and `x-openproxy-connection-id` (media.rs:2343-2344) and the provider resolver accepts only `x-connection-id` (media.rs:1794) — `x-9router-connection-id` is accepted nowhere.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy renamed the create/poll response header to `x-openproxy-connection-id` and does not accept the canonical `x-9router-connection-id`, so a client following 9Router's documented contract reads no connection id and polls against the wrong account.
- **Why it matters**: Video jobs are account-bound upstream. A client that follows the documented contract gets `undefined` for the id, polls `/v1/videos/{id}` with no pin, and OpenProxy falls back to the highest-priority connection — which may not be the account that created the job. Upstream then returns not-found/unauthorized for a job that actually exists and is still rendering, and the client reports a hard failure. This is a compatibility break on a published contract, even though OpenProxy is internally self-consistent for clients written against its own header.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/sse/handlers/videoGeneration.js:97-104 (`headers.set("x-9router-connection-id", ...)`), :173 and :231 (applied on create/poll), :203 (poll reads `x-connection-id`); .tmp/9router/skills/9router-video/SKILL.md:44, :52, :73. OpenProxy: src/server/api/media.rs:1474-1478 (create), :1625-1629 (poll), :399-403 (raw path), :2269-2273 (vertex) — all `x-openproxy-connection-id`; :2342-2347 and :1793-1798 — accepted pin headers omit `x-9router-connection-id`.
- **Parity fix**: Emit BOTH `x-9router-connection-id` and `x-openproxy-connection-id` on every video response (one `HeaderMap::insert` each, at media.rs:402/1446/1477/1628/2272), and accept `x-9router-connection-id` as a third alias in `select_video_connection` (media.rs:2343) and `resolve_video_get_provider` (media.rs:1794). This is exactly the interop fix already recorded in docs/parity-9router-impl.md:796 and docs/parity-9router-FULL.md:2005, which has not been implemented.

## P155-001 · P1 · Settings → Single Sign-On (SSO) → OIDC save validation

- **9router**: saveOidcSettings guards on all three credential fields before enabling OIDC: `if (authMode !== "password" && (!issuerUrl || !clientId || !secret) && !settings.oidcConfigured) { setOidcStatus({ type: "error", message: "Issuer URL, client ID, and client secret are required to enable OIDC." }); return; }` (profile/page.js:357-360). The `secret` is `oidcClientSecret.trim()` (profile/page.js:355) and is only included in the PATCH payload when non-empty (profile/page.js:375-377), so an already-configured provider can be re-saved without retyping the secret.
- **OpenProxy**: saveOidcSettings drops `secret` from the guard entirely: `if (authMode !== "password" && (!issuerUrl || !clientId) && !settings.oidcConfigured) { setOidcStatus({ type: "error", message: "Issuer URL and client ID are required to enable OIDC." }); return; }` (ProfilePageClient.tsx:525-531). `secret` is still trimmed at :521 and still conditionally attached to the payload at :546-548, but no longer gates the save.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy no longer requires the OIDC client secret before enabling OIDC, so a first-time configuration can be saved with no secret at all.
- **Why it matters**: First-time OIDC setup is the common case where `settings.oidcConfigured` is false, so the guard is the only thing standing between a blank client secret and `authMode: "oidc"` being persisted. With the secret check gone, the settings PATCH succeeds, the login page then renders the "Sign in with OIDC" button (LoginPageClient.tsx:145-152 gates on `oidcConfigured === true`, which the backend now reports true), and every OIDC login attempt fails at the token exchange with an opaque error — while password login is disabled because `authMode !== "password"`. The user is locked out of the dashboard.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/profile/page.js:350-360, 375-377. OpenProxy: web/src/components/ProfilePageClient.tsx:515-531 (guard at 525-531), 546-548; web/src/components/LoginPageClient.tsx:145-152
- **Parity fix**: Restore the secret to the guard and the message: change the condition at ProfilePageClient.tsx:525 to `if (authMode !== "password" && (!issuerUrl || !clientId || !secret) && !settings.oidcConfigured)` and the message to `"Issuer URL, client ID, and client secret are required to enable OIDC."` — matching profile/page.js:357-358 exactly, including the `!settings.oidcConfigured` escape hatch that lets an already-configured provider be re-saved without retyping the secret.

## P156-001 · P1 · Streaming usage accounting

- **9router**: The transform stream accumulates usage across EVERY chunk with a field-wise max-merge: `const extracted = extractUsage(parsed); if (extracted) state.usage = mergeUsage(state.usage, extracted);` (open-sse/utils/stream.js:319-320 for the translated path, :197-199 and :424 for passthrough). `mergeUsage` is explicitly designed for providers that SPLIT usage across events: "Anthropic splits usage across events: message_start has real input+cache, message_delta has the real cumulative output. Max keeps the meaningful value from each without clobbering." On completion the merged object is persisted via `saveUsageStats` and `saveRequestDetail` (open-sse/handlers/chatCore/streamingHandler.js:118-141).
- **OpenProxy**: The Reqwest stream keeps only the last raw frame: `last_data = Some(chunk.clone());` (src/server/api/chat.rs:3854), and `record_streaming_usage` extracts from that single frame: `let usage = last_data.as_ref().and_then(|b| extract_token_usage_from_bytes(b));` (src/server/api/chat.rs:4140-4142). There is no per-chunk extraction and no merge. For Claude the last frame is `message_delta` (output_tokens only) or `message_stop` (no usage at all) → prompt_tokens is lost.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy reads usage from only the final SSE frame; 9Router merges usage field-wise-max across every frame of the stream.
- **Why it matters**: For Anthropic/Claude streams — the dominant Claude Code traffic — the input token count and both cache counters live in the FIRST frame (`message_start`) and the output count in a MIDDLE frame (`message_delta`). OpenProxy records prompt_tokens=0 (or the row's tokens are None entirely), so the Usage page shows 0 input tokens, 0 cache reads and an under-counted or zero cost for every Claude turn, while 9Router reports the true totals. Providers that only put usage in a non-final chunk (Gemini `usageMetadata` mid-stream, Qoder's separate usage frame) are equally affected.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/utils/stream.js:319-320 (merge per chunk), open-sse/utils/stream.js:197-199, open-sse/utils/stream.js:424, open-sse/utils/usageTracking.js:326-341 (mergeUsage + the Anthropic split rationale), open-sse/handlers/chatCore/streamingHandler.js:139 (saveUsageStats with merged usage). openproxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:3854 (`last_data = Some(chunk.clone())`), src/server/api/chat.rs:4033 (same in the Hyper branch), src/server/api/chat.rs:4140-4142 (single-frame extract).
- **Parity fix**: In both stream branches, run `extract_token_usage_from_bytes` on every `data:` frame and fold the result into a running `Option<TokenUsage>` with a field-wise max merge (a small `merge_token_usage(prev, next) -> TokenUsage` that takes the max of each numeric field, matching 9Router's `mergeUsage` including the "ignore NaN" guard). Pass that accumulator to `record_streaming_usage` instead of `last_data`. Keep `last_data` only for the terminal-frame response reconstruction.

## P157-001 · P1 · Trailing-slash deep links under /dashboard

- **9router**: `next.config.mjs` sets no `trailingSlash`, so Next.js default (`false`) normalizes `/dashboard/providers/` to `/dashboard/providers` before routing. The user always gets the list page.
- **OpenProxy**: `src/server/dashboard/mod.rs:175-187` `dynamic_segment_fallback` splits the candidate on the last `/`. For `dashboard/providers/` that yields `parent = "dashboard/providers"`, so it looks up `dashboard/providers/_dynamic.html` — which exists in the build (`web/dist/dashboard/providers/_dynamic.html`, `_dynamic` emitted by `web/src/pages/dashboard/providers/[id]/index.astro:15`). The response is `ProviderDetailPageClient` with an empty id. Same for `web/dist/dashboard/media-providers/tts/_dynamic.html` -> `MediaProvidersKindIdPageClient`.
- **Gap**: A trailing-slash URL on a static list page resolves to that directory's `_dynamic` detail shell instead of the list page.
- **Why it matters**: Any tool, proxy, or hand-typed URL that appends a trailing slash (`/dashboard/providers/`, `/dashboard/media-providers/tts/`) silently lands on a broken detail view with an empty id rather than the intended list. 9router normalizes and shows the list.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/next.config.mjs (no `trailingSlash`, default false) | OpenProxy: src/server/dashboard/mod.rs:175-187; web/src/pages/dashboard/providers/[id]/index.astro:15; build output web/dist/dashboard/providers/_dynamic.html and web/dist/dashboard/media-providers/tts/_dynamic.html
- **Parity fix**: In `normalize_asset_path` (`src/server/dashboard/mod.rs:154-161`) or at the top of `serve_embedded`/`serve_from_disk`, strip any trailing `/` from the candidate before lookup, or reject candidates whose last segment is empty so they fall through to the `.html` / `index.html` probes instead of the dynamic-segment probe.

## P158-001 · P1 · Zed LLM bearer-token lifecycle on `POST /completions` (open-sse/shared/zedAuth.js → src/core/executor/zed.rs)

- **9router**: `fetchZedLlmToken` caches the short-lived bearer in `llmTokenCache` with `LLM_TOKEN_TTL_MS = 50 * 60 * 1000` (zedAuth.js:31) and returns the cached token while it is fresh (zedAuth.js:262-264, 287). `zedLlmFetch` wraps every call and, on `shouldRefreshZedLlmToken(response)` — i.e. HTTP 401, or the `x-zed-expired-token` / `x-zed-outdated-token` response headers (zedAuth.js:291-297) — re-issues the request once with `forceRefresh: true` (zedAuth.js:315-316). The executor routes `/completions` through `zedLlmFetch` (executors/zed.js:243).
- **OpenProxy**: `ZedExecutor::execute_request` calls `self.resolve_llm_token(...)` on every single request (zed.rs:393), which unconditionally POSTs to `https://cloud.zed.dev/client/llm_tokens` (oauth/zed_auth.rs:184-229). There is no token cache and no 401/expired-token retry: `post_completions` (zed.rs:354-387) sends the request and returns the response as-is. `LLM_TOKEN_TTL_SECS = 50 * 60` is declared at oauth/zed_auth.rs:28 but never referenced anywhere (grep count = 1, the definition itself).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy re-mints a Zed LLM token on every request and has no expiry-triggered refresh retry, whereas 9router caches the token for 50 minutes and force-refreshes once on a 401 or expired/outdated-token signal.
- **Why it matters**: Two user-visible consequences. (1) Latency and load: every Zed completion costs an extra authenticated round-trip to `/client/llm_tokens` that 9router performs at most once per 50 minutes; for a CLI agent streaming through Zed this doubles the per-request setup cost and multiplies the rate against Zed's token endpoint. (2) No recovery from mid-session token expiry: if Zed's 50-minute token TTL lapses or Zed revokes it, 9router transparently re-mints and retries the same completion, whereas OpenProxy surfaces the 401 as an upstream error and fails the request — a failure 9router never exhibits.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/shared/zedAuth.js:31 (TTL), :262-264 (cache hit), :287 (cache store), :291-297 (shouldRefreshZedLlmToken), :299-318 (zedLlmFetch retry), .tmp/9router/open-sse/executors/zed.js:243 (call site). OpenProxy: src/core/executor/zed.rs:393 (per-request resolve), :433 (post), :354-387 (post_completions, no retry); src/oauth/zed_auth.rs:28 (unused TTL const), :184-229 (unconditional fetch).
- **Parity fix**: Add a process-wide `Mutex<HashMap<cache_key, (String, Instant)>>` alongside the existing 50-minute constant and use the already-declared `LLM_TOKEN_TTL_SECS` for the expiry. In `ZedExecutor`, resolve the token through a cached helper; in `post_completions` (or a thin wrapper), inspect the response for status 401 or the `x-zed-expired-token` / `x-zed-outdated-token` headers and, on a match, evict the cache entry, force-refresh once, and replay the identical request — mirroring `zedLlmFetch`.

## P159-001 · P1 · Zed LLM token — cache and expired-token retry

- **9router**: `llmTokenCache` is a 50-minute TTL map (zedAuth.js:33, 263-268) keyed by `userId:organizationId:tokenTail`; `fetchZedLlmToken` returns a cached bearer unless `forceRefresh`. `shouldRefreshZedLlmToken` (zedAuth.js:291-296) returns true on HTTP 401 OR presence of the `x-zed-expired-token` / `x-zed-outdated-token` response headers, and `zedLlmFetch` (zedAuth.js:299-317) transparently re-mints with `forceRefresh: true` and re-issues the request once.
- **OpenProxy**: `resolve_llm_token` (src/core/executor/zed.rs:242-303) POSTs `/client/llm_tokens` on every single request — there is no token cache and no TTL. `post_completions` (src/core/executor/zed.rs:354-…) is a single-shot `.send().await`; `execute_request` (src/core/executor/zed.rs:433-436) turns any non-2xx straight into `Err(...)`. No code anywhere references `x-zed-expired-token`, `x-zed-outdated-token`, or a force-refresh flag.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy has neither the 50-minute LLM-token cache nor the 401 / `x-zed-expired-token` / `x-zed-outdated-token` re-mint-and-retry that 9router performs.
- **Why it matters**: Every Zed turn adds a redundant `/client/llm_tokens` round trip and consumes upstream token-mint budget, and a legitimately retryable condition — a 401 or a stale-token response header — becomes a hard failure. 9router recovers transparently; an OpenProxy user sees intermittent `Zed returned HTTP 401` errors that clear only after the 50-minute window rolls over on its own.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/shared/zedAuth.js:33, 263-268, 291-296, 299-317 | openproxy: src/core/executor/zed.rs:242-303, 354-…, 433-436
- **Parity fix**: Add a process-wide `Mutex<HashMap<String, (String, Instant)>>` LLM-token cache in `src/oauth/zed_auth.rs` keyed by `userId:organizationId:token_tail`, with the 50-minute TTL from zedAuth.js:33. Port `should_refresh_zed_llm_token(status, headers)` checking 401 plus the `x-zed-expired-token` / `x-zed-outdated-token` headers, and wrap the `post_completions` call in `execute_request` (src/core/executor/zed.rs:433) so a triggering response re-mints with `forceRefresh` and re-POSTs once, exactly as zedAuth.js:299-317 does.

## P160-001 · P1 · Zed live model catalog resolution (open-sse/shared/zedAuth.js + src/app/api/v1/models/route.js → src/core/executor/zed.rs)

- **9router**: `resolveZedModels` performs a live authenticated `GET /models` against `https://cloud.zed.dev`, maps each entry through `mapZedModel` (normalizing `id`, `name`, `provider`, `supportsTools`, …), drops `isDisabled` entries, and caches the result for `MODEL_CACHE_TTL_MS = 60 * 60 * 1000` with in-flight dedup (zedAuth.js:357-411). `ZedExecutor.resolveModel` calls it per request and, when the requested model is absent from `rawById`, re-fetches once with `forceRefresh: true` before falling back to the name heuristic (executors/zed.js:212-229). The `zed:` entry in the `/v1/models` live-resolver table uses the same call to advertise live Zed models (src/app/api/v1/models/route.js:119-136).
- **OpenProxy**: `ZedExecutor::resolve_model` only reads `provider_specific_data.zed_models` (falling back to `models`) and, when neither is populated, drops straight to the name heuristic in `normalize_zed_provider` (zed.rs:119-145, 53-74). Nothing in production ever writes that key: the sole `psd.insert("zed_models", …)` is at zed.rs:498, which is inside the `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` block that begins at zed.rs:492. There is no Zed `/models` HTTP call anywhere in `src/` (`cloud.zed.dev` appears only at `/client/users/me`, `/client/llm_tokens`, `/completions`). `src/server/api/v1_models.rs` contains zero occurrences of `zed`.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy never performs the live Zed model-catalog fetch that 9router uses for both per-request model→provider resolution and `GET /v1/models` advertising, so it always relies on the name-substring heuristic and can never list Zed's hosted models.
- **Why it matters**: Two concrete consequences. (1) `GET /v1/models` returns no Zed models, so OpenAI-compatible clients (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, Cline) cannot discover any model to route through a Zed connection. (2) Provider selection is heuristic-only: `normalize_zed_provider` matches on substrings `claude`/`gemini`/`grok|xai` and otherwise defaults to `OpenAiResponses`. Any Zed-hosted model whose id does not contain those substrings but whose catalog `provider` is Anthropic or Google is sent to the wrong upstream, producing wrong-format requests and upstream errors. 9router resolves the true provider from the live catalog and only falls back to the same heuristic when the catalog is genuinely unreachable.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/shared/zedAuth.js:321-355 (mapZedModel), :357-411 (resolveZedModels live fetch + 1h cache), .tmp/9router/open-sse/executors/zed.js:212-229 (resolveModel + forceRefresh retry), .tmp/9router/src/app/api/v1/models/route.js:119-136 (zed resolver). OpenProxy: src/core/executor/zed.rs:119-145 (catalog read), :53-74 (heuristic), :492-500 (test-only write, `#[cfg(test)]` at :492); src/server/api/v1_models.rs (0 `zed` matches).
- **Parity fix**: Port `resolveZedModels`: add a `resolve_zed_models(client, credentials)` in `src/oauth/zed_auth.rs` that GETs `{ZED_CLOUD_BASE_URL}/models` with the `buildZedUserAuthHeader` auth and the `x-zed-client-supports-x-ai` header, maps entries into the `{id, name, provider, supports_tools, …}` shape (filtering `is_disabled`), and caches with a 1-hour TTL plus in-flight dedup. Call it from `ZedExecutor::resolve_model` (re-fetching with `force_refresh` when the model is missing) and add a `zed` arm to the `/v1/models` live-resolver set in `v1_models.rs`, writing the catalog into `provider_specific_data.zed_models` so the existing reader works unchanged.

## P161-001 · P1 · Zed login — `provider_specific_data` contract

- **9router**: `postExchange` calls `fetchZedAuthenticatedUser` best-effort, resolves the org via `resolveZedOrganizationId`, and `mapTokens` persists FOUR psd keys: `authMethod`, `userId`, `systemId`, `organizationId` (src/lib/oauth/providers/zed.js:34-59). `systemId` is minted during `prepareConfig` via `createZedNativeAuthData` and injected into the signin URL as `&system_id=` (zedAuth.js:83, 90). At runtime `getSystemId` reads `psd.systemId` and attaches `x-zed-system-id` to both `/client/users/me` (zedAuth.js:205-208) and `/client/llm_tokens` (zedAuth.js:270-272).
- **OpenProxy**: `handle_zed_proxy_connection` persists exactly two psd entries: `userId` and `authMethod` (src/server/api/oauth.rs:6917, 6930-6933). `systemId` and `organizationId` are never stored. The executor's `psd.get("systemId")` (src/core/executor/zed.rs:255) therefore always yields `None`, so the `x-zed-system-id` header is never sent on any Zed cloud call — even though `create_native_auth_data` generated a system id and put it in the signin URL (zed_auth.rs:70).
- **Gap**: The Zed login persists neither `systemId` nor `organizationId` into provider_specific_data, so the system-id header is never sent and the organization must be rediscovered on every single request.
- **Why it matters**: Zed binds the native-app session to the `system_id` supplied at signin; omitting `x-zed-system-id` on `/client/llm_tokens` and `/client/users/me` makes the account look like a different, unbound client to Zed's cloud, and the loss of the persisted `organizationId` pushes every request onto the best-effort discovery path (see P0-003), which is where the hard failure originates.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/lib/oauth/providers/zed.js:34-59 · open-sse/shared/zedAuth.js:83, 90, 169-173, 205-208, 270-272 | openproxy: src/server/api/oauth.rs:6917, 6930-6933 · src/core/executor/zed.rs:255 · src/oauth/zed_auth.rs:70
- **Parity fix**: In `handle_zed_proxy_connection` (src/server/api/oauth.rs:6917-6933), keep the `/client/users/me` result instead of discarding everything but `email`, and persist `systemId` (from the pending session's generated auth data) and `organizationId` (resolved from that userInfo) into `provider_specific_data` alongside `userId`/`authMethod`, matching src/lib/oauth/providers/zed.js:53-59.

## P162-001 · P1 · Zed organization-id resolution (`resolveZedOrganizationId` equivalent)

- **9router**: `resolveZedOrganizationId` (zedAuth.js:227-239) tries, in order: `psd.organizationId || psd.defaultOrganizationId`; then `userInfo.default_organization_id || userInfo.defaultOrganizationId`; then `organizations.find(o => o.is_personal) || organizations[0]`, normalized via `normalizeOrganizationId` (string, `[0]`, or `.id`). If nothing resolves it throws `"No Zed organization selected"` (zedAuth.js:261).
- **OpenProxy**: `resolve_llm_token` (src/core/executor/zed.rs:256-284) reads ONLY `psd.organizationId`, and its `/client/users/me` probe reads ONLY `data.pointer("/organization/id")`, `data.get("organizationId")`, `data.get("organization_id")`. It does not read `psd.defaultOrganizationId`, `default_organization_id`, `defaultOrganizationId`, or the `organizations[]` array. An empty result falls into `zed_auth::fetch_llm_token`, which returns `Err("No Zed organization selected")` (zed_auth.rs:188-190), failing the whole request.
- **Gap**: The two resolvers read disjoint field sets: every key 9router reads (`defaultOrganizationId`, `default_organization_id`, `organizations[].id`) is ignored by OpenProxy, and every key OpenProxy reads (`organization.id`, bare `organization_id`) is ignored by 9router.
- **Why it matters**: Any Zed account whose organization is only discoverable from the user profile — i.e. not already in `psd.organizationId`, which OpenProxy never populates (see P0-002) — resolves to an empty string and every `/v1/chat/completions` request fails hard with "No Zed organization selected". Because `resolve_llm_token` runs before dispatch, the failure is total for that provider, and the combo/account-fallback layer sees an executor error rather than a retryable upstream status.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/shared/zedAuth.js:217-239, 256-261 | openproxy: src/core/executor/zed.rs:256-284 · src/oauth/zed_auth.rs:188-190
- **Parity fix**: Port `resolveZedOrganizationId` verbatim into `src/oauth/zed_auth.rs`: read `psd.organizationId || psd.defaultOrganizationId`, then `userInfo.default_organization_id || userInfo.defaultOrganizationId`, then `organizations.find(is_personal) || organizations[0]`, with a `normalize_organization_id` helper handling string / `[0]` / `.id`. Wire it into `resolve_llm_token` (src/core/executor/zed.rs:256) and keep the existing `"No Zed organization selected"` guard so the failure stays identical when genuinely unresolvable.

## P163-001 · P1 · forceStream provider set — opencode / oc

- **9router**: `const providerRequiresStreaming = PROVIDERS[provider]?.forceStream === true;`. Exactly eight registry entries set `forceStream: true` — api-airforce.js:32, codebuddy-cn.js:23, codebuddy-intl.js:25, codex.js:40, commandcode.js:24, grok-cli.js:46, openai.js:28, zed.js:28 — and the schema default is `forceStream: false`. open-sse/providers/registry/opencode.js (id "opencode", alias "oc") declares no forceStream, and catalogOverride.js only overrides capabilities/limits, never transport flags. So opencode is NOT a forceStream provider: a client posting stream:false gets stream=false upstream, and a client that omits stream gets the plain `body.stream !== false` default.
- **OpenProxy**: `provider_requires_streaming` matches a hand-maintained list that includes "opencode" and "oc" (with the comment '9router PR #4132 (decolua/9router): the OpenCode Zen free-tier gate rejects stream:false … Force upstream streaming'). The checked-out 9router has no such flag in opencode.js, so the list is a superset of the canonical eight.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy force-streams the `opencode`/`oc` provider, which 9router's registry does not mark forceStream.
- **Why it matters**: For any client that sends `stream:false` (or omits stream) against an OpenCode Zen model, 9router performs one non-streaming upstream call and returns the provider's native JSON. OpenProxy forces stream=true upstream and then aggregates the SSE back into JSON (sse_to_json = !client_requested_streaming && provider_forced). That changes latency, the token accounting path, and the exact JSON shape (reconstructed from SSE deltas rather than the upstream body) for the whole OpenCode Free tier. It also means a provider that only rejects `stream:false` with a 403 will now succeed on OpenProxy and fail on 9router — the two are not substitutable for regression comparisons.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:119 (forceStream read), open-sse/providers/schema.js:49 (default false), the eight registry files listed above, open-sse/providers/registry/opencode.js:1-32 (no forceStream), open-sse/providers/catalogOverride.js (no forceStream override). OpenProxy: src/core/utils/stream_flags.rs:11-35, specifically lines 24-30 for "opencode" | "oc"; consumed at src/core/utils/stream_flags.rs:74-78 and :105-110.
- **Parity fix**: Drop "opencode" and "oc" from the `provider_requires_streaming` match in src/core/utils/stream_flags.rs:29-30 so the list matches the eight canonical forceStream providers exactly. If the free-tier 403 is genuinely observed against this 9router build, capture it as a 9router issue rather than an OpenProxy-side divergence — the audit rule is that 9router is canonical.

## P164-001 · P1 · open-sse/executors/cursor.js:1082,859 + open-sse/utils/usageTracking.js vs src/core/executor/cursor.rs:2959-2963,2710-2714

- **9router**: Both `transformProtobufToSSE` (cursor.js:1082) and `transformProtobufToJSON` (cursor.js:859) call `estimateUsage(body, finalContent.length, FORMATS.OPENAI)`, which yields `prompt_tokens = ceil(JSON.stringify(body).length/4)`, `completion_tokens = max(1, floor(len/4))`, `total_tokens = prompt+completion`, an `estimated: true` marker, and then `addBufferToUsage` adds `BUFFER_TOKENS = 2000` to prompt, completion and total (usageTracking.js:21,40,45,50).
- **OpenProxy**: The usage block is hardcoded: `{"prompt_tokens": 0, "completion_tokens": total_content.len()/4, "total_tokens": total_content.len()/4}` (cursor.rs:2959-2963 for SSE, cursor.rs:2710-2714 for JSON). No input-token estimate, no 2000-token buffer, no `estimated` flag, and `total_tokens` equals completion only. The same hardcoded block is used on the AgentService path (cursor.rs:769-773).
- **Gap**: Cursor responses report `prompt_tokens: 0` and a `total_tokens` that ignores the prompt entirely, with no buffer and no `estimated` flag.
- **Why it matters**: Every Cursor request is recorded as zero input tokens, so the usage dashboard, quota tracker and per-key spend all under-report Cursor traffic to ~0% of its real cost. Clients that render token counters (Claude Code's status line, Codex) show a nonsense total, and the missing `estimated: true` makes consumers treat the numbers as authoritative.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/executors/cursor.js:859, :1082; open-sse/utils/usageTracking.js `estimateUsage`/`estimateInputTokens`/`estimateOutputTokens`/`formatUsage`, BUFFER_TOKENS=2000 at usageTracking.js:21. OpenProxy: src/core/executor/cursor.rs:2959-2963, :2710-2714, :769-773.
- **Parity fix**: Port `estimateUsage` (JSON.stringify(body).length/4 + 2000 for prompt, max(1, len/4) + 2000 for completion, sum for total, plus `estimated: true`) and use it for the chat and agent paths in place of the hardcoded blocks.

## P165-001 · P1 · open-sse/executors/cursor.js:822-825,850-853 vs src/core/executor/cursor.rs:2688-2693

- **9router**: `transformProtobufToJSON` computes `visibleComposerContent = isComposerModel(model) ? visibleComposerContentFromThinking(totalThinking) : ""` and `finalContent = totalContent || visibleComposerContent` (cursor.js:822-825), then sets `message.content = finalContent || null` (cursor.js:850-853). `totalThinking` is never written into `content`; for a non-composer model a thinking-only response yields `content: null`.
- **OpenProxy**: `build_chat_completion_response` matches `(Some(t), Some(th)) => Some(format!("{}\n\n{}", th, t))` (cursor.rs:2688-2693), so the raw accumulated thinking text is always prepended to the assistant message content, for every model. Because `transform_protobuf_to_json` has already appended the composer visible tail into `total_content` (cursor.rs:3086-3091), a composer model gets the visible answer twice.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy folds internal reasoning into `message.content`; 9router never does.
- **Why it matters**: Every non-streaming Cursor response from a reasoning model ships the model's scratchpad as the user-visible answer. Clients that display `content` verbatim (basic-chat playground, non-streaming SDK calls) show the reasoning instead of the reply, and token/cost accounting is inflated. For composer models the visible answer is duplicated — once from the thinking tail, once from the real text.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/executors/cursor.js:822-825, :850-853. OpenProxy: src/core/executor/cursor.rs:2688-2693, :3080-3094.
- **Parity fix**: Drop the `Some(th)` arm from `build_chat_completion_response` and pass `thinking` as a separate field (or omit it) so `content` stays `total_content || visible_composer_content`, matching cursor.js:822-825/850-853.

## P166-001 · P1 · open-sse/executors/cursor.js:861-877 vs src/core/executor/cursor.rs:3128-3130

- **9router**: `transformProtobufToJSON` always returns a complete `chat.completion` object — `{id, object, created, model, choices:[{index:0, message, finish_reason}], usage}` — with `content: null` and `finish_reason:"stop"` even when no frame produced text or thinking (cursor.js:850-877).
- **OpenProxy**: `transform_protobuf_to_json` short-circuits with `if text_opt.is_none() && thinking_opt.is_none() && tool_calls.is_empty() { return Ok(String::new()); }` (cursor.rs:3128-3130); `execute` then serves that zero-length body with HTTP 200 and `content-type: application/json` (cursor.rs:2294-2304).
- **Gap**: An empty Cursor turn returns an empty HTTP 200 body instead of a well-formed completion object.
- **Why it matters**: Every strict OpenAI-compatible client (OpenAI SDK, Claude Code, Codex) does `JSON.parse` on the body; a zero-length payload raises a parse error and surfaces as a transport failure rather than an empty assistant turn. This fires whenever Cursor returns frames that carry no decodable text/thinking/tool call (rate-limit bodies, protocol drift, a single unknown-field frame).
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/executors/cursor.js:850-877. OpenProxy: src/core/executor/cursor.rs:3128-3130, :2294-2304.
- **Parity fix**: Remove the early return and always build the completion via `build_chat_completion_response` with `text = None`, yielding `content: null` / `finish_reason:"stop"` like cursor.js:861-877.

## P167-001 · P1 · open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:155 + open-sse/executors/cursor.js:305-307 vs src/core/executor/cursor.rs:2246-2256

- **9router**: chatCore.js:155 assigns `credentials.rawHeaders = clientRawRequest?.headers || {}` on the same credentials object later handed to `executor.execute({... credentials ...})` (chatCore.js:363). `CursorExecutor.transformRequest` then reads `credentials?.rawHeaders?.["user-agent"]` and sets `forceAgentMode = ua.includes("claude-cli") || ua.includes("claude-code") || ua.includes("Claude Code")` (cursor.js:305-307), which flips `IS_AGENTIC`, `SHOULD_DISABLE_TOOLS` and `UNIFIED_MODE_NAME` in the protobuf.
- **OpenProxy**: `CursorExecutor::execute` reads the UA from `request.credentials.provider_specific_data["rawHeaders"]["user-agent"]` (cursor.rs:2246-2256). `request.credentials` is a `ProviderConnection` cloned from the DB (chat.rs:1910), and nothing in the OpenProxy tree ever writes `rawHeaders` into `provider_specific_data` — the only writer is chat.rs:1272, which populates a short-lived `creds` JSON used solely for `translate_request_with_strip`.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's `force_agent_mode` is permanently false because the field it reads is never populated.
- **Why it matters**: Every Claude Code request routed to Cursor is encoded as a non-agentic turn: `IS_AGENTIC=0`, `SHOULD_DISABLE_TOOLS=1`, `UNIFIED_MODE=CHAT` and `UNIFIED_MODE_NAME="Ask"` (cursor.rs:1525-1605). Cursor then disables tool support for the turn, so the exact client AGENTS.md calls out as the primary surface loses tool calling on this provider.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:155 and :363; open-sse/executors/cursor.js:305-307. OpenProxy: src/core/executor/cursor.rs:2246-2256 (read site), src/server/api/chat.rs:1910 (credentials = DB connection clone), src/server/api/chat.rs:1262-1274 (the only rawHeaders writer — a transient translator object, not psd).
- **Parity fix**: Plumb the downstream request headers into `CursorExecutionRequest` (e.g. a `client_headers: Option<HeaderMap>` field) from the same source chat.rs already uses for `client_headers` at chat.rs:1264, and read the UA from there instead of `provider_specific_data`.

## P168-001 · P1 · open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:278-281 vs src/core/rtk/mod.rs:139-148 (+ 209-211, 524-528)

- **9router**: Caveman is injected whenever the toggle and level are set — there is no token-estimate gate anywhere in the codebase (`rg shouldAutoApplyCaveman open-sse/ src/` returns no hits): `if (tokenSaverEnabled && cavemanEnabled && cavemanLevel) { injectCaveman(translatedBody, finalFormat, cavemanLevel); xf.push(`CAVEMAN:${cavemanLevel}`); }` (open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:278-281).
- **OpenProxy**: Injection is gated behind a context-pressure heuristic with no counterpart in 9Router: `if settings.caveman_enabled { if should_auto_apply_caveman(body, model) { ... } }` (src/core/rtk/mod.rs:139-148), where should_auto_apply_caveman = `estimate_context_tokens(body) >= caveman_trigger_threshold(model)` and the threshold is `infer_context_window(model)/8` clamped to [512, 2048] tokens (src/core/rtk/mod.rs:209-211, 524-528).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy only injects the Caveman system prompt when the request already exceeds 512-2048 estimated tokens; 9Router injects it on every request once enabled.
- **Why it matters**: A user who turns on Caveman mode gets terse, low-token answers in 9Router and verbose prose in OpenProxy for every short/medium request (the common case). The requested token-saving behaviour silently does not apply, and the `CAVEMAN:<level>` entry is missing from the request log line.
- **Evidence**: 9router open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:278-281; OpenProxy src/core/rtk/mod.rs:142 (gate), :209-211, :524-528 (threshold)
- **Parity fix**: Drop the context-pressure gate from apply_request_preprocessing and inject whenever `settings.caveman_enabled`, mirroring chatCore.js:278-281. Keep CompressionLevel::parse_or_default since the level-to-prompt mapping already matches CAVEMAN_PROMPTS.

## P169-001 · P1 · open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:291-300 + open-sse/rtk/pxpipe.js:32 vs src/server/api/chat.rs:1322-1423 (+ src/types/mod.rs:616, src/server/api/pxpipe.rs:52)

- **9router**: PXPIPE is the last token saver before dispatch and rewrites the body in place: `if (pxpipeEnabled) { const pxpipeResult = await compressWithPxpipe(translatedBody, { enabled: true, format: finalFormat, model: upstreamModel, minChars: pxpipeMinChars, timeoutMs: pxpipeTimeoutMs, transform: pxpipeTransform }); if (pxpipeResult.body) translatedBody = pxpipeResult.body; }` (open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:291-300), implemented by compressWithPxpipe (open-sse/rtk/pxpipe.js:32) which renders bulky Claude-format context as dense PNGs once the body exceeds minChars (default 25000).
- **OpenProxy**: There is no PXPIPE step in the saver chain at all. The settings that drive it exist (pxpipe_enabled src/types/mod.rs:616, pxpipe_min_chars :626, pxpipe_timeout_ms) but the only read of pxpipe_enabled outside settings persistence is the status echo `settings.pxpipe_enabled` in src/server/api/pxpipe.rs:52. The chat pipeline runs RTK (:1323) then Headroom (:1367) then Caveman/Ponytail (:1390) then dedupe (:1397) then anchor cache (:1411) then TTS strip (:1416) and never calls anything equivalent to compressWithPxpipe.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's PXPIPE toggle is a no-op for request bodies — the service can be installed and started, but no request is ever transformed by it.
- **Why it matters**: A user who enables PXPIPE to shrink bulky image context (the stated purpose, Claude-format only, minChars gate) sees zero effect in OpenProxy: every image-bearing Claude request is forwarded at full size, so the context is not compressed and costs/limits are unchanged, while 9Router compresses it.
- **Evidence**: 9router open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:291-300 and open-sse/rtk/pxpipe.js:32-43; OpenProxy src/server/api/chat.rs:1322-1423 (saver chain, no pxpipe), src/types/mod.rs:616 and src/server/api/pxpipe.rs:52 (setting never consumed)
- **Parity fix**: Add a pxpipe pass as the last saver before dispatch in src/server/api/chat.rs (after step 6, before anchor_claude_cache), reading pxpipe_enabled/pxpipe_min_chars/pxpipe_timeout_ms, gated on the final body format being Claude, with the same below-threshold/timeout/unsupported-format fail-open skips as open-sse/rtk/pxpipe.js:32-43.

## P170-001 · P1 · open-sse/rtk/autodetect.js:84-94 vs src/core/rtk/autodetect.rs:222-231

- **9router**: isPathLike has a Windows drive-letter escape hatch evaluated BEFORE the colon rejection: `if (/^[A-Za-z]:[\\/]/.test(t)) return true; if (t.includes(":")) return false; return t.startsWith(".") || t.startsWith("/") || t.includes("/")` (open-sse/rtk/autodetect.js:91-93), so `C:\Users\me\file.js` counts as path-like.
- **OpenProxy**: is_path_like rejects anything containing a colon with no drive-letter exception: `if t.contains(':') { return false; }` then the dot/slash test (src/core/rtk/autodetect.rs:227-230).
- **Gap**: Windows drive-letter paths are never path-like in OpenProxy, so the `non_empty.every(isPathLike)` find-detection can never fire for Windows find output.
- **Why it matters**: On Windows, find-shaped tool output falls through past the find detector to dedup-log (or smart-truncate), producing a completely different — and less structured — compressed payload than 9Router's per-directory grouping. The LLM loses the dir/file grouping it relies on to reason about the filesystem.
- **Evidence**: 9router open-sse/rtk/autodetect.js:87-93; OpenProxy src/core/rtk/autodetect.rs:222-231
- **Parity fix**: Add the drive-letter check before the colon rejection in is_path_like: `if t.len() >= 3 && t.as_bytes()[0].is_ascii_alphabetic() && t.as_bytes()[1] == b':' && matches!(t.as_bytes()[2], b'/' | b'\\') { return true; }`, matching open-sse/rtk/autodetect.js:91.

## P171-001 · P1 · open-sse/rtk/filters/gitStatus.js:50-52 vs src/core/rtk/filters/mod.rs:277-283

- **9router**: The porcelain branch increments the staged counter AND records the file: `if ("MADRC".includes(x)) { staged++; stagedFiles.push(file); } else if (x === "U") { conflicts++; }` (open-sse/rtk/filters/gitStatus.js:50-55), so the output prints `+ Staged: N files` and the empty-tree check at line 110 sees staged > 0.
- **OpenProxy**: The porcelain branch records the file but never increments the counter: `if "MADRC".contains(x) { if raw.len() > 3 { staged_files.push(raw[3..].trim().to_string()); } } else if x == 'U' { conflicts_count += 1; }` (src/core/rtk/filters/mod.rs:277-283). staged_count stays 0 for every porcelain line.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy never increments staged_count, so the "+ Staged" section is never printed and a repo with only staged changes is reported as "clean — nothing to commit".
- **Why it matters**: For `M  src/a.rs` / `A  new.rs` (staged-only working tree, the normal state right after `git add`), all four counters are 0, so git_status_impl falls through to `out.push_str("clean — nothing to commit\n")` (src/core/rtk/filters/mod.rs:380-382). The model is explicitly told the tree is clean and will redo or overwrite work the user already staged.
- **Evidence**: 9router open-sse/rtk/filters/gitStatus.js:50-52 and :82-88, :110-112; OpenProxy src/core/rtk/filters/mod.rs:277-283 (missing `staged_count += 1`), :337-339 and :380-382
- **Parity fix**: Add `staged_count += 1;` alongside `staged_files.push(...)` in the `"MADRC".contains(x)` branch of git_status_impl (src/core/rtk/filters/mod.rs:277-280), matching open-sse/rtk/filters/gitStatus.js:50-52.

## P172-001 · P1 · open-sse/rtk/filters/grep.js:13 vs src/core/rtk/filters/mod.rs:404-407

- **9router**: grep() splits on the first two colons using an offset search: `const first = line.indexOf(":"); ... const second = line.indexOf(":", first + 1); if (second === -1) continue;` (open-sse/rtk/filters/grep.js:11-14), then groups matches by file and emits the compact `{total} matches in {n}F` form.
- **OpenProxy**: grep_impl computes the second colon with the SAME unanchored search and an impossible guard: `let first = line.find(':')...; let second = match line.find(':') { Some(i) if i > first => i, _ => continue };` (src/core/rtk/filters/mod.rs:400-407). `line.find(':')` always returns `first`, so `i > first` is never true and every line hits `continue`.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's grep filter is a no-op: the second-colon search can never succeed, so no line is ever matched and grep_impl always returns the input verbatim.
- **Why it matters**: autodetect correctly classifies `file:line:text` dumps as grep (src/core/rtk/autodetect.rs:120 uses the correct offset search), so grep_impl is selected and then does nothing. Every grep tool result is forwarded raw to the provider: 0% of the token saving 9Router delivers, larger bills and bigger context windows.
- **Evidence**: 9router open-sse/rtk/filters/grep.js:11-14; OpenProxy src/core/rtk/filters/mod.rs:400-407 vs the working reference in the same crate at src/core/rtk/autodetect.rs:214-217 (`line[first + 1..].find(':')`)
- **Parity fix**: Search for the second colon with an offset, exactly as autodetect's is_grep_line already does: `let second = match line[first + 1..].find(':') { Some(i) => first + 1 + i, None => continue };`

## P173-001 · P1 · open-sse/rtk/headroom.js:162-167 vs src/core/rtk/headroom.rs:590-694

- **9router**: collectKiroHeadroomMessages projects BOTH sources: `if (Array.isArray(state.history)) { for (const item of state.history) visit(item); } if (state.currentMessage) visit(state.currentMessage);` and only bails when nothing was collected: `return messages.length > 0 ? { messages, targets } : null;` (open-sse/rtk/headroom.js:162-167).
- **OpenProxy**: collect_kiro_headroom_messages requires a history array and never visits currentMessage: `let history = state.get("history").and_then(Value::as_array)?;` (src/core/rtk/headroom.rs:598) — the `?` returns None when history is absent, and the loop at :599-690 only walks `history`.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's Kiro Headroom projection ignores conversationState.currentMessage and returns None outright when history is missing/empty, so the newest turn — where the tool-result bloat lives — is never compressed.
- **Why it matters**: For a Kiro request whose tool results are in currentMessage (first turn, or any body with an empty history array), OpenProxy skips Headroom entirely and forwards the full payload; 9Router compresses it. Users enabling Headroom see no token reduction on Kiro traffic and can blow past context limits.
- **Evidence**: 9router open-sse/rtk/headroom.js:162-167; OpenProxy src/core/rtk/headroom.rs:598 (required history) and :599-690 (history-only loop)
- **Parity fix**: Make history optional (`let history = state.get("history").and_then(Value::as_array).unwrap_or(&[]);`), then after the history loop visit `state.get("currentMessage")` with the same visitor and its matching JSON-pointer target, returning Some only when messages is non-empty — mirroring open-sse/rtk/headroom.js:162-167.

## P174-001 · P1 · open-sse/rtk/systemInject.js:164-172 vs src/core/rtk/mod.rs:246-258 (and src/core/translator/ponytail.rs:189-204)

- **9router**: injectResponsesInputSystem only matches existing items with `type === RESPONSES_ITEM.MESSAGE` ("message") and role system/developer, and when none exists it inserts a well-formed Responses item: `const msg = { type: RESPONSES_ITEM.MESSAGE, role: ROLE.SYSTEM, content: [{ type: RESPONSES_ITEM.INPUT_TEXT, text: prompt }] }; arr.unshift(msg);` (open-sse/rtk/systemInject.js:165-171; RESPONSES_ITEM.MESSAGE === "message" per open-sse/translator/schema/blocks.js:29).
- **OpenProxy**: inject_openai_message_prompt is shared by the `messages` and `input` arrays and inserts a bare, untyped, string-content item when no system/developer message exists: `messages.insert(0, json!({ "role": "system", "content": prompt }));` (src/core/rtk/mod.rs:255, identical at src/core/translator/ponytail.rs:204). It also matches any input item carrying role system/developer regardless of `type`.
- **Gap**: For a Responses-API body with an `input` array, OpenProxy injects `{role:"system", content:"<string>"}` — an input item with no `type` and non-array content — instead of 9Router's typed `{type:"message", role:"system", content:[{type:"input_text",...}]}`.
- **Why it matters**: Any /v1/responses request that carries `input` without a top-level `instructions` string, sent while Caveman or Ponytail is enabled, gets an input item that the OpenAI Responses contract rejects (items must declare type "message" and typed content parts) — the upstream answers 400 and the Codex/Responses flow breaks. 9Router builds a valid item.
- **Evidence**: 9router open-sse/rtk/systemInject.js:165-171 (+ open-sse/translator/schema/blocks.js:28-30); OpenProxy src/core/rtk/mod.rs:239-241 and :255, mirrored at src/core/translator/ponytail.rs:189-204
- **Parity fix**: Give the `input` branch its own injector that matches only `type == "message"` items and, when inserting, writes `json!({ "type": "message", "role": "system", "content": [{ "type": "input_text", "text": prompt }] })`, matching open-sse/rtk/systemInject.js:165-171. Keep the `messages` branch inserting `{role:"system", content: prompt}`.

## P175-001 · P1 · open-sse/rtk/systemInject.js:17-19 and :273-292 vs src/core/rtk/mod.rs:213-228

- **9router**: injectSystemPrompt has an explicit Kiro arm that runs before the shape sniff: `if (isKiroBody(body) || format === FORMATS.KIRO) { injectKiroSystem(body, prompt); return; }` (open-sse/rtk/systemInject.js:17-19). injectKiroSystem walks history then currentMessage for the first `userInputMessage` and appends the prompt to its `content` string with SEP dedupe (open-sse/rtk/systemInject.js:273-292).
- **OpenProxy**: inject_caveman_prompt dispatches on three shapes only: `if fields.contains_key("system") { claude } if is_gemini_shape(fields) { gemini } inject_openai_shape(fields)` (src/core/rtk/mod.rs:219-227; identical at src/core/translator/ponytail.rs:145-153). A Kiro body has none of `system`, `systemInstruction`/`system_instruction`/`contents`, `instructions`, `messages` or `input`, so all branches miss and it returns false.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy has no Kiro arm at all — a conversationState body falls through every branch and the prompt is silently dropped.
- **Why it matters**: With Caveman or Ponytail enabled, Kiro (`kr/` models) requests are never annotated in OpenProxy, so the terse/lazy-senior-dev instruction is absent and the model answers in full prose — the exact opposite of what the user configured, with no error surfaced. 9Router appends it to the first user turn.
- **Evidence**: 9router open-sse/rtk/systemInject.js:17-19 and :273-292; OpenProxy src/core/rtk/mod.rs:213-228 and :415-427, src/core/translator/ponytail.rs:145-153
- **Parity fix**: Add a Kiro branch ahead of the shape sniff in both inject_caveman_prompt and inject_ponytail_prompt: when `body.conversationState` exists, find the first `userInputMessage` in `history` (falling back to `currentMessage`) and append `content + SEP + prompt` unless the prompt is already a SEP-delimited segment, exactly as open-sse/rtk/systemInject.js:273-292 does.

## P176-001 · P1 · open-sse/shared/qoder/cosy.js:147-148 vs src/core/executor/qoder.rs:1503-1511

- **9router**: The signature input is assembled with `bodyBuf.toString("latin1")` and then hashed as `md5Hex(Buffer.from(sigInput, "latin1"))` (cosy.js:147-148). latin1 round-trips every byte 0x00-0xFF, so the hashed bytes are byte-identical to the request body — including binary multipart payloads.
- **OpenProxy**: `build_cosy_headers` interpolates `String::from_utf8_lossy(body)` into the signature string (qoder.rs:1503-1511); invalid UTF-8 sequences are replaced with U+FFFD, which changes both the string contents and their UTF-8 byte length. (`Cosy-Bodyhash` at qoder.rs:1512 correctly hashes the raw bytes, so only the signature diverges.)
- **Gap**: The COSY signature is computed over a lossy UTF-8 rendering of the body instead of the body's actual bytes.
- **Why it matters**: For the chat path the signed body is the WAF-encoded ASCII string, so latin1 and UTF-8 coincide and nothing breaks. For image uploads the signed body is a multipart buffer containing raw image bytes (qoder.rs:533-536), and every PNG/JPEG begins with 0x89 which is not valid UTF-8 — so `from_utf8_lossy` mangles the hashed bytes and the `Cosy-*` signature no longer matches what the server computes from the received body. Qoder rejects the upload, i.e. all image/vision attachments fail.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/shared/qoder/cosy.js:147-148. OpenProxy: src/core/executor/qoder.rs:1503-1511 (lossy), :1512 (raw, correct), :533-536 (binary multipart body).
- **Parity fix**: Hash the signature over raw bytes instead of a lossy String: build `sig_input` as `payload_b64.as_bytes() ++ b"\n" ++ cosy_key.as_bytes() ++ b"\n" ++ timestamp.as_bytes() ++ b"\n" ++ body ++ b"\n" ++ sig_path.as_bytes()` and pass that buffer to `md5_hex`.

## P177-001 · P1 · open-sse/shared/zedAuth.js:291-319 vs src/core/executor/zed.rs:393,433-440

- **9router**: `zedLlmFetch` builds the request, then `if (shouldRefreshZedLlmToken(response)) response = await buildRequest(true)` (zedAuth.js:314-317). `shouldRefreshZedLlmToken` fires on HTTP 401 OR when the response carries the `x-zed-expired-token` or `x-zed-outdated-token` headers (zedAuth.js:291-297), so an expired 50-minute LLM token is re-minted and the call transparently retried once.
- **OpenProxy**: `ZedExecutor::execute_request` resolves the token once (zed.rs:393), posts once (zed.rs:433), and on any non-2xx returns `Err(format!("Zed returned HTTP {code}: {message}"))` (zed.rs:436-440). There is no expired/outdated-token header check and no retry.
- **Gap**: No 401 or `x-zed-expired-token`/`x-zed-outdated-token` detection and no token-refresh retry on the Zed completion path.
- **Why it matters**: The Zed LLM token has a 50-minute lifetime and Zed also rotates it server-side. Once it lapses, a long-lived CLI session (Claude Code / Codex left open) starts failing every Zed completion with a raw upstream error until the user reconnects, where 9router recovers transparently. Zed's expiry signalling is header-based, so even a non-401 response carrying `x-zed-expired-token` is missed.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/shared/zedAuth.js:291-297, :299-319. OpenProxy: src/core/executor/zed.rs:393, :433-440.
- **Parity fix**: Port `shouldRefreshZedLlmToken` (status 401, or presence of `x-zed-expired-token` / `x-zed-outdated-token`) and retry the `/completions` POST once after forcing a fresh `fetch_llm_token`, before surfacing the error.

## P178-001 · P1 · openai→cursor request translation — `max_tokens`

- **9router**: `openaiToCursorRequest` destructures `{ user, metadata, tool_choice, stream_options, system, ...rest }`, replaces messages, and then returns `{ ...rest, messages, max_tokens: DEFAULT_MIN_TOKENS }` (openai-to-cursor.js:178-183). `DEFAULT_MIN_TOKENS = 32000` (runtimeConfig.js:66). The client's `max_tokens` is UNCONDITIONALLY discarded — 9router always sends 32000 upstream.
- **OpenProxy**: After the same field strip, OpenProxy reads `body.max_tokens.or(body.max_completion_tokens)` and keeps it when `> 0`, only defaulting to 32000 otherwise (src/core/translator/request/openai_to_cursor.rs:256-266). The in-source comment states this is a deliberate change: "9router bug fixed: previously always hardcoded 32000, ignoring the client's requested value".
- **Gap**: For any client that sets `max_tokens`/`max_completion_tokens`, OpenProxy forwards that value upstream to Cursor while 9router always forwards 32000.
- **Why it matters**: Output length is a client-contracted parameter. A client that caps generation at 4096 or 8192 (Claude Code, Codex and Cline all do) gets 32000 in 9router and its own cap in OpenProxy, so Cursor bills and emits against a different ceiling, completions can overrun the client's declared budget, and response-cache keys / declared max-output differ between the two proxies. The divergence is silent and one-sided — it only shows up on requests that set the field.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/translator/request/openai-to-cursor.js:178-183 · open-sse/config/runtimeConfig.js:66 | openproxy: src/core/translator/request/openai_to_cursor.rs:256-266
- **Parity fix**: Replace the client-value branch at src/core/translator/request/openai_to_cursor.rs:256-266 with an unconditional `rest["max_tokens"] = Value::Number(32000.into());` (hoist the 32000 constant next to the other Qoder/Cursor constants) so the upstream payload matches openai-to-cursor.js:181. If the divergence is intentional it belongs in `docs/parity-9router.md` as an explicit entry rather than only an inline comment.

## P179-001 · P1 · openproxy: src/core/executor/codex.rs:405-409 (transform_request_body) | 9router: open-sse/executors/codex.js:428

- **9router**: codex.js:428 tests `if (!body.instructions || body.instructions.trim() === "")`. The `.trim()` collapses all-whitespace strings, so an instructions value of "   ", "\n\t ", or any other whitespace-only string is treated as absent and is replaced by CODEX_DEFAULT_INSTRUCTIONS.
- **OpenProxy**: codex.rs:405-409 uses `.filter(|s| !s.is_empty())`, which tests raw byte length only. A whitespace-only instructions string is non-empty, so it is preserved and the default is NOT injected.
- **Gap**: The default-instructions trigger tests emptiness in 9Router but only raw non-emptiness in OpenProxy, so a whitespace-only `instructions` value suppresses the default Codex agent prompt in OpenProxy while 9Router still injects it.
- **Why it matters**: A whitespace-only system/instructions value is a realistic outcome of prompt templating, of a client that sends a system message whose content is blank-but-not-empty, and of the array-content case in P0-001 once partially addressed. Under 9Router the request still receives the full Codex agent contract; under OpenProxy the model receives whitespace and loses the entire agent instruction set — the same silent persona/policy loss as P0-001, reached through a different input. The divergence is also a pure behavioral mismatch in a documented trigger, not a stylistic one.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/executors/codex.js:428 — `if (!body.instructions || body.instructions.trim() === "")`. openproxy: src/core/executor/codex.rs:405-409 — `.filter(|s| !s.is_empty())`. Verified by executing a standalone Rust harness containing the exact codex.rs:405-409 expression: instructions "   " => KEEP, "\n\t " => KEEP; the JS `.trim() === ""` predicate yields DEFAULT for both. The three matching cases (missing, "", non-string) agree.
- **Parity fix**: In src/core/executor/codex.rs:405-409, change the emptiness predicate to a trim-aware test so it matches codex.js:428 exactly. Replace `.filter(|s| !s.is_empty())` with a filter that treats a string whose `trim()` is empty as absent, e.g. `.filter(|s| !s.trim().is_empty())`, leaving the `unwrap_or(Self::DEFAULT_CODEX_INSTRUCTIONS)` fallback in place. This preserves the three already-matching cases (missing key, empty string, non-string value) and closes the whitespace-only gap without altering the non-whitespace KEEP path.

## P180-001 · P1 · requestDetails observability records

- **9router**: Every request writes a `requestDetails` row via `saveRequestDetail(buildRequestDetail({...}))` — three distinct sites: a pre-stream "in progress" row with a stable `streamDetailId` (open-sse/handlers/chatCore/streamingHandler.js:92-107), a completion update reusing the SAME id (streamingHandler.js:113-141), and a full non-streaming row carrying `request` (full request config incl. messages/tools), `providerRequest`, `providerResponse`, `response{content,thinking,finish_reason}` and `latency{ttft,total}` (nonStreamingHandler.js:378-401). Error paths also write rows (chatCore.js:385-400, :452-467).
- **OpenProxy**: The `requestDetails` TABLE exists (`src/db/sqlite/repo/request_repo.rs`, DDL in `sqlite/schema.rs`) and a `save` function is defined, but nothing ever calls it — grep for `request_repo` returns only the `pub mod` declaration. The `/api/usage/request-details` endpoint instead synthesizes rows from the `usageHistory` table (`build_request_detail_records`, src/server/api/usage.rs:1695-1735), reading `request`, `providerRequest`, `providerResponse`, `response`, `latency` and `id` from `entry.extra` — but `track_request` always constructs `extra: Default::default()` (src/core/usage/tracker.rs:89), so every one of those fields is permanently absent.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy never writes a requestDetails row; its request-details endpoint synthesizes them from usageHistory where every payload field is unconditionally empty.
- **Why it matters**: The dashboard's Request Details tab (open-sse `RequestDetailsTab.js` ↔ `usage/RequestDetailsTab.tsx`) shows no request body, no provider request/response, no response text and no TTFT/total latency for any request — the "what exactly was sent upstream and what came back" debugging surface that 9Router provides is empty. Latency reads as `{ttft:0,total:0}` (RequestLatency::default) for every row, so the UI's latency column is uniformly zero.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/handlers/chatCore/streamingHandler.js:92-107 (pre-stream row), open-sse/handlers/chatCore/streamingHandler.js:118-141 (completion update), open-sse/handlers/chatCore/nonStreamingHandler.js:378-401 (full non-streaming row), open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:385-400 (error row). openproxy: src/db/sqlite/repo/request_repo.rs:7 (`pub fn save` — no callers), src/db/sqlite/repo/mod.rs:12 (only the mod decl), src/server/api/usage.rs:1695-1735 (synthesizes from usageHistory + entry.extra), src/core/usage/tracker.rs:89 (`extra: Default::default()`).
- **Parity fix**: Populate `UsageEntry.extra` in `track_request` (or add a dedicated `request_repo::save` call at the three 9Router sites) with the same shape 9Router's `buildRequestDetail` produces: `extra["id"]`, `extra["latency"] = {ttft, total}`, `extra["request"]` (the `extractRequestConfig` projection), `extra["providerRequest"]`, `extra["providerResponse"]`, `extra["response"]`, and `extra["status"]`. Generate the stream id as `${ts_ms}-${random9}` (9Router's `streamDetailId` format) and reuse it for the pre-stream and completion writes so the row updates in place rather than duplicating.

## P181-001 · P1 · src/core/config/error_config.rs:182-206 → src/core/combo/mod.rs:570-604

- **9router**: `ERROR_RULES` contains five status rules: 401, **402**, 403, 404, 429 (errorConfig.js:71-75). A 402 (`billing_error`) matches `{ status: 402, cooldownMs: COOLDOWN.long }` → `{shouldFallback: true, cooldownMs: 120000}` with `newBackoffLevel` undefined, so the account's `backoffLevel` is left untouched (`applyErrorState`, accountFallback.js:202-215).
- **OpenProxy**: `ERROR_RULES` only has status 401, 403, 404, 429 (error_config.rs:184, 190, 196, 202). A 402 falls through to `ErrorClassification::NoMatch` → `FallbackDecision { should_fallback: true, cooldown: TRANSIENT_COOLDOWN, new_backoff_level: None }` (combo/mod.rs:587-592), and the caller then does `decision.new_backoff_level.unwrap_or(current_backoff + 1)` (chat.rs:2889), incrementing the backoff level.
- **Gap**: The 402 status rule is missing, so a billing failure gets a 30s transient lock instead of 120s — and additionally inflates `backoffLevel`.
