- **Why it matters**: A credit-exhausted provider is re-dispatched 4× more often than in 9router, and the escalating backoffLevel degrades the very next legitimate 429. The dashboard CooldownTimer shows 0:30 where 9router shows 2:00, so users read the retry cadence as a bug.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/config/errorConfig.js:45-48, 71-75; .tmp/9router/open-sse/services/accountFallback.js:23-50, 202-215. OpenProxy: src/core/config/error_config.rs:131-206, 182-206; src/core/combo/mod.rs:570-604; src/server/api/chat.rs:2889
- **Parity fix**: Insert `ErrorRule { text: None, status: Some(402), cooldown: Some(Duration::from_millis(LONG_MS)), backoff: false }` between the 401 and 403 entries in error_config.rs, and make the fallback caller use `decision.new_backoff_level.unwrap_or(current_backoff)` for non-backoff classifications so a fixed-cooldown rule does not bump the level.

## P182-001 · P1 · src/core/executor/codex.rs:676-721; src/server/api/chat.rs:2751-2762, 3336-3371; src/core/config/error_config.rs:25

- **9router**: `parseUpstreamError(response, executor)` calls `executor.parseError`, and the Codex executor maps `usage_limit_reached` to `{status: 429, message, resetsAtMs}` from `resets_at` / `resets_in_seconds` (codex.js:374-391). chatCore.js:455, 474 carries `resetsAtMs` into `markAccountUnavailable`, which uses it as the exact cooldown, clamped to `MAX_RATE_LIMIT_COOLDOWN_MS` (30 min) for non-antigravity providers, un-clamped for antigravity, and resets `backoffLevel` to 0 (auth.js:254-260; errorConfig.js:42).
- **OpenProxy**: `CodexExecutor::parse_error` implements the same extraction (codex.rs:676-721) but is called only from its own unit test (codex.rs:1260). The runtime path is `extract_error_message_and_retry_after_with_body` (chat.rs:4694-4739), which reads only the `Retry-After` header and a `retryAfter` JSON field; `codex`'s `resets_at` is never read. The resulting cooldown (chat.rs:2760-2762) is the raw `(retry_after - now)` delta with no 30-minute cap, and `MAX_RATE_LIMIT_COOLDOWN_MS` (error_config.rs:25) is dead code — `grep` finds no other reference.
- **Gap**: Provider-reported quota reset times are neither parsed from the Codex error body nor clamped, and `resets_at` is computed but discarded.
- **Why it matters**: Two failure modes: (a) a Codex account reporting `usage_limit_reached` with a 6-hour `resets_at` is hammered on the 2s→5min exponential ladder instead of being parked, burning retries and surfacing repeated 429s to the client; (b) a provider that returns `Retry-After: 21600` locks the account for 6 hours in OpenProxy where 9router caps it at 30 minutes, taking a healthy account out of rotation for 12× longer.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/executors/codex.js:374-391; .tmp/9router/open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:455, 474; .tmp/9router/src/sse/services/auth.js:254-260; .tmp/9router/open-sse/config/errorConfig.js:41-42. OpenProxy: src/core/executor/codex.rs:676-721, 1260; src/server/api/chat.rs:2751-2762, 4694-4739, 3336-3371; src/core/config/error_config.rs:22-25
- **Parity fix**: Add a `resets_at_ms: Option<u64>` field to `ComboAttemptError`; in `extract_error_message_and_retry_after_with_body` (or a per-provider hook mirroring `executor.parseError`) populate it from `error.resets_at` / `error.resets_in_seconds` for codex. In the cooldown computation at chat.rs:2760-2762 use `min(resets_at - now, MAX_RATE_LIMIT_COOLDOWN_MS)` for non-antigravity providers, the un-clamped value for antigravity, and pass `backoff_level: 0` in that case.

## P183-001 · P1 · src/server/api/chat.rs (no 409 branch)

- **9router**: When the provider is antigravity and the result status is 409 or 429, `handleSingleModelChat` calls `handleAntigravityQuotaError` (chat.js:312-321), which refreshes live quota, returns the exact per-model `resetAt` when exhausted, applies a 3-strikes-in-60s breaker that blocks the pair for 15 minutes, and forces `shouldFallback: true` so the request never hits the exhausted account again (antigravityQuota.js:138-187). The quota cache is also consulted pre-dispatch by `getProviderCredentials` (auth.js:65-79, 91-112).
- **OpenProxy**: `grep -n "409" src/server/api/chat.rs` returns no hits and there is no antigravity quota cache. A 409 from any provider falls through `ERROR_RULES` to `NoMatch` → 30s transient cooldown, `should_fallback: true`, and the connection is simply re-selected 30 seconds later.
- **Gap**: No antigravity 409/429 live-quota resolution exists, so an exhausted Antigravity pool is re-dispatched every 30s.
- **Why it matters**: Antigravity signals pool exhaustion with 409 as well as 429 and the real reset can be hours out. Without the cache every retry re-hits the exhausted pool, the CLI agent appears to hang on repeated 409s, and the client never receives the clean 503+Retry-After that the exhausted-accounts branch would produce. The 3-strike 15-minute breaker that protects against transient-409 poisoning is also absent.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/sse/handlers/chat.js:312-321, 325-330; .tmp/9router/src/sse/services/antigravityQuota.js:138-187; .tmp/9router/src/sse/services/auth.js:65-79, 91-112. OpenProxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:2759-2893 (only the generic check_fallback_error path); src/core/config/error_config.rs:131-206 (no 409 rule)
- **Parity fix**: Port `handleAntigravityQuotaError` and the quota cache: add a 409/429 branch in the non-ok arm of `forward_with_provider_fallback` that, for `provider == "antigravity"`, refreshes live quota, sets `retry_after` to the exact `resetAt` and `cooldown` to the un-clamped delta, and add a `getAntigravityQuotaCache` check to `select_connection` mirroring auth.js:70-78.

## P184-001 · P1 · src/server/api/chat.rs:1697-1706

- **9router**: When no account is selectable and nothing has been excluded yet, `handleSingleModelChat` returns `errorResponse(HTTP_STATUS.NOT_FOUND, "No active credentials for provider: ${provider}")` (chat.js:244-246) — 404.
- **OpenProxy**: The Direct arm of `chat_completions_impl` returns `attempt_error_response(error)` directly (chat.rs:910-915), so the `ComboAttemptError` at chat.rs:1697-1706 is emitted verbatim: `status: 400`, `message: "No credentials for provider: {provider}"`.
- **Gap**: A direct (non-combo) request to a provider with no usable credentials returns 400 `No credentials for provider: X` instead of 404 `No active credentials for provider: X`.
- **Why it matters**: OpenAI/Anthropic SDKs treat 400 as a malformed request the caller must fix and will not retry or re-read configuration; 404 signals a routing/credential problem and is what clients use to prompt for re-auth. A mistyped provider prefix (`foo/bar`) therefore produces "bad request" in OpenProxy and "provider not found" in 9router.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/sse/handlers/chat.js:244-246. OpenProxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:1697-1706, 910-915
- **Parity fix**: Change chat.rs:1698 to `status: if retry_after.is_some() { 503 } else { 404 }` and chat.rs:1702 to `format!("No active credentials for provider: {provider}")`. Note the combo path already overrides this to 503 via the `contains("no credentials")` check at combo/mod.rs:1020, which matches 9router's combo branch.

## P185-001 · P1 · src/server/api/chat.rs:265; src/server/api/mod.rs:2238-2301; src/types/mod.rs:524

- **9router**: `requireApiKey` and `requireLogin` are two independent settings, both defaulting to `true` (settingsRepo.js:26-27). `handleChat` gates the /v1 API-key check on `settings.requireApiKey` ONLY (chat.js:71-81), while `requireLogin` guards the dashboard. The Endpoint page renders a dedicated `requireApiKey` toggle (EndpointPageClient.js:986-991) separate from the password/login control.
- **OpenProxy**: There is no `requireApiKey` setting at all. `chat_completions_impl` gates on `state.db.snapshot().settings.require_login` (chat.rs:265), `UpdateSettingsRequest` (mod.rs:2238-2301) has no `require_api_key` field so the dashboard's `PATCH {"requireApiKey": …}` (web/src/components/EndpointPageClient.tsx:413) is silently dropped, and `Settings` only declares `require_login` (types/mod.rs:524).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's "Require API key" toggle is a no-op — the /v1 401 gate is driven by the dashboard's "Require login" setting instead.
- **Why it matters**: A user who disables "Require login" to browse the dashboard also silently opens every `/v1/*` route; a user who turns "Require API key" ON while login is off gets no 401 at all. `GET /api/settings` also never returns `requireApiKey`, so the toggle always renders OFF and misreports the server's actual state.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/sse/handlers/chat.js:71-81; .tmp/9router/src/lib/db/repos/settingsRepo.js:26-27; .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/endpoint/EndpointPageClient.js:986-991, 248. OpenProxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:265; src/server/api/mod.rs:2238-2301; src/types/mod.rs:524; web/src/components/EndpointPageClient.tsx:130, 365, 413
- **Parity fix**: Add `require_api_key: bool` to `Settings` (camelCase `requireApiKey`, default `true`) and to `UpdateSettingsRequest`; switch chat.rs:265 to read `settings.require_api_key`, and switch `require_dashboard_session` to `require_login` (it already does). The dashboard toggle then persists and gates /v1 independently of the dashboard session.

## P186-001 · P1 · src/server/api/chat.rs:3756-3795

- **9router**: The guard fires on ANY non-empty content-type that contains neither `text/event-stream` nor `application/json` (streamingHandler.js:62-63) — so JSON is deliberately allowed through. It preserves the upstream status (`providerResponse.status || 502`, :69), extracts `<title>`, strips tags, collapses newlines, clamps to 160 chars, and returns `{error:{message:"[<status>]: <shortMsg>"}}` with `Content-Type: application/json` and `streamController.handleError` invoked (:70-79).
- **OpenProxy**: The guard fires only when the content-type contains `text/html`, `application/json` or `text/plain` (chat.rs:3760-3762) — so it BLOCKS `application/json` (which 9router allows) and IGNORES `text/xml`, `application/problem+json`, `text/csv`, `application/xhtml+xml` (which 9router blocks). It always returns 502 (chat.rs:3788), passes the raw body into the message with no tag stripping and a 500-byte clamp, and does not notify a stream controller.
- **Gap**: The non-SSE stream guard triggers on a different content-type set, hard-codes 502 instead of the upstream status, and stops sanitising the message text.
- **Why it matters**: (a) An upstream that answers a streaming request 200 with `application/json` (opencode-go does this — see the NOTE at compat.rs:166-170) is turned into a 502 in OpenProxy but piped and handled in 9router. (b) An upstream XML/CSV error page is piped as if it were SSE in OpenProxy, producing garbage frames instead of a clean JSON error. (c) Raw HTML tags reach `error.message`, which 9router's own comment warns "the UI may render as HTML".
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/handlers/chatCore/streamingHandler.js:62-80. OpenProxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:3756-3795
- **Parity fix**: Invert the condition to `!ct.is_empty() && !ct.contains("text/event-stream") && !ct.contains("application/json")`; use `status` (falling back to 502) instead of `StatusCode::BAD_GATEWAY`; and reuse the existing `extract_html_title` / `strip_html_tags` / 160-char clamp from `src/core/utils/error.rs:267-293` for the message.

## P187-001 · P1 · src/server/api/chat.rs:3766-3771

- **9router**: `.slice(0, 160)` operates on a JS string (UTF-16 code units) and is therefore always safe; the extracted `<title>` is additionally run through `.replace(/<[^>]*>/g, '')` and `.replace(/[\r\n]+/g, ' ')` (streamingHandler.js:66).
- **OpenProxy**: `let msg = String::from_utf8_lossy(&body_bytes);` then `if msg.len() > 500 { format!("{}…", &msg[..500]) }` (chat.rs:3766-3768). `String::len()` is a BYTE length and `&msg[..500]` slices at byte offset 500, which panics when offset 500 is not a UTF-8 character boundary.
- **Gap**: The non-SSE guard truncates a Rust `String` by byte index and panics on a non-UTF-8 boundary.
- **Why it matters**: Cloudflare/nginx/CloudFront 5xx pages routinely contain non-ASCII (localised titles, curly quotes, emoji in a product name). One such page panics the handler, so the client gets a dropped connection with no JSON error at all — strictly worse than the clean 502 9router produces.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/handlers/chatCore/streamingHandler.js:64-68. OpenProxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:3765-3771
- **Parity fix**: Use `msg.chars().take(160).collect::<String>()` (as the tracing line at chat.rs:3777 already does) instead of `&msg[..500]`, and strip HTML tags before truncating.

## P188-001 · P1 · src/server/api/chat.rs:3851-3943 (no terminal synthesis)

- **9router**: For a Responses→Responses passthrough, `onAbortTerminal = buildAbortedResponsesTerminalBytes` (streamingHandler.js:84-88) is threaded into `pipeWithDisconnect` (streamHandler.js:252). On disconnect, stall, or upstream error the wrapper emits `event: response.failed` with `error.code: "stream_disconnected"` / `message: "stream closed before response.completed"` followed by `data: [DONE]` (responsesStreamHelpers.js:29-50, streamHandler.js:104-112, 158-161), and the transform's `flush()` does the same when the upstream simply EOFs (stream.js:465-480).
- **OpenProxy**: `grep -rn "stream_disconnected|response\.failed" src/` finds only a *decode* branch at core/translator/response/openai_responses.rs:1262. The reqwest stream loop (chat.rs:3851-3943) just `return`s after writing one `data: {"error":…}` frame; the hyper loop (chat.rs:4012+) is the same. No terminal event is synthesized.
- **Gap**: When a Responses passthrough stream ends without a terminal event, OpenProxy never emits `response.failed` + `[DONE]`.
- **Why it matters**: Codex CLI and the OpenAI Responses SDK wait for a terminal event. With the 180s total timeout (P0-004) and the 180s stall timer, a long run is cut off mid-stream and the client reports "stream ended without a terminal event" and re-runs the whole turn, instead of surfacing the actionable `response.failed { code: stream_disconnected }` that 9router sends.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/utils/responsesStreamHelpers.js:29-50; .tmp/9router/open-sse/utils/streamHandler.js:29-30, 104-112, 158-161, 252; .tmp/9router/open-sse/utils/stream.js:264-270, 465-480; .tmp/9router/open-sse/handlers/chatCore/streamingHandler.js:84-88. OpenProxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:3851-3943, 4012-4079; src/core/translator/response/openai_responses.rs:1262
- **Parity fix**: Add `build_aborted_responses_terminal_bytes()` (port of responsesStreamHelpers.js:29-50) and, in both the reqwest and hyper stream arms, set a `responses_terminal_seen` flag whenever a terminal event type is seen; on stall (chat.rs:3855), on `Ok(Err(_))` (chat.rs:3929) and at end-of-stream (before the final `finish_request`), emit the `response.failed` frame + `data: [DONE]` when `source_format == target_format == Format::OpenAiResponses` and the flag is false.

## P189-001 · P1 · src/server/api/chat.rs:4839-4848, 4884-4897

- **9router**: `errorResponse` always sets `"Content-Type": "application/json"` and `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` (error.js:30-38); `unavailableResponse` sets `Content-Type: application/json` + `Retry-After` (error.js:119-127).
- **OpenProxy**: `attempt_error_response` returns `(status_code, Body::from(body_bytes)).into_response()` (chat.rs:4841) — axum's `(StatusCode, Body)` `IntoResponse` does not set a Content-Type. The only headers added afterwards come from `with_cors_response` (chat.rs:4884-4897): `Access-Control-Allow-Origin/Headers/Methods`. So the verbatim error path ships an HTML error page, a plain-text gateway error, or a provider JSON blob with no declared media type.
- **Gap**: Error responses produced through the raw-body passthrough carry no `Content-Type` header at all.
- **Why it matters**: `fetch`/undici and several OpenAI-compatible SDKs branch on Content-Type before parsing; an unlabelled body degrades to an opaque "network error"/"Unexpected token <" instead of the provider's real message. Reverse proxies and `curl -i` users also cannot tell JSON from HTML.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/utils/error.js:30-38, 119-127. OpenProxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:4839-4848, 4884-4897
- **Parity fix**: When returning the raw body, set `content-type: application/json` explicitly (or better, resolve P0-003 and always return the OpenAI envelope). `let mut response = (status_code, [(header::CONTENT_TYPE, "application/json")], body_bytes).into_response();`

## P190-001 · P1 · src/server/api/quota_auto_ping.rs:733-767 (spawn_boot_resume)

- **9router**: `runHeavyStartup()` in src/shared/services/initializeApp.js:82-108 reads settings and resumes the two tunnels with two INDEPENDENT `if` statements guarded by separate once-per-process latches: `if (settings.tunnelEnabled && !g.tunnelAutoResumed) { g.tunnelAutoResumed = true; safeRestartTunnel("startup")... }` (:86-91), then `if (settings.tailscaleEnabled && !g.tailscaleAutoResumed) { g.tailscaleAutoResumed = true; safeRestartTailscale("startup")... }` (:93-98). A following independent `if (settings.tunnelEnabled) ensureCloudflared()` also runs. When BOTH flags are persisted true, both tunnels are restored.
- **OpenProxy**: `spawn_boot_resume` chains the two resume branches with `else if`: `if settings.tunnel_enabled { tunnel_mgr.start(Cloudflare, port) } else if settings.tailscale_enabled { tunnel_mgr.start(Tailscale, port) }` (src/server/api/quota_auto_ping.rs:740-767). The tailscale branch is only reachable when the tunnel flag is FALSE. Both flags persisted true → only the Cloudflare tunnel restarts; the Tailscale funnel is silently left down. There is also no `ensureCloudflared()`-equivalent step.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's boot resume uses `if / else if`, so when both `tunnelEnabled` and `tailscaleEnabled` are persisted, the Tailscale funnel is never restored on restart, whereas 9router resumes both.
- **Why it matters**: This is exactly the "survives a server restart" contract for remote access. The dashboard still reports the persisted Tailscale state as enabled, and the endpoint page's Tailscale status poll will show the funnel as down — but nothing in the restart path attempts recovery, so a user who relies on the Tailscale URL to reach OpenProxy from another machine finds it dead after any restart and must manually re-enable it. Because the persisted setting and the live process state disagree, the UI shows a persistent enabled-but-unreachable state that only a manual toggle clears. The asymmetry is also silent: no warning is logged, since the branch is simply never taken.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/shared/services/initializeApp.js:86-91 (independent `if (settings.tunnelEnabled && !g.tunnelAutoResumed)`) and :93-98 (independent `if (settings.tailscaleEnabled && !g.tailscaleAutoResumed)`), both inside runHeavyStartup. OpenProxy: src/server/api/quota_auto_ping.rs:740 (`if settings.tunnel_enabled {`), :755 (`} else if settings.tailscale_enabled {`).
- **Parity fix**: Change `} else if settings.tailscale_enabled {` to a standalone `} if settings.tailscale_enabled {` in spawn_boot_resume (src/server/api/quota_auto_ping.rs:755), matching 9router's two independent guards. Since each branch calls `tunnel_mgr.start(...)` with a distinct provider, the two are already independent operations and no additional latch is needed for single-boot semantics. If repeated-start protection is wanted, add per-provider once-flags as 9router does with `g.tunnelAutoResumed` / `g.tailscaleAutoResumed`.

## P191-001 · P1 · web/src/components/cli-tools/ApiKeySelect.tsx (whole file) and web/src/components/cli-tools/BaseUrlSelect.tsx:147-187 (save path)

- **9router**: `cliEndpointPresets.js` builds TWO independent localStorage-backed stores via a `createStore({storageKey, changeEvent, itemField, ...})` factory: `endpoints` keyed on `"9router.cliToolEndpointPresets"` with `itemField:"baseUrl"` (:52-60) and `apiKeys` keyed on `"9router.cliToolApiKeyPresets"` with `itemField:"key"` (:62-66). Both export read/subscribe/upsert/remove (:68-76). `ApiKeySelect.js` consumes the key store: on mount `sync()` reads saved keys and `subscribeKeyPresets(sync)` re-reads on every write (:16-19); saved keys are appended to the dropdown as `{value: "saved:<name>", label: p.key}` (:25); selecting `__save_key__` calls `upsertKeyPreset((value||"").trim())` so a typed custom key can be persisted by name (:41-44); a delete button removes the saved preset and falls back to another option (:63-71). 13 tool cards import ApiKeySelect.
- **OpenProxy**: `ApiKeySelect.tsx` is a plain component with NO persistence at all. It has no `readKeyPresets`/`upsertKeyPreset`, no `subscribe`, no `saved:` option, and no `__save_key__` branch — the options array is only `apiKeys.map(...)` plus `Custom...` (:85-89). `BaseUrlSelect.tsx` re-implements the endpoint store inline as module-local `readSavedPresets`/`writeSavedPresets` bound to `openproxy.cliToolEndpointPresets` (:6, :34-50) with no cross-component `subscribe`/CustomEvent channel, so a preset saved on one tool card is not reflected on another open card. There is no `cliEndpointPresets.ts` module at all (grep for `readKeyPresets|upsertKeyPreset|rememberEndpoint` across web/src returns nothing); only 4 cards import ApiKeySelect (GrokBuild, Jcode, OpenClaw, DefaultToolCard) vs 13 in 9router.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy ships only one of 9router's two CLI-tool preset stores — the browser-local API-key store (`9router.cliToolApiKeyPresets`) is missing entirely, and the endpoint store that does exist is a private per-component copy with no cross-card sync.
- **Why it matters**: The API-key store is what makes a hand-typed key reusable: a user who is wiring several CLI tools to the same OpenProxy instance types/pastes a key once, saves it under a name, and thereafter every tool card's dropdown offers it. Without it, every one of the 4 remaining ApiKeySelect cards forces a full manual re-entry of the same secret on every visit, and the value is lost on refresh. The endpoint store's missing cross-card sync is a second, independent loss: `rememberEndpoint` in 9router writes the applied endpoint and dispatches `9router:endpoint-presets-changed` (:19) so sibling cards and the custom-input slot reconcile; OpenProxy's `writeSavedPresets` has no event, so a preset saved via BaseUrlSelect on card A never appears in card B's dropdown until a hard reload, and the in-progress `customInput` is not adopted into the `saved:` slot.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/cli-tools/components/cliEndpointPresets.js:52-66 (two createStore instances, keys `9router.cliToolEndpointPresets` / `9router.cliToolApiKeyPresets`), :19 (write dispatches changeEvent), :68-76 (read/subscribe/upsert/remove exports); ApiKeySelect.js:16-19 (mount read + subscribe), :25 (saved: option), :41-44 (__save_key__ → upsertKeyPreset), :63-71 (delete saved). 13 consumer cards under src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/cli-tools/components/. OpenProxy: web/src/components/cli-tools/ApiKeySelect.tsx:78-98 (select has no saved-key options and no save branch), 4 consumers only; web/src/components/cli-tools/BaseUrlSelect.tsx:6,34-50 (module-local STORAGE_KEY + read/write, no subscribe/event), :147-187 (save/delete touch only local state).
- **Parity fix**: Port `cliEndpointPresets.js` to `web/src/components/cli-tools/cliEndpointPresets.ts` as a single shared module exporting two `createStore` instances (`openproxy.cliToolEndpointPresets` / `openproxy.cliToolApiKeyPresets`) with the same upsert/remove/subscribe semantics and the same `CustomEvent` change notifications, plus the `rememberEndpoint(baseUrl, {tunnelPublicUrl, tailscaleUrl, cloudUrl})` helper that skips built-in URLs. Rewrite `ApiKeySelect.tsx` to consume `readKeyPresets`/`upsertKeyPreset`/`deleteKeyPreset`/`subscribeKeyPresets` and render the `saved:<name>` options, the `+ Save current as...` entry and the delete button. Replace `BaseUrlSelect.tsx`'s module-local read/write with the shared store's `readPresets`/`upsertPreset`/`deletePreset`/`subscribePresets` and add the `rememberEndpoint(...)` call in each tool card's apply-success branch (9router does this in all 13 cards, e.g. CodexToolCard.js:124). Keep the `openproxy.*` key names — only the store's internal key strings are unobservable to users, unlike 9router's own key names.

## P192-001 · P1 · web/src/components/usage/OverviewCards.tsx:5-12, 25-46

- **9router**: OverviewCards renders a 5-column grid (1 / sm:2 / md:3 / lg:5): "Total Requests" → stats.totalRequests, "Total Input Tokens" → stats.totalPromptTokens (text-primary), "Cached Tokens" → stats.totalCachedTokens (text-info), "Output Tokens" → stats.totalCompletionTokens (text-success), "Est. Cost" → `~{fmtCost(stats.totalCost)}` in text-warning with the sub-caption "Estimated, not actual billing" (9router: `src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/usage/components/OverviewCards.js:13-34`).
- **OpenProxy**: OverviewCards renders a 3-column grid (1 / sm:2 / md:3, no lg tier) with only three cards: "Total Tokens" (prompt+completion+reasoning), "Input Tokens", "Output Tokens". There is no Cached Tokens card and no Est. Cost card; requests is demoted to an 11px sub-caption under Total Tokens (web/src/components/usage/OverviewCards.tsx:25-46). The Stats interface at :5-12 even declares totalCost but it is never read.
- **Gap**: The usage Overview counters are missing two of five cards — Cached Tokens and Est. Cost — and no card reports Total Requests as a headline metric.
- **Why it matters**: Cost is the number users open the Usage page to see, and the backend already ships it: build_usage_stats populates total_cached_tokens and total_cost on every payload (src/server/usage_stream.rs:242-251, :325-326), and the value is only surfaced inside the per-row UsageTable. A user monitoring spend on the free-tier OAuth providers has to expand every table row to learn it, and cache-hit rate — the main lever for reducing quota burn — has no overview card at all.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/usage/components/OverviewCards.js:13-34 (5 cards incl. stats.totalCachedTokens and stats.totalCost). OpenProxy: web/src/components/usage/OverviewCards.tsx:5-12 (totalCost declared), :25-46 (only 3 cards rendered). Backend confirms the data exists: src/server/usage_stream.rs:242-251, :325-326.
- **Parity fix**: Extend the grid to the 9router 5-card layout in web/src/components/usage/OverviewCards.tsx: add a "Total Requests" card (stats.totalRequests, 2xl bold), a "Cached Tokens" card (stats.totalCachedTokens, text-info), and an "Est. Cost" card rendering `~{fmtCost(stats.totalCost)}` in text-warning with the "Estimated, not actual billing" sub-caption; restore the lg:grid-cols-5 tier and add `totalCachedTokens?: number` to the Stats interface. Keep OpenProxy's compact Intl.NumberFormat for the value if desired, but restore the five metrics.

## P193-001 · P1 · web/src/components/usage/ProviderLimits/index.tsx:186-200, 565-584, 654-712

- **9router**: fetchConnections(targetPage = page) builds a URLSearchParams with page, pageSize, accountStatus, sort:"priority" and an optional provider, then GETs `/api/providers/client?…`, and stores the response's `pagination`, `providerOptions` and `totals` into state (9router: `src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/usage/components/ProviderLimits/index.js:176-216`). The page renders a pagination footer with getConnectionsPaginationSummary ("Showing X-Y of Z"), an ACCOUNT_PAGE_SIZE_OPTIONS select (10/20/50/100 + Custom) with a numeric custom input clamped to ACCOUNT_PAGE_SIZE_MAX=500, a "Page N / M" label and First/Prev/Next/Last buttons (index.js:1320-1440). A second `<select>` bound to `accountFilter` filters All accounts / Active / Turned off (index.js:919-939).
- **OpenProxy**: fetchConnections() takes no arguments, GETs the unfiltered `/api/providers` list, and returns every connection (web/src/components/usage/ProviderLimits/index.tsx:186-200). There is no `pagination`, `page`, `pageSize`, `accountFilter`, `providerOptions` or `totals` state; refreshAll and the 60 s interval call the same arg-less fetchConnections (index.tsx:565-584). The whole pagination footer and the account-status select are absent. The eight helpers that would drive them — getSafePagination, getSafeTotals, getPaginationPageValue, shouldResetPage, getPageSizeLabel, getConnectionsPaginationSummary, sortRequestFromExpiringFirst, plus ACCOUNT_FILTER_OPTIONS / ACCOUNT_PAGE_SIZE_OPTIONS — are ported into web/src/components/usage/ProviderLimits/utils.ts but referenced zero times from index.tsx (verified by grep count 0 for each).
- **Gap**: The quota page lost server-side pagination and the account-status filter: OpenProxy loads every connection at once and renders them all in one unbounded grid, with no page controls and no Active/Turned-off filter.
- **Why it matters**: refreshAll fires every 60 s and Promise.all's a quota fetch for *every* eligible connection. With the 100-account setups these OAuth providers accumulate, one 60-second tick issues 100 upstream quota requests (Claude throttled to every 10th tick), and each response re-renders 100 cards — the page becomes slow and can trip provider rate limits purely from having the tab open. The user also loses the primary reason the account filter exists: narrowing to just active or just turned-off accounts.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/usage/components/ProviderLimits/index.js:176-216 (fetchConnections with page/pageSize/accountStatus), :919-939 (accountFilter select), :1320-1440 (pagination footer), :474-486 (refreshAll→fetchConnections(page)). OpenProxy: web/src/components/usage/ProviderLimits/index.tsx:186-200 (fetchConnections(), no params), :565-584 (refreshAll→fetchConnections()), :654-712 (interval effect); dead helpers in web/src/components/usage/ProviderLimits/utils.ts:124-175.
- **Parity fix**: Add `page`, `pageSize`, `accountFilter`, `pagination`, `totals` and `providerOptions` state; change fetchConnections to accept a target page and call `GET /api/providers/client?page=…&pageSize=…&accountStatus=…&sort=priority[&provider=…]` exactly as 9router does; wire the already-ported ACCOUNT_FILTER_OPTIONS select, the page-size select + custom input, and the First/Prev/Next/Last footer using the already-ported getConnectionsPaginationSummary / getPageSizeLabel / shouldResetPage helpers. Note the OpenProxy route at src/server/api/providers.rs:1135 currently answers `/api/providers/client` with a *client-info* payload (clientId/clientName/version/provider) rather than a paginated connection list — that handler must be reshaped to return {connections, pagination, providerOptions, totals} to match 9router's app/api/providers/client/route.js.

## P194-001 · P1 · web/src/shared/components/UsageStats.tsx:459-461; dead components at web/src/components/usage/UsageChart.tsx:1-30 and UsageChartInner.tsx:1-118

- **9router**: UsageStats imports UsageChart (`src/shared/components/UsageStats.js:20`) and renders it in the Overview between the topology/Recent-Requests row and the usage table: `{loading ? spinner : <UsageChart period={period} />}` (`src/shared/components/UsageStats.js:483-484`). The chart fetches `/api/usage/chart?period=…`, computes `hasData = data.some(d => d.tokens > 0 || d.cost > 0)`, and renders one of three states: a h-48 "Loading..." block, a h-48 "No data for this period" block, or a 220px-high ResponsiveContainer with a Tokens/Cost toggle, gradient fills and a themed tooltip (`src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/usage/components/UsageChart.js:25-136`).
- **OpenProxy**: UsageStats renders no chart at all. Where 9router has `{loading ? spinner : <UsageChart period={period} />}` (shared/components/UsageStats.js:483-484), OpenProxy has `{loading ? spinner : (stats.byProvider && Object.keys(stats.byProvider).length > 0 ? <ProviderBreakdownTable byProvider={stats.byProvider} /> : null)}` (web/src/shared/components/UsageStats.tsx:459-461). UsageChart.tsx and UsageChartInner.tsx still exist but grep across web/src finds no importer — the module is dead code. Its inner component does already read the correct `{data:[…]}` envelope the OpenProxy backend returns (`result.data`, UsageChartInner.tsx:47; backend src/server/api/usage.rs:1283), so the port would work once re-mounted.
- **Gap**: The token/cost trend chart was dropped from the usage Overview and replaced by a per-provider totals table; the chart component is still in the tree but nothing renders it.
- **Why it matters**: Period switching (Today/24h/7D/30D/60D) in 9router re-renders the chart with a matched period, so the user can see the shape of consumption over time and spot a quota-burn spike. In OpenProxy the period buttons only change the counters and the table — there is no time axis anywhere in the Overview, and the "No data for this period" empty state that tells a user their filter selected an empty window is also gone.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/shared/components/UsageStats.js:20 (import), :483-484 (`{loading ? spinner : <UsageChart period={period} />}`); src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/usage/components/UsageChart.js:25-136 (3-state render). OpenProxy: web/src/shared/components/UsageStats.tsx:459-461 (ProviderBreakdownTable substituted, no chart); web/src/components/usage/UsageChart.tsx:1-30 and UsageChartInner.tsx:1-118 exist with no importer; backend envelope src/server/api/usage.rs:1283.
- **Parity fix**: Re-mount the chart in web/src/shared/components/UsageStats.tsx at the 9router position — between the topology/RecentRequests grid and the table selector — as `{loading ? spinner : <UsageChart period={period} />}`, and port the `hasData` guard so the component shows "No data for this period" rather than an empty axis when every bucket is zero. Keep the already-correct `result.data` unwrap in UsageChartInner.tsx:47.

## P195-001 · P2 · /callback route: no-params branch and page shell

- **9router**: `src/app/callback/page.js:75-78`: when the callback carries no `code`, `token` or `error`, the page sets `status = "manual"` and `:112-125` renders a "Copy This URL" card containing the full `window.location.href` in a `<code>` block — unconditionally, so a user can always recover the URL. The page is a normal App Router page, so it inherits `src/app/layout.js` (ThemeProvider, RuntimeI18nProvider, pre-paint theme script, GA).
- **OpenProxy**: `web/src/pages/callback.astro` is a hand-rolled standalone HTML document (`<!doctype html>` at `:10`, inline `<style is:global>` at `:16-54`, inline `<script>` at `:63-206`) with no `Layout.astro` wrapper and hard-coded dark colors. The no-params branch at `:164-172` only sets a text message; the URL + "Copy URL" button at `:175-204` are gated behind `if (!sentByChannel && !sentByStorage && !sentByMessage)`, and BroadcastChannel/localStorage essentially always succeed (`:97-115`).
- **Gap**: The no-params branch no longer surfaces the callback URL, and the page ignores the user's theme and i18n.
- **Why it matters**: When an IdP returns a callback with no code/token/error, 9router always shows the URL for manual copy-paste into the OAuth modal's manual-code field; OpenProxy shows only a sentence, because the relay "succeeded" and suppressed the fallback. The manual-paste OAuth flow has no URL to copy. The page also renders dark-only regardless of the user's chosen theme.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/callback/page.js:75-78, :112-125; .tmp/9router/src/app/layout.js | OpenProxy: web/src/pages/callback.astro:10-54, :164-172, :175-204
- **Parity fix**: In `web/src/pages/callback.astro`, move the URL block + copy button out of the `:175` guard so they render whenever `!code && !error` (mirroring 9router's `status === "manual"` branch), and convert the page to a normal `Layout.astro` page so ThemeProvider and RuntimeI18nProvider apply.

## P196-001 · P2 · /dashboard/cli-tools route table: devin and opendesign

- **9router**: `src/shared/constants/cliTools.js` `CLI_TOOLS` (starting `:76`) contains 18 keys including `devin` (`:406`) and `opendesign` (`:432`). `src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/cli-tools/[toolId]/page.js:8` serves any `CLI_TOOLS` key, so `/dashboard/cli-tools/devin` and `/dashboard/cli-tools/opendesign` are valid routes.

- **Parity fix**: Port the `devin` and `opendesign` entries from 9router's `CLI_TOOLS` into `web/src/shared/constants/cliTools.ts` (with their `configType`/guide steps and model mappings), add a `DevinToolCard` to the `ToolDetailClient.tsx:177` switch, and make sure the corresponding `/api/cli-tools/devin-settings` route is present so the build emits the two pages.

## P198-001 · P2 · /dashboard/providers/new — Enter-to-submit on the create-provider form

- **9router**: The card body is a real `<form onSubmit={handleSubmit} className="flex flex-col gap-6">` (src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/providers/new/page.js:94) and the primary action is `<Button type="submit" loading={loading} fullWidth>` (:211), so pressing Enter in the Display Name or API Key field fires the native form submit and creates the provider.
- **OpenProxy**: There is no `<form>` anywhere in the component. web/src/components/ProvidersNewPageClient.tsx renders `<Card>` -> `<div className="flex flex-col gap-5">` (:281-283) and the primary action is a plain `<Button onClick={() => void handleConnect()} disabled={!selected || saving}>` with no `type` (:407-414). The `<Input>` fields for Display Name (:361) and API Key (:370) sit inside a `div`, so Enter performs no implicit submission.
- **Gap**: Pressing Enter in the API-key field of /dashboard/providers/new creates the provider in 9router but does nothing in OpenProxy.
- **Why it matters**: The API key is a long pasted string; the universal muscle-memory 'paste then Enter' flow silently no-ops in OpenProxy, so the operator must deliberately Tab to the button. Every other multi-field form in both apps (login x2, profile password change, profile outbound proxy) is a native `<form>`, so this page is the lone outlier.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/providers/new/page.js:94,211 || openproxy: web/src/components/ProvidersNewPageClient.tsx:281,283,361,370,407
- **Parity fix**: Change `<Card padding="lg"><div className="flex flex-col gap-5">` to `<Card padding="lg"><form onSubmit={(e) => { e.preventDefault(); void handleConnect(); }} className="flex flex-col gap-5">` and set the primary `<Button type="submit">`; the Cancel button already carries `type="button"`, so it will not submit.

- **9router**: The route file lives at `src/app/dashboard/settings/pricing/page.js` — deliberately OUTSIDE the `src/app/(dashboard)/` route group. It therefore renders with no `DashboardLayout`, no `Sidebar` and no `Header`; `src/app/dashboard/settings/pricing/page.js:54` draws its own `max-w-6xl mx-auto p-6` shell.

## P201-001 · P2 · /dashboard/usage?tab= — accepted and rendered tab values

- **9router**: `src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/usage/page.js:31-33` allowlists exactly `["overview", "logs", "details"]`; the SegmentedControl at `:46-53` offers only `Overview` and `Details`. A `?tab=providers` deep link falls back to `overview`.
- **OpenProxy**: `web/src/components/usage/UsagePageClient.tsx:45-50` allowlists `["overview", "logs", "details", "providers", "analytics", "compression"]`; the SegmentedControl at `:65-76` offers `Overview / Providers / Analytics / Compression / Details` and `:98-101` renders `ProviderBreakdownTable`, `UsageAnalyticsGrid` and `CompressionStats` for the three extra values.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy exposes three additional tab values in the URL allowlist and the tab strip that 9router does not have.
- **Why it matters**: `?tab=` is a shareable URL. A 9router link with any of the three extra values silently degrades to Overview in 9router but renders a different screen in OpenProxy, so the two are not interchangeable — and the tab strip itself has a different width and ordering (Usage is now the 4th of 5 tabs, not the 2nd).
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/usage/page.js:31-33, :46-53 | OpenProxy: web/src/components/usage/UsagePageClient.tsx:45-50, :65-76, :98-101
- **Parity fix**: Restrict the allowlist at `web/src/components/usage/UsagePageClient.tsx:48` and the SegmentedControl at `:66-72` to `overview` and `details` so `?tab=` has the same meaning in both products; keep the three extra views reachable from dedicated routes if they are wanted.

## P202-001 · P2 · 9router open-sse/handlers/videoCore.js:40, :188-208 vs OpenProxy src/server/api/media.rs:1700-1732, :1025-1052

- **9router**: buildHeaders (videoCore.js:39-45) always sets `Accept: application/json`, and the success path (videoCore.js:188-208) constructs a NEW Response with exactly two headers: the resolved Content-Type and `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`. No upstream header is forwarded to the client.
- **OpenProxy**: build_video_headers (media.rs:1700-1732) delegates to build_media_headers (media.rs:843-892), which sets only CONTENT_TYPE plus the provider auth header — the `Accept: application/json` added in 9router's buildHeaders is absent for the xAI and OpenRouter video paths (it is only present on the Vertex branch at media.rs:1712). On the way back, proxy_upstream_response (media.rs:1025-1052) copies every non-hop-by-hop upstream header onto the client response.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy omits the Accept: application/json request header on xAI/OpenRouter video calls and echoes the full upstream response header set instead of only Content-Type.
- **Why it matters**: Without Accept: application/json some upstreams content-negotiate differently or return a non-JSON representation, which the video JSON passthrough cannot interpret. Echoing upstream headers additionally leaks upstream implementation details (server, x-request-id, internal rate-limit headers) to the client, and can forward an upstream Content-Length that no longer matches after redaction on the error path.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/handlers/videoCore.js:39-45 (Accept at :40), :199-208 (only Content-Type + CORS emitted). OpenProxy: src/server/api/media.rs:843-892 (build_media_headers, no Accept), :1700-1732 (video headers; Accept only for vertex at :1712), :1045-1049 (all upstream headers copied).
- **Parity fix**: Add `Accept: application/json` to build_video_headers for the non-Vertex branch (media.rs:1719-1731) so it matches videoCore.js:40. On the response side, restrict the headers copied by proxy_video_response/proxy_upstream_response to Content-Type (plus the CORS header) to match videoCore.js:199-208, and in particular never copy an upstream Content-Length — it is already stripped as hop-by-hop at media.rs:1058, so verify the same filter is applied on the error path at media.rs:912-915.

## P203-001 · P2 · 9router open-sse/handlers/videoProviders/vertex.js:75-84, 148  ⟷  openproxy src/server/api/media.rs:1860, 2083-2097

- **9router**: toVertexBody guards each parameter with a null check or a truthiness check, not mere key presence: `if (body.n != null)`, `if (body.duration != null)`, `if (body.seed != null)`, `if (body.generate_audio != null)` (vertex.js:75,76,79,84) and plain truthiness `if (body.aspect_ratio)`, `if (body.resolution)`, `if (body.negative_prompt)`, `if (body.storage_uri)` (vertex.js:77,78,80,83) — so an explicit JSON null, an empty string, 0-as-a-false-flag, and an absent key are all treated identically (field omitted). The create-side validation uses the same truthiness: `if (!body.prompt && !body.image && !body.image_url) return { error: "Vertex video requires a prompt or an image" }` (vertex.js:148).
- **OpenProxy**: to_vertex_body uses serde presence throughout: `if let Some(aspect) = body.get("aspect_ratio")` (media.rs:2083), `resolution` (:2086), `seed` (:2089), `negative_prompt` (:2092), `storage_uri` (:2097) — and `Value::get` returns Some for a key explicitly set to null, so those values are copied into `parameters` even when null or empty. validate_vertex_create_body uses the same presence test: `let has_image = body.get("image").is_some() || body.get("image_url").is_some();` (media.rs:1860), which is true for `"image": null`, so media.rs:1861 lets the request through and to_vertex_body then produces an `instances: [{}]` body with no prompt and no image.
- **Gap**: The Vertex request translation and its pre-flight validation test for key presence where 9router tests for a non-null, non-empty value, so explicit nulls and empty strings are forwarded into the Vertex `parameters` object and can defeat the prompt-or-image guard.
- **Why it matters**: A body of `{"model":"vertex/veo-3.1-generate-preview","image":null}` passes OpenProxy's validation and is submitted to Vertex as `{"instances":[{}]}`, which Vertex rejects with its own opaque 400; 9router returns the precise 400 "Vertex video requires a prompt or an image" before spending a billable call. Likewise `{"seed":null}` and `{"aspect_ratio":""}` are forwarded as `parameters.seed = null` and `parameters.aspectRatio = ""`, where 9router omits them — an empty enum value is a hard Vertex validation error, so an OpenProxy client that serialises optional fields as null/"" gets a confusing upstream failure instead of a clean request.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/handlers/videoProviders/vertex.js:75-84 (per-field != null / truthiness guards), vertex.js:148 (truthy prompt-or-image check). openproxy: src/server/api/media.rs:1860-1866 (presence-based has_image and the guard it drives), media.rs:2083-2097 (presence-based parameter copies), media.rs:2035-2054 (image branch that then emits nothing for a null, producing the empty instance).
- **Parity fix**: Add a shared `is_present(v: &Value) -> bool` helper (false for Null, and matching 9router's truthiness for the string-typed fields) and use it in both places: gate has_image at media.rs:1860 on it, and wrap the aspect_ratio / resolution / negative_prompt / storage_uri copies so an empty or null value is skipped, mirroring vertex.js:77-83. seed keeps 9router's `!= null` semantics specifically (vertex.js:79), so a numeric 0 seed is still forwarded — use a null-only predicate for that field rather than the truthiness one.

## P204-001 · P2 · 9router open-sse/services/combo.js:315-327, 371-381 + open-sse/utils/error.js:116-128; OpenProxy src/server/api/chat.rs:4861-4865 + src/core/combo/mod.rs:973-978

- **9router**: handleComboChat reads retryAfter from the failing member's JSON body via `errorBody?.retryAfter || null` (combo.js:319) and tracks the earliest (combo.js:325-327). But the ONLY producer of a per-member rate-limit response, unavailableResponse, puts the value in a Retry-After HEADER and emits the body {"error":{"message":"... (reset after ...)"}} with no retryAfter field (error.js:121-127). So errorBody.retryAfter is always undefined, earliestRetryAfter is always null, and the combo's final response is always the bare {"error":{"message":msg}} at combo.js:378-381 with no Retry-After header.
- **OpenProxy**: ComboAttemptError.retry_after is populated by the inner account loop and accumulated as earliest_retry_after in iterate_combo_models (mod.rs:973-978), and attempt_error_response sets a retry-after header whenever it is present (chat.rs:4861-4865). It also parses retryAfter out of the upstream body via parse_retry_after_from_body (mod.rs:212-236).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy populates the combo's earliestRetryAfter and emits a Retry-After header on the final combo error, a signal 9router never produces because its own unavailableResponse never puts retryAfter in the JSON body.
- **Why it matters**: The same all-members-failed condition returns different response metadata: 9router 503 with no Retry-After; OpenProxy 503/429 with Retry-After: N. Clients that honour Retry-After (retry middleware, Claude Code's backoff, shell scripts) will wait N seconds on OpenProxy and immediately retry on 9router. The divergence is invisible in the body and only shows up in header-driven retry logic.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/services/combo.js:319 (`errorBody?.retryAfter`), :325-327, :371-381; open-sse/utils/error.js:121-127 (body has no retryAfter; the value goes in the Retry-After header). OpenProxy: src/core/combo/mod.rs:973-978 (earliest_retry_after accumulation), src/server/api/chat.rs:4861-4865 (header emission), src/core/combo/mod.rs:212-236 (parse_retry_after_from_body).
- **Parity fix**: To be byte-compatible with 9router, iterate_combo_models should populate earliest_retry_after only from a retryAfter field present in the member's JSON BODY (as combo.js:319 does) and not from the per-account Retry-After header, and attempt_error_response should then leave the header off for the combo path. If the OpenProxy behaviour is deliberately preferred, record it in docs/parity-9router.md as an intentional divergence rather than leaving it implicit.

## P205-001 · P2 · 9router open-sse/services/combo.js:371-375 + open-sse/utils/error.js:116-128; OpenProxy src/server/api/chat.rs:4827-4869

- **9router**: When the whole combo fails and an earliestRetryAfter was collected, handleComboChat calls unavailableResponse(status, msg, earliestRetryAfter, retryHuman) (combo.js:374), and unavailableResponse builds the client-visible message as `${message} (${retryAfterHuman})` (error.js:120) - for example `...rate limited (reset after 2m 30s)`. When no retryAfter is present it returns the bare {"error":{"message":msg}} (combo.js:378-381).
- **OpenProxy**: combo_error_response (chat.rs:4827-4834) forwards the message verbatim to attempt_error_response (chat.rs:4836-4869), which runs friendly_error_message / HTML-stripping / whitespace collapsing (chat.rs:4856-4857) and emits build_error_body. The `(reset after ...)` suffix is never appended. The Retry-After header IS set when retry_after is present (chat.rs:4861-4865).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's final combo-failure message never carries the human-readable `(reset after ...)` suffix that 9router appends.
- **Why it matters**: The reset hint is the only place a user or an agent can learn when to retry from the message text. Claude Code and other OpenAI-compatible clients surface error.message verbatim in the transcript; without the suffix the operator sees a bare upstream error with no recovery window, even though the Retry-After header carries the machine-readable value.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/services/combo.js:371-375, open-sse/utils/error.js:116-128 (line 120 appends ` (${retryAfterHuman})`). OpenProxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:4827-4869 (combo_error_response / attempt_error_response - no suffix).
- **Parity fix**: When earliest_retry_after is Some, format it with the 9router formatRetryAfter shape (hours/minutes/seconds, "reset after 0s" when already past - accountFallback.js:90-103) and append ` ({human})` to the message before building the body in attempt_error_response, mirroring error.js:120.

## P206-001 · P2 · 9router src/app/api/settings/route.js:91-97; OpenProxy src/server/api/mod.rs:2441-2445, 2498-2499

- **9router**: The settings PATCH route clears ALL cached combo rotation state when any routing knob changes: if hasOwnProperty(body, "comboStrategy") || hasOwnProperty(body, "comboStickyRoundRobinLimit") || hasOwnProperty(body, "comboStrategies"), then resetComboRotation() (settings/route.js:91-97). resetComboRotation() with no argument does comboRotationState.clear() (combo.js:243-246), so the very next round-robin request for every combo starts at index 0.
- **OpenProxy**: The settings PATCH stores db.settings.combo_strategy (mod.rs:2441-2442), db.settings.combo_strategies (mod.rs:2444-2445) and db.settings.combo_sticky_round_robin_limit (mod.rs:2498-2499) but never calls reset_combo_rotation. The only call sites in the whole tree are combo CRUD (admin_items.rs:526,529,561) and batch combo delete (admin_items.rs:1116).
- **Gap**: Changing the global or per-combo strategy, or the sticky limit, does not invalidate the cached rotation index, so rotation continues from a stale position.
- **Why it matters**: When an operator switches a combo from Fallback to RoundRobin, or changes the sticky limit, OpenProxy starts rotating at whatever index the previously-unused or differently-configured state happens to hold instead of the configured first member. A combo switched TO round-robin with a newly raised sticky limit also inherits a stale COMBO_ROTATION_STICKY_COUNT, so the first member may be skipped or pinned for the wrong number of requests. 9router guarantees a clean index after the change; OpenProxy does not.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/app/api/settings/route.js:91-97 (resetComboRotation() at :96), open-sse/services/combo.js:243-246 (clear-all). OpenProxy: src/server/api/mod.rs:2441-2445 and :2498-2499 (assignment with no reset); `grep -rn reset_combo_rotation src/` shows calls only in admin_items.rs:526,529,561,1116.
- **Parity fix**: After the settings update commits, add `if req.combo_strategy.is_some() || req.combo_strategies.is_some() || req.combo_sticky_round_robin_limit.is_some() { crate::core::combo::reset_combo_rotation(None); }` - reset_combo_rotation(None) already clears both the index map and the sticky map (mod.rs:652-654).

## P207-001 · P2 · 9router src/app/api/v1/models/route.js:305-315 + open-sse/services/combo.js:254-266; OpenProxy src/server/api/v1_models.rs:126-133 + src/core/combo/mod.rs:723-732 + src/server/api/chat.rs:496-499

- **9router**: Combos have no active/inactive flag at all - the record is {id, name, kind, models, createdAt, updatedAt} (combosRepo.js:7-16) and getCombos() returns every row (combosRepo.js:19-23). buildModelsList lists every combo that matches the kind filter (models/route.js:306-315), and getComboModelsFromData returns combo.models whenever models.length > 0 with no active check (combo.js:261-265). A combo that exists in the DB is always dispatchable.
- **OpenProxy**: Combo::is_active() reads extra["isActive"], defaulting to true (types/mod.rs:421-429). get_combo_models_from_data requires combo.is_active() (mod.rs:730), so an inactive combo returns None; chat.rs:496-499 then returns 400 `Unknown combo model`. /v1/models also skips inactive combos (v1_models.rs:131). The flag is written only by `openproxy combo enable/disable` (src/cli/combo.rs:282, 429-446) and by PUT /api/combos/{id} with isActive (admin_items.rs:507-511); the dashboard never sets it.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy treats a combo as dispatchable only when extra.isActive is true, while 9router has no such flag and dispatches any combo that has at least one member.
- **Why it matters**: After `openproxy combo disable X`, a request naming combo X returns 400 `Unknown combo model` - an error that tells the client the model does not exist rather than that it is administratively disabled - and X disappears from GET /v1/models while remaining in GET /api/combos. On 9router the same request is served. The 400 status is the trigger for invalid_request_error handling in several OpenAI-compatible clients, which will not retry, whereas 9router returns a 200 stream.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/lib/db/repos/combosRepo.js:7-23 (no active field), open-sse/services/combo.js:261-265 (models.length > 0 only), src/app/api/v1/models/route.js:306-315 (all combos listed). OpenProxy: src/core/combo/mod.rs:730 (combo.is_active() in the finder), src/server/api/chat.rs:496-499 (400 Unknown combo model), src/server/api/v1_models.rs:126-133 (hidden from /v1/models), src/types/mod.rs:421-429 (is_active default).
- **Parity fix**: Either (a) drop the is_active() predicate from get_combo_models_from_data (mod.rs:730) and from v1_models.rs:131 so any persisted combo dispatches and lists exactly as in 9router, keeping isActive purely as a dashboard annotation; or (b) keep the gate but return a 503 with a retry-after rather than 400, and keep the combo listed in /v1/models with an isActive: false field so clients can distinguish disabled from unknown.

## P208-001 · P2 · 9router src/app/globals.css:412-414 + src/shared/components/Drawer.js:45,54 + src/shared/components/Modal.js:50,60  vs  openproxy web/src/shared/components/styles/global.css:773-775 + web/src/shared/components/Drawer.tsx:57,66 + web/src/shared/components/Modal.tsx:63,73

- **9router**: globals.css defines the unprefixed classes: `.slide-in-right { animation: slideInFromRight 0.25s cubic-bezier(0.22,1,0.36,1) forwards; }` (:412), `.fade-in { animation: fadeIn 0.2s ease-out forwards; }` (:413), `.slide-in-top` (:414). Drawer.js:45 uses `fade-in` on the overlay and :54 uses `slide-in-right` on the panel; Modal.js:50 (overlay) and :60 (panel) use `fade-in`. All four resolve, so drawers slide in over 250ms and modals fade over 200ms with `forwards` fill.
- **OpenProxy**: global.css defines ONLY the `animate-`-prefixed names: `.animate-slide-in-right` (:773, 0.3s ease-out), `.animate-fade-in` (:774), `.animate-slide-in-top` (:775) — and none of those prefixed names is referenced anywhere in the 233 .tsx/.astro files. Meanwhile Drawer.tsx:57 uses `fade-in` and :66 uses `slide-in-right`, and Modal.tsx:63 and :73 use `fade-in` — the unprefixed names that do not exist. Confirmed in the shipped bundle: `.fade-in{` and `.slide-in-right{` return zero matches in web/dist/_astro/dashboard.DmzKc7D6.css; only `.animate-fade-in` is present.
- **Gap**: Six unprefixed animation class references in OpenProxy's Drawer and Modal resolve to nothing, so the drawer and every modal pop in instantly with no slide and no fade.
- **Why it matters**: Every modal (AddConnection, EditConnection, AddCustomModel, ModelSelectModal, ComboFormModal, OAuth, Confirm, ...) and the Drawer lose their entrance motion. 9router's slideInFromRight also carries `forwards` fill-mode so the drawer stays put after animating; OpenProxy's unused animate-slide-in-right omits `forwards` and uses 0.3s ease-out instead of 0.25s cubic-bezier(0.22,1,0.36,1), so even after renaming, the easing and duration would still differ.
- **Evidence**: 9R: src/app/globals.css:412,413,414; src/shared/components/Drawer.js:45,54; src/shared/components/Modal.js:50,60. OP: web/src/shared/components/styles/global.css:773,774,775; web/src/shared/components/Drawer.tsx:57,66; web/src/shared/components/Modal.tsx:63,73; web/dist/_astro/dashboard.DmzKc7D6.css has no `.fade-in{` or `.slide-in-right{` rule.
- **Parity fix**: Rename the three CSS rules in web/src/shared/components/styles/global.css:773-775 to the unprefixed `.slide-in-right` / `.fade-in` / `.slide-in-top` (no component edits needed; all call sites already use the unprefixed names) and align the timing to 9router: `.slide-in-right { animation: slideInFromRight 0.25s cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1) forwards; }`, `.fade-in { animation: fadeIn 0.2s ease-out forwards; }`, `.slide-in-top { animation: slideInFromTop 0.18s cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1) forwards; }`.

## P209-001 · P2 · 9router src/lib/db/migrate.js:198-213,256-263 · OpenProxy src/db/mod.rs:83-142

- **9router**: `LEGACY_FILES` declares four legacy JSON files — `db.json`, `usage.json`, `disabledModels.json`, `request-details.json` (`src/lib/db/paths.js:9-13`) — and `runMigrationOnce` imports all four inside one transaction via `importLegacyMain` / `importLegacyUsage` / `importLegacyDisabled` (`migrate.js:198-203`, reading `data.disabled` into the `disabledModels` kv scope) / `importLegacyDetails` (`:205-213`, reading `data.records` into `requestDetails`). The `hasLegacy` gate at `:261` checks all four.
- **OpenProxy**: `Db::load_from` checks only two files: `db_json_path` and `usage_json_path` (`src/db/mod.rs:85-86`), imports only those, and writes the `.migrated-from-json` marker (`:134`) which permanently closes the gate. No code anywhere in `src/` references `disabledModels.json` or `request-details.json`.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's one-time legacy JSON import covers only `db.json` and `usage.json`; 9router also imports `disabledModels.json` and `request-details.json`.
- **Why it matters**: An upgrading user with a legacy `disabledModels.json` finds every previously disabled model re-enabled after the switch (the provider page's Available Models state changes), and any legacy `request-details.json` request log is dropped. Once the marker is written the import is never retried, so there is no recovery path short of hand-editing the database.
- **Evidence**: 9router `.tmp/9router/src/lib/db/paths.js:9-13`, `.tmp/9router/src/lib/db/migrate.js:198-213,256-263` vs OpenProxy `src/db/mod.rs:83-142`
- **Parity fix**: Add the two legacy paths to `load_from`, fold them into the `hasLegacy` gate, and translate them the same way `migrate.js:198-213` does — `data.disabled` into the `disabledModels` table via `diff_disabled_models`/`patch.rs:294-310`, `data.records` into `requestDetails` — before writing the marker.

## P210-001 · P2 · 9router src/lib/db/repos/settingsRepo.js:42 · OpenProxy src/types/mod.rs:736

- **9router**: `DEFAULT_SETTINGS.enableObservability = false` (`settingsRepo.js:42`), and the writer uses the key `enableObservability` (`9router src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/profile/page.js:642`). `getObservabilityConfig` (`requestDetailsRepo.js:31-34`) reads that same key, falling back to `process.env.OBSERVABILITY_ENABLED !== "false"`. A fresh 9router install records no request details.
- **OpenProxy**: `Settings::default().observability_enabled = true` (`src/types/mod.rs:736`, serde name `observabilityEnabled`), and `UpdateSettingsRequest` declares `observability_enabled` (`src/server/api/mod.rs:2249`, applied at `:2462-2463`). The OpenProxy dashboard writes the matching camelCase key (`web/src/components/ProfilePageClient.tsx:291`), so its own toggle round-trips — but the persisted default is the opposite of 9router's, and a settings blob produced by 9router (key `enableObservability`) is dropped on import as an unknown field.
- **Gap**: The persisted default for the observability flag is `true` in OpenProxy versus `false` in 9router, and the settings key name differs, so cross-imports lose the flag.
- **Why it matters**: A fresh OpenProxy install starts with observability on (request bodies/responses recorded, `observabilityMaxRecords` rows accumulating) where 9router starts off; and importing a 9router settings export leaves the flag stuck at OpenProxy's `true` because `enableObservability` is not a recognised key. The two products disagree about whether request payloads are being persisted by default.
- **Evidence**: 9router `.tmp/9router/src/lib/db/repos/settingsRepo.js:42`, `.tmp/9router/src/lib/db/repos/requestDetailsRepo.js:31-34` vs OpenProxy `src/types/mod.rs:736`, `src/server/api/mod.rs:2249,2462-2463`
- **Parity fix**: Set `observability_enabled: false` in `Settings::default()`, and accept `enableObservability` as a serde alias on the field (and on `UpdateSettingsRequest`) so a 9router settings export imports cleanly.

## P211-001 · P2 · 9router src/lib/db/repos/usageRepo.js:290-298 · OpenProxy src/db/mod.rs:349-370

- **9router**: `saveRequestUsage` performs all three writes in ONE transaction (`usageRepo.js:253-305`): the `usageHistory` insert, the `usageDaily` read-modify-upsert (`aggregateEntryToDay` + `INSERT … ON CONFLICT(dateKey) DO UPDATE` at `:290-298`), and the lifetime-counter increment. The daily rollup is durable state in its own table.
- **OpenProxy**: `Db::update_usage` (`src/db/mod.rs:349-370`) appends only the new `usageHistory` rows. `usage_repo::upsert_daily` (`src/db/sqlite/repo/usage_repo.rs:70-83`) has NO caller anywhere in `src/` (verified by grep) — the `usageDaily` table is never populated at runtime. `UsageDb::normalize` (`src/types/mod.rs:857-867`) re-derives `daily_summary` in memory by folding `history`, and startup hydration reads history with a hard cap: `SELECT … FROM usageHistory ORDER BY timestamp DESC LIMIT 10000` (`src/db/sqlite/export.rs:241`).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy never writes the `usageDaily` rollup table and reconstructs daily summaries from a 10,000-row-capped history on every load, instead of maintaining the durable rollup 9router writes transactionally.
- **Why it matters**: Once an installation exceeds 10,000 recorded requests, every day older than the 10,000 most recent vanishes from the in-memory `daily_summary` after a restart, so the Usage page's per-day chart and per-day token/cost totals silently under-report. The retention job that prunes `usageDaily` (`src/main.rs:644-670`) operates on a table that is always empty.
- **Evidence**: 9router `.tmp/9router/src/lib/db/repos/usageRepo.js:253-305` vs OpenProxy `src/db/mod.rs:349-370`, `src/db/sqlite/repo/usage_repo.rs:70-83`, `src/db/sqlite/export.rs:241`, `src/types/mod.rs:857-867`, `src/main.rs:644-670`
- **Parity fix**: Call `usage_repo::upsert_daily` from `update_usage` inside the same `with_transaction` as the history appends (aggregate each appended entry into its `dateKey` bucket first, as `aggregateEntryToDay` does at `usageRepo.js:290-297`), and stop deriving `daily_summary` from the capped history. Also raise or remove the `LIMIT 10000` on the history read, or page it.

## P212-001 · P2 · 9router src/lib/db/repos/usageRepo.js:300-304 · OpenProxy src/db/sqlite/export.rs:262-268

- **9router**: The lifetime request counter is durable state in the `_meta` table, incremented atomically in the same transaction as the history insert: `SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key = 'totalRequestsLifetime'` then `INSERT … ON CONFLICT(key) DO UPDATE` (`usageRepo.js:300-304`). It is also seeded from the legacy JSON at migration time (`migrate.js:193-195`). It is independent of how many `usageHistory` rows exist.
- **OpenProxy**: `totalRequestsLifetime` is not persisted at all. `export_usage_impl` hardcodes `"totalRequestsLifetime": history.len()` (`src/db/sqlite/export.rs:262-268`), where `history` is the same `LIMIT 10000` query (`:241`). `UsageDb::normalize` (`src/types/mod.rs:857-867`) only raises the in-memory value to `history.len()`, never above. Nothing writes a lifetime counter to `_meta` (which holds only `schema_version`).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy derives the lifetime request count from the size of the capped history instead of persisting an independent counter, so the number freezes at the cap.
- **Why it matters**: After 10,000 requests the "Total requests" figure reported by `/api/usage/stats` (`src/server/api/usage.rs:377,450`), `/api/usage/stream` (`src/server/usage_stream.rs:237`), `/api/observability` (`src/server/api/observability.rs:121`) and the CLI (`src/cli/logs.rs:184`) stops increasing even though every new request is being recorded — a permanently wrong lifetime metric that the 9router UI shows correctly.
- **Evidence**: 9router `.tmp/9router/src/lib/db/repos/usageRepo.js:300-304`, `.tmp/9router/src/lib/db/migrate.js:193-195` vs OpenProxy `src/db/sqlite/export.rs:241,262-268`, `src/types/mod.rs:857-867`
- **Parity fix**: Persist the counter in `_meta` under `totalRequestsLifetime` (the same key 9router uses): read it in `export_usage_impl` instead of `history.len()`, and increment it in the `update_usage` transaction alongside the history appends.

## P213-001 · P2 · 9router src/shared/components/Button.js:15-17,35-36  vs  openproxy web/src/shared/components/Button.tsx:43-45,60-65

- **9router**: Base class string is `inline-flex items-center justify-center gap-2 font-semibold transition-all duration-150 ease-out cursor-pointer` plus `active:scale-[0.97] disabled:opacity-50 disabled:cursor-not-allowed disabled:active:scale-100` (Button.js:35-36). Sizes: sm `h-7 px-3 text-xs rounded-[8px]` (28px), md `h-9 px-4 text-sm rounded-[10px]` (36px), lg `h-11 px-6 text-sm rounded-[10px]` (44px) (:15-17). Radius is per-size.
- **OpenProxy**: Base string is `inline-flex items-center justify-center gap-2 rounded-mini-md leading-none` + `transition-colors duration-150 ease-out cursor-pointer tracking-tight` and `active:scale-[0.99] disabled:cursor-not-allowed disabled:active:scale-100` (Button.tsx:60-65). Sizes: sm `h-8 px-3 text-[12px] font-medium` (32px), md `h-10 px-4 text-[14px] font-medium` (40px), lg `h-12 px-6 text-[15px] font-medium` (48px) (:43-45). Radius is a single 8px `rounded-mini-md` for all sizes. `disabled:opacity-50` appears ZERO times in the file.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's Button is 4px taller at every size, 100 weight-lighter, uses a 0.99 press-scale instead of 0.97, and because `disabled:opacity-50` was dropped, disabled buttons render at full opacity instead of dimmed.
- **Why it matters**: The Button is the most-reused component in both dashboards (every modal footer, form action and toolbar). The 4px height delta changes the vertical rhythm of every form and modal footer. The press-scale difference is visible on click. The missing disabled opacity is the most obvious: 9router's disabled Primary/Save/Apply buttons fade to 50% so users can tell they are inert; OpenProxy's only cue is cursor-not-allowed, and since the secondary/outline/ghost variants supply their own `disabled:text-muted`, the dimming is inconsistent per variant.
- **Evidence**: 9R: src/shared/components/Button.js:15,16,17,35,36. OP: web/src/shared/components/Button.tsx:43,44,45,60,61,62,63,64,65 (zero matches for `disabled:opacity-50` in the file).
- **Parity fix**: In web/src/shared/components/Button.tsx set sizes to `sm: "h-7 px-3 text-xs"`, `md: "h-9 px-4 text-sm"`, `lg: "h-11 px-6 text-sm"` (:43-45); drop the per-size `font-medium` in favour of `font-semibold` on the base string (:61); re-add `disabled:opacity-50` to the base string beside `disabled:cursor-not-allowed` (:65); and change `active:scale-[0.99]` to `active:scale-[0.97]` (:65). Keep the extra primary-on-dark / secondary-on-dark variants — they are additive.

## P214-001 · P2 · 9router src/shared/components/Card.js:17-22,29-31,46,49,82,103  vs  openproxy web/src/shared/components/Card.tsx:57,65-72,75,104,130,148,152,159,192,213

- **9router**: Card: radius `rounded-[14px]`; `elev ? "rounded-[14px] shadow-[var(--shadow-elev)]" : "rounded-[14px] shadow-[var(--shadow-soft)]"` (Card.js:29) — the non-elevated default card still carries a soft shadow. hover adds `hover:shadow-[var(--shadow-warm)] hover:border-brand-500/30 transition-all cursor-pointer` (:30). Paddings: md `p-6`, lg `p-8` (:21-22). Title renders at the inherited 16px (`text-text-main font-semibold`, :46); subtitle `text-sm` 14px with no margin (:49). Card.Row and Card.ListItem hover is the semi-transparent `hover:bg-surface-2/50` (:82, :103).
- **OpenProxy**: Card default radius is `rounded-mini-lg` = 12px (:57, :75). Non-elevated cards get `shadow-none` and only elev gets `shadow-card` (:130) — the default card is flat. hover is `hover:border-ink/30 hover:shadow-soft` plus `transition-colors` (:104 + :148). Paddings: md `p-5`, lg `p-6`, xl `p-8` (:69-71) — the default card is 20px padded vs 9router's 24px, and `lg` shifted from 32px to 24px. Title is `font-semibold text-[15px]` (:152); subtitle `text-[13px] mt-0.5` (:159). Card.Row and Card.ListItem hover is the opaque `hover:bg-surface-soft` (:192, :213).
- **Gap**: The default Card is 4px tighter in padding, 2px smaller in radius, 1-2px smaller in its title and subtitle, has no shadow by default, and its hover tint is opaque rather than a 50% wash.
- **Why it matters**: There are 109 `<Card>` usages in 9router and 141 in openproxy, of which only 32 and 46 pass an explicit `padding` — so roughly 77 and 95 call sites each inherit this default. The p-6 to p-5 change alone reflows every card-based page (token-saver, pxpipe, quota, proxy-pools, pricing, combos). Losing shadow-soft flattens the default card against the canvas where 9router's cards read as raised panels. The opaque bg-surface-soft hover on Card.Row/ListItem is a bigger visual jump than 9router's bg-surface-2/50 on list rows.
- **Evidence**: 9R: src/shared/components/Card.js:21,22,29,30,46,49,82,103. OP: web/src/shared/components/Card.tsx:57,69,70,71,75,104,130,132,148,152,159,192,213.
- **Parity fix**: In web/src/shared/components/Card.tsx set paddings to `md: "p-6"`, `lg: "p-8"`, `xl: "p-10"` (:69-71); set the default radius to 14px (:57, :75); change :130 to `elev ? "shadow-[var(--shadow-elev)]" : "shadow-[var(--shadow-soft)]"`; change the canvas hover (:104) to `hover:border-brand-500/30 hover:shadow-[var(--shadow-warm)] transition-all`; drop the `text-[15px]` override on the title (:152) so it inherits 16px; and change the Card.Row / Card.ListItem hover (:192, :213) to `hover:bg-surface-2/50`.

## P215-001 · P2 · 9router src/shared/components/Header.js:230,260,275,285,291,296,324,328  vs  openproxy web/src/shared/components/Header.tsx:247,277,292,302,308,313,355-358

- **9router**: Header bar is `px-4 lg:px-8 pt-3 pb-2 border-b border-border-subtle bg-surface/60 backdrop-blur-xl lg:bg-transparent lg:backdrop-blur-none` (Header.js:230) — 12px top / 8px bottom, 60% white on mobile, and fully TRANSPARENT at lg so the page background shows through. The page H1 is Inter `text-base lg:text-2xl font-semibold tracking-tight truncate` (16px to 24px) in both the breadcrumb and title branches (:275, :291); description is `hidden lg:block text-sm text-text-muted truncate` with no margin (:296). Breadcrumb link is `text-text-muted hover:text-primary transition-colors` (:260). The title icon row is gap-2 (:285). The Donate glyph is `text-[18px]` (:324). Right actions end with ThemeToggle, HeaderLanguage and HeaderMenu (:326-329).
- **OpenProxy**: Header bar is `px-4 lg:px-8 pt-4 pb-4 border-b border-hairline-soft bg-canvas/95 backdrop-blur-xl lg:bg-canvas lg:backdrop-blur-none` (Header.tsx:247) — 16px top / 16px bottom, 95% opaque on mobile, and OPAQUE bg-canvas at lg. The page H1 is `font-serif font-normal text-[20px] lg:text-[30px] tracking-[-0.02em] text-ink truncate leading-tight` — 20px to 30px Instrument Serif at weight 400 (:292, :308). Description is `text-[13px] ... mt-1` (:313). Breadcrumb link is `text-muted hover:text-ink transition-colors text-[13px] font-medium` (:277). Icon row is gap-2.5 (:302). Donate glyph is `text-[20px]`. Right actions are only ThemeToggle and HeaderMenu (:355-358) — no HeaderLanguage.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's page header is 12px taller, opaque at desktop instead of transparent, uses a 20-to-30px serif title instead of 16-to-24px Inter semibold, sets the description 1px smaller with a top margin, uses a 10px icon gap, and omits the language button.
- **Why it matters**: The H1 appears on every dashboard page. Inter-600 at 24px reads as a compact product title; Instrument Serif-400 at 30px reads as an editorial hero, shifting the whole page hierarchy one notch larger so titles visually compete with the `h2 text-lg font-semibold` section headings beneath them. The lg:bg-transparent to lg:bg-canvas change means OpenProxy paints an opaque bar across the top of the content area where 9router lets the landing-grid texture run underneath. The missing HeaderLanguage button removes one control from the right-hand action cluster.
- **Evidence**: 9R: src/shared/components/Header.js:230,260,275,285,291,296,324,326,327,328,329. OP: web/src/shared/components/Header.tsx:247,277,292,302,308,313,355,356,357,358 (no HeaderLanguage import or render).
- **Parity fix**: In web/src/shared/components/Header.tsx:247 change to `px-4 lg:px-8 pt-3 pb-2 border-b border-border-subtle bg-surface/60 backdrop-blur-xl lg:bg-transparent lg:backdrop-blur-none`; change both H1s (:292, :308) to `text-base lg:text-2xl font-semibold text-ink tracking-tight truncate`, dropping the serif overrides; change the description (:313) to `hidden lg:block text-sm text-text-muted truncate`; change the breadcrumb link (:277) to `text-text-muted hover:text-primary transition-colors`; change gap-2.5 to gap-2 (:302); change the Donate glyph to `text-[18px]`; and port HeaderLanguage.js from 9router into web/src/shared/components/HeaderLanguage.tsx and render it between `<ThemeToggle />` and `<HeaderMenu />` (:356-357) as 9router does at Header.js:328.

## P216-001 · P2 · 9router src/shared/components/HeaderMenu.js:100-127 + src/shared/components/Header.js:326-329  vs  openproxy web/src/shared/components/HeaderMenu.tsx:140-160 + web/src/shared/components/Header.tsx:355-358

- **9router**: The header renders `<HeaderLanguage />` as a dedicated right-action control between `<ThemeToggle />` and `<HeaderMenu />` (Header.js:326-329); HeaderLanguage.js is a flag-emoji button (`LOCALE_FLAGS[locale]`, `title="Language"`, `data-i18n-skip="true"`) that opens the LanguageSwitcher. The menu has five items: Change Log, Language, Theme, Remote, Logout (:140-160) — there is no Shutdown row and no shutdown ConfirmModal anywhere in HeaderMenu.tsx.

Separately, a user who wants to stop the server from the dashboard has no Shutdown row in the overflow menu. In web/src/shared/components/HeaderMenu.tsx restore the Shutdown row between Theme (:149) and Remote (:152) as `<MenuItem icon="power_settings_new" label="Shutdown" danger onClick={() => { close(); setShutdownOpen(true); }} />` and add the ConfirmModal with `title="Close Proxy"`, `message="Are you sure you want to close the proxy server?"`, `confirmText="Close"`, `cancelText="Cancel"`, `variant="danger"` plus the existing isShuttingDown loading state.

## P217-001 · P2 · 9router src/shared/components/Input.js:31,37-51  vs  openproxy web/src/shared/components/Input.tsx:30,38,48-69

- **9router**: Input is padding-driven with no fixed height: `w-full py-2.5 px-3 text-sm text-text-main bg-surface-2 rounded-[10px] border border-transparent` (Input.js:37-38) with `text-[16px] sm:text-sm` (:41) and the explicit comment "iOS zoom fix". The mobile 16px font therefore also grows the box to roughly 45px, keeping the tap target comfortable and preventing iOS auto-zoom. Icon glyph is `text-[20px]` (:31). Error and hint are `text-xs` 12px (:45, :49). Error state uses a ring: `ring-1 ring-red-500 ... border-red-500/40` (:42).
- **OpenProxy**: Input has a hard `h-10` (40px) alongside `py-2.5` (Input.tsx:48) while still carrying the same `text-[16px] sm:text-[14px]` iOS-zoom token (:53) — the 16px mobile font is now crammed into a fixed 40px box. Also `bg-canvas` instead of `bg-surface-2`, `rounded-mini-md` 8px instead of 10px, and a visible `border-hairline` instead of `border-transparent` (:48-49). Icon is `text-[18px]` (:38). Error and hint use `type-body-sm` = 14px (:63, :69). Error state uses a border colour, not a ring (:56).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's Input clamps mobile 16px text into a fixed 40px box, uses 8px corners and a hairline border instead of 10px corners and a transparent border, a 2px smaller icon, and 14px hint/error text where 9router uses 12px.
- **Why it matters**: Every form in the dashboard inherits this (AddConnection, EditConnection, AddCustomModel, proxy pool, OIDC/SAML, profile). On iOS the 16px font inside a 40px h-10 box is visibly cramped relative to 9router's ~45px padding-driven box and the padding is partly wasted. The text-xs to 14px change on hint and error text alters two-line form density, and the error ring is replaced by a plain red border so error fields read flatter.
- **Evidence**: 9R: src/shared/components/Input.js:31,37,38,41,42,45,47,49,51. OP: web/src/shared/components/Input.tsx:30,38,48,49,53,56,63,69.
- **Parity fix**: In web/src/shared/components/Input.tsx:48 drop the fixed `h-10`, restore `bg-surface-2` and `rounded-[10px]`, and restore `border-transparent`; set the size token back to `text-[16px] sm:text-sm` (:53); restore the icon to `text-[20px]` (:38); restore the error ring `ring-1 ring-red-500 focus:ring-2 focus:ring-red-500/40 border-red-500/40` (:56); and change the error (:63) and hint (:69) rows from `type-body-sm` back to `text-xs` to match 9router Input.js:45,49.
