## P218-001 · P2 · 9router src/shared/components/Modal.js:15,71-95 + src/shared/components/Tooltip.js:3,11-12  vs  openproxy web/src/shared/components/Modal.tsx:27,79-97 + web/src/shared/components/Tooltip.tsx:7-13

- **9router**: `closeOnOverlay = true` (Modal.js:15) so the dimmed backdrop dismisses the modal. Traffic lights are `hidden md:flex` (desktop only) with three 16px `w-4 h-4` dots: the red one is a real close button wrapped in `<Tooltip text="Close" position="top" color="#FF5F56">` and reveals a ✕ glyph on hover, while the other two are dimmed placeholders `bg-[#3a3a3a]/20 dark:bg-white/15 cursor-not-allowed` (:71-83). A separate ✕ icon button exists but is `md:hidden` (:94) — so desktop shows exactly one close affordance. Tooltip accepts a `color` prop (Tooltip.js:3, 11-12) that tints the bubble.
- **OpenProxy**: `closeOnOverlay = false` (Modal.tsx:27) so the backdrop does NOT dismiss. All three traffic lights always render (no hidden md:flex), are 12px `w-3 h-3`, all in full macOS red/yellow/green, and none is interactive — no Tooltip, no hover ✕ (:84-86). The ✕ button (:96) has no `md:hidden`, so it shows on desktop too. Tooltip's `color` prop was dropped entirely (Tooltip.tsx:7-13).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's modals show three bright non-functional 12px dots plus a ✕ on desktop where 9router shows one working 16px red dot, and OpenProxy refuses backdrop dismissal by default.
- **Why it matters**: Every modal's header chrome is visibly different: 9router's desktop header is a minimal red-dot-plus-title bar, OpenProxy's is a yellow/green/rainbow traffic-light row plus a second ✕ button, and the red dot no longer closes. Combined with closeOnOverlay=false, a user who clicks the dimmed backdrop to dismiss — the reflex 9router trains — gets no response at all. Dropping Tooltip's color prop removes the only API that tinted a tooltip to match a surface; its sole consumer was this red dot (Tooltip.js:11-12, Modal.js:72).
- **Evidence**: 9R: src/shared/components/Modal.js:15,50,51,60,71,72,77,82,83,94; src/shared/components/Tooltip.js:3,11,12. OP: web/src/shared/components/Modal.tsx:27,63,64,73,79,84,85,86,96; web/src/shared/components/Tooltip.tsx:7,13.
- **Parity fix**: Set `closeOnOverlay = true` in web/src/shared/components/Modal.tsx:27. Replace the traffic-light block (:79-86) with 9router's markup: wrap it in `hidden md:flex items-center gap-2 mr-4 ml-2`, use `w-4 h-4` dots, make the red one a `<Tooltip text="Close" position="top" color="#FF5F56">`-wrapped button with the group/dot hover ✕, and render the other two as `w-4 h-4 rounded-full bg-[#3a3a3a]/20 dark:bg-white/15 cursor-not-allowed`. Add `md:hidden` to the ✕ button (:96). Re-add `color?: string` to the Tooltip props in web/src/shared/components/Tooltip.tsx:7 and apply it via inline backgroundColor as 9router's Tooltip.js:11-12 does.

## P219-001 · P2 · 9router src/shared/components/ModelSelectModal.js:496-501,536-545  vs  openproxy web/src/shared/components/ModelSelectModal.tsx:576-616,633-650,674-688

- **9router**: The modal opens with a coral hint bar directly under the header: `flex items-center gap-2 mb-3 px-2.5 py-2 bg-primary/8 border border-primary/20 rounded-lg text-xs text-text-muted` containing a 14px info glyph and the copy "Click to add, click again to remove. Changes are saved automatically." (ModelSelectModal.js:498-501). Combo chips have THREE states — selected `bg-primary text-white border-primary`, already-added-but-not-focused `bg-primary border-primary text-white hover:bg-primary-hover` prefixed with a 10px check glyph, and plain (:536-545) — so a user editing a combo can see which members are already included. The modal passes `footer={null}` (:496) and has no Done button.
- **OpenProxy**: No hint bar at all — the modal body starts with the search field (ModelSelectModal.tsx:576 onward). Combo chips have only TWO states, selected and plain (:674-688); the already-added fill and check marker are gone. A new horizontal provider-outline chip row (`flex gap-1.5 overflow-x-auto pb-2 -mx-1 px-1 scrollbar-thin` with 11px rounded-full chips, a coloured dot and a model count) is inserted between the search field and the list (:633-650). A footer is rendered: a multi-select bar with `{n} selected` plus Close/Apply when selectionMode is "multi", or a full-width Done button when closeOnSelect is false (:583-616).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's model picker lost 9router's instructional hint bar and its already-added combo-chip state, and gained a provider-outline chip row plus footer buttons that 9router does not have.
- **Why it matters**: ModelSelectModal is the single model picker used everywhere (per AGENTS.md core surface #4, with 19 consumer sites in openproxy). Losing the hint bar removes the only in-modal explanation of the click-to-add, click-again-to-remove, autosave interaction, so first-time users get no guidance. Losing the already-added chip state means a user editing an existing combo can no longer tell which members are already in it from the chip itself. The added outline row pushes the actual model list 30+px further down in an already-capped max-h-[400px] scroller.
- **Evidence**: 9R: src/shared/components/ModelSelectModal.js:496,498,499,500,501,536,537,538,539,540,541,542,543,544,545. OP: web/src/shared/components/ModelSelectModal.tsx:583-616 (footer), 633-650 (outline row), 674-688 (two-state chip); no info-bar block present.
- **Parity fix**: Re-insert the hint bar in web/src/shared/components/ModelSelectModal.tsx immediately before the search block (before :616) using 9router's exact classes `flex items-center gap-2 mb-3 px-2.5 py-2 bg-primary/8 border border-primary/20 rounded-lg text-xs text-text-muted` with the 14px info glyph and the same copy. Restore the third combo-chip state at :676-687 (the `addedModelValues.includes(combo.name)` branch with `bg-primary border-primary text-white hover:bg-primary-hover` and a 10px check glyph) to match ModelSelectModal.js:538-545. Leave the outline row and footer in place only where openproxy's multi-select consumers need them; gate them behind the selectionMode prop so the default closeOnSelect render matches 9router.

## P220-001 · P2 · 9router src/shared/components/SegmentedControl.js:12-15,19-38  vs  openproxy web/src/shared/components/SegmentedControl.tsx:35,39-41,79-104,113-146

- **9router**: SegmentedControl has a single, always-grouped render: one container `inline-flex items-center p-1 rounded-[10px] overflow-x-auto bg-surface-2` (:19-23) holding `shrink-0 px-4 rounded-[8px] font-medium transition-all` buttons (:27-29); the active tab is a raised surface pill `bg-surface text-text-main shadow-sm` (:34) and inactive tabs are bare `text-text-muted hover:text-text-main` (:35). Sizes: sm `h-7 text-xs`, md `h-9 text-sm`, lg `h-11 text-base` (:12-15).
- **OpenProxy**: The component grew a `variant` prop whose DEFAULT is `"pill"` (SegmentedControl.tsx:35), which renders a different control: no shared container, `inline-flex items-center gap-2 flex-wrap` with an 8px gap between separate `shrink-0 rounded-full font-semibold` buttons whose active state is `bg-ink text-canvas border border-ink` (:79-104). The 9router-equivalent grouped control exists but is only reachable by explicitly passing `variant="segmented"` (:113-146). Sizes also differ: sm `h-7 text-[12px] px-3`, md `h-9 text-[13px] px-4`, lg `h-11 text-[14px] px-5` (:39-41) — 13px/14px where 9router uses 14px/16px.
- **Gap**: Every `<SegmentedControl>` call that omits `variant` renders OpenProxy's free-standing ink-filled pill row instead of 9router's single grouped segmented bar, and the type scale is 1-2px smaller.
- **Why it matters**: All three call sites omit `variant`: 9router src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/usage/page.js:46 and :56 vs openproxy web/src/components/usage/UsagePageClient.tsx:65 and :78, plus web/src/components/compression/CompressionPageClient.tsx:94. So the Usage page tab bar (Overview/Providers/Analytics/Compression/Details), the period selector (Today/24h/7d/30d/60d) and the whole Compression page period selector render as pills instead of a segmented control — the most prominent control on those two pages.
- **Evidence**: 9R: src/shared/components/SegmentedControl.js:12,13,14,15,19,21,27,34,35; call sites src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/usage/page.js:46,56. OP: web/src/shared/components/SegmentedControl.tsx:35,39,40,41,79,89,96,113; call sites web/src/components/usage/UsagePageClient.tsx:65,78 and web/src/components/compression/CompressionPageClient.tsx:94.
- **Parity fix**: Change the default in web/src/shared/components/SegmentedControl.tsx:35 from `variant = "pill"` to `variant = "segmented"`, set the segmented sizes (:39-41) to `sm: "h-7 text-xs"`, `md: "h-9 text-sm"`, `lg: "h-11 text-base"`, and set its container to `inline-flex items-center p-1 rounded-[10px] overflow-x-auto bg-surface-2` with buttons `shrink-0 px-4 rounded-[8px] font-medium transition-all`, active `bg-surface text-text-main shadow-sm`, inactive `text-text-muted hover:text-text-main` — byte-identical to 9router SegmentedControl.js:12-38. Leave the pill and underline variants in place as opt-in extras.

## P221-001 · P2 · 9router src/shared/components/Sidebar.js:112,145-158,308-323  vs  openproxy web/src/shared/components/Sidebar.tsx:20-22,190,242-257

- **9router**: Sidebar `<aside>` is `w-72 ... bg-vibrancy backdrop-blur-xl` (Sidebar.js:112); `.bg-vibrancy` applies `backdrop-filter: blur(20px)` and a translucent `rgba(255,255,255,0.72)`, so the panel is a frosted macOS-vibrancy surface. Nav rows are `px-3 py-1 rounded-lg transition-all` (:145); active is `bg-primary/10 text-primary` (:146); the icon span is `isActive(item.href) ? "fill-1" : "group-hover:text-primary transition-colors"` (:152-153) so hovering an inactive row turns its glyph coral; the label is `text-[13px] font-medium` (:158). The System section also contains a 9English row linking to 9english.net (:308-323).
- **OpenProxy**: Sidebar `<aside>` is `w-72 ... bg-canvas` (Sidebar.tsx:190) — an opaque flat surface with no blur, so the frosted translucency is gone. NAV_ITEM_BASE is `relative flex items-center gap-3 pl-4 pr-3 py-1.5 rounded-mini-md transition-colors group` (:20) — 6px vertical padding instead of 4px, 16px left instead of 12px, and transition-colors not transition-all. Active is `bg-surface-card text-ink` plus a 3px coral left rail injected via a `before:` pseudo-element (:21). The icon span is `isActive(item.href) ? "fill-1" : ""` (:251) — the group-hover:text-primary hover colour is gone, so inactive icons never recolour on hover. The label is `text-[13px]` with no font-medium (:255). There is no 9English row.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's sidebar is an opaque panel instead of a frosted-vibrancy one, its nav rows are 4px taller with different insets, its active state is a left rail instead of a coral tint, inactive nav icons no longer recolour on hover, active labels lost their 500 weight, and the 9English row is missing.
- **Why it matters**: The sidebar is on screen on every dashboard page. The vibrancy loss is the most visible change — 9router's sidebar shows a blurred hint of the page behind it, OpenProxy's is a flat block. The taller rows (+4px each across ~13 rows) push Settings further down. The dropped group-hover:text-primary removes the coral-icon-on-hover affordance on every nav item. The missing 9English row makes the System section one entry shorter.
- **Evidence**: 9R: src/shared/components/Sidebar.js:112,145,146,152,153,158,308,310,322; src/app/globals.css `.bg-vibrancy` (blur(20px), rgba(255,255,255,0.72)). OP: web/src/shared/components/Sidebar.tsx:20,21,22,190,242,251,255 (no 9English match in the file).
- **Parity fix**: In web/src/shared/components/Sidebar.tsx:190 change `bg-canvas` to `bg-vibrancy backdrop-blur-xl`. Change NAV_ITEM_BASE (:20) to `flex items-center gap-3 px-3 py-1 rounded-lg transition-all group` (keep only `relative` if the rail is retained) and NAV_ITEM_ACTIVE (:21) to `bg-primary/10 text-primary`, dropping the before: rail. Restore the icon hover in the nav render (:251) to `isActive(item.href) ? "fill-1" : "group-hover:text-primary transition-colors"`, restore font-medium on the label (:255), and re-add the 9English anchor row from 9router Sidebar.js:308-323 after the Remote row.

## P222-001 · P2 · 9router src/shared/components/layouts/DashboardLayout.js:8-27,44-61  vs  openproxy web/src/shared/components/layouts/DashboardLayout.tsx:14-42,96-109

- **9router**: Toasts are severity-washed: getToastStyle returns a per-type wrapper such as `border-green-500/30 bg-green-500/10 text-green-600 dark:text-green-400` (DashboardLayout.js:12, and the red/amber/blue equivalents at :17, :22, :26). The toast is `rounded-lg border px-3 py-2 shadow-lg backdrop-blur-sm` (:48) — 12px horizontal / 8px vertical padding with a frosted blur. Inside: icon `text-[18px] leading-5` (:51), title `text-xs font-semibold mb-0.5` (:53), message `text-xs whitespace-pre-wrap break-words` (:54), all inheriting the toast's severity colour. Row gap is gap-2; the dismiss button is `text-current/70 hover:text-current` (:60), tinted by the toast's own colour.
- **OpenProxy**: Toasts are neutral: every type's wrapper is `bg-surface border border-hairline before:bg-<colour>` (DashboardLayout.tsx:17, :25, :33, :40) and the toast body adds a 3px before: rail `before:absolute before:left-0 before:top-0 before:bottom-0 before:w-[3px]` (:96) — severity colour is carried only by that rail and the icon, not the surface. Geometry is `rounded-mini-md pl-4 pr-3 py-3 shadow-modal` (:96) — 16px left / 12px right / 12px vertical, and backdrop-blur-sm is gone. Icon is `text-[20px] leading-5` (:100), title `text-[13px] font-semibold mb-0.5` (:101), message `text-[12px] leading-snug text-text-muted` (:102) in a fixed muted colour rather than the severity colour. Row gap is gap-2.5; dismiss is `text-text-muted hover:text-ink` (:108).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's toasts lost their per-severity tinted background, border and text, gained a 3px left colour rail, are 4px taller, use a 20px icon and 13px title instead of 18px and 12px, and dropped the frosted blur.
- **Why it matters**: Toasts are the primary feedback channel for every mutation in the dashboard (provider create/delete, combo edit, model disable, key toggle). 9router's success toast is unmistakably green and its error toast unmistakably red at a glance; OpenProxy's are all cream with a 3px rail, so severity must be read from a 3px stripe and a 20px glyph. The 4px height increase plus the larger icon and title makes OpenProxy toasts noticeably chunkier. This matters most on the error path, where the toast may be the only feedback.
- **Evidence**: 9R: src/shared/components/layouts/DashboardLayout.js:12,17,22,26,48,51,53,54,60. OP: web/src/shared/components/layouts/DashboardLayout.tsx:17,25,33,40,96,100,101,102,108.
- **Parity fix**: Restore 9router's per-type wrapper strings in getToastStyle (web/src/shared/components/layouts/DashboardLayout.tsx:14-42) — success `border-green-500/30 bg-green-500/10 text-green-600 dark:text-green-400`, error `border-red-500/30 bg-red-500/10 text-red-600 dark:text-red-400`, warning `border-amber-500/30 bg-amber-500/10 text-amber-600 dark:text-amber-400`, info `border-blue-500/30 bg-blue-500/10 text-blue-600 dark:text-blue-400` — drop the before:bg-* rail, and change the toast container (:96) to `rounded-lg border px-3 py-2 shadow-lg backdrop-blur-sm ${style.wrapper}`. Restore icon `text-[18px] leading-5` (:100), title `text-xs font-semibold mb-0.5` (:101), message `text-xs whitespace-pre-wrap break-words` with no text-text-muted (:102), row gap gap-2 (:99) and dismiss `text-current/70 hover:text-current` (:108). Keep OpenProxy's added role="alert" / aria-live attributes — they are an accessibility improvement, not a visual regression.

## P223-001 · P2 · 9router src/sse/handlers/fetch.js:97,106; OpenProxy src/server/api/web_fetch.rs:168 -> src/core/combo/mod.rs:754-762, 814

- **9router**: handleFetch resolves `const comboStickyLimit = settings.comboStickyRoundRobinLimit` (fetch.js:97) and passes it to handleComboChat as comboStickyLimit (fetch.js:106), so a round-robin web/search combo honours the same sticky setting as chat. search.js:77,86, tts.js:52,61 and imageGeneration.js:54,63 do the same.
- **OpenProxy**: web_fetch.rs:168 calls crate::core::combo::execute_combo_strategy(models, combo_name, strategy, ...), which delegates to execute_combo_strategy_with_capacity (mod.rs:754-762) and that hardcodes `1, // sticky default` (mod.rs:814) and None for required_caps. settings.combo_sticky_round_robin_limit is read only by the chat path (chat.rs:533).
- **Gap**: The /v1/web/fetch combo path hardcodes a sticky limit of 1, ignoring settings.comboStickyRoundRobinLimit.
- **Why it matters**: With a sticky limit of 5 configured, a round-robin web/search combo rotates on every single request on OpenProxy instead of pinning each member for 5 requests. That defeats the purpose of stickiness (connection reuse, prompt-cache reuse, avoiding per-provider cold starts) and produces a different distribution of load across the combo's providers than 9router.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/sse/handlers/fetch.js:97 (`comboStickyLimit = settings.comboStickyRoundRobinLimit`), :106 (passed to handleComboChat). OpenProxy: src/server/api/web_fetch.rs:168 (execute_combo_strategy), src/core/combo/mod.rs:754-762 and :814 (`1, // sticky default`).
- **Parity fix**: Add a sticky parameter to execute_combo_strategy (or call execute_combo_strategy_full directly from web_fetch.rs:168) and pass state.db.snapshot().settings.combo_sticky_round_robin_limit.max(1), matching fetch.js:97.

## P224-001 · P2 · 9router src/sse/handlers/videoGeneration.js:177-186, :234-236 vs OpenProxy src/server/api/media.rs:1459-1479, :1624-1630

- **9router**: Every video failure calls markAccountUnavailable(credentials.connectionId, result.status, sanitizeSecrets(result.error, ...), provider, model) (videoGeneration.js:177-179, and :234-236 for polls), which writes a model lock, testStatus:"unavailable", lastError, errorCode and an incremented backoffLevel (src/sse/services/auth.js:239-287). Success calls clearAccountError (videoGeneration.js:171, :230). The cooldown state is therefore shared with the chat pipeline.
- **OpenProxy**: grep for account_registry / mark_account / is_account_unavailable / apply_error_state in src/server/api/media.rs returns nothing — the video path never touches the account-fallback registry that chat.rs uses (chat.rs:1535). Video failures leave the connection's rate_limited_until, lastError and backoffLevel untouched, and account selection (select_media_connections, media.rs:726-742) filters only on provider/is_active/has-credentials — it never consults is_account_unavailable, so a video rate-limit does not quarantine the account for subsequent video or chat requests.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's video path does not record failures or cooldowns in the shared account-fallback state, so a rate-limited video account is not excluded from later selection.
- **Why it matters**: A video 429 does not put the account into cooldown, so the next video create immediately retries the same exhausted account and burns latency before falling through; and because the state is shared in 9router, a user who sees a video rate-limit correctly stops routing chat traffic to that account there but not in OpenProxy.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/sse/handlers/videoGeneration.js:177-179, :234-236 (markAccountUnavailable), :171, :230 (clearAccountError), implementation at src/sse/services/auth.js:239-287. OpenProxy: src/server/api/media.rs:726-742 (select_media_connections filters only provider/is_active/credentials — no is_account_unavailable call), and no account_registry reference anywhere in the file.
- **Parity fix**: On a non-rotation video failure, call the existing account_fallback::apply_error_state (or the registry equivalent used by chat.rs:1535) with the upstream status and the sanitized error, and on success call reset_account_state — mirroring markAccountUnavailable/clearAccountError. Then filter select_media_connections through account_fallback::is_account_unavailable (account_fallback/mod.rs:603) so video requests skip accounts already in cooldown, which is what 9router achieves via isModelLockActive in getProviderCredentials (src/sse/services/auth.js:87).

## P225-001 · P2 · 9router: open-sse/handlers/embeddingProviders/openai.js:20-28 and embeddingProviders/gemini.js:16-34 · openproxy: src/core/media/embeddings/base.rs:32-37, :166-170, :285-326

- **9router**: The OpenAI-family adapter coerces: `if (dimensions != null && dimensions !== "") { const dim = Number(dimensions); if (Number.isFinite(dim) && dim > 0) body.dimensions = dim; }` (openai.js:23-26). `Number()` means `"256"` → 256, `256.0` → 256, `1.5` → 1.5, `true` → 1, all forwarded; `"abc"`, `0`, `-1`, `null` and `""` are dropped. The Gemini adapter uses the identical `Number(dimensions)` → `outputDimensionality` coercion (gemini.js:18-19).
- **OpenProxy**: `EmbeddingRequest::dimensions()` is `body.get("dimensions").and_then(|v| v.as_u64()).filter(|&n| n > 0)` (base.rs:32-37). `serde_json::Value::as_u64` only succeeds for a genuine unsigned-integer JSON token, so `"256"`, `256.0`, `1.5` and `true` all yield `None` and the field is silently omitted from the upstream body (base.rs:166-170 for OpenAI, :291/:304-308/:319-323 for Gemini's `outputDimensionality`). Only a bare positive JSON integer survives.
- **Gap**: A `dimensions` value that is not a bare JSON integer (notably the string `"256"`, and also `256.0`) is forwarded by 9router and silently dropped by OpenProxy.
- **Why it matters**: The client asked for a specific vector width and gets a different one with HTTP 200 and no warning — the worst failure mode for a vector store, which only complains later at insert time with a dimension-mismatch. Numeric-string dimensions appear in hand-written curl calls, shell/JSON-template configs and some SDK wrappers that stringify query values, so this is reachable in practice and the divergence is invisible until the index blows up.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/handlers/embeddingProviders/openai.js:20-28; open-sse/handlers/embeddingProviders/gemini.js:16-34 · openproxy: src/core/media/embeddings/base.rs:32-37, :156-172, :285-326
- **Parity fix**: Change `EmbeddingRequest::dimensions()` to reproduce the JS `Number()` semantics over a `f64` rather than `u64`: accept `as_f64()`, keep the value when `is_finite() && > 0.0`, and return `Option<f64>`; emit it as a JSON number (or, to match 9router's `1.5`/`true`→`1` behaviour, `json!(value)` after the same finite/positive test). Note the existing tests `build_body_includes_dimensions_when_set` (base.rs:448-458) and `gemini_picks_batch_for_array_input` only cover integer inputs and will still pass.

## P226-001 · P2 · 9router: open-sse/handlers/embeddingsCore.js:32-38 (+ embeddingProviders/index.js:25-31) · openproxy: src/core/media/embeddings/mod.rs:46-68, src/server/api/media.rs:533-544, :546, :790-800, :830-841

- **9router**: `getEmbeddingAdapter(provider)` returns null for anything outside the 11 OpenAI-compatible ids, gemini/google_ai_studio, selfhosted-embedding, and the `openai-compatible-*` / `custom-embedding-*` node prefixes (embeddingProviders/index.js:25-31). `handleEmbeddingsCore` then returns `createErrorResult(400, "Provider '<p>' does not support embeddings.")` (embeddingsCore.js:32-38) with no network call at all. This is a live case: `venice` and `tokenrouter` both declare `serviceKinds: ["embedding"]` with a populated `embeddingConfig` (registry/venice.js:46-50, registry/tokenrouter.js:54-58) so they appear in the dashboard's embedding page, yet they are absent from `OPENAI_COMPAT_PROVIDERS`, so 9router 400s on them.
- **OpenProxy**: `get_embedding_adapter` (mod.rs:46-68) mirrors the same id list, so `dispatch` returns `None`; `try_provider_adapter` propagates the `None` and `execute_media_provider` falls through to the generic forwarder (media.rs:546). `build_media_url`'s `"embeddings"` arm (media.rs:790-800) does `format!("{}/embeddings", base_url.trim_end_matches('/'))` where `base_url` comes from `get_provider_base_url` (media.rs:830-841), which returns the provider's full CHAT endpoint from `PROVIDER_CONFIGS` or, failing that, `https://api.{provider}.com/v1`. For `anthropic` that is `https://api.anthropic.com/v1/messages` (default.rs:46-48) → the live POST goes to `https://api.anthropic.com/v1/messages/embeddings`; for `venice` it is `https://api.venice.ai/api/v1/chat/completions` (default.rs:241-244) → `.../chat/completions/embeddings`.
- **Gap**: For a provider with no embedding adapter 9router refuses locally with a clear 400, while OpenProxy issues a live POST to a nonsense URL built by appending `/embeddings` to the provider's chat-completions endpoint.
- **Why it matters**: The input text and the provider API key leave the machine for an endpoint that does not exist — a request 9router never makes — and the user gets a 404/400 from the upstream instead of "Provider 'anthropic' does not support embeddings." The status code and message a client sees now depend on the provider's CDN rather than on the router, and the failure is indistinguishable from a genuinely misconfigured base URL.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/handlers/embeddingsCore.js:32-38; open-sse/handlers/embeddingProviders/index.js:7-31; open-sse/providers/registry/venice.js:46-50; open-sse/providers/registry/tokenrouter.js:54-58 · openproxy: src/core/media/embeddings/mod.rs:46-68, src/server/api/media.rs:533-544, :546, :790-800, :830-841, src/core/executor/default.rs:46-48, :241-244
- **Parity fix**: Either return the 400 before the generic forwarder for the embeddings route, or make the generic arm correct. Preferred parity fix: in `try_provider_adapter`/`execute_media_provider`, when `route_kind == "embeddings"` and `embeddings::get_embedding_adapter(provider)` is `None`, return `json_error_response(StatusCode::BAD_REQUEST, &format!("Provider '{provider}' does not support embeddings."))` instead of falling through. (The `openai-embedding` / `cohere-embedding` branches at media.rs:791-796 are dead — neither id exists in `PROVIDER_CONFIGS` or the dashboard registries — so nothing regresses when the fallthrough is removed for this route kind.)

## P227-001 · P2 · 9router: open-sse/handlers/embeddingsCore.js:33-38 + src/sse/handlers/embeddings.js:78-82 (explicit 400s) vs OpenProxy: src/core/media/embeddings/mod.rs:30 / 66 + src/server/api/media.rs:1092-1101 (silent fall-through to the generic proxy)

- **9router**: getEmbeddingAdapter returns null for any provider outside the 11 OPENAI_COMPAT_PROVIDERS + gemini/google_ai_studio + selfhosted-embedding + the openai-compatible-*/custom-embedding-* namespaces (index.js:7-30), and the core converts that into an explicit 400 `Provider 'X' does not support embeddings.` (embeddingsCore.js:33-38). The route layer likewise returns 400 `Invalid model format` when getModelInfo yields no provider (embeddings.js:78-82) and 400 `No credentials for provider: X` when none exist (embeddings.js:108-111). Every rejection is a clean, named 400 — the request never leaves the process.
- **OpenProxy**: get_embedding_adapter returns None for an unrecognised provider (mod.rs:66), which makes embeddings::dispatch return None (mod.rs:30), which makes try_provider_adapter return None (media.rs:1101, `Some(media_result_to_response(result?))` short-circuits on the None), which makes execute_media_provider fall through to the generic forwarder (media.rs:546). There, build_media_url appends "/embeddings" to get_provider_base_url (media.rs:790-800, :830-841), which falls back to `provider_config_base_url(provider)` and, failing that, to the synthetic `https://api.{provider}.com/v1` (media.rs:840). The body is then POSTed to that guessed URL. The error string `does not support embeddings` does not exist anywhere in the Rust tree.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy has no unsupported-provider rejection for embeddings — an unrecognised provider silently falls through to a generic proxy that POSTs the request (and the API key) to a guessed api.<provider>.com URL instead of returning the named 400.
- **Why it matters**: A request naming a provider with no embeddings adapter (cohere, venice, tokenrouter, any typo) is not rejected — it is forwarded to a synthesised URL. For a provider absent from PROVIDER_CONFIGS the target is https://api.<provider>.com/v1/embeddings, which is a real outbound request carrying the user's input text and their API key to a host that was never configured. For a provider present in PROVIDER_CONFIGS but without an adapter (cohere, venice, tokenrouter), it appends /embeddings to the CHAT base URL — e.g. https://api.venice.ai/api/v1/chat/completions/embeddings — producing a confusing 404 from the provider instead of the actionable `Provider 'venice' does not support embeddings.` The client also loses the 400-vs-502 distinction it needs to tell "you configured this wrong" from "the provider is down".
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/handlers/embeddingProviders/index.js:7-11 (11 providers), :25-30 (null for anything else); open-sse/handlers/embeddingsCore.js:33-38 (400 `Provider 'X' does not support embeddings.`); src/sse/handlers/embeddings.js:78-82 (400 Invalid model format), :108-111 (400 No credentials). OpenProxy: src/core/media/embeddings/mod.rs:30 (`get_embedding_adapter(provider)?` → None), :66 (None arm); src/server/api/media.rs:1092-1094 + :1101 (`result?` short-circuit → None), :546 (fall-through), :790-800 (build_media_url appends /embeddings), :830-841 (get_provider_base_url, `https://api.{provider}.com/v1` at :840); grep for "does not support embeddings" across src/ and web/src/ returns nothing.
- **Parity fix**: Make the unsupported case explicit. In try_provider_adapter (media.rs:1073-1102), distinguish "no adapter for this route_kind" from "adapter exists": for route_kind == "embeddings", call get_embedding_adapter(provider) explicitly and, when it is None, return Some(json_error_response(StatusCode::BAD_REQUEST, &format!("Provider '{provider}' does not support embeddings."))) instead of letting the `?` fall through. Separately, make get_provider_base_url (media.rs:830-841) fail closed rather than synthesising https://api.{provider}.com/v1 — return an error when neither provider_specific_data.baseUrl nor provider_config_base_url yields a value, so no request text or key is ever sent to a guessed host.

## P228-001 · P2 · 9router: open-sse/handlers/embeddingsCore.js:85-117 · openproxy: src/core/media/embeddings/handler.rs:78-140

- **9router**: On 401/403 (when `!executor.noAuth`), `refreshWithRetry(() => executor.refreshCredentials(credentials, log), 3, log)` is attempted. The re-request is gated on `if (newCredentials?.accessToken || newCredentials?.apiKey)` (embeddingsCore.js:91) — if the refresh yields nothing (the normal case for a plain API-key provider with no refresh token) it only logs "refresh failed" and falls through to `if (!providerResponse.ok)` (line 112) using the ORIGINAL single response. Exactly one upstream request is made.
- **OpenProxy**: Same guard shape `if (status == 401 || status == 403) && !adapter.no_auth()`, but the fall-through is `res = client.post(&url).headers(headers).json(&body).send().await` (handler.rs:131-139, comment "re-fire the original once"). Because the refresh block is skipped entirely when `refresh_token` is empty (handler.rs:85), the common API-key case (`openai`, `openrouter`, `mistral`, `jina-ai`, `selfhosted-embedding`, all `openai-compatible-*` nodes) skips the refresh and lands directly in the re-fire.
- **Gap**: When a 401/403 arrives and there is no refresh token, OpenProxy re-POSTs the identical request to the upstream, doubling the number of upstream calls that 9router makes.
- **Why it matters**: Every rejected key is presented twice, which double-counts failed auth attempts (some providers lock or throttle on repeated 401s), doubles the latency of a failing request, and makes the upstream auth-failure counters diverge from what the router's own logs show. For self-hosted or metered gateways the duplicate is a real second billable attempt at the moment the operator is already debugging a credential problem.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/handlers/embeddingsCore.js:80-110 (:91 gate), :112-117 · openproxy: src/core/media/embeddings/handler.rs:78-86, :130-140
- **Parity fix**: Delete the unconditional re-fire at handler.rs:131-139 and let the original `res` fall through to the `if !res.status().is_success()` check at handler.rs:142-146. The refresh-and-retry should fire only when `dispatch_oauth_refresh` actually returned a token, mirroring the `if (newCredentials?.accessToken || newCredentials?.apiKey)` gate at embeddingsCore.js:91.

## P229-001 · P2 · 9router: open-sse/translator/response/openai-responses.js:415-436; openproxy: src/core/translator/registry.rs:614-658

- **9router**: `openaiResponsesToOpenAIResponse(chunk, state)` (openai-responses.js:415) starts with `if (!chunk) { if (state.finishReasonSent || !state.started) return null; ... return finalChunk; }` (:416-436). stream.js:447 calls `translateResponse(targetFormat, sourceFormat, null, state)` in `flush()` for every stream, so a Codex->OpenAI-client stream that ends without `response.completed` still gets a terminal chunk with `finish_reason` (computed by `computeFinishReason`, :419-424) and any accumulated `usage`.
- **OpenProxy**: `TranslationRegistry::finish_stream` (registry.rs:614-658) returns an empty Vec for every pair except Kiro; the `if source == Format::Kiro || target == Format::Kiro` branch at :628 is the only one with a flush. `responses_to_chat_streaming` only emits its terminal chunk from the `response.completed` arm (openai_responses.rs:1223-1259), which requires the upstream to actually send that event.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy has no end-of-stream flush for the Responses->Chat direction, so a Codex-provider stream that closes without `response.completed` ends with no `finish_reason` frame at all.
- **Why it matters**: An OpenAI-compatible client in front of a Codex/grok-cli Responses upstream never receives `finish_reason`, so `stream_options.include_usage` accounting is skipped, the AI SDK leaves the message without a finish reason, and several CLIs treat the run as still in progress after the socket closes. 9Router always terminates the message.
- **Evidence**: 9router open-sse/translator/response/openai-responses.js:415-436 (flush at :416-436), open-sse/utils/stream.js:447, 456-463 | openproxy src/core/translator/registry.rs:614-658, src/core/translator/response/openai_responses.rs:1223-1259
- **Parity fix**: Extend `finish_stream` to also handle `source == Format::OpenAiResponses && target == Format::OpenAi`: if `finishReasonSent` is not set and `started` is, compute the finish reason with the same rule as `computeFinishReason` (toolCallIndex > 0 || currentToolCallId present -> "tool_calls", else "stop"), build the `chat.completion.chunk` from `chatId`/`created`/`model` with `delta:{}` and that `finish_reason`, attach `state["usage"]` when present, set `finishReasonSent`, and return it as `data: {...}\n\n`.

## P230-001 · P2 · 9router: open-sse/utils/stream.js:88-93, 197-200, 320-321; openproxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:3850, 3876, 4148-4150

- **9router**: Usage is merged per event: stream.js:320-321 does `const extracted = extractUsage(parsed); if (extracted) state.usage = mergeUsage(state.usage, extracted);` for every translated chunk, and stream.js:197-200 does the same in passthrough mode. `finalizeStream` (stream.js:83-107) then logs with the merged value, falling back to `estimateUsage(body, totalContentLength, ...)` when nothing valid arrived (stream.js:90-93).
- **OpenProxy**: `let mut last_data: Option<Bytes> = None;` (chat.rs:3850) is overwritten on every chunk (`last_data = Some(chunk.clone())`, chat.rs:3876 / 4039) and `record_streaming_usage` reads only that one chunk: `last_data.as_ref().and_then(|b| extract_token_usage_from_bytes(b))` (chat.rs:4148-4150).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy records streaming usage from the single last raw chunk only; 9Router merges usage across every chunk and falls back to an estimate.
- **Why it matters**: When the provider puts usage in a dedicated final frame and the transport delivers a trailing `data: [DONE]` (or a keep-alive comment) as the last read, the usage frame is overwritten and lost — tokens record as 0. This is a dashboard/quota-accuracy bug: per-provider cost and quota-reset tracking silently under-report for exactly the providers that use the standard `stream_options.include_usage` frame.
- **Evidence**: 9router open-sse/utils/stream.js:88-93, 197-200, 320-321 | openproxy src/server/api/chat.rs:3850, 3876, 4039, 4148-4150
- **Parity fix**: Accumulate usage across the stream instead of keeping only the last frame: parse each chunk with `extract_token_usage_from_bytes` and merge into a running `Option<TokenUsage>` (keep the last non-null value; prefer a later frame over an earlier one for the same key). Pass that accumulator to `record_streaming_usage`, and apply the ST-005 estimator as a final fallback so a stream with no usage at all still records non-zero tokens.

## P231-001 · P2 · 9router: src/app/api/v1/models/info/route.js:18-40 · openproxy: src/server/api/v1_models.rs:16-33, :679-702

- **9router**: `buildInfo` copies per-model metadata straight off the registry entry, including `if (model.dimensions) out.dimensions = model.dimensions;` (line 29), plus `owned_by: alias` (line 23), `endpoint: KIND_ENDPOINT[kind] || null` (lines 5-14, 24) and `params` / `capabilities` / `options` / `contextWindow`. `dimensions` survives into `PROVIDER_MODELS` because `normalizeModel` returns the raw registry object untouched when `name` is present (open-sse/providers/models/schema.js:22-26), so every embedding model that declares it is served — `gemini/embedding-001` → 768 (registry/gemini.js:64), `jina-ai/jina-embeddings-v3` → 1024, `-v2-base-en` → 768, `-v2-base-code` → 768 (registry/jina-ai.js:28, :33, :38).
- **OpenProxy**: `ProviderCatalogModel` has no `dimensions` field (v1_models.rs… catalog.rs:16-33) and the merge block in `models_info` (v1_models.rs:679-702) emits only `name`, `quotaFamily`, `strip`, `targetFormat`, `upstreamModelId`, `contextWindow`, `capabilities`, `kind`. `grep -c '"dimensions"' src/core/model/provider_catalog.json` → 0, so the data is not present either. There is no `owned_by` and no `endpoint` field. Note: on the `/v1/embeddings` RESPONSE itself the two sides match — neither emits a dimension count, the OpenAI-family adapter is a passthrough (9router embeddingProviders/openai.js:29; openproxy base.rs:52-55) and both Gemini normalizers emit exactly `{object,data,model,usage:{prompt_tokens:0,total_tokens:0}}` (gemini.js:35-53 / base.rs:328-368). The gap is on the metadata endpoint.
- **Gap**: `/v1/models/info` never returns the embedding dimension count (nor `owned_by`, nor the per-kind `endpoint`), so a client cannot size its vector index from the router's own metadata.
- **Why it matters**: Dimension discovery is the whole reason to call an info endpoint for an embedding model. A client that reads `dimensions` gets `undefined` and must hardcode 1536/768/1024 per model, or worse, inserts a 3072-dim vector into an index sized for 1536 and fails at write time with an opaque dimension-mismatch error instead of a clear message. `endpoint` disappearing also removes the only machine-readable signal that an `embedding`-kind model belongs on `/v1/embeddings` rather than `/v1/chat/completions`.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/app/api/v1/models/info/route.js:29, :23, :5-14, :24; open-sse/providers/registry/gemini.js:64; open-sse/providers/registry/jina-ai.js:28,33,38; open-sse/providers/models/schema.js:22-26 · openproxy: src/core/model/catalog.rs:16-33 (no `dimensions` field), src/server/api/v1_models.rs:679-702 (no dimensions/owned_by/endpoint branch); `grep -c '"dimensions"' src/core/model/provider_catalog.json` = 0
- **Parity fix**: Add `#[serde(default)] pub dimensions: Option<u32>` to `ProviderCatalogModel` (catalog.rs:16-33), populate it in `provider_catalog.json` for the models that declare it, and emit it in the merge block of `models_info` alongside `contextWindow`: `if let Some(v) = cm.dimensions { info["dimensions"] = json!(v); }`. Also add `owned_by` (the resolved provider alias) and `endpoint` (mapping `kind` → `/v1/embeddings`, `/v1/chat/completions`, `/v1/audio/speech`, `/v1/audio/transcriptions`, `/v1/images/generations`, `/v1/search`, `/v1/fetch`) to match `KIND_ENDPOINT`.

## P232-001 · P2 · 9router: src/sse/handlers/embeddings.js:73-76 (+ open-sse/handlers/embeddingsCore.js:25-27, pinned by tests/unit/embeddingsCore.test.js:415-425) · openproxy: src/core/media/embeddings/handler.rs:38-45 + src/server/api/media.rs:470-482

- **9router**: `if (!body.input) return errorResponse(400, "Missing required field: input")` (embeddings.js:73) — a JS truthiness test, so `""`, `0`, `false` and `null` are all rejected before any model resolution. `handleEmbeddingsCore` repeats the same falsy check (embeddingsCore.js:25). The unit test pins this explicitly: "empty string input passes validation" / "Empty string is falsy → treated as missing" asserts `success === false, status === 400` (tests/unit/embeddingsCore.test.js:415-425). An empty ARRAY is truthy and does reach the provider (test at :427-437).
- **OpenProxy**: `generic_media_handler` validates only `model` (media.rs:475-482) and never looks at `input`. `handle_embeddings` then does `request.input().ok_or_else(|| Validation("Missing required field: input"))` (handler.rs:38-40) — `body.get("input")` returns `Some(Value::String(""))` for an empty string, so the `ok_or` does not fire, and the following guard `if !input.is_string() && !input.is_array()` (handler.rs:41) passes. `""` is therefore serialized into the upstream body by `OpenAiCompatAdapter::build_body` (base.rs:156-171) and sent. Only `0`/`false`/`null` are caught, and they return the message "input must be a string or array of strings" instead of 9router's "Missing required field: input".
- **Gap**: An empty-string `input` is rejected locally with 400 by 9router but forwarded to the provider by OpenProxy (and `null`/`0` yield a different 400 message).
- **Why it matters**: Same request, different status code and different error text depending on which router answers. An empty-string embed is a common client bug (unread file, empty template); 9router answers instantly with a precise 400, while OpenProxy spends a network round trip and returns whatever the provider says — an OpenAI-shaped 400 with a different message, or a 200 with a degenerate vector for a permissive server. Clients that branch on the error string to detect their own bug stop matching.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/sse/handlers/embeddings.js:73-76; open-sse/handlers/embeddingsCore.js:25-30; tests/unit/embeddingsCore.test.js:415-425 (empty string → 400), :427-437 (empty array → reaches provider) · openproxy: src/core/media/embeddings/handler.rs:38-45, src/server/api/media.rs:470-482, src/core/media/embeddings/base.rs:156-171
- **Parity fix**: Mirror the truthiness gate in `handle_embeddings` before the type check: treat `Value::Null`, `Value::String(s)` where `s.is_empty()`, `Value::Bool(false)`, and `Value::Number(n)` where `n.as_f64() == Some(0.0)` as missing — return `EmbeddingsHandlerError::Validation("Missing required field: input")` (HTTP 400) — keeping the existing "input must be a string or array of strings" only for objects/other types, exactly matching embeddingsCore.js:25-30. Leave the empty-array case passing through.

## P233-001 · P2 · Account-fallback lock behavior on a 4xx that matches no rule

- **9router**: `checkFallbackError` (`open-sse/services/accountFallback.js:23-51`) walks the single ordered `ERROR_RULES` list top-to-bottom. Text rules precede status rules, so for a 401 whose message does not contain any of the text needles, control reaches the status arm `{status:401, cooldownMs: COOLDOWN.long}` (`open-sse/config/errorConfig.js:75`) and returns `{shouldFallback:true, cooldownMs: 120000}`. An unmatched status falls to the final `return {shouldFallback:true, cooldownMs: TRANSIENT_COOLDOWN_MS}` (30 s). Every path returns `shouldFallback:true`.
- **OpenProxy**: `classify_error` (`src/core/config/error_config.rs:227-255`) walks the same ordered `ERROR_RULES`, then adds an extra terminal block absent from 9router: `if matches!(status, Some(400) | Some(401) | Some(402) | Some(403)) { return ErrorClassification::Permanent; }` (:251-253). `check_fallback_error` (`src/core/combo/mod.rs:570-605`) maps `Permanent` to `{should_fallback:true, cooldown: LONG_COOLDOWN}` — so 400/401/402/403 that match NO text rule get the long cooldown. The inline comment at `src/core/combo/mod.rs:596-599` claims 9router parity, but 9router's equivalent status rules make 401/402/403 long-cooldown anyway and give 400 the 30 s transient cooldown.
- **Gap**: For a 400 whose message matches no text rule, OpenProxy assigns a 2-minute account lock where 9router assigns the 30-second transient cooldown.
- **Why it matters**: 400 is the most common recoverable transient on these upstreams — a malformed-request 400 clears on the next correctly-formed request, and 9router's 30 s transient reflects that. OpenProxy's 2-minute lock means a single malformed request (a bad tool schema from a coding agent, an over-long field) takes that account out of rotation for 12× longer than intended. With one account configured, the user sees a hard failure for two minutes after a request that should have succeeded immediately on retry; with several, a burst of 400s across agents drains the pool far faster than intended and starves the sticky round-robin. The 401/402/403 statuses are unaffected in practice because the status rules already produce the long cooldown, so this is specifically a 400 regression.
- **Evidence**: 9router: `.tmp/9router/open-sse/services/accountFallback.js:23-51` (single ordered walk, final transient default), `.tmp/9router/open-sse/config/errorConfig.js:59-80` (no 400 status rule → falls through to 30 s). OpenProxy: `src/core/config/error_config.rs:251-253` (extra `Permanent` arm covering 400), `src/core/combo/mod.rs:596-600` (`Permanent` → `LONG_COOLDOWN`).
- **Parity fix**: Drop 400 from the terminal `Permanent` set in `classify_error` (`src/core/config/error_config.rs:251`) so it falls through to `NoMatch` → `TRANSIENT_COOLDOWN` (30 s), matching `accountFallback.js`'s absence of a 400 status rule. Keep 401/402/403 in the set only if the status rules that already give them the long cooldown are removed — the simplest parity-preserving change is to leave 401/402/403 listed and remove only 400, since the status rules make the former three behave identically either way.

## P234-001 · P2 · Add connection — unknown provider id accepted

- **9router**: POST /api/providers validates the provider against the registry before anything else: isValidProvider accepts APIKEY_PROVIDERS, FREE_TIER_PROVIDERS, dual-auth providers with authModes including apikey, WEB_COOKIE_PROVIDERS and the three compatible prefixes. Anything else returns HTTP 400 Invalid provider. Compatible providers additionally get prefix/baseUrl/nodeName resolved from the provider node, and a missing node returns 404.
- **OpenProxy**: create_provider_api only checks that the provider string is non-empty, then proceeds to write the connection. There is no registry membership test and no provider-node resolution or 404 check.
- **Gap**: An unrecognised provider id is accepted and persisted instead of rejected with 400.
- **Why it matters**: A typo or a stale provider id creates a live, active connection that the router can never dispatch; it shows in the connection list as a healthy row and consumes nothing, so the failure is invisible until a user tries to route a model through it. Compatible-provider connections also skip the node resolution that populates baseUrl, so they are created without the base URL their executor needs.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/providers/route.js:104-118 (isValidProvider plus the 400 Invalid provider return) and :131-162 (compatible node resolution with 404 on missing node). OpenProxy: src/server/api/mod.rs:1106-1127 (only empty-string checks on provider and name) and :1156-1213 (no registry lookup, no node resolution).
- **Parity fix**: Reproduce the isValidProvider gate before the name/key checks: accept providers present in the API-key, free-tier, web-cookie or dual-auth tables or matching the openai-compatible-, anthropic-compatible- and custom-embedding- prefixes, and return 400 Invalid provider otherwise. For the three compatible prefixes, resolve the provider node and return 404 when missing, copying prefix, apiType, baseUrl and nodeName into providerSpecificData as 9router does.

## P235-001 · P2 · Add connection — validation of no-auth providers

- **9router**: Validation reads AI_PROVIDERS[provider]?.noAuth === true from the registry and skips the key requirement entirely, so every registry entry flagged noAuth returns {valid:true} without a network call. Ten registry files set noAuth: true (searxng, devin-cli, mimo-free, opencode, tortoise, edge-tts, local-device, google-tts, mmf, coqui).
- **OpenProxy**: A hardcoded six-entry array short-circuits to {valid:true} (edge-tts, local-device, sdwebui, comfyui, ollama-local, opencode-zen). Anything else with no key hits the generic api_key.is_empty() guard and returns HTTP 400 API key is required before any registry lookup.
- **Gap**: The no-auth set is hardcoded and narrower than the provider registry, so six no-auth providers cannot be validated.
- **Why it matters**: SearXNG, Google TTS, Coqui TTS, Tortoise TTS, MMF and Devin CLI are declared noAuth: true in OpenProxy's own provider constants, but validating any of them with an empty key returns 400. The Add-Connection modal treats a 400 as failed, so a legitimate no-auth provider can never be added through the validated save path.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/providers/validate/route.js:91-94 (AI_PROVIDERS[provider]?.noAuth), with noAuth: true present in ten files under .tmp/9router/open-sse/providers/registry/ (searxng.js, google-tts.js, coqui.js, tortoise.js, mmf.js, devin-cli.js and others). OpenProxy: src/server/api/provider_validate.rs:112-123 (hardcoded 6-entry no_auth array) and :131-137 (empty-key 400). Registry mismatch visible at web/src/shared/constants/providers.ts:169-173, :188, :238 (google-tts, coqui, tortoise, searxng, mmf, devin-cli all noAuth: true).
- **Parity fix**: Drive the no-auth check from the provider registry/catalog table lookup on noAuth rather than a literal list, so any provider whose registry entry declares noAuth skips the key requirement and returns {valid:true}.

## P236-001 · P2 · CLI + agent-skill surface for video

- **9router**: `cli/cli.js:73` registers `const { run } = require("./src/cli/commands/xaiVideo")` as `9router xai video`, a 300-line one-shot that submits a job, polls with progress every 5 s up to a 600 s timeout, downloads `video.url` to `video.mp4.part` and atomically renames, honouring Ctrl+C. It is documented in `skills/9router-video/SKILL.md:60-69` with the full option set (`--model --duration --aspect-ratio --resolution --image --timeout --port --api-key`). `skills/9router-video/SKILL.md` also exists as a shippable agent skill (not listed in the dashboard SKILLS array, but fetchable by raw URL).
- **OpenProxy**: `MediaCmd` in `src/cli/media.rs:19-120` exposes Providers, Combo, Tts, Stt, Embed, Image, Search, Web, List, Add, Edit, Delete — there is no `Video` variant anywhere in the CLI. `web/src/shared/constants/skills.ts:21-107` lists eleven skills (openproxy, chat, image, tts, stt, embeddings, web-search, web-fetch, providers, combos, cli-tools, rtk) and none covers `/v1/videos/*`.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy ships no video CLI one-shot command and no video agent skill, while 9Router ships both.
- **Why it matters**: Even though the REST surface is largely present, a user or agent following 9Router's documented workflow has no way to run the one-shot generate-and-download flow, and the dashboard's Skills page gives an agent no video entry point, so the video capability is effectively undiscoverable in OpenProxy even where the routes work.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/cli/cli.js:73 (command registration); .tmp/9router/cli/src/cli/commands/xaiVideo.js:1-20 (flow doc + DEFAULT_MODEL/TIMEOUT/POLL_INTERVAL); .tmp/9router/skills/9router-video/SKILL.md:1-3 and :60-69. OpenProxy: src/cli/media.rs:19-120 (MediaCmd variant list, no Video); web/src/shared/constants/skills.ts:21-107 (SKILLS array, no video entry).
- **Parity fix**: Add a `Media Video { ... }` subcommand to `MediaCmd` in `src/cli/media.rs` that POSTs `/v1/videos/generations`, polls `/v1/videos/{request_id}` (reading the connection id from the create response — see VID-005 so the correct header is used), downloads `video.url` to a `.part` file and renames atomically, plus an `openproxy-video` entry in `web/src/shared/constants/skills.ts` pointing at the corresponding SKILL.md.

## P237-001 · P2 · CLI Tools — POST/GET/DELETE /api/cli-tools/devin-settings

- **9router**: `GET /api/cli-tools/devin-settings` reports whether the Devin CLI is installed, probing the same candidate binary paths the executor uses (`~/.local/share/devin/bin/devin`, `~/.devin/bin/devin`, `~/.local/bin/devin`, `/opt/homebrew/bin/devin`, `/usr/local/bin/devin`, `/usr/bin/devin`, plus the Windows equivalents) so the dashboard's status matches what the runtime will actually spawn — `.tmp/9router/src/app/api/cli-tools/devin-settings/route.js:12-38, 67`.
- **OpenProxy**: The route does not exist anywhere in `src/server/api/`. `src/server/api/cli_tools.rs:2990-3000` merges the 11 per-tool settings submodules (claude, cline, cowork, deepseek-tui, grok-build, hermes, jcode, kilo, openclaw, plus the inline codex/copilot/opencode/droid) and declares no devin entry — `grep -rl "devin-settings" src/server/api/` returns nothing.
- **Gap**: `/api/cli-tools/devin-settings` is missing, so the Devin CLI has no install-status surface even though the `devin-cli` executor exists in OpenProxy.
- **Why it matters**: A user who installed the Devin CLI cannot confirm from the dashboard that OpenProxy can find it. The status card falls back to its default ("Not configured"), which is indistinguishable from "not installed", so a PATH/prefix problem between the executor's binary resolution and the user's shell is undiagnosable from the UI. The mirror-image concern is real too: because the probe mirrors the executor's search paths, the two drifting apart is exactly the class of bug this route exists to prevent.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/cli-tools/devin-settings/route.js:12-38 (candidate paths, "so detection matches runtime resolution"), :67 (GET export). OpenProxy: src/server/api/cli_tools.rs:2990-3000 (routes() merge list, no devin); no match for "devin-settings" under src/server/api/.
- **Parity fix**: Add `src/server/api/cli_tools/devin_settings.rs` with `GET /api/cli-tools/devin-settings` returning `{installed, path, version?}` by probing the same candidate list as the 9router route, and `.merge(devin_settings::routes())` into `cli_tools::routes()` (`src/server/api/cli_tools.rs:2990`). The candidate list must be derived from the same constant the `devin-cli` executor uses to resolve the binary so the two cannot drift.

## P238-001 · P2 · Client-visible `usage` object in responses

- **9router**: Every client-visible `usage` block gets +2000 buffer tokens (`addBufferToUsage`: `result.prompt_tokens += BUFFER_TOKENS` etc.) and is filtered to the target format's allowed field list (`filterUsageForFormat`, open-sse/utils/usageTracking.js:59-106: Claude gets `input_tokens/output_tokens/cache_read_input_tokens/cache_creation_input_tokens/estimated`; OpenAI gets `prompt_tokens/completion_tokens/total_tokens/cached_tokens/reasoning_tokens/*_details/estimated`; Gemini gets `promptTokenCount/candidatesTokenCount/...`).
- **OpenProxy**: No equivalent exists — grep for `add_buffer_to_usage` / `BUFFER_TOKENS` / `filter_usage_for_format` across `src/` returns nothing. The upstream usage object is forwarded verbatim by the response translators (e.g. src/core/translator/response_transform.rs:754 emits `usage` straight from the accumulated state) and by the pass-through paths.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy forwards the raw provider usage object; 9Router adds a 2000-token buffer and filters fields to the target format.
- **Why it matters**: Clients that display token usage see a different number than under 9Router — exactly 2000 fewer on prompt/input. Codex/Claude Code show a context-remaining estimate derived from these fields, so the reported headroom drifts by 2000 tokens per turn, and the drift compounds visually across a long session. The per-format field filter also means 9Router strips provider-specific keys (e.g. `cache_read_input_tokens` on an OpenAI-format response) that OpenProxy leaks through, which can confuse strict clients.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/utils/usageTracking.js:20 (BUFFER_TOKENS=2000), open-sse/utils/usageTracking.js:33-56 (addBufferToUsage), open-sse/utils/usageTracking.js:59-106 (filterUsageForFormat per-format field lists), open-sse/handlers/chatCore/nonStreamingHandler.js:363, open-sse/utils/stream.js:212, open-sse/utils/stream.js:364. openproxy: src/core/translator/response_transform.rs:754 (usage forwarded verbatim); no `add_buffer_to_usage`/`filter_usage_for_format` symbol exists anywhere under src/ (verified by grep).
- **Parity fix**: Port `addBufferToUsage` (constant 2000) and `filterUsageForFormat` (the four per-format field lists) into `src/core/usage/`, and apply them at the same two seams: (1) the terminal usage line emitted by the streaming translators, and (2) the non-streaming response before it is returned. Keep the buffer/filter on the CLIENT-visible copy only — 9Router deliberately persists the unbuffered `state.usage` for cost/stats (`// Add buffer and filter usage for client (but keep original in state.usage for logging)`), so the persisted `UsageEntry` must stay unbuffered.

## P239-001 · P2 · Codex default-instructions constant text (src/core/executor/codex.rs:368)

- **9router**: `open-sse/config/codexInstructions.js:3-119` defines `CODEX_DEFAULT_INSTRUCTIONS` as a template literal that contains four U+2011 NON-BREAKING HYPHEN characters (not ASCII `-`): line 90 `follow final‑answer formatting`, line 109 `self‑contained`, line 115 `workspace‑relative`, line 116 `1‑based`. The raw template-literal source (11695 bytes) unescapes to a 11692-byte runtime string.
- **OpenProxy**: `src/core/executor/codex.rs:368` holds the LIVE constant as an inline `&'static str` literal with ZERO U+2011 characters — all four were normalized to ASCII `-` (`follow final-answer formatting`, `self-contained`, `workspace-relative`, `1-based`). A byte-exact comparison of the 11692-char unescaped JS runtime value vs the Rust literal shows 4 differing lines. A second, CORRECT copy exists at `src/core/config/codex_instructions.rs:7` via `include_str!("data/codex_default_instructions.txt")` — that data file has all 4 U+2011 intact and matches the JS byte-for-byte — but the module is dead code: `src/core/config/mod.rs:9` declares `pub mod codex_instructions;` and nothing anywhere imports it (`rg "codex_instructions::"` → no hits). Only the drifted inline literal is ever sent upstream.
- **Gap**: The live instructions constant that OpenProxy actually injects has four U+2011 non-breaking hyphens replaced with ASCII hyphens, and the byte-exact port sitting in `src/core/config/codex_instructions.rs` is dead code that nothing imports.
- **Why it matters**: These are the literal bytes of the system prompt sent to the Codex upstream, and the divergence is a silent content drift between the canonical prompt and what OpenProxy ships. A non-breaking hyphen (U+2011) is a distinct code point from ASCII `-`, so any prompt-integrity diff, golden-prompt test, or prompt-cache prefix comparison between a 9Router-backed deployment and an OpenProxy-backed one reports a false mismatch, and the two routers do not produce the same prompt bytes for the same request. The severity is bounded because the substitution is confined to 4 hyphens inside prose lines of the "Presenting your work" and "File References" sections — it does not alter any executable instruction, tool contract, or the agent-control rules. The more consequential issue is structural: because the accurate port is dead and the drifted copy is live, any future fix or upstream prompt refresh will edit the file nobody reads, leaving the divergence permanently in the request path. `src/core/executor/codex.rs:1179-1195` (`test_codex_instructions_matches_js`) only asserts `starts_with`, `contains("## Editing constraints")` and `contains("## Plan tool")` — all of which pass on the drifted text, so the existing guard test cannot catch this class of drift.
- **Evidence**: 9router: /Users/tranquangdang21/Projects/openproxy/.tmp/9router/open-sse/config/codexInstructions.js:90, :109, :115, :116 (U+2011 present) | OpenProxy live: /Users/tranquangdang21/Projects/openproxy/src/core/executor/codex.rs:368 (U+2011 count = 0; ASCII `-` substituted) | OpenProxy correct-but-dead copy: /Users/tranquangdang21/Projects/openproxy/src/core/config/codex_instructions.rs:7 + /Users/tranquangdang21/Projects/openproxy/src/core/config/data/codex_default_instructions.txt (U+2011 count = 4; byte-identical to JS) | dead-module proof: /Users/tranquangdang21/Projects/openproxy/src/core/config/mod.rs:9 declares it, no importer exists | insufficient guard test: /Users/tranquangdang21/Projects/openproxy/src/core/executor/codex.rs:1179-1195
- **Parity fix**: Make the byte-exact port authoritative instead of leaving it dead. Point the live constant at the existing correct data file — replace the inline literal at `src/core/executor/codex.rs:368` with `include_str!("../config/data/codex_default_instructions.txt")` (or re-export `crate::core::config::codex_instructions::CODEX_DEFAULT_INSTRUCTIONS`), so the U+2011 characters are restored and the two copies can never drift again. This preserves 9Router behaviour exactly: 9Router sends the U+2011 bytes upstream, and only the data file reproduces them. Additionally tighten `test_codex_instructions_matches_js` at `src/core/executor/codex.rs:1179` so it compares the constant against the data file byte-for-byte (e.g. `assert_eq!(CodexExecutor::DEFAULT_CODEX_INSTRUCTIONS, CODEX_DEFAULT_INSTRUCTIONS)` plus a length/char-count assertion on the U+2011 characters) rather than the current `starts_with`/`contains` spot-checks, so a future prompt refresh cannot silently reintroduce the drift.

## P240-001 · P2 · Codex default-instructions injection guard

- **9router**: `open-sse/executors/codex.js:428` — `if (!body.instructions || body.instructions.trim() === "") { body.instructions = CODEX_DEFAULT_INSTRUCTIONS; }`. The guard TRIMS before testing emptiness, so any whitespace-only value (`"   "`, `"\n"`, `"\t \n "`) is treated as absent and the full default Codex prompt is injected. Verified by differential test: js=DEFAULT for `""`, `"   "`, `"\n"`, `"\t \n "`, `undefined`, `null`; js=KEEP only for values with non-whitespace content.
- **OpenProxy**: `src/core/executor/codex.rs:405-409` — `body.get("instructions").and_then(Value::as_str).filter(|s| !s.is_empty()).unwrap_or(Self::DEFAULT_CODEX_INSTRUCTIONS)`. The filter tests `s.is_empty()` with NO trim, so a whitespace-only string passes the filter and is forwarded verbatim. Verified by differential test: rust=KEEP for `"   "`, `"\n"`, `"\t \n "`. Note the same untrimmed idiom also appears at `src/core/executor/codex.rs:482` for a second field.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy injects the default Codex instructions only for a literally-empty string, whereas 9Router injects them for any blank-or-whitespace-only string, so a whitespace-only `instructions` value suppresses the entire default Codex agent prompt on OpenProxy but not on 9Router.
- **Why it matters**: `instructions` is the system prompt that drives Codex's agentic behavior. When a client sends a syntactically present but blank `instructions` (a common artifact of templating, `instructions: systemPrompt?.trim() ?? ''` against an empty system prompt, or a Responses request that echoes an empty field), 9Router substitutes the full Codex coding-agent prompt and the model behaves as an agent; OpenProxy forwards only whitespace and the model runs with NO agent instructions — losing the plan-tool rules, sandboxing/approval contract, dirty-worktree guardrails and the `rg`/ASCII/edit constraints. The divergence is silent: no error, just materially degraded agent behavior. OpenProxy's own translator makes this reachable — `src/core/translator/request/openai_responses.rs:737` sets `result["instructions"] = Value::String(String::new())` when no system message is present, and `:630-634` copies a client `instructions`/system `content` value through untrimmed, so a whitespace-only client value reaches the executor guard verbatim.
- **Evidence**: 9router: /Users/tranquangdang21/Projects/openproxy/.tmp/9router/open-sse/executors/codex.js:428 (`body.instructions.trim() === ""`) | OpenProxy: /Users/tranquangdang21/Projects/openproxy/src/core/executor/codex.rs:408 (`.filter(|s| !s.is_empty())`) with the default applied at :409. Reachable producers: src/core/translator/request/openai_responses.rs:630-634 and :737.
- **Parity fix**: Change `src/core/executor/codex.rs:405-409` to test the trimmed value, matching 9Router. Replace `.filter(|s| !s.is_empty())` with an equivalent of `.filter(|s| !s.trim().is_empty())` so whitespace-only input falls through to `Self::DEFAULT_CODEX_INSTRUCTIONS`. Keep the surviving value untrimmed, since 9Router's `body.instructions = ...` only replaces on the blank branch and otherwise forwards the original string verbatim (`" real prompt "` is preserved with surrounding spaces on both sides — my differential test confirmed js=KEEP and rust=KEEP for that case). Apply the same untrimmed-vs-trimmed audit to the second occurrence at `src/core/executor/codex.rs:482`, and add a unit test asserting a whitespace-only `instructions` yields the default.

## P241-001 · P2 · Cost computation for cached / reasoning tokens

- **9router**: Cost is computed by `calculateCostFromTokens` (open-sse/providers/pricing.js:418-448) which charges the cache subsets SEPARATELY and deducts them from the input term to avoid double-charging: `const nonCachedInput = Math.max(0, inputTokens - cachedTokens - cacheCreationTokens); cost += nonCachedInput * (pricing.input / 1e6); if (cachedTokens > 0) cost += cachedTokens * ((pricing.cached || pricing.input) / 1e6); cost += outputTokens * (pricing.output / 1e6); if (reasoningTokens > 0) cost += reasoningTokens * ((pricing.reasoning || pricing.output) / 1e6); if (cacheCreationTokens > 0) cost += cacheCreationTokens * ((pricing.cache_creation || pricing.input) / 1e6);`. It is fed `canonicalizeUsage`-normalized tokens (cache folded into `prompt_tokens`, `cached_tokens` + `cache_creation_input_tokens` as subsets).
- **OpenProxy**: `track_request` passes the raw extracted token fields straight through — `prompt_tokens` (NOT canonicalized/folded) plus `cache_creation_input_tokens` plus `cache_read_input_tokens` — into `Pricing::calculate_cost`, whose inner model (src/core/usage/pricing.rs:122-139) simply SUMS all four independent terms: `input*input_price + output*output_price + cache_creation*cache_creation_price + cache_read*cache_read_price`. There is no `reasoning_tokens` term and no subtraction of the cache subsets from the input term.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's cost model adds cache_read and cache_creation on top of the full prompt_tokens instead of subtracting them from it, and omits the reasoning-token rate.
- **Why it matters**: For OpenAI-format providers `prompt_tokens` is already cache-INCLUSIVE (the comment in 9Router's canonicalizeUsage says so explicitly: "OpenAI/Gemini report cached_tokens already counted inside prompt, so we pass through"). OpenProxy therefore charges the cached portion TWICE — once at the full input rate and again at the (much cheaper) cache-read rate — inflating cost for every cache-hit-heavy Claude Code / Codex turn. Conversely, reasoning-heavy models (o-series, deepseek-reasoner) are billed at the plain output rate instead of 9Router's separate reasoning rate, under-reporting there.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/providers/pricing.js:418-448 (calculateCostFromTokens, `nonCachedInput` subtraction, reasoning term), open-sse/utils/usageTracking.js:150-212 (canonicalizeUsage folds cache into prompt_tokens, `cached_tokens`/`cache_creation_input_tokens` as subsets), open-sse/handlers/chatCore/requestDetail.js:120-131 (saveUsageStats canonicalizes before persisting). openproxy: src/core/usage/tracker.rs:43-60 (raw prompt/cache fields, no canonicalization), src/core/usage/pricing.rs:122-139 (all four terms summed, no subtraction, no reasoning term), src/server/api/chat.rs:4519-4568 (extract_token_usage_from_bytes emits both prompt_tokens and cached_tokens from the same object).
- **Parity fix**: Port `canonicalizeUsage` into `src/core/usage/` and call it on the extracted `TokenUsage` in `track_request` before persisting (so `prompt_tokens` is cache-inclusive and `cached_tokens`/`cache_creation_input_tokens` are subsets). Then change `ModelPricing::calculate_cost` to mirror `calculateCostFromTokens`: `non_cached_input = max(0, input - cached - cache_creation)`, charge `non_cached_input * input_price`, `cached * cached_price`, `output * output_price`, `reasoning * (reasoning_price or output_price)`, `cache_creation * cache_creation_price`. `TokenUsage` already has a `reasoning_tokens` field, so the data is present — only the pricing term is missing.

## P242-001 · P2 · Edit connection — region-aware providers

- **9router**: EditConnectionModal reads AI_PROVIDERS[connection.provider]?.regions, seeds the region from connection.providerSpecificData.region (or the default/first region), renders a region Select, and on submit sends updates.providerSpecificData = buildRegionSpecificData() so the stored region is updated. The same providerSpecificData is forwarded to /api/providers/validate.
- **OpenProxy**: EditConnectionModal has no region state, no region Select, and handleSubmit only attaches providerSpecificData for azure and cloudflare-ai. The stored region is left untouched and is not forwarded to the validate call.
- **Gap**: Region-aware providers have no region editor; the region can only be set at creation time.
- **Why it matters**: xiaomi-tokenplan keys are cluster-specific (sgp, cn and ams each point at a different host). A user who picked the wrong region at creation, or who subscribes to a different cluster later, has no way to correct it from the provider page; the only remedy is deleting and recreating the connection. The validate call also omits the region, so validation may probe a different host than the connection will use.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/shared/components/EditConnectionModal.js:51-54 (seed region), :68-72 (providerRegions and buildRegionSpecificData), :139-141 (forwarded to validate), :170-173 (persisted on submit), :267-273 (region Select). OpenProxy: web/src/shared/components/EditConnectionModal.tsx has no region symbol anywhere; handleSubmit at :137-205 sends providerSpecificData only under isAzure (:188-195) and isCloudflareAi (:196-198); region capability declared at web/src/shared/constants/providers.ts:143 (xiaomi-tokenplan regions and defaultRegion).
- **Parity fix**: Add region state seeded from connection.providerSpecificData?.region (falling back to AI_PROVIDERS[provider].defaultRegion then regions[0].id), render the region Select when AI_PROVIDERS[provider]?.regions exists, and include providerSpecificData with the region in both the validate payload and the submit payload.

## P243-001 · P2 · Embeddings 401/403 handling without a refresh token

- **9router**: On 401/403 the core calls `refreshWithRetry(() => executor.refreshCredentials(credentials, log), 3, log)` (open-sse/handlers/embeddingsCore.js:85-89). For API-key providers `DefaultExecutor.refreshCredentials` returns null immediately — `if (!credentials.refreshToken) return null` (open-sse/executors/default.js:220) and `if (!refresher) return null` (:236) — so the `if (newCredentials?.accessToken || newCredentials?.apiKey)` guard at embeddingsCore.js:91 is false and control reaches the `else` at :107, which only logs `refresh failed`. The retry fetch at :99 is never reached, so exactly ONE upstream request is made.
- **OpenProxy**: On 401/403 the handler enters the block (src/core/media/embeddings/handler.rs:78), but the refresh itself is guarded by `if !refresh_token.is_empty()` (:85). For every API-key embedding provider (openai, openrouter, mistral, voyage-ai, fireworks, together, nebius, github, nvidia, jina-ai, vercel-ai-gateway, selfhosted-embedding) there is no refresh token, so the whole block is skipped and control falls through to the unconditional re-send at :133-139, which POSTs the identical request with the identical still-rejected credentials.
- **Gap**: When there is nothing to refresh, OpenProxy re-fires the identical failing request instead of returning the original 401/403.
- **Why it matters**: Every 401/403 from an API-key embedding provider costs two upstream round trips and two failed-auth events instead of one. Providers that count authentication failures or apply a short lockout after N will lock the key sooner; providers that bill per rejected attempt double the attempt count. The client also waits for the second round trip before it sees the error, roughly doubling latency on the failure path.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/handlers/embeddingsCore.js:85-89, :91, :99, :107, open-sse/executors/default.js:220, :236 | OpenProxy: src/core/media/embeddings/handler.rs:78, :85, :133-139
- **Parity fix**: Make the fallback re-send conditional on a refresh having actually occurred. Move the `res = client.post(...)` at :133 inside a flag set only when `build_url`/`build_headers`/`build_body` succeed against refreshed credentials, mirroring the JS control flow where the retry at :99 lives inside the `if (newCredentials?.accessToken || newCredentials?.apiKey)` branch. When no refresh token exists, fall through directly to the status check at :142 and return the original 401/403.

## P244-001 · P2 · Embeddings input validation for empty string

- **9router**: `const input = body.input; if (!input) return createErrorResult(400, "Missing required field: input")` (open-sse/handlers/embeddingsCore.js:24-27). JavaScript falsiness means an empty string fails this check and is rejected locally with 400. An empty array `[]` is truthy and passes.
- **OpenProxy**: `request.input()` is `self.body.get("input")` (src/core/media/embeddings/base.rs:18-20), which yields `Some(Value::String(""))` for an empty string. The guard is `if !input.is_string() && !input.is_array()` (src/core/media/embeddings/handler.rs:41), and `is_string()` is true for the empty string, so validation passes and the empty string is forwarded upstream in the built body.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy forwards an empty-string `input` to the provider; 9router rejects it locally with 400 before any upstream call.
- **Why it matters**: The router's uniform, provider-independent 400 is replaced by whatever the provider decides — a provider-specific error shape, a 200 with a degenerate vector, or a paid no-op call. The provider-agnostic 400 is what lets clients rely on the OpenAI error contract, and it avoids spending a billable request to learn the request was empty. The dashboard Example card masks this with its own `!input.trim()` guard (web/src/components/media-providers/EmbeddingExampleCard.tsx:68), so only direct API clients hit it.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/handlers/embeddingsCore.js:24-27 | OpenProxy: src/core/media/embeddings/handler.rs:38-45, src/core/media/embeddings/base.rs:18-20
- **Parity fix**: Add a JSON-falsy check mirroring JS truthiness before the type check in `handle_embeddings` (src/core/media/embeddings/handler.rs:38): treat `Value::Null`, `Value::Bool(false)`, `Value::Number(0)` and `Value::String("")` as missing and return `Validation("Missing required field: input")`. Keep the existing string/array type check after it so `[]` still passes, exactly as the JS does.

## P245-001 · P2 · Embeddings upstream request timeout

- **9router**: The fetch is bounded by `signal: AbortSignal.timeout(FETCH_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_MS)` (open-sse/handlers/embeddingsCore.js:68-70), and `FETCH_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_MS` defaults to 60 s (open-sse/config/runtimeConfig.js:59). A hung embeddings upstream is aborted at 60 s, which is caught and converted to a 502 via `formatProviderError(error, provider, model, BAD_GATEWAY)` (:72-76).
- **OpenProxy**: `handle_embeddings` issues a bare `client.post(&url).headers(...).json(&body).send()` with no per-request deadline (src/core/media/embeddings/handler.rs:67-73). The pooled reqwest client sets `.connect_timeout(30s)` and `.timeout(180s)` for the whole request including body read (src/core/executor/client_pool.rs:19-20, :190-191, `DEFAULT_STREAM_TIMEOUT = 180s`).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy allows a hung embeddings upstream to occupy the request for 180 s where 9router aborts at 60 s.
- **Why it matters**: A hung embedding provider ties up the request and its connection-pool slot three times longer than 9router would, and the failure mode shifts: clients with their own timeout shorter than 180 s abort client-side and see a transport error, whereas 9router returns a clean 502 at 60 s. Under partial upstream failure this compounds — with no account rotation (see P0-001) a single slow account blocks the request for the full 180 s with no failover.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/handlers/embeddingsCore.js:64-76, open-sse/config/runtimeConfig.js:59 | OpenProxy: src/core/media/embeddings/handler.rs:67-73, src/core/executor/client_pool.rs:19-20, :190-191
- **Parity fix**: Apply a 60 s deadline to the embeddings call. Either pass a `ClientTimeout { connect: 60s, stream: 60s }` variant through `ClientPool::with_timeout` (src/core/executor/client_pool.rs:75) for the media client, or wrap the `send()` future in `tokio::time::timeout(Duration::from_secs(60), ...)` and map expiry to `EmbeddingsHandlerError::Upstream`, which already yields 502 — matching the 9router abort path at embeddingsCore.js:72-76.

## P246-001 · P2 · Embeddings upstream-error message content

- **9router**: `parseUpstreamError` parses the upstream body as JSON and extracts the human message: `json.error?.message || json.message || json.error || bodyText` (open-sse/utils/error.js:79-83), then `handleEmbeddingsCore` wraps it via `formatProviderError` as `[${code}]: ${message}` (open-sse/handlers/embeddingsCore.js:114, open-sse/utils/error.js:139-147). A provider error `{"error":{"message":"Incorrect API key provided","type":"invalid_request_error"}}` surfaces to the client as `error.message === "[400]: Incorrect API key provided"`.
- **OpenProxy**: On a non-ok status the handler stores the raw body verbatim: `let text = res.text().await.unwrap_or_default(); return Err(EmbeddingsHandlerError::Http(status, text))` (src/core/media/embeddings/handler.rs:142-145).
