The `Display` impl formats it as `HTTP {0}: {1}` (:11-12), and `json_error_response` only collapses whitespace (src/core/utils/error.rs:80) — it does not extract `error.message`. The same upstream body therefore surfaces as `error.message === "HTTP 400: {\"error\":{\"message\":\"Incorrect API key provided\",\"type\":\"invalid_request_error\"}}"`.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy returns the whole raw upstream error body instead of the extracted `error.message`, and uses an `HTTP n:` prefix instead of 9router's `[n]:` form.
- **Why it matters**: Every user-facing embeddings error shows a raw JSON dump, so the dashboard's Example card renders the whole envelope (web/src/components/media-providers/EmbeddingExampleCard.tsx:79 reads `data?.error?.message`), and CLI clients log a multi-line blob. The nested JSON also makes substring-based error handling in downstream tooling fail, since the expected phrase is now surrounded by punctuation and escaped quotes rather than being the whole message.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/handlers/embeddingsCore.js:113-116, open-sse/utils/error.js:79-83, open-sse/utils/error.js:139-147 | OpenProxy: src/core/media/embeddings/handler.rs:11-12, :142-145, src/server/api/media.rs:1107-1109, src/core/utils/error.rs:65-80
- **Parity fix**: Extract the message before wrapping: parse the upstream text as JSON and take `error.message || message || error`, falling back to the raw text; then format as `[${status}]: ${message}` so the shape matches `formatProviderError`. A shared helper in `src/core/utils/error.rs` mirroring `parseUpstreamError` would keep the stt/image/tts paths consistent too.

## P247-001 · P2 · Error classification table — Permanent tier and the 402 rule

- **9router**: ERROR_RULES has three text rules ('no credentials' → 2min, 'request not allowed' → 5s, 'improperly formed request' → 2min), five backoff text rules, and five status rules: 401, 402, 403, 404 (each 2min) and 429 (backoff). There is no 400 rule and no 'permanent' concept. A plain 400 matches nothing and falls through to `return { shouldFallback: true, cooldownMs: TRANSIENT_COOLDOWN_MS }` — 30 seconds. The 402 status rule is present at errorConfig.js:72.
- **OpenProxy**: ERROR_RULES reproduces the same text rules and the status rules 401, 403, 404, 429 — the 402 status rule is absent. classify_error then adds a tier 9router does not have: `if matches!(status, Some(400) | Some(401) | Some(402) | Some(403)) { return ErrorClassification::Permanent; }`, and check_fallback_error maps Permanent to `cooldown: LONG_COOLDOWN` (Duration::from_secs(120)) rather than TRANSIENT_COOLDOWN (30s).
- **Gap**: A plain HTTP 400 locks the account for 120s in OpenProxy versus 9router's 30s transient cooldown, because of an invented 'Permanent' classification; the 402 status rule is also missing from the table.
- **Why it matters**: Both implementations still fall back to the next account, so routing is intact, but the lockout is 4x longer. A client that trips a 400 once (an unsupported parameter, a too-long tool schema) loses that account for two full minutes instead of thirty seconds. With a two-account pool the recovery window doubles, and with a single account the user sees a spurious 'all accounts cooling down' 503 far longer than 9router would produce — which reads as a rate limit rather than the client-side 400 it actually was.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/config/errorConfig.js:59-76 (ERROR_RULES, 402 at :72, no 400), open-sse/config/errorConfig.js:39 (TRANSIENT_COOLDOWN_MS = 30s), open-sse/services/accountFallback.js:28-49 (top-to-bottom walk + the 30s default). OpenProxy: src/core/config/error_config.rs:169-182 (ERROR_RULES — 402 missing), src/core/config/error_config.rs:222-227 (Permanent), src/core/combo/mod.rs:597-601 (Permanent → LONG_COOLDOWN 120s), src/core/combo/mod.rs:28 (TRANSIENT_COOLDOWN 30s).
- **Parity fix**: Restore parity in two edits: (1) add the `{ status: 402, cooldown: LONG }` rule to ERROR_RULES in src/core/config/error_config.rs between the 401 and 403 entries; (2) delete the Permanent branch at src/core/config/error_config.rs:222-227 and its arm at src/core/combo/mod.rs:597-601 so an unmatched 400 falls through to ErrorClassification::NoMatch → TRANSIENT_COOLDOWN, matching accountFallback.js:47-49.

## P248-001 · P2 · GET /api/usage/chart — response envelope and bucket key

- **9router**: `getChartData(period = "7d")` returns a **bare array** of buckets, each shaped `{label, tokens, cost}` — `.tmp/9router/src/lib/db/repos/usageRepo.js:661-689` (today branch), `:691-707` (24h). The route passes it straight through: `NextResponse.json(data)` — `.tmp/9router/src/app/api/usage/chart/route.js:14-17`. Default period is `7d` (`:9`). The chart component reads `dataKey="label"` — `.tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/usage/components/UsageChart.js:87`.
- **OpenProxy**: Wraps the array in an envelope and renames the bucket key: `Json(json!({"data": build_usage_chart(&usage_db, period)}))` — `src/server/api/usage.rs:1284`, with `struct UsageChartBucket { date, tokens, cost }` at `:1258-1262`. Default period is `today`, not `7d` (`:1273`). The ported chart was adapted to match: `web/src/components/usage/UsageChartInner.tsx:45` reads `result.data || []` and `:96` uses `dataKey="date"`.
- **Gap**: The endpoint returns `{data:[…]}` with `{date,…}` buckets where 9Router returns a bare array with `{label,…}`, and the no-param default period is `today` rather than `7d`.
- **Why it matters**: A script or external dashboard written against the 9router contract gets a non-array from `res.json()` and an undefined x-axis (every `label` missing), so the chart renders empty rather than erroring. The period default is the lesser issue because both in-tree components pass an explicit period, but any caller that relies on the documented default silently gets a 1-day window instead of a 7-day one.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/lib/db/repos/usageRepo.js:661-689 (bare `{label,tokens,cost}` array), .tmp/9router/src/app/api/usage/chart/route.js:9 (default "7d"), :14-17 (pass-through); .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/usage/components/UsageChart.js:87 (dataKey="label"). OpenProxy: src/server/api/usage.rs:1258-1262 (UsageChartBucket.date), :1273 (default "today"), :1284 (`{"data": ...}` envelope); web/src/components/usage/UsageChartInner.tsx:45, :96.
- **Parity fix**: Rename `UsageChartBucket.date` to `label` and return the array bare: `Json(Json(build_usage_chart(&usage_db, period)))` in `get_usage_chart` (`src/server/api/usage.rs:1284`). Change the default at `:1273` from `"today"` to `"7d"`. Update `web/src/components/usage/UsageChartInner.tsx:45` to `setData(result)` and `:96` to `dataKey="label"`. If the `{data}` envelope is wanted for other reasons, serve it under an additive `?envelope=1` so the parity surface stays bare.

## P249-001 · P2 · Idempotency-Key on the 401 -> refresh -> retry create

- **9router**: `buildHeaders({ token, contentType, idempotencyKey })` sets `Idempotency-Key` whenever an idempotency key is present, and `defaultPlan()` is a factory invoked per attempt by `doFetch`, which closes over `idempotencyKey`. The 401/403 refresh path therefore re-sends the POST with the same `Idempotency-Key` as the first attempt — the upstream de-duplicates if the first request actually created a job.
- **OpenProxy**: The Idempotency-Key is read once and inserted into `upstream_headers` at media.rs:1346-1358 for the first attempt only. The refresh-retry at media.rs:1431-1437 builds `retry_headers` from `build_video_headers` alone, which never adds the idempotency key, so the retry POST is sent without it.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy drops the `Idempotency-Key` on the post-refresh retry create; 9Router re-sends it on every attempt.
- **Why it matters**: Creation is billable. If the first POST reached the upstream and created a job but the response surfaced as 401/403 (e.g. a token revoked mid-flight, or an intermediary rewrite), the un-keyed retry can create a SECOND billable video job for the same request, and the client only ever sees one id. 9Router's re-send of the same key makes the pair idempotent.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/handlers/videoCore.js:39-45 (`if (idempotencyKey) headers["Idempotency-Key"] = idempotencyKey`), :102-113 (defaultPlan factory), :117-133 (doFetch rebuilt per attempt), :164-170 (retry calls doFetch again). OpenProxy: src/server/api/media.rs:1346-1358 (key inserted into `upstream_headers` once), :1431-1437 (retry uses only `build_video_headers` output).
- **Parity fix**: Read the Idempotency-Key once into a local and re-insert it into the retry header map alongside the Authorization rebuild, i.e. capture the key before the loop and apply it to `retry_headers` (or to a small `with_idempotency_key(headers, key)` helper) immediately before the retry `.send()`.

## P250-001 · P2 · Model catalog management — POST /api/models/catalog-sync

- **9router**: `GET /api/models/catalog-sync` returns `{...getSyncState(), catalog:{syncedAt, models, providers, bytes}}`; `POST` runs `syncModelCatalog()` immediately and returns 503 `{error: lastError || "sync in progress"}` when the sync is not available — `.tmp/9router/src/app/api/models/catalog-sync/route.js:6-30`. This is the manual "resync the remote catalog" lever backed by `lib/modelCatalog/sync.js`.
- **OpenProxy**: The route does not exist. The only equivalent is a fire-and-forget background task started at boot — `src/main.rs:432` calls `core::model::catalog_overlay::spawn_model_catalog_sync(state.db.data_dir.clone())` (`src/core/model/catalog_overlay.rs:661`). There is no HTTP surface to inspect sync state or force a sync, and `grep -rn "catalog-sync\|catalog_sync" src/ web/src/` returns only that boot-time call.
- **Gap**: No endpoint exists to read catalog-sync state or trigger an on-demand catalog sync; the sync runs only on a background timer.
- **Why it matters**: When a provider ships a new model, the operator has no way to force a resync or see when the last one happened or how many models/providers the catalog currently holds. They must restart the binary and wait for the background timer. A stale catalog is the exact failure that presents as "the new model 400s upstream even though the dashboard lists it".
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/models/catalog-sync/route.js:6-30 (GET state, POST forced sync, 503 branch). OpenProxy: no route registered in src/server/api/mod.rs:421 or any submodule; only src/main.rs:432 → src/core/model/catalog_overlay.rs:661 (background spawn, no HTTP trigger).
- **Parity fix**: Add `src/server/api/catalog_sync.rs` with `GET /api/models/catalog-sync` (returning the overlay's last-sync timestamp, model count, provider count and file size) and `POST /api/models/catalog-sync` (invoking the `catalog_overlay` sync entry point directly and returning `{success:true, result}` or 503 `{error: lastError || "sync in progress"}` while one is already running), and `.merge(catalog_sync::routes())` into the admin tier in `src/server/api/mod.rs`. Expose the needed state from `catalog_overlay.rs:661` if it is not already tracked.

## P251-001 · P2 · OpenProxy `src/core/auth/mod.rs:180`

- **9router**: `src/sse/services/auth.js:361-364` `isValidApiKey` delegates to `validateApiKey` (`src/lib/db/repos/apiKeysRepo.js:75-79`), which is a plain boolean: a paused key (`isActive = 0`) returns `false`. The chat gate at `src/sse/handlers/chat.js:78` then emits `401` with message `"Invalid API key"`. There is no distinct "inactive" concept in the error surface.
- **OpenProxy**: `require_api_key` (`src/server/auth/mod.rs:349-367`) has three distinct failure modes; `AuthError::Inactive` maps to the message `"Inactive API key"` (`:180`) and `auth_error_response` (`src/server/api/mod.rs:2985-2998`) returns it verbatim as a 401 `authentication_error` / `invalid_api_key`.
- **Gap**: A paused API key yields the message "Inactive API key" in OpenProxy and "Invalid API key" in 9router; both are 401 with the same `type`/`code`.
- **Why it matters**: Clients and scripts that surface or match the error string (support runbooks, CLI retry heuristics, log greps) see a message that does not exist in 9router. A user who paused a key sees a different explanation than the reference implementation gives, and the two cannot be told apart by a substring match on "Invalid API key".
- **Evidence**: 9router: `src/sse/services/auth.js:361-364`, `src/lib/db/repos/apiKeysRepo.js:75-79`, `src/sse/handlers/chat.js:77-79`. OpenProxy: `src/server/auth/mod.rs:175-183,349-367`, `src/server/api/mod.rs:2985-2998`
- **Parity fix**: Change `AuthError::Inactive => "Inactive API key"` to `"Invalid API key"` at `src/server/auth/mod.rs:180`, keeping the distinct enum variant internally but collapsing the client-visible string to the 9router text.

## P252-001 · P2 · OpenProxy `src/core/auth/mod.rs:257` vs `src/shared/utils/apiKey.ts:25`

- **9router**: `src/shared/utils/apiKey.js:20-25` computes the CRC as HMAC-SHA256 hex truncated to 8 chars, giving key format `sk-{machineId16}-{keyId6}-{crc8}` = 35 characters. `parseApiKey` (`:52-75`) recomputes the 8-char CRC and compares it.
- **OpenProxy**: `src/core/auth/mod.rs:252-257` `generate_crc` truncates the HMAC hex to 12 chars, so keys are `sk-{machineId16}-{keyId6}-{crc12}` = 39 characters. `parse_api_key` (`:203-230`) recomputes a 12-char CRC and `timing_safe_eq`s it (which returns false immediately on a length mismatch, `:190-193`). The dashboard's `web/src/shared/utils/apiKey.ts:25-31` is a dead copy that still assumes 8.
- **Gap**: The generated-key format differs in the CRC segment width — 8 hex chars in 9router vs 12 in OpenProxy — so keys are 35 vs 39 characters and the two parsers reject each other's keys.
- **Why it matters**: Any key material moved between the two systems stops working: a 9router key presented to OpenProxy fails the CRC comparison and returns 401 `Invalid API key`, and an OpenProxy key presented to 9router fails the same way. The prefix (`sk-`), the machineId segment (sha256 → 16 hex, same salt default) and the keyId alphabet/length (`a-z0-9`, 6) do match — only the CRC width diverges, which makes the mismatch look like a random auth bug rather than a format difference.
- **Evidence**: 9router: `src/shared/utils/apiKey.js:20-25`, `:52-75`. OpenProxy: `src/core/auth/mod.rs:203-230`, `:252-257`, `web/src/shared/utils/apiKey.ts:25-31`
- **Parity fix**: Truncate to 8 hex chars in `src/core/auth/mod.rs:257` (`hex::encode(mac.finalize().into_bytes())[..8]`) to match `apiKey.js:25`, and update the two doc comments/`format:` lines that still say `sk-{machineId}-{keyId}-{crc8}` vs `{crc12}`. If existing 12-char keys must keep working, widen `timing_safe_eq`'s CRC comparison to accept both widths rather than changing generation outright.

## P253-001 · P2 · OpenProxy `src/server/api/chat_search.rs:209`

- **9router**: `src/sse/handlers/search.js:49` gates on `settings.requireApiKey` — off means the search handler accepts anonymous callers.
- **OpenProxy**: `handle_search_completions` calls `require_api_key_with_reload` at `src/server/api/chat_search.rs:209` with no settings condition at all, so the route is key-mandatory even when the key requirement is switched off.
- **Gap**: The search surface has the opposite polarity from 9router: OpenProxy requires a key unconditionally where 9router makes it conditional.
- **Why it matters**: Once the correct `require_api_key` predicate lands (P0-001/002), this route would still reject callers that the setting is supposed to admit, so a search-enabled deployment behaves differently from every other modality.
- **Evidence**: 9router: `src/sse/handlers/search.js:49-57`. OpenProxy: `src/server/api/chat_search.rs:205-212`
- **Parity fix**: Wrap the call in `if state.db.snapshot().settings.require_api_key { … }`, matching `src/sse/handlers/search.js:49`.

## P254-001 · P2 · OpenProxy `src/server/api/mod.rs:1826`

- **9router**: `src/app/api/keys/[id]/route.js:34-36` — `const updated = await updateApiKey(id, updateData); return NextResponse.json({ key: updated })`. `updateApiKey` (`src/lib/db/repos/apiKeysRepo.js:48-61`) returns `merged = { ...rowToKey(row), ...data }`, and `rowToKey` (`:5-14`) includes the real `key` column, so the response carries the plaintext key.
- **OpenProxy**: `src/server/api/mod.rs:1826` clones the updated record and overwrites `response_key.key = "***".to_string()` before serialising `{ "key": response_key }`. `GET /api/keys` (`src/server/api/mod.rs:1686-1692`) does return the real keys, so the masking is inconsistent within OpenProxy's own API.
- **Gap**: A `PUT /api/keys/{id}` response in OpenProxy replaces the key with the literal string `***`; 9router returns the real key.
- **Why it matters**: A script that pauses a key and then reads `response.key.key` to re-store it silently persists `***` as the credential. The dashboard does not read it (it only checks `res.ok`), so the bug is invisible until an external consumer is written.
- **Evidence**: 9router: `src/app/api/keys/[id]/route.js:34-36`, `src/lib/db/repos/apiKeysRepo.js:5-14,48-61`. OpenProxy: `src/server/api/mod.rs:1819-1828`
- **Parity fix**: Delete the `response_key.key = "***".to_string();` line at `src/server/api/mod.rs:1826` and return the cloned key unmodified, matching `apiKeysRepo.js:59`.

## P255-001 · P2 · OpenProxy `src/server/api/mod.rs:331-332`

- **9router**: `src/app/api/keys/[id]/route.js:5-15` exports a `GET` that returns `{ key }` or `404 {"error":"Key not found"}`.
- **OpenProxy**: `src/server/api/mod.rs:331-332` registers only `delete(delete_key_api)` and `put(update_key_api)` on `/api/keys/{id}`; there is no `get` handler anywhere, so a `GET` returns 405 Method Not Allowed.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy has no `GET /api/keys/{id}` route; 9router does.
- **Why it matters**: Any external script that fetches a single key by id (a common pattern when rotating or auditing credentials) gets a 405 from OpenProxy and a 200 from 9router.
- **Evidence**: 9router: `src/app/api/keys/[id]/route.js:5-15`. OpenProxy: `src/server/api/mod.rs:331-332`
- **Parity fix**: Add `.route("/api/keys/{id}", get(get_key_api))` at `src/server/api/mod.rs:331` with a handler that reuses the `require_dashboard_or_management_api_key` + `api_keys.iter().find(|k| k.id == id)` pattern already in `update_key_api`/`delete_key_api` and returns `{"key": <row>}` or `404 {"error":"Key not found"}`.

## P256-001 · P2 · OpenProxy `web/src/components/EndpointPageClient.tsx:116-120`

- **9router**: `src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/endpoint/endpointConstants.js:19-26` is the canonical list with six levels: `lite`, `full`, `ultra`, `wenyan-lite`, `wenyan`, `wenyan-ultra` (the last three carry `wenyan: true`). The 9router endpoint page has no Caveman UI at all — the level picker lives only on `/dashboard/token-saver` (`TokenSaverClient.js:72-73`), where all six are used.
- **OpenProxy**: OpenProxy added a "Token Saver (RTK + Caveman)" card to the endpoint page (`web/src/components/EndpointPageClient.tsx:1202-1270`) but declared a second, shorter `CAVEMAN_LEVELS` with only three entries (`:116-120`) — while its own token-saver page declares all six (`web/src/components/TokenSaverPageClient.tsx:42-49`). The picker renders whatever is in the local list (`:1253`).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's endpoint-page Caveman picker is a truncated three-level copy of the six-level canonical list, so the three 文言文 levels are unreachable from that page.
- **Why it matters**: A user who selects `wenyan` (or any wenyan level) on the token-saver page and then opens the endpoint page sees no level selected at all, and cannot re-select or even see the level currently in force. The two pages disagree about what settings exist.
- **Evidence**: 9router: `src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/endpoint/endpointConstants.js:19-26`, `src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/token-saver/TokenSaverClient.js:72-81`. OpenProxy: `web/src/components/EndpointPageClient.tsx:116-120,1253-1270`, `web/src/components/TokenSaverPageClient.tsx:42-49,110-112`
- **Parity fix**: Export the six-level `CAVEMAN_LEVELS` (with the `wenyan` flag and `WENYAN_LOCALES`) from a single shared module — e.g. `web/src/shared/constants/caveman.ts` — and import it in both `EndpointPageClient.tsx` and `TokenSaverPageClient.tsx`, plus the locale-driven `visibleCavemanLevels` filter and the `current?.wenyan && !isWenyanLocale → patchSetting({cavemanLevel:"ultra"})` effect that `TokenSaverPageClient.tsx:110-121` already implements.

## P257-001 · P2 · POST /api/models/test and /api/providers/{id}/test-models — chat probe budget

- **9router**: The chat probe body is `{ model, max_tokens: 1024, stream: false, messages: [{ role: "user", content: "hi" }] }`, with the comment "1024 tokens: reasoning models (ClinePass/kimi-k3, deepseek-v4-pro, etc.) spend their budget on chain-of-thought before emitting an answer. A tiny probe like max_tokens:16 starves the answer and yields a false 'no choices' failure. See issue #3010." The success test then soft-passes the resulting shape: `hasChoices && finish_reason === "length" && contentEmpty && hasReasoning → { ok: true, note: "reasoning-only response (length-limited)" }`.
- **OpenProxy**: `test_model` sends `{ model, max_tokens: 1, stream: false, messages: [{ role: "user", content: "hi" }] }` (providers.rs:384-390) and `test_provider_models::ping_model` sends the same `max_tokens: 1` (provider_model_tests.rs:160-165). The success predicate is `has_choices || has_anthropic_content || has_gemini_candidates` (providers.rs:556-581) with no reasoning-only branch.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy starves reasoning models with a 1-token budget and has no reasoning-only soft pass.
- **Why it matters**: Every reasoning model in the catalog returns `finish_reason: "length"` with empty `content` and non-empty reasoning/reasoning_content on a 1-token budget. OpenProxy reports "Provider returned no completion choices for this model" and paints a red dot on a healthy model; the user then disables a working model. This is the exact false negative issue #3010 fixed upstream.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/models/test/ping.js:129-141 (body with `max_tokens: 1024` and the issue-#3010 rationale), :196-200 (the reasoning-only soft pass). OpenProxy: src/server/api/providers.rs:383-390, :556-581; src/server/api/provider_model_tests.rs:160-165
- **Parity fix**: Set `max_tokens: 1024` in both `test_model` (providers.rs:386) and `ping_model` (provider_model_tests.rs:162), and add the soft pass to `test_model` before the `has_choices` check: if `choices[0].finish_reason == "length"` and `content` is blank but `reasoning`/`reasoning_content`/`thinking`/`thinking_content` is non-empty, return `ok: true` with a `note` field. Add the `note` field to `TestModelResponse` and to `ProviderModelTestResult`.

## P258-001 · P2 · POST /api/providers/{id}/test — ollama proxy handling

- **9router**: `case "ollama"` calls bare `fetch("https://ollama.com/api/tags", { headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${connection.apiKey}` } })` — not `fetchWithConnectionProxy` — so the connection proxy is bypassed and no `AbortSignal.timeout(15000)` is attached. (`case "ollama-local"` at :695-698 likewise uses bare `fetch` against the resolved local host.)
- **OpenProxy**: `"ollama" => simple_get_bearer_test(..., "https://ollama.com/api/tags", "Invalid API key")`, which routes through `execute_request` and therefore applies the resolved connection-proxy `ProxyTarget` from the shared client pool and the 15 s per-request timeout.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy applies the connection proxy and a request timeout to the ollama probe where 9router does not.
- **Why it matters**: With a connection proxy configured, an operator on a network that can only reach ollama.com through that proxy sees the ollama connection go green in OpenProxy and red in 9router. This is the one case in this audit where OpenProxy is strictly more capable, so the parity fix is a regression unless the divergence is recorded.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/providers/[id]/test/testUtils.js:691-694 (bare `fetch`), contrasted with the proxied arms such as :643-646. OpenProxy: src/server/api/provider_connection_test.rs:676-685 (`simple_get_bearer_test`), :1651-1686 (`execute_request` applies `proxy_target` and `DEFAULT_TIMEOUT`)
- **Parity fix**: Do not regress the proxy. Instead, record the divergence in `docs/parity-9router.md` next to the other intentional-divergence entries: "the `ollama` connection test honours the connection proxy; 9router's does not (9router testUtils.js:692 uses bare `fetch`)". If strict byte-parity is required instead, use `execute_simple_request` with an `EffectiveProxy::default()` for this one arm.

## P259-001 · P2 · POST /api/providers/{id}/test — perplexity probe URL

- **9router**: `const res = await fetchWithConnectionProxy("https://api.perplexity.ai/v1/models", { headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${apiKey}` } }, effectiveProxy); return { valid: res.ok, error: res.ok ? null : "Invalid API key" };`
- **OpenProxy**: `simple_get_bearer_test(state, connection, effective_proxy, "https://api.perplexity.ai/models", "Invalid API key")` — the `/v1` segment is absent.
- **Gap**: The connection test probes a different path than 9router for the perplexity provider.
- **Why it matters**: Different endpoint, therefore a different upstream status and a different `valid` verdict when Perplexity serves one path and not the other. Note OpenProxy is internally consistent (provider_validate.rs:161 uses the same `/models`), so the mismatch is specifically against 9router's contract and will surface as a difference the moment Perplexity changes routing.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/providers/[id]/test/testUtils.js:659-662. OpenProxy: src/server/api/provider_connection_test.rs:596-605; same URL on the validate side at src/server/api/provider_validate.rs:161
- **Parity fix**: Change the literal to `https://api.perplexity.ai/v1/models` in provider_connection_test.rs:601 and, to keep the two OpenProxy surfaces consistent, at src/server/api/provider_validate.rs:161.

## P260-001 · P2 · POST /api/providers/{id}/test-models — per-kind dispatch and result shape

- **9router**: The route resolves each model's kind (`first.kind || first.type || "llm"`) and calls `pingModelByKind`, which routes to `/api/v1/embeddings` for `embedding`, `/api/v1/images/generations` for `image`, `/api/v1/audio/transcriptions` for `stt` (with a synthesized 250 ms silent WAV) and `/api/v1/chat/completions` otherwise. Every result row is `{ modelId, name, ...firstResult }`, so it carries `status` and (on the reasoning soft pass) `note`.
- **OpenProxy**: `ProviderModelTestResult` is `{ model_id, name, ok, latency_ms, error }` — no `status`, no `note` — and `ping_model` unconditionally builds the chat body and calls `chat::chat_completions`, ignoring each catalog model's `kind`.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy always pings the chat endpoint and drops the `status`/`note` fields the result consumers expect.
- **Why it matters**: On a media provider connection (embedding, image, stt), "Test Models" sends an image or embedding model to `/v1/chat/completions` and reports a guaranteed failure. The UI also loses the upstream HTTP status, so an auth error and a model-not-found error render identically.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/providers/[id]/test-models/route.js:44-56 (kind dispatch + `...firstResult` spread), .tmp/9router/src/app/api/models/test/ping.js:60-136 (the four kind branches). OpenProxy: src/server/api/provider_model_tests.rs:30-37 (result struct), :92-107 (kind ignored), :145-163 (chat-only body)
- **Parity fix**: Add `status: Option<u16>` and `note: Option<String>` to `ProviderModelTestResult`, and give `ping_model` a `kind` parameter taken from the catalog model's `kind` field, dispatching to `chat::chat_completions` / the embeddings / image / stt handlers the same way 9router does. Keep the existing first-model-warm-up-then-`join_all(rest)` shape, which already matches.

## P261-001 · P2 · POST /v1/videos/* — rejection of unsupported provider and combo models

- **9router**: `resolveVideoProvider` returns `errorResponse(HTTP_STATUS.BAD_REQUEST, "Combos are not supported for video generation")` for a combo, and `errorResponse(HTTP_STATUS.BAD_REQUEST, "Provider '<p>' does not support video generation")` for a prefixed model whose provider has no videoConfig. `errorResponse` uses the status it is given verbatim.
- **OpenProxy**: Both messages are passed to `json_error_response`, which first calls `infer_status_from_message(status, message)` and then `friendly_error_message`. For the combo message, `infer_status_from_message` matches `lower.contains("not supported")` and returns 406, so the client gets HTTP 406 instead of 400. For the provider message the status stays 400 but `friendly_error_message` matches `does not support` and replaces the text with the generic `"This model is not supported by the provider."`, losing the provider name.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy rewrites the video handlers' own 400s: the combo rejection becomes 406, and the provider rejection's message is replaced with a generic model-support string.
- **Why it matters**: 406 Not Acceptable tells the client the request media type is unacceptable, so SDKs and CLI tools surface a nonsensical error and will not treat it as a routing/configuration mistake; the correct signal (400, fix the model) is lost. Losing `Provider 'x'` also removes the only indication of which provider prefix was rejected, so a multi-provider user cannot tell a typo from a genuine capability gap.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/sse/handlers/videoGeneration.js:84 (`errorResponse(HTTP_STATUS.BAD_REQUEST, "Combos are not supported for video generation")`), :92 (`errorResponse(HTTP_STATUS.BAD_REQUEST, "Provider '${modelInfo.provider}' does not support video generation")`); .tmp/9router/open-sse/utils/error.js:30-39 (errorResponse uses the status verbatim). OpenProxy: src/server/api/media.rs:1655-1658 (combo) and :1665-1670 (provider); :1114-1121 (json_error_response runs infer_status_from_message then friendly_error_message); src/core/utils/error.rs:241-242 (returns 406 on "not supported"), :136-141 (rewrites "does not support").
- **Parity fix**: Give the video module its own error constructor that does not run the chat-oriented `infer_status_from_message`/`friendly_error_message` rewrite — e.g. `video_error_response(status, message)` calling `build_error_body` directly plus `with_cors_response` — and use it at media.rs:1655-1658, :1665-1670 and the other video validation sites (:1512-1513, :1838-1868, :2287) so the exact 9router status and message text survive.

## P262-001 · P2 · POST /v1/videos/* — request body forwarding fidelity

- **9router**: `handleVideoCreate` starts from the exact request bytes and only substitutes when the provider prefix was actually stripped: `let forwardBody = bodyInfo.raw; if (bodyInfo.parsed && model && bodyInfo.parsed.model !== model) { forwardBody = JSON.stringify({...bodyInfo.parsed, model}); }`. The OpenRouter adapter documents and implements this as a contract — "Response bodies are passed through verbatim" and "Forwards the raw body byte-for-byte (JSON or multipart) — no reshaping" — and the unit test asserts `expect(init.body).toBe(raw)`.
- **OpenProxy**: `let body_bytes = serde_json::to_vec(&forward_body)` unconditionally re-serializes the parsed `Value` for every JSON create, whether or not the model prefix changed, so key order, whitespace, number formatting and duplicate keys are all normalised.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy always re-serializes JSON video bodies instead of forwarding the received bytes, breaking 9Router's documented byte-for-byte/verbatim forwarding contract.
- **Why it matters**: The upstream shape is a documented opaque passthrough, not an OpenAI schema OpenProxy is entitled to normalise. Re-serialising can drop duplicate keys, reorder fields an upstream signature or validation depends on, and convert integer-valued floats (e.g. `seed: 3` re-emitted as `3.0`), so a request that 9Router forwards unchanged can be altered or rejected by the provider. It also means the OpenRouter adapter's verbatim guarantee is not actually met.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/sse/handlers/videoGeneration.js:122-125 (raw body preserved unless the prefix was stripped); .tmp/9router/open-sse/handlers/videoProviders/openrouter.js:32-37 (`body: rawBody`); .tmp/9router/open-sse/handlers/videoCore.js:57-58 ("Forwards the raw body byte-for-byte"); .tmp/9router/tests/unit/video-providers.test.js:66 (`expect(init.body).toBe(raw); // verbatim`). OpenProxy: src/server/api/media.rs:1266 (`serde_json::to_vec(&forward_body)`) with the parsed body produced at :1189 and :1260-1264.
- **Parity fix**: Keep the received bytes alongside the parsed value (as 9router's `readForwardableBody` does with `{raw, parsed}`) and forward `raw` unchanged unless the provider prefix was actually rewritten, in which case re-serialize only that one path. Thread `Bytes` into `video_create_handler` instead of taking `Json<Value>` directly.

## P263-001 · P2 · PXPIPE lifecycle — POST /api/pxpipe/{start,stop,restart,install}

- **9router**: Four lifecycle routes exist: `POST /api/pxpipe/start` returns `getPxpipeStatus()` and, when the package is missing and `pxpipeAutoInstall` is off, 409 `{error:"PXPIPE is not installed", code:"NOT_INSTALLED"}` — `.tmp/9router/src/app/api/pxpipe/start/route.js:12-24`; `POST /api/pxpipe/install` reinstalls `@latest` and returns `{...info, health}` — `.tmp/9router/src/app/api/pxpipe/install/route.js:10-19`; plus `stop` and `restart`. The pxpipe dashboard page drives start/stop/restart/install from these — `.tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/pxpipe/PxpipeClient.js`.
- **OpenProxy**: Only the four read-only routes are registered: `GET /api/pxpipe/status`, `GET|POST /api/pxpipe/health`, `GET /api/pxpipe/stats`, `GET /api/pxpipe/logs` — `src/server/api/pxpipe.rs:23-28`. The module doc explicitly says OpenProxy "does not manage its lifecycle" (`:5-7`) and hardcodes `installed:false` / `healthy:false` (`:44-56, :65-79`). The 409 `NOT_INSTALLED` status code — the whole point of which is to tell the UI *why* start was refused — has no counterpart.
- **Gap**: The four lifecycle routes are absent, so the pxpipe page can only display a permanently-disabled skeleton with no start/stop/restart/install and no `NOT_INSTALLED` signalling.
- **Why it matters**: The `/dashboard/pxpipe` page (routable in OpenProxy at `web/src/components/PxpipePageClient.tsx`, 342 lines) shows a status, health, stats and logs view, but the controls that would make PXPIPE actually do anything are 404. A user who reads the page cannot tell "PXPIPE is not installed, install it" from "OpenProxy cannot manage PXPIPE" — both render as permanently false, because the distinguishing 409 status code has nowhere to come from.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/pxpipe/start/route.js:12-24 (409 NOT_INSTALLED), .tmp/9router/src/app/api/pxpipe/install/route.js:10-19; also src/app/api/pxpipe/{stop,restart}/route.js. OpenProxy: src/server/api/pxpipe.rs:23-28 (four read-only routes), :5-7 (explicit non-management note), :44-56 (hardcoded installed:false), :65-79 (hardcoded healthy:false).
- **Parity fix**: Either (a) add the four lifecycle routes to `src/server/api/pxpipe.rs` with the 9router status codes — notably returning 409 `{error:"PXPIPE is not installed", code:"NOT_INSTALLED"}` from `POST /api/pxpipe/start` when the module is absent and `pxpipeAutoInstall` is off — or (b) make the stub's `status` / `health` responses carry the 9router `code` field so the UI can render the "not installed" state explicitly rather than a flat false. Option (a) preserves 9router behaviour; option (b) is the minimum that removes the ambiguity.

## P264-001 · P2 · Provider status machine — manual clear-cooldown

- **9router**: `POST /api/models/availability {action:"clearCooldown"}` writes `{[lockKey]: null, testStatus:"active", lastError:null, lastErrorAt:null, backoffLevel:0}` through `updateProviderConnection`, which then runs `resetHealthStateOnActivation` and therefore ALSO nulls `errorCode`, `rateLimitedUntil`, and every OTHER `modelLock_*` key on that connection.
- **OpenProxy**: `clear_cooldown` nulls only the single requested `lock_key`, and when `test_status == "unavailable"` also clears `last_error` / `last_error_at` / `backoff_level`. It never touches `error_code`, `rate_limited_until`, or the other models' `modelLock_*` keys.
- **Gap**: Clearing one model's cooldown resets far less state in OpenProxy than in 9router.
- **Why it matters**: The "clear cooldown" button on a provider's model badge removes one lock in OpenProxy but the whole lock set in 9router. A user clearing model A still sees model B cooling down in OpenProxy, with a stale `error_code` on the record, after the same click.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/models/availability/route.js:76-95 combined with .tmp/9router/src/lib/db/repos/connectionsRepo.js:15-33. OpenProxy: src/server/api/models_availability.rs:161-193.
- **Parity fix**: Route `clear_cooldown` through a shared `apply_activation_reset` helper (the P0-003 fix) so clearing a cooldown performs the same full health reset: null all `modelLock_*`, set `error_code = None` and `rate_limited_until = None`.

## P265-001 · P2 · Provider-detail breadcrumb icon resolution

- **9router**: `src/shared/components/Header.js:34` sets `image: getProviderIconSrc(providerId)` and `:66` sets `image: getProviderIconSrc(providerInfo.id)`. `getProviderIconSrc` (`src/shared/utils/providerIcon.js:30-33`) lowercases the id, applies `ICON_ALIASES` (`:4-9`: perplexity-agent->perplexity, gitlab-duo->gitlab, vercel-ai-gateway->vercel, ollama-search->ollama) and returns `null` for ids already 404'd this session.
- **OpenProxy**: `web/src/shared/components/Header.tsx:47` sets `image: \`/providers/${providerId}.png\`` and `:79` sets `image: \`/providers/${providerInfo.id}.png\`` — the alias table and the session 404-cache are bypassed even though `web/src/shared/utils/providerIcon.ts:22-35` is a byte-for-byte port of the same helper.
- **Gap**: The header breadcrumbs build the icon path by string interpolation instead of calling `getProviderIconSrc`, so aliased provider ids and the session failure cache are ignored.
- **Why it matters**: For the four aliased ids the breadcrumb requests a non-existent file and falls back to a two-letter text badge instead of the real provider logo, on the Providers detail page — one of the four core product surfaces. The session 404-cache is also lost, so a missing icon is re-requested on every header mount.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/shared/components/Header.js:34, :66; .tmp/9router/src/shared/utils/providerIcon.js:4-9, :20-33 | OpenProxy: web/src/shared/components/Header.tsx:47, :79; web/src/shared/utils/providerIcon.ts:6-11, :22-35
- **Parity fix**: Import `getProviderIconSrc` in `web/src/shared/components/Header.tsx` and replace both template literals: `image: getProviderIconSrc(providerId)` at `:47` and `image: getProviderIconSrc(providerInfo.id)` at `:79`.

## P266-001 · P2 · Providers list / provider page — connection ordering

- **9router**: getProviderConnections sorts by (priority || 999) ascending before returning, and GET /api/providers returns that order. The provider-detail page filters by provider but does not re-sort, so the list always renders in priority order and the #N labels match the displayed order.
- **OpenProxy**: The non-paginated branch of GET /api/providers maps the snapshot slice directly with no sort; the sort is only applied in the paginated branch via sort_provider_connections. The provider-detail page filters by provider but never sorts, so it receives insertion order.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's connection list endpoint returns DB insertion order, not priority order.
- **Why it matters**: After a priority swap or a delete the optimistic client-side array looks right until the page is reloaded; on reload the rows snap back to insertion order while still displaying the mutated #N numbers, and the up/down arrows then reorder a list whose displayed order no longer reflects routing order. Account selection at request time still uses priority, so the visible list and the actual tried-first account disagree.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/lib/db/repos/connectionsRepo.js:101 (sort by priority), consumed by .tmp/9router/src/app/api/providers/route.js:52-79 and rendered without re-sort at .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/providers/[id]/page.js:315. OpenProxy: src/server/api/mod.rs:983-990 (unsorted map) versus :1045 (sorted only in the paginated branch), rendered without re-sort at web/src/components/providers/ProviderDetailPageClient.tsx:278.
- **Parity fix**: Sort the non-paginated list by (priority.unwrap_or(999), provider) before serialising, matching connectionsRepo.js:101, so both branches of GET /api/providers return priority order.

## P267-001 · P2 · Providers page — batch test spinner scope (dead `all`/`global` chain)

- **9router**: Only three single-mode batch tests exist (`oauth`, `free`, `apikey`); `handleBatchTest` sets `testingMode` to exactly that mode and each section's Test All button spins only while its own mode runs. There is no client-side cross-mode chain.
- **OpenProxy**: `handleBatchTest` added an `if (mode === "all" || mode === "global")` branch that client-side chains `["oauth", "free", "apikey"]` sequentially, aggregating summaries, and each of the three buttons renders `testingMode === "<mode>" || testingMode === "all"` for both its spinner and its "Testing..." label. But no call site ever passes `"all"` or `"global"` — the only invocations are `handleBatchTest("oauth")`, `handleBatchTest("free")`, `handleBatchTest("apikey")`.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy carries a client-side three-mode sequential chain with an "all" spinner state that no button can ever trigger, so the three Test All buttons all display a spinner for the whole batch run instead of only their own section.
- **Why it matters**: Dead branch aside, the widened `|| testingMode === "all"` conditions are live code: had the chain been reachable, all three section headers would flip to "Testing..." simultaneously and each button's `disabled={!!testingMode}` would already be true, so the user could not tell which section is actually being tested. As shipped the "always spins even when count is 0" comment describes behaviour the UI never reaches — 9router's `if (data.summary)` guard simply produces no toast for an empty group, whereas OpenProxy's chain would have emitted `notify.warning("No providers connected")`. Because the branch is unreachable, the empty-group case in the reachable path is a silent no-toast, which happens to match 9router, but the divergence is unverified dead weight on the loading path.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/providers/page.js:241-264 (single-mode handler), :477/522/583 (only three call sites), :248-252 (`if (data.summary)` guard). OpenProxy: web/src/components/providers/ProvidersPageClient.tsx:445-483 (the `all`/`global` chain branch), :485-505 (reachable single-mode path), :858-873, :911-926, :975-990 (the three buttons and their `|| testingMode === "all"` conditions), :858/911/975 (the only call sites — all single-mode).
- **Parity fix**: Delete the `mode === "all" || mode === "global"` branch from `handleBatchTest` (web/src/components/providers/ProvidersPageClient.tsx:445-483) and revert the three button conditions to the 9router form — `testingMode === "oauth"`, `testingMode === "free"`, `testingMode === "apikey"` for the icon animation, the label, and the visible state. If a global cross-section test is genuinely wanted, it should be a real button posting `mode: "all"` once P0-001 is fixed so the backend does the selection, rather than a client-side chain.

## P268-001 · P2 · SSE stall watchdog timeout

- **9router**: `pipeWithDisconnect` defaults to `STREAM_STALL_TIMEOUT_MS = 360 * 1000` (360 s) and allows a per-provider override: `const stallTimeoutMs = PROVIDERS[provider]?.stallTimeoutMs || STREAM_STALL_TIMEOUT_MS` (`open-sse/handlers/chatCore/streamingHandler.js:87`). Qoder sets `stallTimeoutMs: 120000` in its registry (`open-sse/providers/registry/qoder.js:23`). The watchdog tracks RAW UPSTREAM byte activity via an `upstreamTap` TransformStream, deliberately not transform output, so a reasoning model emitting zero SSE output while raw bytes keep flowing is not falsely killed (`open-sse/utils/streamHandler.js:158-190`).
- **OpenProxy**: `const SSE_STALL_TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(180);` (`src/server/api/chat.rs:110`) — 180 s, half of 9router's value — and it is a single module constant applied to every provider, applied to the transform-output loop at `:3852` and `:4012`. There is no per-provider override. Separately, `STREAM_STALL_TIMEOUT_MS: u64 = 6 * 60 * 1000` is declared at `src/core/config/runtime_config.rs:44` with a comment saying it matches 9router's default — but it has zero callers; the 180 s local constant shadows it.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's SSE stall timeout is a hardcoded 180 s applied to all providers, versus 9router's 360 s default with a 120 s per-provider Qoder override — and OpenProxy's own correct 360 s constant is dead code.
- **Why it matters**: 180 s halves the thinking budget for every provider. A reasoning model on a slow upstream that legitimately goes quiet for 3 minutes between tool-call bursts — well within 9router's tolerance — is killed in OpenProxy, the stream is terminated with a synthetic `Upstream SSE stream stalled` error frame, and the accumulated output is lost. Because the client sees a mid-stream error rather than a clean finish, Claude Code and Codex discard the partial turn and retry, multiplying load exactly when the upstream is already slow. The Qoder override matters separately: Qoder's 120 s setting is tuned to its long-poll agent endpoint, and inheriting 180 s makes OpenProxy slower to detect a genuinely dead Qoder stream than 9router is.
- **Evidence**: 9router: `.tmp/9router/open-sse/config/runtimeConfig.js:53` (360 s default), `.tmp/9router/open-sse/handlers/chatCore/streamingHandler.js:87` (per-provider override), `.tmp/9router/open-sse/providers/registry/qoder.js:23` (Qoder 120 s), `.tmp/9router/open-sse/utils/streamHandler.js:158-190` (raw-byte tap). OpenProxy: `src/server/api/chat.rs:110` (180 s constant), `:3852` and `:4012` (both call sites); dead constant at `src/core/config/runtime_config.rs:44` (0 callers).
- **Parity fix**: Delete the local `SSE_STALL_TIMEOUT` at `src/server/api/chat.rs:110` and use `crate::core::config::runtime_config::STREAM_STALL_TIMEOUT_MS` at both call sites, then make the timeout per-provider: resolve it as `qoder => 120_000, _ => STREAM_STALL_TIMEOUT_MS` in the same shape as `PROVIDERS[provider]?.stallTimeoutMs`, matching `streamingHandler.js:87`. Keep the watchdog on raw upstream chunks rather than transform output, as the existing `last_data`/byte loop already does.

## P269-001 · P2 · Shared Modal — close affordance accessible name (affects all ~40 modal mounts)

- **9router**: The desktop traffic-light is a real focusable `<button onClick={onClose} aria-label="Close" title="Close">` (src/shared/components/Modal.js:72-81) and the mobile X is a separate `<button onClick={onClose} aria-label="Close">` (:91-97). Both close controls carry an explicit accessible name, and a keyboard user can Tab to either and activate with Enter/Space.
- **OpenProxy**: The traffic lights are three non-interactive `<div>` dots (web/src/shared/components/Modal.tsx:82-88) — the red one is no longer a button, so the desktop close affordance is gone. The single remaining X is a bare `<button onClick={onClose}>` with NO aria-label and no title (:93-100); its only text content is the Material Symbols ligature `<span className="material-symbols-outlined text-[20px]">close</span>`, which screen readers announce as the raw ligature name (or nothing) depending on font-loading, so the control is commonly exposed to assistive tech as an unlabelled button.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's modal close button has no accessible name, and 9router's focusable desktop traffic-light close button was downgraded to a non-interactive div.
- **Why it matters**: This is the single dismissal control for ConfirmModal (destructive delete/reset confirmations), every provider/combo/key form modal, and the ModelSelectModal. A screen-reader user hears 'button' with no name, and a sighted keyboard user on desktop now has one fewer reachable close target. Escape still works in both, so the impact is confined to non-Escape and assistive-tech paths.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/shared/components/Modal.js:72,74,75,91,92,93 || openproxy: web/src/shared/components/Modal.tsx:82,83,84,93,94,98
- **Parity fix**: Add `aria-label="Close"` to the single X button at web/src/shared/components/Modal.tsx:94, and restore the red traffic light as `<button type="button" onClick={onClose} aria-label="Close" title="Close" className="w-3 h-3 rounded-full bg-[#FF5F56] ...">` inside the dots group at :84 (keeping the yellow/green dots as non-interactive divs), mirroring 9router's :72-84.

## P270-001 · P2 · Shared ModelSelectModal — model chip element, tab stops per row, listbox semantics

- **9router**: Each model chip is a native `<button key={model.value} onClick={() => handleSelect(model)} ...>` (.tmp/9router/src/shared/components/ModelSelectModal.js:583-618). That is one tab stop per model, natively activated by Enter and Space, announced as a button, and containing no nested interactive elements. The surrounding container is a plain `<div className="flex flex-wrap gap-1.5">` (:578) with no listbox role.
- **OpenProxy**: The chip was replaced by `<div role="option" aria-selected={...} tabIndex={0} onKeyDown={(e) => { if (e.key === "Enter" || e.key === " ") { e.preventDefault(); rowClick(); } }}>` (web/src/shared/components/ModelSelectModal.tsx:738-758) inside `<div role="listbox" aria-multiselectable={...} aria-label="Available models">` (:716-719). Because the wrapper is a div rather than a button, OpenProxy nests a favourite `<button>` (:774-786) and a multi-select `<input type="checkbox">` (:788-798) inside each option, giving up to three tab stops per model. The listbox has no arrow-key handler and no roving tabindex, so all options are individually tabbable inside a container that advertises the listbox pattern.
- **Gap**: The model chip changed from one native button tab stop to a div[role=option] whose per-row tab-stop count is multiplied by the nested favourite button and multi-select checkbox, inside a role="listbox" that has no arrow-key navigation.
- **Why it matters**: ModelSelectModal is the single picker used by the combos page, the judge-model selector, the media-combo picker and 16 CLI-tool cards. In multi-select mode Tab now walks three stops per model, so a 100-model list becomes ~300 tab presses. The listbox role with no arrow-key handling is a known screen-reader pitfall (VoiceOver/NVDA expect Up/Down to move the active option, not Tab). 9router's native `<button>` avoided both problems while keeping the same reachability.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/shared/components/ModelSelectModal.js:578,583,584,585 || openproxy: web/src/shared/components/ModelSelectModal.tsx:716,717,738,751,753,754,755,774,788
- **Parity fix**: Render the chip as a native `<button type="button" key={model.value} onClick={rowClick}>` with no role/tabIndex/onKeyDown (Enter and Space then work for free), and drop role="listbox"/role="option"/aria-multiselectable from the container. If multi-select must stay, keep the checkbox outside the chip button (as 9router's button has no nested controls) or promote the chip to aria-pressed instead of role="option" so it is not inside a listbox that lacks arrow-key support.

- **Gap**: The sidebar tail is restructured into a new footer, Shutdown moves out of the header overflow menu into that footer, 9English is dropped, and 9Remote is renamed Remote.

## P273-001 · P2 · Suggested models — filter catalogue

- **9router**: FILTERS defines four filters: openrouter-free (pricing 0/0 and context length at least 200000, sorted by contextLength desc), opencode-free (ids ending in -free OR in KNOWN_FREE_OPENCODE_MODELS big-pickle, excluding DEAD_FREE_OPENCODE_MODELS deepseek-v4-flash-free), mimo-free (ids starting with mimo or names containing mimo), and airforce-free (tier free or :free suffix, supports_chat true, chat/text media). An unknown type returns 400 Unknown filter type.
- **OpenProxy**: filter_suggested_models implements only openrouter-free and opencode-free, and the latter matches ids ending in -free only, with no big-pickle inclusion and no deepseek-v4-flash-free exclusion. mimo-free and airforce-free fall through to Err(Unknown filter type) and return HTTP 400.
- **Gap**: Two of the four filters are missing and the opencode-free filter has a different membership rule.
- **Why it matters**: OpenProxy's own mimo-free provider declares modelsFetcher type mimo-free, so its suggested-model fetch always gets a 400 and yields an empty list and the provider page shows no free MiMo models. The opencode-free divergence also surfaces deepseek-v4-flash-free, a model 9router deliberately hides because upstream returns Model is unavailable, and hides big-pickle.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/providers/suggested-models/filters.js:3-6 (KNOWN_FREE_OPENCODE_MODELS and DEAD_FREE_OPENCODE_MODELS) and :9-32 (all four filters). OpenProxy: src/server/api/providers.rs:35-76 (only two arms; opencode-free at :62-73 is the plain -free suffix test) with caller web/src/shared/utils/providerModelsFetcher.ts:38-40, and provider constant web/src/shared/constants/providers.ts:21 (mimo-free with modelsFetcher.type mimo-free).
- **Parity fix**: Port the mimo-free and airforce-free filter arms and the KNOWN_FREE_OPENCODE_MODELS / DEAD_FREE_OPENCODE_MODELS constants into filter_suggested_models so the declared fetchers resolve, and match 9router's opencode-free membership rule.

## P274-001 · P2 · Target-format resolution — transport vs model-target precedence

- **9router**: `const targetFormat = useTransport?.format || modelTargetFormat || getTargetFormat(provider, credentials);` — the source-format-matched transport is checked FIRST and wins over the model-level targetFormat. The adjacent comment states the rule explicitly: 'A source-format-matched endpoint keeps the request lossless. Prefer it over a model-level targetFormat, which is only the fallback for clients whose wire format has no supported transport (for example MiniMax-M3: OpenAI clients should stay on /chat/completions; other clients can fall back to its declared Claude target).'
- **OpenProxy**: `let mut target_format = model_target.or_else(|| transport.as_ref().map(|t| t.format)).unwrap_or_else(|| registry::get_target_format_for_provider(provider));` — the model-level target is checked first and the transport only fills in when there is none. The code comment above it documents the inversion as intentional ('9router useTransport guard (modelTargetFormat is checked first in Rust, transport second)'), which contradicts the canonical file. The inversion is currently latent because OpenProxy's provider_catalog.json carries model-level targetFormat only for opencode-go muse-spark-1.2/1.3-contributor, whose supportedFormats guard neutralises the conflict — 9router ships the conflicting rows this ordering depends on (minimax `MiniMax-M3` targetFormat claude, minimax-cn the same, xiaomi-tokenplan `mimo-v2.5-pro-claude` targetFormat claude) and OpenProxy's catalog has none of them.
- **Gap**: When a model declares a targetFormat AND the client source format matches one of the provider's transports, 9router picks the transport and OpenProxy picks the model target — the two orderings are opposite.
- **Why it matters**: When it is reachable the two orderings produce different upstream wire formats for the same request, and the two halves are inconsistent: OpenProxy would pick target_format=Claude from the model row while still stamping the OpenAI transport's base_url into runtime_transport, i.e. a Claude-shaped body sent to /chat/completions — an upstream 400 on every request. It is a latent trap rather than a live break today only because the catalog rows that would trigger it are absent, which means the moment those models are added to the catalog (or supplied as user custom models) the failure appears with no other code change.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:99 (transport first, with the MiniMax-M3 rationale at :94-98), open-sse/providers/registry/minimax.js:43-56 (transports) and the model row `{ id: "MiniMax-M3", targetFormat: "claude" }`, open-sse/providers/registry/xiaomi-tokenplan.js:41-50 (transports) and `{ id: "mimo-v2.5-pro-claude", targetFormat: "claude", upstreamModelId: "mimo-v2.5-pro" }`. OpenProxy: src/core/chat/mod.rs:97-99 (model target first), src/core/chat/mod.rs:89-96 (guard, with the contradicting comment), src/core/model/provider_catalog.json providerModels (minimax/minimax-cn have no MiniMax-M3 row; only opencode-go muse-spark-* carry targetFormat).
- **Parity fix**: Swap the two arms in src/core/chat/mod.rs:97-99 so the transport wins, matching chatCore.js:99: `let transport = ...; let mut target_format = transport.as_ref().map(|t| t.format).or(model_target).unwrap_or_else(|| registry::get_target_format_for_provider(provider));`. The supportedFormats guard at src/core/chat/mod.rs:95-96 must stay exactly where it is, since in 9router the guard is applied to the transport before the precedence chain. While in there, add the missing 9router catalog rows (minimax `MiniMax-M3`, minimax-cn `MiniMax-M3`, xiaomi-tokenplan `mimo-v2.5-pro-claude` with upstreamModelId `mimo-v2.5-pro`) so the models are routable and listed in /v1/models.

## P276-001 · P2 · Token Saver page — PXPIPE section

- **9router**: TokenSaverClient.js embeds a full PXPIPE control block (60 `pxpipe`/`PXPIPE` references). It exposes a status line (`pxpipeStatusLabel` + `v${version}`), a per-check health list rendered from `pxpipeHealth.checks` with ok/pending dots and `detail` text (TokenSaverClient.js:930-947), an Install button gated on `disabled={pxpipeActionLoading || pxpipeStatus.installing}` with a busy label (…:949-959), Start/Stop/Restart/Open Logs actions (…:962-988), and a "Minimum prompt size (chars)" text Input bound to `pxpipeMinChars` with `onBlur={handlePxpipeMinCharsBlur}` and placeholder "25000" (…:990-1004).
- **OpenProxy**: TokenSaverPageClient.tsx contains **zero** references to pxpipe or PXPIPE (`grep -c` returns 0 against 60 in 9router). The PXPIPE surface was split out to a separate nav-hidden page, web/src/components/PxpipePageClient.tsx, which is read-only: it fetches `/api/pxpipe/status`, `/api/pxpipe/stats` and `/api/pxpipe/logs?limit=50` (PxpipePageClient.tsx:111-113) and posts only to `/api/pxpipe/health` (:118). It renders stats/graphs but exposes no install, start, stop, restart, or minChars input.

- **Why it matters**: `pxpipeEnabled`, `pxpipeAutoInstall`, `pxpipeMinChars` and `pxpipeTimeoutMs` are all read by the Rust backend (src/server/api/pxpipe.rs:52-55) but have no writer in the dashboard, so the feature cannot be enabled or tuned from the UI at all. A user following 9router's workflow lands on Token Saver, finds no PXPIPE section, and has to conclude the capability is absent.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/token-saver/TokenSaverClient.js:930-1004 (health list, install, lifecycle actions, minChars input). OpenProxy: web/src/components/TokenSaverPageClient.tsx (no pxpipe references; 0 vs 60 match count); web/src/components/PxpipePageClient.tsx:111-118 (read-only fetches); src/server/api/pxpipe.rs:52-55 (settings read, no dashboard writer)
- **Parity fix**: Port the PXPIPE block from TokenSaverClient.js:930-1004 into TokenSaverPageClient.tsx: the status line, the `pxpipeHealth.checks` list, Install/Start/Stop/Restart actions hitting the existing `/api/pxpipe/*` endpoints, and the minChars Input with `onBlur` commit. Add a `PATCH /api/settings` write of `pxpipeEnabled` / `pxpipeAutoInstall` / `pxpipeMinChars` / `pxpipeTimeoutMs` on the Rust side (src/server/api/pxpipe.rs currently only reads them at :52-55) so the toggles have somewhere to land.

## P277-001 · P2 · Zed `/completions` request headers (open-sse/executors/zed.js → src/core/executor/zed.rs)

- **9router**: The `/completions` POST sets, besides the bearer, three headers OpenProxy never sends: `Accept: "application/x-ndjson, text/event-stream, */*"`, `User-Agent: "9router/zed"`, and `x-zed-version: this.config?.appVersion?.toString() || "0.200.0"` (executors/zed.js:248-256).
- **OpenProxy**: `ZedExecutor::post_completions` sets only `Content-Type`, `Authorization`, and the three `x-zed-client-supports-*` capability headers (zed.rs:360-375). There is no `Accept`, no `User-Agent`, and no `x-zed-version` (grep for `ACCEPT|USER_AGENT|x-zed-version` in zed.rs returns 0). OpenProxy's response handling then parses the stream as NDJSON by splitting on `\n` (zed.rs:443-455).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy omits the `Accept`, `User-Agent` and `x-zed-version` headers that 9router sends on the Zed completions request, while still assuming an NDJSON response body.
- **Why it matters**: OpenProxy's NDJSON line parser is driven entirely by the response being newline-delimited JSON. 9router advertises `application/x-ndjson` first in `Accept` and additionally sends a concrete `x-zed-version`; with no `Accept` at all (reqwest sends none by default) and no version header, Zed's content negotiation and any version-gated behaviour are driven by different inputs than in 9router. If Zed content-negotiates to `text/event-stream` (also advertised by 9router) rather than NDJSON, OpenProxy's line splitter will not recognise `data:` framing and the stream will be mangled. This is a request-fidelity divergence that can only be fully validated against the live Zed service.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/executors/zed.js:243-256 (fetch + headers). OpenProxy: src/core/executor/zed.rs:360-375 (header set), :443-455 (NDJSON line parsing).
- **Parity fix**: In `ZedExecutor::post_completions`, add the three missing headers before the capability headers: `Accept: application/x-ndjson, text/event-stream, */*`, `User-Agent: 9router/zed`, and `x-zed-version` sourced from the running app version with the `0.200.0` fallback.

## P278-001 · P2 · Zed live model catalog resolution

- **9router**: `resolveZedModels` (zedAuth.js:357-411) performs a LIVE `GET /models` through `zedLlmFetch` (sending `x-zed-client-supports-x-ai: true`), maps each entry through `mapZedModel`, drops `isDisabled` models, and caches the result for 60 minutes with in-flight de-duplication. The executor consumes it at zed.js:212-224 to read the authoritative `provider` field per model (with a `forceRefresh` retry on a miss), and `/api/v1/models` lists the live catalog at src/app/api/v1/models/route.js:119-136. The doc comment is explicit: "Never hardcoded — always a live fetch."
- **OpenProxy**: `resolve_model` (src/core/executor/zed.rs:105-136) reads a static array from `psd.zed_models` or `psd.models`. Neither key is ever written by production code — the only writer is the unit-test helper `conn_with_models` at src/core/executor/zed.rs:498. There is no live `/models` fetch anywhere in the Zed path and no `zed` live-catalog resolver in `provider_models.rs` or `v1_models.rs`. The executor therefore always falls through to `normalize_zed_provider`'s name heuristics.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy never fetches the Zed model catalog live; it reads a `provider_specific_data` array that nothing populates, so provider inference is permanently heuristic and `/v1/models` cannot surface account-scoped Zed models.
- **Why it matters**: Two consequences: (1) the `provider` field that decides whether Zed routes to Anthropic / OpenAI-Responses / Google / xAI is never authoritative, so any model whose name does not contain claude/gemini/grok/xai is routed to the OpenAI Responses backend and silently gets the wrong wire format; (2) clients that discover models via `/v1/models` never see the account's actual Zed hosted catalog, so they cannot enumerate or select those models.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/shared/zedAuth.js:357-411, 367-375 · open-sse/executors/zed.js:212-224 · src/app/api/v1/models/route.js:119-136 | openproxy: src/core/executor/zed.rs:105-136, 498
- **Parity fix**: Port `resolveZedModels` / `mapZedModel` / `normalizeZedModelId` into `src/oauth/zed_auth.rs` (a `zed_llm_fetch` helper with the `x-zed-expired-token`/`x-zed-outdated-token` retry from P0-004, a 60-minute cache, and an in-flight map), call it from `resolve_model` in src/core/executor/zed.rs with a `forceRefresh` retry on a catalog miss, and register a `zed` live-catalog entry in `src/server/api/provider_models.rs` so `/v1/models` mirrors src/app/api/v1/models/route.js:119-136. Keep the existing `normalize_zed_provider` heuristics strictly as the catalog-unavailable fallback.

## P279-001 · P2 · Zero-usage rows and total_requests_lifetime accounting

- **9router**: A request whose usage is all-zero is NOT written to `usageHistory` at all — `saveUsageStats` returns before calling `saveRequestUsage`, so no history row, no `usageDaily` aggregation, and no `totalRequestsLifetime` increment. Separately, the existing-row lookup (usageRepo.js:262-276) compares `promptTokens`/`completionTokens` (the collapsed scalar columns), so two genuine zero-token streaming requests with the same timestamp+provider+model+connection+apiKey DO dedupe.
- **OpenProxy**: `track_request` always inserts a row: the dedupe guard is `if a.tokens.is_none() || b.tokens.is_none() { return false; }` (src/core/usage/tracker.rs:229-233) and then unconditionally `db.history.push(entry)` and `if db.total_requests_lifetime < db.history.len() { db.total_requests_lifetime = db.history.len(); }` (src/core/usage/tracker.rs:90-95). `record_streaming_usage` calls this unconditionally on every stream end even when `usage` is `None`.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy persists a usage row (and bumps the lifetime counter) for every stream, including the many SSE responses that carry no usage at all; 9Router writes nothing for those.
- **Why it matters**: OpenProxy's `total_requests_lifetime`, the Usage page request count, and the daily `requests` aggregate are inflated relative to 9Router by every usage-less response — which is the majority of streaming traffic. The two implementations also disagree in the opposite direction on genuine duplicates: 9Router's SQL dedupe can collapse two same-timestamp zero-token requests into one row, while OpenProxy's `same_usage_row` explicitly refuses to (`tokens: None → never a duplicate`). Any budget/quota gate keyed on the request count therefore fires at different thresholds.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/handlers/chatCore/requestDetail.js:103-124 (`if (inTokens === 0 && outTokens === 0) return;` before saveRequestUsage), open-sse/handlers/chatCore/streamingHandler.js:139 (streaming calls saveUsageStats), src/lib/db/repos/usageRepo.js:241-309 (saveRequestUsage, scalar-column dedupe, lifetime increment). openproxy: src/core/usage/tracker.rs:35-99 (track_request always pushes), src/core/usage/tracker.rs:90-95 (lifetime bump), src/core/usage/tracker.rs:222-233 (`same_usage_row` returns false when either tokens is None), src/server/api/chat.rs:4139-4156 (record_streaming_usage called unconditionally at stream end).
- **Parity fix**: Add the 9Router zero-usage guard to `track_request`: compute `in_tokens`/`out_tokens` first and return early when both are 0, so no history row, no daily aggregate and no `total_requests_lifetime` increment is produced for usage-less responses. Separately, change `same_usage_row` to compare the collapsed `(prompt, completion)` scalars unconditionally (as 9Router's SQL does) rather than bailing out on `tokens: None`. Reconcile the `total_requests_lifetime` update with 9Router's atomic `prev + 1` counter rather than deriving it from `history.len()`, which is also wrong after any retention pruning (see main.rs:585-641).

## P280-001 · P2 · open-sse/executors/codex.js:428 vs src/core/executor/codex.rs:405-409

- **9router**: open-sse/executors/codex.js:428 — inside `transformRequest(model, body, stream, credentials)`, AFTER `normalizeCodexTools(body)` (line 422) and after `body.stream = true` (line 425): `if (!body.instructions || body.instructions.trim() === "") { body.instructions = CODEX_DEFAULT_INSTRUCTIONS; }`. The presence test is `trim() === ""`, so an all-whitespace string counts as ABSENT and is overwritten with the full 11,692-char Codex agent prompt.
- **OpenProxy**: src/core/executor/codex.rs:405-409 — inside `transform_request_body(body, actual_model, _stream)`: `let instructions = body.get("instructions").and_then(Value::as_str).filter(|s| !s.is_empty()).unwrap_or(Self::DEFAULT_CODEX_INSTRUCTIONS);`. The presence test is `!s.is_empty()`, which has no `trim()`, so an all-whitespace string counts as PRESENT and is forwarded to chatgpt.com verbatim as `instructions`.
- **Gap**: The emptiness test is `trim() === \"\"` in 9Router but `is_empty()` (no trim) in OpenProxy, so whitespace-only `instructions` is replaced by the full Codex default upstream and preserved downstream.
- **Why it matters**: The `instructions` field is the entire system prompt for the Codex `/responses` surface. On OpenProxy a whitespace-only value reaches the model, so the model receives no agent instructions at all (no `rg` guidance, no sandbox/approval policy, no apply_patch/edit constraints, no final-answer structure), while the identical request through 9Router gets the complete Codex coding-agent prompt. Responses produced by the same model id therefore diverge in tool choice (e.g. grep instead of rg) and output formatting, and the divergence is invisible at the HTTP layer because both return 200.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/executors/codex.js:428 (`if (!body.instructions || body.instructions.trim() === "")`) and open-sse/config/codexInstructions.js:3 (the injected constant). OpenProxy: src/core/executor/codex.rs:405-409 (`.filter(|s| !s.is_empty()).unwrap_or(Self::DEFAULT_CODEX_INSTRUCTIONS)`). Confirmed the empty-string case matches on both sides (the Responses translator emits `instructions: ""` when no system message is present — open-sse/translator/formats/responsesApi.js vs src/core/translator/request/openai_responses.rs:736-738), so the divergence is isolated to whitespace-only input.
- **Parity fix**: Change the filter at src/core/executor/codex.rs:408 from `.filter(|s| !s.is_empty())` to `.filter(|s| !s.trim().is_empty())` so the Rust presence test matches 9Router's `body.instructions.trim() === \"\"` exactly, then use the trimmed-or-original value as 9Router does (9router only uses the trimmed value for the comparison, it replaces the whole field). Add a unit test in the existing `mod tests` block asserting that a body carrying `{"instructions": "   "}` yields `out["instructions"] == CodexExecutor::DEFAULT_CODEX_INSTRUCTIONS`.

## P281-001 · P2 · open-sse/rtk/autodetect.js:28-71 vs src/core/rtk/autodetect.rs:95-103 + :163-190

- **9router**: autoDetectFilter has exactly 12 exit points and no test-runner, JSON or tabular stages: git-log, git-diff, git-status, build-output, porcelain, grep, find, tree, ls, search-list, read-numbered, dedup-log, smart-truncate, null (open-sse/rtk/autodetect.js:28-71). Large JSON/NDJSON dumps therefore fall to dedupLog (`if (nonEmpty.length >= 5) return dedupLog` at :66).
- **OpenProxy**: Three extra detectors are inserted ahead of the dedup-log fallback: test-runner when RE_TEST_RUNNER matches and the head has at least TEST_RUNNER_MIN_LINES (src/core/rtk/autodetect.rs:95-103), json-summary for bodies of at least 2000 bytes that start with `{`/`[` or are 80%+ NDJSON (:163-177), and SmartCrusher for tabular CSV/TSV/JSON-array bodies of at least 500 bytes (:179-190).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy routes three whole classes of tool output (test-runner logs, large JSON/NDJSON blobs, tabular data) to filters that do not exist in 9Router, changing the compressed payload for those inputs.
- **Why it matters**: For a large JSON API dump or a cargo test log, OpenProxy emits a summary/table rendering while 9Router emits a dedup-log rendering. The upstream request content (and the recorded compression stats / filter name in the RTK log line) differ for the same input, so token accounting and the text the model reads are not 1:1.
- **Evidence**: 9router open-sse/rtk/autodetect.js:28-71 (12 filters, dedup-log at :66); OpenProxy src/core/rtk/autodetect.rs:95-103, :163-190, with the dedup-log fallback still at :192-197
- **Parity fix**: For strict parity remove the test-runner, json-summary and SmartCrusher stages from auto_detect_filter so the filter chosen for every input matches open-sse/rtk/autodetect.js:28-71 (the compressors may stay available, but must not change filter selection).

## P282-001 · P2 · open-sse/rtk/autodetect.js:41 + :61 vs src/core/rtk/autodetect.rs:155-156 + :242-256

- **9router**: read-numbered detection is evaluated against the HEAD window's lines: `const lines = head.split("\n")` (open-sse/rtk/autodetect.js:41) and `if (lines.length >= SMART_TRUNCATE_MIN_LINES && isLineNumbered(lines))` (:61), where head is at most 1024 characters (roughly 30-50 typical lines), so isLineNumbered's 100-line sample is also drawn from the head.
- **OpenProxy**: The gate and the sample both use the full body: `let text_lines: Vec<&str> = text.lines().collect(); if text_lines.len() >= SMART_TRUNCATE_MIN_LINES && is_line_numbered(&text_lines)` (src/core/rtk/autodetect.rs:155-156), with is_line_numbered sampling the first 100 lines of the whole text (:245).
- **Gap**: The read-numbered activation threshold counts lines in the whole body in OpenProxy but only within the 1024-char detection window in 9Router, so any long numbered file dump selects a different filter.
- **Why it matters**: For a Cursor/Codex read_file dump of a 300-1000 line file, 9Router's head window holds fewer than SMART_TRUNCATE_MIN_LINES (250) lines, so it falls through to dedup-log; OpenProxy counts the full body and selects read-numbered. The two produce different truncated content in the upstream request, so token counts and the text the model sees diverge.
- **Evidence**: 9router open-sse/rtk/autodetect.js:30 (head), :41, :61; OpenProxy src/core/rtk/autodetect.rs:54-58 (head), :155-156, :242-256
- **Parity fix**: Evaluate the read-numbered gate against the same `head` used by every other detector (count head lines, sample head lines) so the threshold reflects the detection window, as in open-sse/rtk/autodetect.js:41,61.

## P283-001 · P2 · open-sse/rtk/constants.js:21-29 vs src/core/rtk/constants.rs:46-60

- **9router**: LS_NOISE_DIRS lists the full noise set: node_modules, .git, target, __pycache__, .next, dist, build, .cache, .turbo, .vercel, .pytest_cache, .mypy_cache, .tox, .venv, venv, env, coverage, .nyc_output, .DS_Store, Thumbs.db, .idea, .vscode, .vs, *.egg-info, .eggs (open-sse/rtk/constants.js:21-29), and ls_impl skips any row whose name matches: `if (LS_NOISE_DIRS.includes(parsed.name)) continue;` (open-sse/rtk/filters/ls.js:46).
- **OpenProxy**: LS_NOISE_DIRS lists only: node_modules, .git, target, __pycache__, .next, dist, build, .venv, venv, .cache, .idea, .vscode, .DS_Store (src/core/rtk/constants.rs:46-60); ls_impl skips with `if LS_NOISE_DIRS.contains(&parsed.2) { continue; }` (src/core/rtk/filters/mod.rs:611-613).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's ls noise-directory list drops 12 of 9Router's entries: .turbo, .vercel, .pytest_cache, .mypy_cache, .tox, env, coverage, .nyc_output, Thumbs.db, .vs, *.egg-info, .eggs.
- **Why it matters**: On an `ls -la` of a typical JS/Python project, OpenProxy keeps coverage/, .turbo/, .vercel/, *.egg-info, Thumbs.db etc. in the compressed output and in the dir count, so the payload sent upstream is larger and the Summary line reports more dirs than 9Router's — and junk entries like Thumbs.db are surfaced to the model as real content.
- **Evidence**: 9router open-sse/rtk/constants.js:21-29 and open-sse/rtk/filters/ls.js:46; OpenProxy src/core/rtk/constants.rs:46-60 and src/core/rtk/filters/mod.rs:611-613
- **Parity fix**: Extend LS_NOISE_DIRS in src/core/rtk/constants.rs to the full 9Router set, adding .turbo, .vercel, .pytest_cache, .mypy_cache, .tox, env, coverage, .nyc_output, Thumbs.db, .vs, *.egg-info, .eggs (keeping .env excluded as the JS comment requires).

## P284-001 · P2 · open-sse/rtk/headroom.js:65-77 vs src/core/rtk/headroom.rs:370 + :890-892

- **9router**: buildCompressEndpoint parses the URL, replaces the pathname with `<base>/v1/compress`, clears the hash and PRESERVES the query: `parsed.pathname = parsed.pathname.replace(/\/$/, "") + "/v1/compress"; parsed.hash = ""; return parsed.toString();` with a string fallback that re-appends the query (`return query ? endpoint + "?" + query : endpoint`) — open-sse/rtk/headroom.js:65-77.
- **OpenProxy**: `let endpoint = format!("{}/v1/compress", config.url.trim_end_matches('/'));` (src/core/rtk/headroom.rs:370; helper at :890-892) — the query string is discarded while the base path is preserved.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's /v1/compress endpoint drops the URL query string, so a Headroom URL that carries an auth token or tenant selector in the query is called without it.
- **Why it matters**: Against an authenticated or tunnelled Headroom deployment the proxy call 401s/404s; because the pass is fail-open, compression silently never happens (no error to the user, only the skip diagnostic). 9Router works with the same URL.
- **Evidence**: 9router open-sse/rtk/headroom.js:65-77; OpenProxy src/core/rtk/headroom.rs:370 and src/core/rtk/headroom.rs:890-892
- **Parity fix**: Build the endpoint with the `url` crate: parse config.url, set the path to the trimmed path plus /v1/compress, clear the fragment and keep the query — mirroring open-sse/rtk/headroom.js:65-77, with a string fallback that re-appends the query when parsing fails.

## P285-001 · P2 · open-sse/rtk/index.js:18-21 vs src/core/rtk/mod.rs:596-601 + :721-726

- **9router**: compressMessages picks the array to work on and mutates it in place, never reassigning the other key: `const items = Array.isArray(body.messages) ? body.messages : Array.isArray(body.input) ? body.input : null; if (!items) return null;` (open-sse/rtk/index.js:18-21) — the JS object graph means compressed values land back in whichever key they came from.
- **OpenProxy**: The write-back key is chosen by key PRESENCE, not by which array was compressed: `let key = if body.as_object().is_some_and(|f| f.contains_key("messages")) { "messages" } else { "input" }; body[key] = Value::Array(items);` (src/core/rtk/mod.rs:721-726), after the array was selected as `messages.as_array().or(input.as_array())` (:598-600).
- **Gap**: When `messages` exists but is not an array while `input` is, OpenProxy compressed `input` and then wrote it into `messages`, clobbering the original field.
- **Why it matters**: A body carrying a non-array `messages` alongside an `input` array is rewritten so `messages` becomes the input array — the original `messages` value is destroyed and the upstream receives a malformed request. 9Router compresses `input` in place and leaves `messages` untouched.
