- **Evidence**: 9router open-sse/rtk/index.js:18-21 (select-and-mutate); OpenProxy src/core/rtk/mod.rs:598-600 (selection) vs :721-726 (key chosen by presence)
- **Parity fix**: Track which key was actually selected (compute `let key = if fields.get("messages").and_then(Value::as_array).is_some() { "messages" } else { "input" };` alongside the selection) and write back to that same key, mirroring open-sse/rtk/index.js:18-21.

## P286-001 · P2 · open-sse/rtk/index.js:26-27 and :64-67 vs src/core/rtk/mod.rs:596-601 + :611 + :673

- **9router**: compressMessages iterates defensively and mutates in place: `const msg = items[i]; if (!msg) continue;` (open-sse/rtk/index.js:26-27) and `const block = msg.content[j]; if (!block || block.type !== "tool_result") continue;` (:64-67). A non-object element is skipped and every other element is still compressed; whatever was mutated stays mutated because the array is the body's own array.
- **OpenProxy**: compress_messages CLONES the array (`let mut items = { ...; arr.or(input)?.clone() };` src/core/rtk/mod.rs:596-601) and then uses `?` on element access: `let msg_fields = msg.as_object_mut()?;` (:611) and `let block_fields = block.as_object_mut()?;` (:673). A single non-object element returns None from the whole function, and because nothing was written back, NO compression is applied to the request.
- **Gap**: One non-object element in messages/content aborts OpenProxy's entire RTK pass and leaves the body untouched; 9Router skips that element and compresses the rest.
- **Why it matters**: Payloads that legitimately contain a non-object content part (Ollama-style content arrays of plain strings, a null entry, a bare string) silently disable RTK for the whole request in OpenProxy: no filter runs, no token is saved, and the full raw tool output is billed — while the same request is compressed in 9Router.
- **Evidence**: 9router open-sse/rtk/index.js:26-27, :64-67 (skip + in-place mutation); OpenProxy src/core/rtk/mod.rs:596-601 (clone), :611, :673 (`?` early return), :721-726 (write-back only on the success path)
- **Parity fix**: Replace both `?` with skip-style continues (`let Some(msg_fields) = msg.as_object_mut() else { continue; };` and the same for block) so one malformed element does not abort the pass, matching open-sse/rtk/index.js:26-27 and :64-67.

## P287-001 · P2 · open-sse/utils/cursorProtobuf.js:417-425 vs src/core/executor/cursor.rs:1247-1270

- **9router**: `encodeMetadata` sends the real runtime fingerprint: `process.platform` ("darwin"/"win32"/"linux"), `process.arch` ("arm64"/"x64"), `process.version` (e.g. "v22.14.0"), `process.cwd()` (the real working directory), and `new Date().toISOString()` ("2026-09-24T12:34:56.789Z") (cursorProtobuf.js:417-425).
- **OpenProxy**: `encode_metadata` sends `std::env::consts::OS` ("macos"/"windows"/"linux"), `std::env::consts::ARCH` ("aarch64"/"x86_64"), a literal `"v20.0.0"`, a literal `"/"` for cwd, and `chrono::Utc::now().to_rfc3339()` ("2026-09-24T12:34:56.789456789+00:00") (cursor.rs:1247-1270).
- **Gap**: Five of the six protobuf metadata fields differ: platform/arch use different vocabularies, version and cwd are hardcoded, and the timestamp uses a different format and precision.
- **Why it matters**: The `Metadata` sub-message is part of the signed protobuf body Cursor receives on every request. The strings OpenProxy emits are not ones the real Cursor IDE could produce (no macOS build reports "aarch64" in `process.arch`, no build reports "x86_64" there), and the `+00:00` fractional form is not what `toISOString()` emits. Any server-side freshness or device-fingerprint check on this message diverges from the reference client.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/utils/cursorProtobuf.js:417-425. OpenProxy: src/core/executor/cursor.rs:1247-1270.
- **Parity fix**: Map `consts::OS`/`ARCH` to the Node vocabulary (darwin/win32/linux, arm64/x64), thread the real process version and cwd through from the server config, and format the timestamp as `to_rfc3339_opts(SecondsFormat::Millis, true)` so it ends in `Z` at millisecond precision.

## P288-001 · P2 · openai→cursor request translation — `developer` role messages

- **9router**: `convertMessages` (openai-to-cursor.js:82-88) converts only `ROLE.SYSTEM` messages into a `{role:"user", content:"[System Instructions]\n…"}` message. A message with `role:"developer"` matches neither the `system` nor the `tool` branch, and the remaining loop body is guarded by `if (msg.role === ROLE.USER || msg.role === ROLE.ASSISTANT)` (openai-to-cursor.js:103) with no `else` — so `developer` messages are SILENTLY DROPPED.
- **OpenProxy**: src/core/translator/request/openai_to_cursor.rs:114-121 emits a `[System Instructions]\n…` user message for `role == "system" || role == "developer"` — the `developer` role is preserved and injected into the prompt.
- **Gap**: 9router silently discards `developer`-role messages on the Cursor path; OpenProxy forwards them to Cursor as `[System Instructions]` user turns.
- **Why it matters**: `developer` is the successor to `system` in the OpenAI Responses/Chat spec and is emitted by Codex and several current clients. Under 9router parity those instructions never reach Cursor; under OpenProxy they do. The two proxies therefore send materially different prompts for the same request, producing different completions and different safety adherence for the same input.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/translator/request/openai-to-cursor.js:82-88, 103-160 | openproxy: src/core/translator/request/openai_to_cursor.rs:114-121
- **Parity fix**: Narrow the guard at src/core/translator/request/openai_to_cursor.rs:114 from `role == "system" || role == "developer"` to `role == "system"` so `developer` messages fall through and are dropped, matching openai-to-cursor.js:82-88 and :103.

## P289-001 · P2 · openproxy: src/core/executor/codex.rs:65-69 and :86-95 (strip_codex_tool_patterns) | 9router: open-sse/utils/codexToolSchema.js:57-68 (stripNode properties branch)

- **9router**: codexToolSchema.js:57 gates the special-cased `properties` walk on `key === "properties" && value && typeof value === "object" && !Array.isArray(value)`. When `properties` is an array the guard fails, control falls through to the generic `const cleaned = stripNode(value, stats)` at :69, which recurses into the array elements and strips their `pattern`. Verified by executing the real module: input `{type:'object', properties:[{type:'string', pattern:'^[^\\p{Cc}]+$'}]}` returns `{"type":"object","properties":[{"type":"string"}]}` — the pattern is removed.
- **OpenProxy**: codex.rs:65-69 builds `other_keys` by filtering OUT both "properties" and "pattern", so the generic recursion at :81-85 never visits a `properties` key. The dedicated walk at :86-95 is itself gated on `if let Some(Value::Object(props)) = ... get_mut("properties")`, so an array-valued `properties` matches nothing and is never traversed. Verified by executing a standalone harness containing the verbatim codex.rs:26-104 functions: the same input returns `{"properties":[{"pattern":"^[^\\p{Cc}]+$","type":"string"}],...}` — the pattern survives.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy never recurses into an array-valued `properties` node, so a `\p{...}` pattern nested there is never stripped and reaches Codex, whereas 9Router's fall-through recursion strips it.
- **Why it matters**: This is the #3922 failure mode the strip exists to prevent, surviving in one nested position. `properties` as an array is non-canonical but is accepted by permissive clients and schema generators, and a Claude-Code-supplied Artifact/WebFetch schema is exactly the kind of deeply nested, machine-generated structure where an array-valued node can appear. When it does, the `\p{...}` pattern ships to Codex, which answers 400 Invalid schema for function — deterministically, on every account, so the combo pays a full failover cycle before landing on a provider that accepts it. The blast radius is small (one nesting shape) but the consequence is the full deterministic-failure loop the strip was added to prevent.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/utils/codexToolSchema.js:57 (array guard rejects array `properties`) falling through to :69 (generic recursion covers it). openproxy: src/core/executor/codex.rs:65-69 (`other_keys` filters out "properties") and :86-95 (dedicated walk gated on Value::Object). Both behaviors confirmed by execution against the real JS module and a verbatim copy of the Rust functions; the three existing Rust unit tests (codex.rs:112-145) all use object-valued `properties` and therefore do not exercise this path.
- **Parity fix**: Make the Rust walk mirror the JS control flow at codexToolSchema.js:57-69. In strip_codex_tool_patterns (src/core/executor/codex.rs:53-104), stop excluding "properties" from `other_keys` at :67 — instead treat the key exactly as JS does: keep the dedicated object-walk for `Value::Object`, and let any other shape (notably `Value::Array`) fall through to the generic recursion. Concretely, change the filter at :67 to exclude only "pattern", and at :86 change the gate so the `properties` recursion is taken only for objects while non-object `properties` values are still visited by the loop at :81-85. Add a regression test mirroring the JS assertion, covering `properties` as an array containing a `\p{...}` pattern.

## P290-001 · P2 · shared/components/Modal.tsx — overlay-click dismissal default

- **9router**: `Modal({ ..., closeOnOverlay = true, ... })` — the default is `true`, so the overlay `onClick={closeOnOverlay ? onClose : undefined}` fires `onClose` on any click outside the dialog. No call site in the 9router tree passes `closeOnOverlay` explicitly, so all 39 `<Modal>` usages dismiss on outside click.
- **OpenProxy**: `Modal({ ..., closeOnOverlay = false, ... })` — the default is inverted. No call site in the OpenProxy tree passes `closeOnOverlay`, so all 42 `<Modal>` usages ignore overlay clicks.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy flipped the `closeOnOverlay` default from `true` to `false`, so every modal in the dashboard stops dismissing on an outside click.
- **Why it matters**: This is the primary "get out of a loading state" affordance. A user mid-submit (adding a connection, editing a combo, confirming a delete) who clicks the dimmed backdrop to dismiss an in-flight-looking form now has nothing happen and must hunt for the ✕; conversely the many destructive confirm dialogs (delete key, bulk delete connections, delete proxy pool) no longer close on an accidental outside click, so the user must find Cancel. It is the single behavioural delta affecting every dialog at once.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/shared/components/Modal.js:15 (`closeOnOverlay = true`), :51 (overlay onClick). OpenProxy: web/src/shared/components/Modal.tsx:27 (`closeOnOverlay = false`), :64 (overlay onClick). Neither tree overrides the prop at any call site (`grep -rn "closeOnOverlay={"` returns nothing on both sides); call-site counts 39 (9router) vs 42 (OpenProxy).
- **Parity fix**: Change the default in web/src/shared/components/Modal.tsx:27 to `closeOnOverlay = true` to match 9router, keeping the prop so individual destructive dialogs can still opt out with an explicit `closeOnOverlay={false}`.

## P291-001 · P2 · src/app/api/headroom/proxy/[...path]/route.js:68-92 vs src/server/api/headroom.rs:1036-1132

- **9router**: The reverse proxy streams: `const response = await fetch(target, {...}); ... return new NextResponse(response.body, { status: response.status, headers });` (src/app/api/headroom/proxy/[...path]/route.js:68-92) — the upstream body is passed through as a live stream, so SSE endpoints (notably `transformations/feed`, which this same route explicitly rewrites at :52-57) work.
- **OpenProxy**: proxy_handler buffers the entire upstream response before replying: `let resp_body = match resp.bytes().await {...}` (src/server/api/headroom.rs:1091) under a client with `.timeout(std::time::Duration::from_secs(60))` (:1036-1038), then emits it as a single Bytes body (:1133).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy fully buffers the upstream response with a 60 s cap, so any long-lived or streaming Headroom endpoint never reaches the browser.
- **Why it matters**: The Headroom dashboard is embedded through this route (web/src/components/TokenSaverPageClient.tsx:447 points the iframe at /api/headroom/proxy/dashboard). Its live transformations/feed stream — which this very route rewrites to stay same-origin — hangs for 60 s and then returns 502 instead of streaming, so the dashboard's live updates are broken in OpenProxy while working in 9Router.
- **Evidence**: 9router src/app/api/headroom/proxy/[...path]/route.js:68-92 (streamed) and :52-57 (feed rewrite); OpenProxy src/server/api/headroom.rs:1036-1038 (60 s timeout) and :1091-1133 (full buffering)
- **Parity fix**: Stream the upstream body into the axum response (e.g. axum::body::Body::from_stream(resp.bytes_stream())) and drop the 60 s whole-request timeout, applying a timeout only to establishing the connection — matching the streaming `new NextResponse(response.body, ...)` in proxy/[...path]/route.js:92.

## P292-001 · P2 · src/app/api/headroom/stop/route.js:9-10 + start/route.js:30-33 vs src/server/api/headroom.rs:490-495 + :526-555 + :427-433

- **9router**: stop maps the result to a status code: `const status = result.stopped ? 200 : 409; return NextResponse.json({ ...result }, { status });` (src/app/api/headroom/stop/route.js:9-10) where stopHeadroomProxy returns `{ stopped: false, reason: "not_running" }` when there is no managed pid (src/lib/headroom/process.js:114). start returns `{ success: true, ...result }` and is idempotent: `const existing = getManagedPid(); if (existing) return { pid: existing, alreadyRunning: true };` (src/lib/headroom/process.js:65; route at start/route.js:30). A missing CLI yields 400 via `error.code === "NOT_INSTALLED" ? 400 : 500` (start/route.js:32).
- **OpenProxy**: stop always answers 200 with a stopped:true body whether or not a process was running (src/server/api/headroom.rs:526-530 and :551-555). start answers `started/pid/message` with no `success` field and first kills any managed process before respawning (src/server/api/headroom.rs:436-443, :490-495). A missing CLI/binary yields 412 PRECONDITION_FAILED (src/server/api/headroom.rs:427-433).
- **Gap**: Stop returns 200 for a no-op instead of 409, start lacks the `success` field and restarts an already-running proxy instead of reporting alreadyRunning, and the not-installed case returns 412 instead of 400.
- **Why it matters**: The dashboard Stop button reports success when nothing was stopped, so users lose the only signal that the proxy they think they stopped is still serving; a second Start tears down a healthy proxy (dropping in-flight /v1/compress calls) rather than being a no-op. Clients branching on res.ok or on success/alreadyRunning behave differently.
- **Evidence**: 9router src/app/api/headroom/stop/route.js:9-10, src/lib/headroom/process.js:65 and :114, src/app/api/headroom/start/route.js:30-33; OpenProxy src/server/api/headroom.rs:551-555, :490-495, :436-443, :427-433
- **Parity fix**: Return 409 with {stopped:false, reason:"not_running"} from stop when no managed pid exists; in start, return {success:true, pid, alreadyRunning:true} when a managed pid is alive instead of killing it; and use 400 (not 412) with code NOT_INSTALLED when the headroom binary is missing.

## P293-001 · P2 · src/core/utils/error.rs:224-265 → media.rs:1116; stt.rs:1180; chat.rs:4853, 4872

- **9router**: The response status is always `response.status` (or `parsed.status` from an executor override) — error.js:72, 88. No status is ever re-derived from the message body.
- **OpenProxy**: `infer_status_from_message` re-derives the status from the message text: "rate limit"/"too many requests" → 429; "insufficient"/"quota"/"balance" → 403 (402 if "payment"/"billing"); "not supported" → 406; "model not found" → 404; "invalid api key"/"unauthorized" → 401; "upstream"/"bad gateway"/"connection reset" → 502 when the original was <500. Because `error.type` and `error.code` are keyed off the status, all three change together.
- **Gap**: The HTTP status can be rewritten from the message text on media, STT and empty-body chat error paths.
- **Why it matters**: Client retry semantics change with the status: the OpenAI SDK auto-retries 429/5xx and gives up immediately on 400/406. A TTS provider returning 400 `Model x is not supported` is re-emitted as 406 (give up) and 400 `insufficient balance` as 403; conversely a 401 from a free console proxy is masked as 401 rather than surfacing 9router's 401 either way but with the original text. 9router never reclassifies.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/utils/error.js:72, 88, 98-106. OpenProxy: src/core/utils/error.rs:224-265; src/server/api/media.rs:1116; src/server/api/stt.rs:1180; src/server/api/chat.rs:4853, 4872
- **Parity fix**: Remove the `infer_status_from_message` calls from `media.rs:1116`, `stt.rs:1180`, `chat.rs:4853` and `chat.rs:4872` and use the upstream status verbatim, matching `buildErrorBody` in error.js.

## P294-001 · P2 · src/core/utils/error.rs:65-207 → chat.rs:4851-4858, 4871-4879; media.rs:1114-1121; stt.rs:1178-1184; codex.rs:718; grok_cli.rs:267

- **9router**: `parseUpstreamError` returns the provider's own message verbatim (`json.error.message || json.message || json.error || bodyText`, else `DEFAULT_ERROR_MESSAGES[status]` else `Upstream error: ${status}`) and `formatProviderError` only prefixes `[<code>]: ` and appends `(cause: …)` (error.js:58-89, 139-147). Nothing is rewritten, reworded, or truncated.
- **OpenProxy**: `friendly_error_message` replaces the text with canned copy for eleven phrase families (quota, rate limit, model-not-supported, model-not-found, gateway, invalid key, billing, overloaded, timeout), strips a leading `[code]:` wrapper (error.rs:106-114), strips `Error from provider (Console):`-style prefixes, and clamps to 280 chars with an ellipsis. It is called on every chat, media, STT and codex/grok error path.
- **Gap**: Upstream error text is rewritten into generic marketing copy instead of being passed through with a `[<status>]: ` prefix.
- **Why it matters**: Diagnostic detail is lost — `Model minimax-m3-free is not supported` becomes `This model is not supported by the provider`, so a user cannot tell WHICH model to disable. It also removes the `[<status>]: ` prefix that 9router's own dashboard sniffs for (`getConnectionErrorTag`, providers/page.js:75-93), and the 280-char clamp silently truncates long provider explanations.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/utils/error.js:58-89, 139-147; .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/providers/page.js:75-93. OpenProxy: src/core/utils/error.rs:65-207, 106-114; src/server/api/chat.rs:4851-4858, 4871-4879; src/server/api/media.rs:1114-1121; src/server/api/stt.rs:1178-1184
- **Parity fix**: Delete `friendly_error_message` from the response-building path and restore the 9router shape: `build_error_body(status, Some(&format!("[{status}]: {raw_message}")))`. Keep the HTML-title extraction and length clamp only for the non-SSE stream guard (P1-012), where 9router also applies them.

## P295-001 · P2 · src/lib/headroom/detect.js:145-162 vs src/server/api/headroom.rs:380-388

- **9router**: getHeadroomStatus reports install state from the binary on disk: `const path = findHeadroomBinary(); const installed = Boolean(path); ... return { installed, path, running, python, localUrl, canStart: installed && localUrl, version, extras }` (src/lib/headroom/detect.js:145-162) — installed means the CLI is present, and canStart is installed && loopback, independent of running.
- **OpenProxy**: The status handler invents its own semantics: `"installed": running || local_url`, `"canStart": local_url && !running`, `"python": std::env::var("HEADROOM_PYTHON").ok()`, `"managedPid": get_managed_pid().is_some()` and no `path`, `version` or `extras` fields (src/server/api/headroom.rs:380-388).
- **Gap**: `installed` is true for any loopback URL — including the default http://localhost:8787 — regardless of whether headroom-ai exists, and `canStart` inverts the 9Router rule.
- **Why it matters**: With a fresh install and the default URL, OpenProxy reports installed=true, so the token-saver page shows "Stopped" instead of "Not installed" and renders the extras panel (gated on headroomStatus.installed) plus an enabled Start button that then 412s. When the proxy IS running, canStart flips to false so the Start control disappears, whereas 9Router keeps it available and idempotent.
- **Evidence**: 9router src/lib/headroom/detect.js:145-162; OpenProxy src/server/api/headroom.rs:380-388, consumed at web/src/components/TokenSaverPageClient.tsx:434-441 (a direct port of src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/token-saver/TokenSaverClient.js:449-457)
- **Parity fix**: Derive status from the same probes: installed = find_headroom_binary().is_some(), canStart = installed && local_url, and include path, version and extras (sourced from get_installed_extras) in the response, matching src/lib/headroom/detect.js:145-162.

## P296-001 · P2 · src/server/api/chat.rs:1697-1706, 4839-4848

- **9router**: When every account is model-locked, `handleSingleModelChat` calls `unavailableResponse(503, "[provider/model] <lastError>", retryAfter, retryAfterHuman)` (chat.js:239-242), which builds `{"error":{"message":"[provider/model] <lastError> (reset after 4m 12s)"}}` and sets `Retry-After` (error.js:116-129). The human hint comes from `formatRetryAfter` (accountFallback.js:90-103).
- **OpenProxy**: The same condition produces `"All accounts for {provider}/{model} are cooling down"` (chat.rs:1700) with only the `Retry-After` header. `grep -rn "reset after|format_retry_after" src/` returns nothing — there is no `formatRetryAfter` port.
- **Gap**: The 503 all-accounts-cooling-down body drops the underlying last error and the human-readable `(reset after Xm Ys)` suffix.
- **Why it matters**: Users see a bare "cooling down" with no cause and no timing, so they cannot tell whether to wait seconds or hours or which provider is at fault; 9router clients key off the `(reset after …)` suffix to schedule their own retry.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/sse/handlers/chat.js:238-242; .tmp/9router/open-sse/utils/error.js:116-129; .tmp/9router/open-sse/services/accountFallback.js:90-103. OpenProxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:1697-1706, 4839-4848
- **Parity fix**: Port `formatRetryAfter` (h/m/s formatting) and set the message to `format!("[{provider}/{model}] {} ({})", last_error, format_retry_after(retry_after))` in the cooling-down branch, keeping the `Retry-After` header as-is.

## P297-001 · P2 · src/server/api/chat.rs:2615-2624; src/core/executor/default.rs:899-918

- **9router**: `getExecutor(provider)` never fails: exact map hit or a lazily cached `new DefaultExecutor(provider)` (executors/index.js:67-71). `BaseExecutor.buildUrl` then resolves `config.baseUrl` (possibly `undefined`) and the fetch either succeeds or throws → the 502 path at chatCore.js:398-402.
- **OpenProxy**: `DefaultExecutor::new` returns `ExecutorError::UnsupportedProvider` when the provider has neither a `PROVIDER_CONFIGS` entry nor a compatible provider node (default.rs:912, 918), and chat.rs:2620-2624 maps it to `status: 500, message: "Default executor creation failed: UnsupportedProvider(\"…\")"`.
- **Gap**: An unknown/unconfigured provider produces a 500 configuration error, not the 502 gateway error 9router produces for the same input.
- **Why it matters**: The client is told the proxy server crashed rather than that the upstream could not be reached, so it will not retry or fall back. The message also names an internal Rust enum variant instead of the provider, and a 500 marks the request as a server bug in every dashboard error badge.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/executors/index.js:67-71; .tmp/9router/open-sse/executors/base.js:30-44; .tmp/9router/open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:398-402. OpenProxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:2615-2624; src/core/executor/default.rs:899-918, 704, 751-757
- **Parity fix**: Map `ExecutorError::UnsupportedProvider` to `ComboAttemptError { status: 502, message: format!("[502]: no upstream configured for provider {provider}"), .. }` in the `map_err` at chat.rs:2620, matching the status 9router's fetch-failure path produces.

## P298-001 · P2 · web/src/components/EndpointPageClient.tsx:116-120, 1251-1266, 453

- **9router**: The caveman level picker lives on the token-saver page and reads the shared six-entry CAVEMAN_LEVELS list — lite, full, ultra, plus wenyan-lite, wenyan, wenyan-ultra (the last three flagged `wenyan: true`) — filtered to the non-wenyan three unless the active locale is zh-CN/zh-TW (9router: `src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/endpoint/endpointConstants.js:19-26`, consumed at `src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/token-saver/TokenSaverClient.js:70-81, :661-682`).
- **OpenProxy**: TokenSaverPageClient correctly carries the full six-entry list including the three wenyan levels (web/src/components/TokenSaverPageClient.tsx:42-49) — parity. But EndpointPageClient re-declares its own three-entry CAVEMAN_LEVELS with no wenyan variants (web/src/components/EndpointPageClient.tsx:116-120) and renders all three unconditionally at :1253-1266, with no locale gating. Both pickers write the same `cavemanLevel` setting via patchSetting (EndpointPageClient.tsx:453, TokenSaverPageClient.tsx:344).
- **Gap**: The caveman picker duplicated onto the endpoint page ships only 3 of the 6 levels, so a user who selected a wenyan level in token-saver sees no matching button on the endpoint page.
- **Why it matters**: A user on the endpoint page who has cavemanLevel set to "wenyan-ultra" sees a row of three buttons with none highlighted and no way to tell which mode is active, and cannot select a wenyan level from that page at all. The two controls write the same setting while presenting different option sets, so the displayed state contradicts the persisted state.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/endpoint/endpointConstants.js:19-26 (6 levels incl. 3 wenyan), src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/token-saver/TokenSaverClient.js:70-81 (locale filter), :661-682 (render). OpenProxy: web/src/components/EndpointPageClient.tsx:116-120 (3 levels, no wenyan), :1253-1266 (render), :453 (patchSetting); full list at web/src/components/TokenSaverPageClient.tsx:42-49.
- **Parity fix**: Replace the inline three-entry list in EndpointPageClient.tsx with the shared six-entry CAVEMAN_LEVELS (extract to a shared constants module alongside endpointConstants) and apply the same `isWenyanLocale ? CAVEMAN_LEVELS : CAVEMAN_LEVELS.filter(l => !l.wenyan)` gate TokenSaverPageClient already uses, so both pickers expose an identical, locale-aware option set.

## P299-001 · P2 · web/src/components/EndpointPageClient.tsx:78-84

- **9router**: endpointConstants.js exports eight polling constants: TUNNEL_PING_INTERVAL_MS=2000, TUNNEL_PING_MAX_MS=300000, STATUS_POLL_FAST_MS=5000, STATUS_POLL_SLOW_MS=30000, REACHABLE_MISS_THRESHOLD=5, CLIENT_PING_FAST_MS=10000, CLIENT_PING_SLOW_MS=60000, CLIENT_PING_TIMEOUT_MS=5000 (9router: `src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/endpoint/endpointConstants.js:11-17`). The endpoint client imports the two FAST intervals (5 s status poll at EndpointPageClient.js:116, 10 s client ping at :152) and stops polling entirely once healthy; the two SLOW constants are declared but unreferenced.
- **OpenProxy**: EndpointPageClient re-declares the constants inline instead of importing a shared module: TUNNEL_PING_INTERVAL_MS=2000, TUNNEL_PING_MAX_MS=300000, STATUS_POLL_INTERVAL_MS=5000, REACHABLE_MISS_THRESHOLD=5, CLIENT_PING_FAST_MS=10000, CLIENT_PING_TIMEOUT_MS=5000 (web/src/components/EndpointPageClient.tsx:78-84). The two SLOW constants (STATUS_POLL_SLOW_MS=30000, CLIENT_PING_SLOW_MS=60000) are not ported at all.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy hardcodes the endpoint polling constants locally and omits the two SLOW_* constants, so there is no single source of truth and the degradation-tier values cannot be tuned the way 9router's shared endpointConstants module allows.
- **Why it matters**: Only the effective cadence matters at runtime today and it is identical (5 s degraded status poll, 10 s degraded client ping, both stopped when healthy, 5-miss debounce, 5 s client-ping timeout), so there is no user-visible cadence drift today. The risk is maintenance: the same numbers are now encoded independently in two files, and the shared constants module 9router centralises is not reproduced, so any change to the ping cadence in one place silently drifts from the other.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/endpoint/endpointConstants.js:11-17 (8 constants incl. STATUS_POLL_SLOW_MS=30000 and CLIENT_PING_SLOW_MS=60000). OpenProxy: web/src/components/EndpointPageClient.tsx:78-84 (6 constants re-declared inline, both SLOW_* missing). Cadence consumers match: 9router EndpointPageClient.js:116, :152 vs OpenProxy EndpointPageClient.tsx:282, :344.
- **Parity fix**: Port endpointConstants.js as a shared module (e.g. web/src/shared/constants/endpointConstants.ts) exporting all eight constants and import it in EndpointPageClient, so the cadence lives in one place and the SLOW_* tier is present for parity even if the current "stop polling when healthy" strategy still uses only the FAST values.

## P300-001 · P2 · web/src/components/usage/ProviderLimits/index.tsx:832-849

- **9router**: The quota page has three distinct empty states driven by `getConnectionsEmptyMessage(totals, providerFilter, accountFilter)` (9router: `src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/usage/components/ProviderLimits/index.js:773-777`, rendered at :800-818 with the helper's icon/title/description): (1) eligibleConnections === 0 → "No Providers Connected" / "Connect to providers with OAuth to track your API quota limits and usage."; (2) providerFilteredConnections === 0 → icon filter_alt_off, "No Accounts Match Current Filters", description interpolating the account filter ("No turned off / active / matching accounts found for <provider>."); (3) otherwise → "No Accounts On This Page" / "Try moving to another page or refreshing the current filters." (helper at `.../ProviderLimits/utils.js:121-146`).
- **OpenProxy**: A single hardcoded empty state renders whenever `!connectionsLoading && sortedConnections.length === 0`: icon cloud_off, title "No Providers Connected", description "Connect to providers with OAuth to track your API quota limits and usage." (web/src/components/usage/ProviderLimits/index.tsx:832-849). getConnectionsEmptyMessage is present and correct in web/src/components/usage/ProviderLimits/utils.ts:124-153 but never called (grep: 0 references in index.tsx), and no `totals` object is computed.
- **Gap**: Only the "no providers at all" empty state is implemented; the filter-mismatch and out-of-page empty states are missing, so a filtered-to-empty quota page wrongly tells the user they have no providers connected.
- **Why it matters**: Once the provider filter is narrowed (or a page lands empty) the user is shown "No Providers Connected — Connect to providers with OAuth", which is factually wrong: their providers are connected, the current filter just matches none. It sends them to the providers page instead of telling them to clear the filter, and it is the state users hit most often while hunting for a specific account.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/usage/components/ProviderLimits/index.js:773-777 (helper call), :800-818 (render), utils.js:121-146 (three branches). OpenProxy: web/src/components/usage/ProviderLimits/index.tsx:832-849 (hardcoded cloud_off state); unused helper at web/src/components/usage/ProviderLimits/utils.ts:124-153.
- **Parity fix**: Compute `totals = { eligibleConnections: filteredConnections.length, providerFilteredConnections: providerFilteredConnections.length }` in index.tsx, call the already-ported getConnectionsEmptyMessage(totals, providerFilter, accountFilter), and render emptyState.icon / .title / .description in the same three-branch structure as 9router. Reuse the ConnectionsTotals type already declared in utils.ts:34-37.

## P301-001 · P2 · web/src/components/usage/ProviderTopology.tsx:225-231, 246-250, 264-271

- **9router**: ProviderTopology maintains a firstSeenRef map keyed by active provider plus a 1 s tick (FE_ACTIVE_TICK_MS = 1000), and filters the rendered active set through `now - ts < FE_ACTIVE_TIMEOUT_MS` with FE_ACTIVE_TIMEOUT_MS = 60000, so a provider whose backend in-flight record is stuck (never cleared) stops being shown as active after 60 s (9router: `src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/usage/components/ProviderTopology.js:18-19, :371-399`). It also installs a ResizeObserver that re-runs fitView on container resize (:421-430) and re-fits 50 ms after nodes.length changes (:432-438), and enables panning/zooming plus a `<Controls>` widget (:447-468).
- **OpenProxy**: ProviderTopology builds activeSet straight from the active-provider key with no firstSeen map, no 1 s tick, and no 60 s staleness cut-off (web/src/components/usage/ProviderTopology.tsx:225-231). fitView is called once inside onInit after a 50 ms timeout (:246-250) and never again; there is no ResizeObserver, no re-fit on node-count change, and pan/zoom are explicitly disabled (panOnDrag={false} zoomOnScroll={false} zoomOnPinch={false} zoomOnDoubleClick={false}, :268-271) with no <Controls> child.
- **Gap**: The topology lost its 60-second stuck-provider timeout, its container-resize / node-count re-fit, and all pan/zoom/controls interaction.
- **Why it matters**: Two concrete effects. (1) A provider whose active flag is never cleared by the backend (crashed in-flight request, a client that disconnected without aborting) stays rendered with the green pulsing border and animated edge indefinitely in OpenProxy, permanently signalling "busy" for a provider that is actually dead; 9router self-heals after 60 s. (2) With 40+ configured providers the ellipse radius exceeds the 320/480 px viewport, the single fitView-on-mount leaves nodes clipped, and with pan/zoom disabled and no Controls the user cannot reach the off-screen providers or inspect a crowded graph at all.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/usage/components/ProviderTopology.js:18-19 (FE_ACTIVE_TIMEOUT_MS=60000, FE_ACTIVE_TICK_MS=1000), :371-399 (firstSeen map + tick + 60 s filter), :421-438 (ResizeObserver + nodes.length re-fit), :447-468 (pan/zoom + Controls). OpenProxy: web/src/components/usage/ProviderTopology.tsx:225-231 (no firstSeen/tick/timeout), :246-250 (fitView only in onInit), :268-271 (pan/zoom all false, no Controls).
- **Parity fix**: Port the constants and mechanism verbatim: add `const FE_ACTIVE_TIMEOUT_MS = 60000; const FE_ACTIVE_TICK_MS = 1000;`, a firstSeenRef populated in a useEffect on rawActiveSet, a 1 s setTick interval active only while rawActiveSet.size > 0, and filter activeSet through the 60 s window. Re-add the ResizeObserver fitView effect and the 50 ms re-fit on nodes.length, and restore minZoom={0.1} maxZoom={2} panOnDrag zoomOnScroll zoomOnPinch zoomOnDoubleClick with the <Controls showInteractive={false}> child.

## P302-001 · P2 · web/src/pages/callback.astro:106-112 (localStorage relay + 1s removal) vs web/src/shared/components/OAuthModal.tsx:474-484 (mount-time pickup)

- **9router**: All three relays run UNCONDITIONALLY and the localStorage key is left in place for the consumer to read and remove: `postMessage` per allowlisted origin, then `BroadcastChannel`, then `localStorage.setItem("oauth_callback", JSON.stringify({...callbackData, timestamp: Date.now()}))` (src/app/callback/page.js:40-71). There is no `removeItem` on the writer side. The consumer `OAuthModal` reads it on mount with a 30s staleness window — `const stored = localStorage.getItem("oauth_callback"); if (stored) { const data = JSON.parse(stored); if (data.timestamp && Date.now() - data.timestamp < 30000) handleCallback(data); localStorage.removeItem("oauth_callback"); }` (src/shared/components/OAuthModal.js:554-566) — so a payload that arrived before the modal's effect ran is still recoverable, and the same code also clears the key it consumed.
- **OpenProxy**: The relays run in a different order (BroadcastChannel first, then localStorage, then postMessage) but the storage write is self-deleting: `localStorage.setItem('oauth_callback', JSON.stringify(data)); setTimeout(function () { try { localStorage.removeItem('oauth_callback'); } catch (_) {} }, 1000);` (web/src/pages/callback.astro:106-112). The consumer keeps 9router's 30s staleness check and post-consume removal (`web/src/shared/components/OAuthModal.tsx:474-484`). Because the writer deletes the key after 1s, the mount-time recovery path only works if OAuthModal's effect ran within that 1s window.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's OAuth callback deletes the `oauth_callback` localStorage key 1000ms after writing it, so the consumer's 30-second stale-tolerant mount-time recovery can miss the payload; 9router leaves the key in place for the consumer to claim.
- **Why it matters**: The 1-second self-deletion defeats the purpose of the durable fallback relay. The 30s timestamp check in OAuthModal exists precisely to absorb the race where the callback popup finishes before the dashboard's modal has mounted its listeners — but with a 1s self-delete, a dashboard tab that is backgrounded, still fetching providers, or simply slow to mount loses the OAuth code permanently: the user must restart the whole provider-add flow. The failure is intermittent and timing-dependent (fast machines never see it, slow ones or throttled background tabs always do), which makes it hard to diagnose and it manifests as "the OAuth popup closed and nothing happened."
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/callback/page.js:67-70 (setItem with timestamp, no removeItem) and src/shared/components/OAuthModal.js:556-564 (30s window + consumer-side removeItem). OpenProxy: web/src/pages/callback.astro:106-112 (setItem immediately followed by setTimeout removeItem 1000ms) vs web/src/shared/components/OAuthModal.tsx:474-484 (unchanged 30s-window consumer that now finds the key already gone).
- **Parity fix**: Remove the `setTimeout(..., 1000)` + `removeItem` block from web/src/pages/callback.astro:108-111 so the writer leaves the key in place, matching 9router's writer at callback/page.js:67-70. The existing consumer-side cleanup in OAuthModal.tsx:467 (storage event), :482 (mount-time read) already removes the key after consuming it, so no unbounded growth is introduced. Optionally re-order the relays to 9router's sequence (postMessage → BroadcastChannel → localStorage) so a successful fast-path relay is attempted before touching persistent storage.

## P303-001 · P2 · web/src/shared/components/UsageStats.tsx:216-217, 424-432

- **9router**: UsageStats reads `sortBy = searchParams.get("sortBy") || "rawModel"` and `sortOrder = searchParams.get("sortOrder") || "asc"` from the URL, and toggleSort calls `router.replace('?' + params, { scroll: false })` so the chosen column and direction are written back into the query string (9router: `src/shared/components/UsageStats.js:207-208, :310-317`). Sorting survives a reload and a tab switch, and is shareable/bookmarkable.
- **OpenProxy**: Sort state is component-local: `const [sortBy, setSortBy] = useState("rawModel")` and `const [sortOrder, setSortOrder] = useState("asc")`, with toggleSort doing a pure setState (web/src/shared/components/UsageStats.tsx:216-217, :424-432). The URL is never read or written for sortBy/sortOrder.
- **Gap**: Usage-table sorting is not persisted to the URL in OpenProxy, so it is lost on reload, on switching to another usage tab, and cannot be shared via link.
- **Why it matters**: Users who sort the model table by cost to find the expensive entry lose that ordering the moment they hit refresh, switch to the Details tab and back, or open the page in a second tab. In 9router the ?sortBy=cost&sortOrder=desc URL is bookmarkable and survives a reload, so the view the user set up is the view they get back.
- **Evidence**: 9router: src/shared/components/UsageStats.js:207-208 (reads searchParams), :310-317 (router.replace writes sortBy/sortOrder). OpenProxy: web/src/shared/components/UsageStats.tsx:216-217 (useState only), :424-432 (toggleSort is a pure setState).
- **Parity fix**: Read sortBy/sortOrder from window.location.search via the same URLSearchParams shim UsagePageClient already uses (web/src/components/usage/UsagePageClient.tsx:26-35) and have toggleSort write them back with history.replaceState plus a state update, exactly mirroring 9router's searchParams/router.replace.

## P304-001 · P3 · /login — initial autofocus predicate on the password field

- **9router**: The password input uses `autoFocus={!oidcAvailable}` (src/app/login/page.js:228), where `oidcAvailable = isSsoEnabled && activeSsoType === "oidc" && oidcConfigured` (:135). On a SAML-only deployment (activeSsoType === "saml", samlConfigured) oidcAvailable is false, so the password field receives initial focus.
- **OpenProxy**: The same input uses `autoFocus={!ssoAvailable}` (web/src/components/LoginPageClient.tsx:420), where `ssoAvailable = samlAvailable || oidcAvailable` (:150). On a SAML-only deployment ssoAvailable is true, so nothing is auto-focused and the first Tab lands on the 'Sign in with SAML 2.0 Single Sign-On' button.
- **Gap**: The initial-focus predicate on the login password field was changed from !oidcAvailable to !ssoAvailable, so SAML-only deployments autofocus a different element.
- **Why it matters**: Only reachable in the SAML-only configuration, but there the divergence is total: 9router drops the operator straight into the password field, whereas OpenProxy leaves focus on `<body>` so the first keystroke/tab goes to the SSO button. The opening Tab position of the login form differs from 9router.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/login/page.js:135,136,228 || openproxy: web/src/components/LoginPageClient.tsx:148,149,150,420
- **Parity fix**: Change `autoFocus={!ssoAvailable}` to `autoFocus={!oidcAvailable}` at web/src/components/LoginPageClient.tsx:420 to match 9router:228 (the oidcAvailable binding already exists at :149).

## P305-001 · P3 · /login — password field disabled during rate-limit lockout

- **9router**: The password `<Input type="password" ... required autoFocus={!oidcAvailable} />` is never disabled (src/app/login/page.js:225-232); only the submit `<Button type="submit" disabled={retryAfter > 0}>` (:241-244) is. `handleLogin` (:66-91) has no retryAfter guard, so Enter still submits and the server rejects it, refreshing retryAfter from the 429 body (:90).
- **OpenProxy**: The password input carries `disabled={retryAfter > 0}` (web/src/components/LoginPageClient.tsx:410) and `handleLogin` opens with `if (retryAfter > 0) return;` (:170), so during the lockout the field is both unfocusable and unsubmittable.
- **Gap**: During a rate-limit lockout OpenProxy removes the password field from the tab order and swallows Enter, where 9router keeps it focusable and lets Enter resubmit (server-side rejection).
- **Why it matters**: Disabling a focused input drops focus to `<body>`, so the next Tab restarts from the top of the login page rather than from where the user was. Combined with the early return, a keyboard user who mistypes three times loses both the field and the ability to retry without re-navigating. 9router's behaviour keeps the caret and the tab position stable throughout the countdown.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/login/page.js:66,90,225,241 || openproxy: web/src/components/LoginPageClient.tsx:170,410
- **Parity fix**: Remove `disabled={retryAfter > 0}` from the password Input at web/src/components/LoginPageClient.tsx:410 so it stays in the tab order during lockout, and drop the `if (retryAfter > 0) return;` guard at :170 to match 9router's handleLogin (:66-91), which lets the request reach /api/auth/login and refreshes retryAfter from the error response.

## P306-001 · P3 · 9router open-sse/handlers/videoCore.js:39-45 + videoProviders/openrouter.js:11-17  ⟷  openproxy src/server/api/media.rs:1700-1731, 843-856

- **9router**: buildHeaders (videoCore.js:39-45) always sets `Accept: "application/json"` first, then Authorization, then Content-Type and Idempotency-Key only when `method === "POST"` (videoCore.js:107-111) — so a GET poll carries Accept + Authorization and deliberately omits Content-Type and Idempotency-Key. The OpenRouter adapter is consistent: its headers() helper (openrouter.js:11-17) sets `Accept: "application/json"`, and the poll branch (openrouter.js:23-25) returns those headers with no Content-Type.
- **OpenProxy**: build_video_headers (media.rs:1700-1731) delegates the non-vertex case to build_media_headers, which sets only `Content-Type: application/json` and `Authorization` (media.rs:849-850, 884-887); Accept is inserted solely in the vertex branch (media.rs:1712). So the xAI and OpenRouter create requests carry no Accept at all, and the OpenRouter poll (media.rs:1541 → 1560-1563) sends a bodyless GET with `Content-Type: application/json` attached.
- **Gap**: The xAI and OpenRouter video requests omit the `Accept: application/json` header that 9router always sends, and the OpenRouter poll sends a Content-Type header on a bodyless GET that 9router omits.
- **Why it matters**: Header fidelity on the upstream call, per the project's own OpenRouter header-fidelity rules. Dropping Accept removes the signal that the client wants JSON, so an upstream that content-negotiates or returns an HTML error page for an edge case can be returned to the client verbatim — which P1-003 then re-emits untranslated. The stray Content-Type on the GET poll is cosmetic to the providers but is a gratuitous divergence that will show up in any header-level diff.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/handlers/videoCore.js:40 (Accept always), :107-111 (Content-Type/Idempotency-Key POST-only), open-sse/handlers/videoProviders/openrouter.js:13 and :23-25 (Accept on both verbs, no Content-Type on poll). openproxy: src/server/api/media.rs:849-850 and 884-887 (Content-Type + Authorization only), media.rs:1712 (Accept, vertex only), media.rs:1541 + 1560-1563 (poll reuses the create header builder).
- **Parity fix**: Insert `Accept: application/json` in build_media_headers alongside Content-Type (media.rs:850) so the xAI and OpenRouter arms match videoCore.js:40 and openrouter.js:13, and make Content-Type conditional on the request being a POST — thread a `is_create: bool` (or pass the headers in) into build_video_headers from video_create_handler (media.rs:1318) and the poll sites (media.rs:1541, 2303) so build_media_headers omits it for GET, matching videoCore.js:109.

## P307-001 · P3 · 9router open-sse/services/combo.js:208-237; OpenProxy src/core/combo/mod.rs:605-643

- **9router**: getRotatedModels computes `const rotationKey = comboName || "__default__"` (combo.js:213), so a round-robin combo invoked without a name still rotates, using one shared process-wide slot. The sticky counter is stored on the same key (combo.js:216-234), and normalizeStickyLimit clamps a missing or invalid limit to 1 (combo.js:186-189).
- **OpenProxy**: get_rotated_models returns the unrotated list when combo_name is None: `let Some(combo_name) = combo_name else { return models.to_vec(); }` (mod.rs:615-617). Sticky counting only happens for sticky_limit > 1 (mod.rs:630-640); at limit 1 the index is advanced unconditionally (mod.rs:638-640), which matches 9router's nextUseCount(1) >= 1 branch.
- **Gap**: With no combo name supplied, OpenProxy disables round-robin entirely instead of falling back to 9router's shared "__default__" rotation slot.
- **Why it matters**: Any caller that dispatches a round-robin combo without a name (the public execute_combo_strategy helper, web_fetch.rs:168) silently gets fallback semantics. Today the chat path always passes Some(&combo_name), so the blast radius is limited to new/CLI call sites, but the behaviour is inverted relative to 9router and the failure is silent.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/services/combo.js:213 (`comboName || "__default__"`). OpenProxy: src/core/combo/mod.rs:615-617 (`else { return models.to_vec(); }`).
- **Parity fix**: Replace the None early-return with a synthesized key: `let key = combo_name.unwrap_or("__default__");` and index the rotation maps by key, matching combo.js:213.

## P308-001 · P3 · 9router src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/cli-tools/components/ClaudeToolCard.js:441-448  vs  openproxy web/src/components/cli-tools/ClaudeToolCard.tsx:493-504

- **9router**: The "Filter naming" row uses a `sm:grid-cols-[8rem_auto_1fr_auto]` layout where the third column is a single `<label className="flex items-center gap-1.5 cursor-pointer select-none">` containing the checkbox, the text "Filter naming requests", AND the `<Tooltip text="Intercepts Claude Code's topic-naming requests...">` wrapping a `text-[14px] cursor-help` info glyph (ClaudeToolCard.js:445-447). The gap-1.5 therefore spaces the text and the info icon inline, and the icon is part of the label's click target.
- **OpenProxy**: The same row's `<Tooltip text="Intercepts Claude Code's topic-naming requests...">` and info glyph is a SIBLING placed AFTER the closing `</label>` (ClaudeToolCard.tsx:500-504). It therefore lands in the grid's 4th `auto` column rather than sitting inline inside the 3rd column, and on mobile (grid-cols-1) it wraps onto its own line beneath the checkbox instead of sitting beside the label text.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy moved the Filter naming info tooltip outside its label, so the glyph occupies a different grid column and drops to its own row on narrow viewports.
- **Why it matters**: This is the exact pattern 9router uses for the 1M context row immediately above, and OpenProxy still has it correct there (ClaudeToolCard.tsx:494-496) — so within one card the two info glyphs are laid out two different ways. The visible result is a misaligned column: the 1M-context glyph sits inline with its label while the Filter-naming glyph is pushed to a separate column, and on mobile the second one sits alone on a line.
- **Evidence**: 9R: src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/cli-tools/components/ClaudeToolCard.js:441,445,446,447,448. OP: web/src/components/cli-tools/ClaudeToolCard.tsx:493,499,500,501,502,503,504.
- **Parity fix**: Move the `<Tooltip text="Intercepts Claude Code's topic-naming requests and returns a fake response locally, saving API tokens.">` block in web/src/components/cli-tools/ClaudeToolCard.tsx:500-504 back INSIDE the `<label className="flex items-center gap-1.5 cursor-pointer select-none">` (before its closing tag), exactly as 9router ClaudeToolCard.js:445-447 does, so the gap-1.5 spacing and grid column match the 1M context row above it.

## P309-001 · P3 · 9router src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/usage/components/ProviderLimits/index.js:1194-1240  vs  openproxy web/src/components/usage/ProviderLimits/index.tsx:1172-1210

- **9router**: The three quota row action buttons (Refresh quota, Edit connection, Delete connection) are each wrapped in the shared `<Tooltip>` component, which renders a styled dark bubble (`bg-gray-900 text-white text-[11px] leading-snug rounded px-2 py-1 max-w-56 z-50` with a `transition-opacity duration-150` fade on hover) — ProviderLimits/index.js:1194, :1209, :1225. Each button is a fixed `flex h-8 w-8 items-center justify-center rounded-lg` 32x32 box, so the three controls align on a consistent grid.
- **OpenProxy**: All three were downgraded to the native `title=` attribute (`title="Refresh quota"`, `title="Edit connection"`, `title="Delete connection"` — ProviderLimits/index.tsx:1177, :1193, :1206), which renders the OS-native tooltip (delayed roughly 0.5-1s, platform styling, no dark bubble) instead of the immediate styled 11px bubble. The button boxes also changed from `flex h-8 w-8 items-center justify-center rounded-lg` to `p-1.5 rounded-lg` — no fixed width or height, so the controls are sized by their glyph and no longer share a 32px alignment box.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's quota action buttons swapped the custom hover Tooltip for native title= and lost their fixed 32x32 hit boxes.
- **Why it matters**: On the Quota Tracker the Refresh/Edit/Delete trio is the main per-connection action set. Native tooltips appear after a noticeable OS-level delay and in the platform's own style, so the hover affordance feels much slower and visually inconsistent with the other custom tooltips still on the same page (for example View Codex reset credit expiry at :1155, which kept the Tooltip component). The narrower boxes also shift the controls out of alignment with the adjacent 32px Codex-credit button.
- **Evidence**: 9R: src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/usage/components/ProviderLimits/index.js:1194,1195,1196,1209,1210,1211,1225,1226,1227. OP: web/src/components/usage/ProviderLimits/index.tsx:1172,1173,1174,1177,1188,1193,1194,1195,1206,1207,1208.
- **Parity fix**: Wrap each of the three buttons in web/src/components/usage/ProviderLimits/index.tsx (:1172, :1188, :1206) in `<Tooltip text="Refresh quota">` / `<Tooltip text="Edit connection">` / `<Tooltip text="Delete connection">` and drop the title= attributes, matching ProviderLimits/index.js:1194, :1209, :1225. Restore `flex h-8 w-8 items-center justify-center rounded-lg` on each button so the trio realigns to 32px.

## P310-001 · P3 · 9router src/app/api/models/disabled/route.js:23-27 · OpenProxy src/server/api/models_disabled.rs:132-140

- **9router**: The POST guard is `if (!providerAlias || !Array.isArray(ids))` → 400 (`route.js:24`). A well-formed request with an EMPTY `ids: []` array passes the guard; `disableModels` (`repos/disabledModelsRepo.js:21-33`) runs the read-merge-write with nothing to add and the route returns `200 {success: true}`.
- **OpenProxy**: The guard is `if provider_alias.is_empty() || req.ids.is_empty()` (`src/server/api/models_disabled.rs:134`), so `ids: []` returns `400 {error: "providerAlias and ids[] required"}` even though the request is well-formed.
- **Gap**: `POST /api/models/disabled` with an empty `ids` array returns 200 in 9router and 400 in OpenProxy.
- **Why it matters**: A client that computes the delta of models to disable and finds none (e.g. re-disabling an already-disabled set) gets a hard error in OpenProxy instead of a no-op success. No built-in dashboard path sends an empty array, so the blast radius is third-party/automation callers only.
- **Evidence**: 9router `.tmp/9router/src/app/api/models/disabled/route.js:23-27`, `.tmp/9router/src/lib/db/repos/disabledModelsRepo.js:21-33` vs OpenProxy `src/server/api/models_disabled.rs:132-140`
- **Parity fix**: Change the guard to `if provider_alias.is_empty()` (dropping the `req.ids.is_empty()` clause) so an empty array is a successful no-op, matching `route.js:24`.

## P311-001 · P3 · 9router src/app/globals.css:465-475  vs  openproxy web/src/shared/components/styles/global.css:780-790

- **9router**: The dashboard background texture `.landing-grid` is a 40x40px grid drawn with `linear-gradient(to right, var(--color-accent) 1px, transparent 1px)` and the same for bottom, at `opacity: 0.08` in light mode and `0.04` in dark (globals.css:465-475). There is no mask — the grid is uniform across the whole main content area.
- **OpenProxy**: `.landing-grid` is a 32x32px grid (global.css:784) drawn with a fixed `rgba(20, 20, 19, 0.035)` in light and `rgba(250, 249, 245, 0.04)` in dark rather than the accent variable, and adds `mask-image: radial-gradient(ellipse at center, black 30%, transparent 80%)` with the -webkit-mask-image fallback, so the grid fades out toward the edges of the viewport.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's dashboard background grid has a different pitch (32px vs 40px), a different colour source (fixed ink/cream rgba vs the brand accent variable), and an added radial mask that 9router does not have.
- **Why it matters**: The grid is painted on main behind every dashboard page (both layouts render `<div className="landing-grid absolute inset-0 pointer-events-none -z-10" />`). The 8px pitch change is directly visible, and the radial mask means the texture fades to nothing near the viewport edges, so the background reads as a centred vignette in OpenProxy versus an even wash in 9router. Because OpenProxy's header is opaque at lg (see PXL-009) while 9router's is transparent, the grid is also visible in a different region on desktop.
- **Evidence**: 9R: src/app/globals.css:465,469,470,471 and the .dark override at :473-475. OP: web/src/shared/components/styles/global.css:780,781,782,783,784,785,786,787,788,789,790.
- **Parity fix**: Change web/src/shared/components/styles/global.css:780-790 to match 9router: use `background-size: 40px 40px`, draw the lines with `var(--color-accent)` rather than fixed rgba values, set `opacity: 0.08` for the base rule and `0.04` for `.dark`, and delete the two mask-image / -webkit-mask-image declarations.

## P312-001 · P3 · 9router src/app/login/page.js:153  vs  openproxy web/src/components/LoginPageClient.tsx:295,306

- **9router**: The login page shell is `min-h-screen flex items-center justify-center bg-bg p-4` (login/page.js:153) — uniform 16px padding, so the login card is exactly vertically centred at every viewport height.
- **OpenProxy**: Both login shells use `min-h-screen flex items-center justify-center bg-canvas px-4 py-12` (LoginPageClient.tsx:295 and :306) — 16px horizontal but 48px vertical.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's login page uses 48px vertical padding instead of 9router's uniform 16px, changing where the centred card sits on short viewports.
- **Why it matters**: On a short window (a small laptop viewport, or a browser window with devtools open) the extra py-12 consumes vertical space and pushes the centred card up relative to 9router, so the card no longer sits on the visual centre line. On tall viewports the flex centring masks the difference. Cosmetic, but it is the first screen every user sees.
- **Evidence**: 9R: src/app/login/page.js:153. OP: web/src/components/LoginPageClient.tsx:295,306.
- **Parity fix**: Change `px-4 py-12` to `p-4` in both shells at web/src/components/LoginPageClient.tsx:295 and :306, and change bg-canvas to bg-bg for the exact 9router token.

## P313-001 · P3 · 9router src/shared/components/Badge.js:15-19,36  vs  openproxy web/src/shared/components/Badge.tsx:52-54,72

- **9router**: Badge sizes are sm `px-2 py-0.5 text-[10px]`, md `px-2.5 py-1 text-xs` (12px), lg `px-3 py-1.5 text-sm` (14px) (Badge.js:15-19). The base class has no leading-none and no letter-spacing override (:36).
- **OpenProxy**: Badge sizes are sm `px-2 py-0.5 text-[10px] tracking-wide`, md `px-2.5 py-1 text-[11px] tracking-wide`, lg `px-3 py-1.5 text-[13px]` (Badge.tsx:52-54) — 1px smaller at md and lg. The base class adds leading-none (:72), so badge text is vertically centred on the font box rather than using the browser default line-height.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's Badge renders md text 1px smaller and lg text 1px smaller than 9router, adds tracking-wide to sm and md, and adds leading-none to the base class.
- **Why it matters**: Badges carry provider status (active/inactive/none), model capability chips and connection state throughout the providers grid, the provider detail page, the combos page and the model picker. The md badge is the default size and is used heavily, so a 1px reduction plus tracking-wide plus leading-none makes every status pill slightly tighter and shorter than 9router's. Individually sub-pixel, collectively noticeable across a dense provider grid.
- **Evidence**: 9R: src/shared/components/Badge.js:15,16,17,18,19,36. OP: web/src/shared/components/Badge.tsx:52,53,54,72.
- **Parity fix**: In web/src/shared/components/Badge.tsx set sizes to `sm: "px-2 py-0.5 text-[10px]"`, `md: "px-2.5 py-1 text-xs"`, `lg: "px-3 py-1.5 text-sm"` (:52-54) and drop tracking-wide. Remove leading-none from the base class (:72) so the default line-height applies as in 9router. The additional new / beta / code variants are additive and should stay.

## P314-001 · P3 · 9router: open-sse/handlers/embeddingProviders/gemini.js:20-33 · openproxy: src/core/media/embeddings/base.rs:292-325

- **9router**: Each batch element is coerced with JavaScript `String(text)` (gemini.js:24, :31). `String([1,2])` is `"1,2"`, `String([[1,2],[3,4]])` is `"1,2,3,4"` (Array.prototype.toString joins recursively with commas), and an object element yields `"[object Object]"`.
- **OpenProxy**: The Rust match is `Value::String(s) => s.clone(), other => other.to_string()` (base.rs:296-299 and :314-317), and `serde_json::Value`'s `Display` is compact JSON: `[1,2]` becomes `"[1,2]"`, `[[1,2],[3,4]]` becomes `"[[1,2],[3,4]]"`, and an object becomes `{"a":1}`.
- **Gap**: Non-string elements of a Gemini batch `input` are stringified differently: `String([1,2])` is `"1,2"` in 9router but `Value::to_string()` is `"[1,2]"` in OpenProxy.
- **Why it matters**: Token-id arrays are a documented OpenAI embeddings input shape, so a client can legitimately send `input: [[101, 2022], [101, 2003]]`. Against 9router's Gemini arm the tokens reach the model flattened as `101,2022`; against OpenProxy they arrive with JSON punctuation embedded in the text and the model embeds the punctuation as content — a silently different vector, with no error on either side. Rare in practice (most clients send strings), but silent and undetectable.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/handlers/embeddingProviders/gemini.js:20-33 (`String(text)` at :24 and :31) · openproxy: src/core/media/embeddings/base.rs:292-311, :313-325 (`other.to_string()` at :298 and :316)
- **Parity fix**: In `GeminiAdapter::build_body`, replace the catch-all `other.to_string()` with an explicit arm that reproduces JS `String()`: for `Value::Array` join the elements recursively with `,` after applying the same stringification (flattening nested arrays and joining numbers without brackets), for `Value::Null` emit `"null"`, and for objects emit `"[object Object]"`. Add a unit test mirroring the JS semantics for `[1,2]` → `"1,2"`.

## P315-001 · P3 · Add API Key modal — bulk-add result message and single/bulk gating

- **9router**: On success the bulk result line renders `✓ {bulkResult.success} added{bulkResult.failed > 0 ? `, ✗ ${bulkResult.failed} failed` : ""}` (AddApiKeyModal.js:215-218), and the Single/Bulk mode switcher is rendered unconditionally for every provider (…:194-197).
- **OpenProxy**: The result line renders `Added {bulkResult.success}{bulkResult.failed > 0 ? `, ${bulkResult.failed} failed` : ""}` — no ✓/✗ glyphs (AddApiKeyModal.tsx:255-264). The mode switcher is additionally gated behind `supportsBulk = !isOllamaLocal && !isAzure && !isXaiApiKey` (AddApiKeyModal.tsx:67, 216), hiding the Bulk Add tab for those three providers where 9router still shows it.
- **Gap**: Two cosmetic divergences in the same modal: the bulk result string differs, and OpenProxy hides the Bulk Add tab for ollama-local/azure/xai where 9router shows it.
- **Why it matters**: The `supportsBulk` gate is the more substantive half — a user adding several Azure deployment keys or xAI keys loses the batch path 9router offers. The message wording is cosmetic only and is not worth separate remediation.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/providers/[id]/AddApiKeyModal.js:194-197, 215-218. OpenProxy: web/src/components/providers/AddApiKeyModal.tsx:67, 216-239, 255-264
- **Parity fix**: Drop the `supportsBulk` gate so the Single/Bulk switcher renders for every provider as in AddApiKeyModal.js:194-197, and restore the result text to `✓ {success} added` / `, ✗ {failed} failed` (AddApiKeyModal.js:215-218). If the Azure/xAI gate is deliberate, it belongs in `docs/parity-9router.md` as a recorded divergence rather than being an undocumented behavioural fork.

## P316-001 · P3 · Add connection — Bulk Add availability

- **9router**: The Single / Bulk Add switcher is rendered unconditionally for every provider, including Ollama Local, Azure and the xAI API-key mode, and the bulk path parses name|apiKey (or name|apiKey|accountId for Cloudflare, name|pt-... for Qoder) via planBulkAdd.
- **OpenProxy**: The switcher is gated behind supportsBulk = !isOllamaLocal and !isAzure and !isXaiApiKey, and the single-key form is shown whenever !supportsBulk or mode === single.
- **Gap**: Bulk Add is hidden for three provider modes where 9router shows it.
- **Why it matters**: A user adding several Azure deployments or xAI keys must add them one at a time in OpenProxy. This is a capability gap, not a correctness break; the single-key path is unchanged.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/providers/[id]/AddApiKeyModal.js:193-197 (unconditional switcher) and :190-191 (mode state defaulting to single). OpenProxy: web/src/components/providers/AddApiKeyModal.tsx:67 (supportsBulk), :216 (switcher gated), :241 (bulk panel gated), :276 (single form condition).
- **Parity fix**: Drop the supportsBulk gate so the switcher renders for every provider. If the Azure and xAI bulk formats stay unsupported, make the bulk parser accept the same fields 9router accepts rather than hiding the mode.

## P317-001 · P3 · Cursor `x-cursor-timezone` request header (open-sse/utils/cursorChecksum.js → src/core/executor/cursor.rs)

- **9router**: Sends `"x-cursor-timezone": Intl.DateTimeFormat().resolvedOptions().timeZone || "UTC"` (cursorChecksum.js:138) — the host's actual IANA timezone, falling back to `UTC` only when resolution yields nothing.
- **OpenProxy**: Sends `header_map.insert("x-cursor-timezone", HeaderValue::from_static("UTC"))` unconditionally (executor/cursor.rs:1730).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy hardcodes the literal `"UTC"` where 9router sends the resolved system timezone.
- **Why it matters**: `x-cursor-timezone` is part of the Cursor client-fingerprint header bundle emitted alongside the checksum, session id and client key. OpenProxy therefore presents a different client fingerprint to Cursor than 9router does from the same host, and any timezone-correlated behaviour on the Cursor side (quota windows, feature gating) sees a different value.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/utils/cursorChecksum.js:138. OpenProxy: src/core/executor/cursor.rs:1730.
- **Parity fix**: Replace the static value with a runtime-resolved IANA timezone, sourced from the system zone (e.g. a `chrono`/`time` local-offset-to-name lookup, cached once per process), defaulting to `UTC` when it cannot be determined — matching `Intl.DateTimeFormat().resolvedOptions().timeZone || "UTC"`.

## P318-001 · P3 · DashboardLayout — toast live-region semantics

- **9router**: The toast host is a bare `<div className="fixed top-4 right-4 z-[80] ...">` (src/shared/components/layouts/DashboardLayout.js:42) and each toast is a bare `<div>` (:45-68). No role, no aria-live, no aria-atomic anywhere in the file; the only aria on the block is `aria-label="Dismiss notification"` on the dismiss button (:61). Toasts render in the top-right corner, outside reading order, and self-destruct after 5s (8s for errors).
- **OpenProxy**: The same host gained `role="status" aria-live="polite" aria-atomic="false" aria-label="Notifications"` (web/src/shared/components/layouts/DashboardLayout.tsx:79-85) and each toast gained `role={n.type === "error" ? "alert" : undefined} aria-live={n.type === "error" ? "assertive" : undefined}` (:94-95). The dismiss button's aria-label="Dismiss notification" is unchanged (:109).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's toast host became an ARIA live region with per-toast role/aria-live, which the 9router dashboard has no equivalent of.
- **Why it matters**: This is a deviation in OpenProxy's favour, but it is still a behavioural difference in the accessibility tree: an assistive technology now announces 9router-silent toasts, and role="alert"/aria-live="assertive" on error toasts will interrupt whatever the user is currently hearing. A parity audit that treats 9router as canonical should either adopt the live region on both sides or drop it here. Mouse and keyboard behaviour are otherwise unchanged, hence P3.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/shared/components/layouts/DashboardLayout.js:42,45,61 || openproxy: web/src/shared/components/layouts/DashboardLayout.tsx:79,81,82,83,84,94,95,109
- **Parity fix**: To hold behavioural identity with 9router, remove role="status" aria-live="polite" aria-atomic="false" aria-label="Notifications" from web/src/shared/components/layouts/DashboardLayout.tsx:81-84 and the per-toast role/aria-live at :94-95, leaving the container a plain div as in 9router:42-70. If the live region is judged the correct target, port it to 9router instead — but do not let the two ship with different announcement behaviour.

## P319-001 · P3 · Embeddings `dimensions` field coercion

- **9router**: `if (dimensions != null && dimensions !== "") { const dim = Number(dimensions); if (Number.isFinite(dim) && dim > 0) body.dimensions = dim; }` (open-sse/handlers/embeddingProviders/openai.js:23-26). `Number()` coerces, so `"512"` becomes 512 and is forwarded, and a non-integer like `8.5` is forwarded as-is. The same coercion feeds Gemini's `outputDimensionality` (open-sse/handlers/embeddingProviders/gemini.js:18-19).
- **OpenProxy**: `dimensions()` is `self.body.get("dimensions").and_then(|v| v.as_u64()).filter(|&n| n > 0)` (src/core/media/embeddings/base.rs:32-37). `as_u64()` returns `None` for a JSON string and for any non-integer number, so the field is silently dropped. This accessor feeds both the OpenAI-compatible body (base.rs:166-170) and Gemini's `outputDimensionality` (base.rs:291).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy silently drops a `dimensions` value that 9router coerces and forwards.
- **Why it matters**: A client sending `dimensions` as a string — a common shape for form-encoded and query-derived values, and the shape `<input type="number">` produces before `Number()` coercion — gets the provider's default vector size instead of the requested one. Matryoshka truncation and dimension-reduction requests silently return the wrong vector length, which fails downstream at similarity-search or vector-store insert time rather than at the API call.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/handlers/embeddingProviders/openai.js:23-26, open-sse/handlers/embeddingProviders/gemini.js:18-19 | OpenProxy: src/core/media/embeddings/base.rs:32-37, :166-170, :291
- **Parity fix**: Coerce before extracting, mirroring `Number(...)`: in `EmbeddingRequest::dimensions` (src/core/media/embeddings/base.rs:32-37), accept a string or a number, parse it, reject non-finite and non-positive values, and return the resulting float rather than `Option<u64>`. That restores `"512"` to 512 and `8.5` to 8.5 for both the OpenAI and Gemini paths.

## P320-001 · P3 · Embeddings combo-model rejection message

- **9router**: For a bare (slash-less) model string, `parseModelCore` returns `isAlias: true` (open-sse/services/model.js:44-50), so `getModelInfo` checks for a combo first and returns `{ provider: null, model }` when one matches (src/sse/services/model.js:71-76). `handleEmbeddings` then hits `if (!modelInfo.provider) return errorResponse(400, "Invalid model format")` (src/sse/handlers/embeddings.js:79-82).
- **OpenProxy**: `get_model_info` classifies a bare combo name as `ModelRouteKind::Combo`, and the handler returns `json_error_response(StatusCode::BAD_REQUEST, &format!("Combos not supported for {}", route_kind))` (src/server/api/media.rs:487-491), i.e. 400 with `Combos not supported for embeddings`.
- **Gap**: Both reject a combo with 400, but the message text differs.
- **Why it matters**: Status and shape agree, so no client branches differently. The message only surfaces in the dashboard Example card's error line (web/src/components/media-providers/EmbeddingExampleCard.tsx:79), where "Invalid model format" is less actionable than OpenProxy's "Combos not supported for embeddings". Recorded for completeness; no routing or compatibility impact.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/sse/handlers/embeddings.js:79-82, open-sse/services/model.js:44-50, :71-76 | OpenProxy: src/server/api/media.rs:487-491
- **Parity fix**: Either align the text with 9router (`Invalid model format`) or, since OpenProxy's wording is strictly more informative, keep it and note the intentional divergence in docs/parity-9router.md alongside the existing embeddings entry at line 26.

## P321-001 · P3 · GET /api/tags — Ollama model list payload

- **9router**: `GET /api/tags` returns `ollamaModels` verbatim — `.tmp/9router/src/app/api/tags/route.js:16-21` — i.e. whatever `open-sse/config/ollamaModels.js` holds, with `OPTIONS` returning 200 (not 204) and CORS headers on both methods (`:6-13`).
- **OpenProxy**: Returns a hardcoded two-entry fixture: llama3.2 and qwen2.5, with fixed timestamps, sizes and digests — `src/server/api/tags.rs:11-46`. The body shape (`{models:[{name, modified_at, size, digest, details:{format,family,parameter_size,quantization_level}}]}`) matches Ollama's `/api/tags`, but the contents are static test data. OPTIONS returns 204 (`:7` + `cors::*`).
- **Gap**: The response is a two-model static fixture rather than the live Ollama catalog, and the OPTIONS status is 204 rather than 200.
- **Why it matters**: Low impact: this is an Ollama-native discovery shim, and no in-tree consumer reads it (neither dashboard fetches `/api/tags`; the OpenProxy route list includes it only for protocol completeness). The shape is correct, so a client that just probes for Ollama compatibility gets a valid answer. The 204-vs-200 difference on preflight is within normal CORS tolerance and is cosmetic.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/tags/route.js:6-13 (OPTIONS → 200 + CORS), :16-21 (GET returns live ollamaModels). OpenProxy: src/server/api/tags.rs:7 (Router/OPTIONS), :11-46 (hardcoded llama3.2 + qwen2.5 fixture).
- **Parity fix**: Replace the hardcoded body in `get_tags` (`src/server/api/tags.rs:11`) with the contents of the Ollama model catalog OpenProxy already ships (the same source `catalog.rs` reads, or the Ollama-native list from `core/config/ollama_models.rs`), and return 200 rather than 204 from the preflight if strict parity is wanted. Neither is blocking — no client in either codebase reads this route.

## P322-001 · P3 · Header right-hand action rail order

- **9router**: `src/shared/components/Header.js:305-330` renders, in JSX order: the OIDC/SAML identity chip (`:306-317`), `<HeaderSearch />` (`:318`), the Donate button (`:319-326`), `<ThemeToggle />` (`:327`), `<HeaderLanguage />` (`:328`), `<HeaderMenu />` (`:329`).
- **OpenProxy**: `web/src/shared/components/Header.tsx:322-349` renders: `<HeaderSearchInput />` (`:323`), the identity chip (`:324-337`), the Donate button (`:338-346`), `<ThemeToggle />` (`:347`), `<HeaderMenu />` (`:348`).
- **Gap**: The identity chip and the search input are swapped, and the language button between the theme toggle and the overflow menu is missing (covered separately as IA-015).
- **Why it matters**: Cosmetic ordering difference in the one horizontal control strip present on every dashboard page; the two controls are visually adjacent and functionally unrelated, so the impact is limited to a one-frame visual mismatch.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/shared/components/Header.js:305-330 | OpenProxy: web/src/shared/components/Header.tsx:322-349
