## P323-001 · P3 · OpenProxy `src/server/api/mod.rs:1800,1866`

- **9router**: `src/app/api/keys/[id]/route.js:15` returns `{"error":"Key not found"}` on 404 and `:53` returns `{"message":"Key deleted successfully"}` on delete. The 400 from `src/app/api/keys/route.js:25` is `{"error":"Name is required"}`.
- **OpenProxy**: `src/server/api/mod.rs:1800` returns `{"error":"API key not found"}` on 404 and `:1866` returns `{"success":true,"message":"API key deleted"}`. The create 400 at `:1501` is `{"error":"Name is required"}`, which does match.
- **Gap**: The key-management API's 404 and delete-success payloads use different strings (and the delete adds a `success` boolean) between the two products.
- **Why it matters**: Cosmetic: the HTTP status codes agree, so client control flow is unaffected, but anything that keys off the message text or expects exactly the fields 9router returns sees a different body.
- **Evidence**: 9router: `src/app/api/keys/[id]/route.js:15,53`, `src/app/api/keys/route.js:25`. OpenProxy: `src/server/api/mod.rs:1800,1866`, `:1501`
- **Parity fix**: Use `{"error":"Key not found"}` at `src/server/api/mod.rs:1800` and `{"message":"Key deleted successfully"}` (drop the `success` field) at `:1866`.

## P324-001 · P3 · POST /api/providers/{id}/test — kimi expired-token path

- **9router**: Both `kimi` and `kimi-coding` declare `{ checkExpiry: true, refreshable: true }`. Because `config.refreshable && tokenExpired && connection.refreshToken` is true, the flow calls `refreshOAuthToken`; that function has no kimi branch and falls through to `return null` (:310), producing `{ valid: false, error: "Token expired and refresh failed" }` (:343).
- **OpenProxy**: `is_refreshable_provider` lists only `claude, codex, gemini-cli, antigravity, qwen, kiro, cline`, so the `if is_refreshable_provider(...) && token_expired` block at :221 is skipped entirely for kimi; `is_check_expiry_provider` then hits :254-256 and returns `invalid("Token expired")`. No refresh is ever attempted.
- **Gap**: Different error string and a different number of attempts: 9router attempts a refresh and reports "Token expired and refresh failed"; OpenProxy reports "Token expired" without trying.
- **Why it matters**: Low impact — both end invalid — but the message shown on the connection row differs, and if a kimi refresh endpoint is ever added, OpenProxy's dead branch means it will not be exercised by the Test button.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/api/providers/[id]/test/testUtils.js:77-78 (checkExpiry+refreshable for kimi/kimi-coding), :336-345 (refresh attempt then the "refresh failed" message), :310 (no kimi branch in `refreshOAuthToken`). OpenProxy: src/server/api/provider_connection_test.rs:1928-1933 (`is_refreshable_provider`), :1935-1940 (`is_check_expiry_provider`), :221-242 (the skipped block), :254-256
- **Parity fix**: Add `"kimi" | "kimi-coding"` to `is_refreshable_provider` and have `refresh_oauth_token` return `Err("Token expired and refresh failed")`-equivalent for them, so the message at :235 matches 9router. If kimi refresh is genuinely unsupported, change the 9router-facing message at :255 to "Token expired and refresh failed" for kimi only — but do not add the refreshable entry without a refresh implementation, or the block becomes dead code.

## P325-001 · P3 · Provider status machine — connection row auth classification

- **9router**: `const rowAuthType = connection.authType || (isOAuth ? "oauth" : "apikey")` is compared case-sensitively: `isOAuthConnection = rowAuthType === "oauth"`, `isCookieConnection = rowAuthType === "cookie"`. Anything else (including `"OAuth"`, `"OAUTH"` and `"device-code"`) renders the key icon and the "API Key" label.
- **OpenProxy**: `const rowAuthType = (connection.authType || (isOAuth ? "oauth" : "apikey")).toLowerCase(); const isRowOAuth = rowAuthType === "oauth" || rowAuthType === "device-code";` — case-insensitive, and `device-code` is an additional OAuth kind.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy recognises two auth-kind spellings that 9router does not, and normalises case.
- **Why it matters**: Cosmetic only: the same persisted connection renders as `key`/`API Key` in 9router and `lock`/`OAuth` in OpenProxy when `authType` carries different casing or the `device-code` value. Routing is unaffected.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/providers/[id]/ConnectionRow.js:76-79. OpenProxy: web/src/components/providers/ConnectionRow.tsx:103-107.
- **Parity fix**: Either match 9router's exact case-sensitive comparison, or (if `device-code` is a real persisted value in OpenProxy) normalise BOTH sides to `.toLowerCase()` and add `device-code` to 9router's predicate so the two render identically.

## P326-001 · P3 · Providers list — enable/disable a provider's connections

- **9router**: handleToggleProvider optimistically flips isActive on every matching connection, fires Promise.allSettled of PUTs, and returns without inspecting the result. If a PUT fails the card keeps the optimistic value until the next unrelated refetch, with no error surfaced.
- **OpenProxy**: handleToggleProvider optimistically flips isActive, then inspects the settled results, collects the failed ids, and rolls back only those rows before re-syncing with the server and raising an error notification.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy reverts a failed enable/disable and reports it; 9router leaves the optimistic value in place.
- **Why it matters**: This is a divergence where OpenProxy preserves the server's state and 9router does not. It is a reliability improvement rather than a regression, but it is a behavioural difference: on a session-expiry 401, 9router shows the provider as enabled while it is still disabled server-side, whereas OpenProxy shows it correctly disabled. Flagged for parity completeness only.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/providers/page.js:222-239 (optimistic update plus allSettled with no result inspection). OpenProxy: web/src/components/providers/ProvidersPageClient.tsx:346-380 (settled inspection, failedIds rollback, re-sync, error notification).
- **Parity fix**: To restore 9router behaviour exactly, drop the settled-result inspection and keep the optimistic update unconditional. Recommended only if strict behavioural identity is required; otherwise keep the OpenProxy version, which is strictly safer and changes no server-side state.

## P327-001 · P3 · Qoder prompt-size estimate feeding context-tier selection (open-sse/shared/qoder/contextTier.js → src/core/executor/qoder.rs)

- **9router**: `estimateQoderPromptTokens` serialises `{system, messages, tools}` and computes `Math.ceil(cjk + (text.length - cjk) / 4)`, where `text.length` is the JavaScript UTF-16 code-unit count (contextTier.js:77-85). Surrogate-pair characters (emoji, astral CJK) therefore count as 2.
- **OpenProxy**: `estimate_qoder_prompt_tokens` computes `(cjk * 4 + rest).div_ceil(4)` where `rest = text.chars().count() - cjk`, i.e. Rust Unicode scalar values, counting each astral character as 1 (qoder.rs:1115-1125). The surrounding arithmetic is algebraically identical to 9router's, so the only difference is the unit of string length.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy counts Unicode scalar values where 9router counts UTF-16 code units, underestimating the Qoder prompt size for content containing astral-plane characters.
- **Why it matters**: The estimate is compared against the model's current context limit (with 15% headroom) to decide whether to escalate to a larger advertised tier; an underestimate can leave a request on a tier the upstream rejects. Measured: a prompt of 600 ASCII chars plus 200 emoji estimates 550 tokens in 9router versus 500 in OpenProxy (9.1% low). Realistic emoji density is well below the ~7% needed to defeat the 15% headroom, so this only bites on emoji-dense prompts near a tier boundary.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/shared/qoder/contextTier.js:77-85 (estimateQoderPromptTokens). OpenProxy: src/core/executor/qoder.rs:1115-1125 (estimate_qoder_prompt_tokens). Divergence magnitude measured by executing both formulas on identical input.
- **Parity fix**: Count UTF-16 code units instead of scalar values: replace `text.chars().count()` with a sum that adds 2 for characters in the astral planes (e.g. `c.len_utf16() as u64`) so `rest` matches JavaScript's `String.prototype.length`. Keep the CJK count and the `(cjk * 4 + rest).div_ceil(4)` shape, which already matches 9router algebraically.

## P328-001 · P3 · Quota page — 'Expiring first' sort toggle pressed state

- **9router**: `<button type="button" onClick={() => setExpiringFirst((prev) => !prev)} aria-pressed={expiringFirst} title="Sort accounts by earliest quota reset time">` (ProviderLimits/index.js:958-970), so assistive tech announces the on/off state of the sort toggle.
- **OpenProxy**: Identical button and identical `title`, but the `aria-pressed={expiringFirst}` attribute is absent (web/src/components/usage/ProviderLimits/index.tsx:948-956). The expiringFirst state and the sort that consumes it are present and correct (:169, :727, :734), so only the announcement is lost.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy's 'Expiring first' toggle is missing the aria-pressed state that 9router exposes.
- **Why it matters**: This is a two-state toggle button. Without aria-pressed a screen reader announces only 'Sort accounts by earliest quota reset time, button' with no indication that the sort is currently active, so a keyboard/AT user cannot tell why the row order is what it is or how to turn it off.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/usage/components/ProviderLimits/index.js:958,960,969 || openproxy: web/src/components/usage/ProviderLimits/index.tsx:948,950,955
- **Parity fix**: Insert `aria-pressed={expiringFirst}` on the `<button>` at web/src/components/usage/ProviderLimits/index.tsx:948, immediately after `type="button"`, matching 9router:960.

## P329-001 · P3 · Quota page — per-connection action buttons' accessible names

- **9router**: Every icon-only row action carries an aria-label: auto-ping `aria-label="Toggle auto-ping"` (ProviderLimits/index.js:1187), `aria-label="Refresh quota"` (:1199), `aria-label="Edit connection"` (:1217), `aria-label="Delete connection"` (:1230). Each is additionally wrapped in a `<Tooltip>` supplying the same text.
- **OpenProxy**: The same four buttons are present but name themselves only via `title`: the auto-ping bolt has `title="Toggle auto-ping warmup"` (web/src/components/usage/ProviderLimits/index.tsx:1108) and no aria-label; refresh (:1175), edit (:1191) and delete (:1202) likewise use title only, and the three lost their `<Tooltip>` wrappers that 9router has. The reset-credit buttons a few lines up do keep aria-label (:1134, :1160), showing the omission is inconsistent within the same row.
- **Gap**: The auto-ping / refresh / edit / delete quota-row buttons use title instead of aria-label in OpenProxy.
- **Why it matters**: `title` is a weak accessible-name source: it is not announced by several screen-reader/browser combinations, is suppressed while typing, and most browsers do not show it on keyboard focus (only on hover). A screen-reader user hears four indistinguishable 'button' entries per connection row. `title` still renders a tooltip on hover, so mouse users see no change.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/usage/components/ProviderLimits/index.js:1187,1199,1217,1230 || openproxy: web/src/components/usage/ProviderLimits/index.tsx:1108,1175,1191,1202 (contrast :1134,1160 which do use aria-label)
- **Parity fix**: Add aria-label="Toggle auto-ping", aria-label="Refresh quota", aria-label="Edit connection" and aria-label="Delete connection" to web/src/components/usage/ProviderLimits/index.tsx:1104, :1170, :1183 and :1197 respectively (keeping title alongside, exactly as 9router does), and restore the `<Tooltip>` wrappers.

## P330-001 · P3 · SSE response headers on the streamed path

- **9router**: Every client-facing SSE response uses `SSE_HEADERS_CORS` (open-sse/utils/sseConstants.js:19-24), which carries exactly four headers: `Content-Type: text/event-stream`, `Cache-Control: no-cache`, `Connection: keep-alive`, `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`. It does NOT emit `X-Accel-Buffering`.
- **OpenProxy**: The streaming path inserts four headers — `Connection: keep-alive`, `X-Accel-Buffering: no`, `Cache-Control: no-cache`, `Content-Type: text/event-stream` (src/server/api/chat.rs:4114-4125) — but does NOT apply `with_cors_response` to the streamed body; the only `with_cors_response` call in chat.rs is at line 3506, inside the non-streaming `proxy_sse_to_json_response`. The outer `chat_completions` handler does wrap the whole thing (`with_cors_response(chat_completions_for_endpoint(...))` at src/server/api/chat.rs:135-139), so CORS is present on the `/v1/chat/completions` entry but NOT necessarily on the `/v1/messages` and `/v1/responses` entry points, which reach the same streaming code through `forward_compat` → `convert_to_responses_api` / `convert_to_messages_api` (those two DO call `with_cors_response`, src/server/api/compat.rs:138,140 — so the gap is closed there, but by a different code path than 9Router's single header table).
- **Gap**: OpenProxy adds `X-Accel-Buffering: no` to client-facing SSE (9Router reserves that for the internal no-buffer variant) and assembles the header set inline rather than from 9Router's single table.
- **Why it matters**: Cosmetic on the wire; the functional risk is that CORS coverage for the streamed path depends on each of the three entry points remembering to wrap the response, whereas 9Router's `SSE_HEADERS_CORS` makes the CORS origin unconditional at the point the stream is created. A future SSE entry point that forgets the wrapper silently drops CORS.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/utils/sseConstants.js:19-24 (SSE_HEADERS_CORS), open-sse/utils/sseConstants.js:12-17 (SSE_HEADERS_NO_BUFFER carries X-Accel-Buffering), open-sse/handlers/chatCore/streamingHandler.js:103-105 (`new Response(transformedBody, { headers: SSE_HEADERS })`). openproxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:4114-4125 (four inline headers incl. X-Accel-Buffering), src/server/api/chat.rs:3506 (only with_cors_response in chat.rs), src/server/api/chat.rs:135-139 (outer wrapper), src/server/api/compat.rs:138,140 (per-mode wrappers).
- **Parity fix**: Introduce an `SSE_HEADERS_CORS` equivalent (a `const` of the four 9Router headers) in `src/server/api/cors.rs` and have the streaming response builder insert it in one place, replacing the inline `Connection`/`X-Accel-Buffering`/`Cache-Control`/`Content-Type` block — dropping `X-Accel-Buffering` from the client-facing variant to match 9Router, and keeping it only on the internal no-buffer path if one exists.

## P331-001 · P3 · Shared Modal — closeOnOverlay default

- **9router**: `closeOnOverlay = true` is the default (src/shared/components/Modal.js:15), wired to the overlay `onClick` at :51. No call site in the codebase passes `closeOnOverlay`, so all 39 `<Modal>` mounts close on an outside click.
- **OpenProxy**: `closeOnOverlay = false` is the default (web/src/shared/components/Modal.tsx:27), same overlay wiring at :64, and no call site overrides it — so all 40 `<Modal>` mounts now require the X button or Escape.
- **Gap**: The shared Modal's default closeOnOverlay was flipped from true to false, changing the dismissal contract for every modal in the app.
- **Why it matters**: Escape still works in both, so keyboard users are unaffected, but pointer users lose the click-outside dismissal they have in 9router for all ~40 modals — including every ConfirmModal, every provider/combo form, and ModelSelectModal, where a stray click outside used to back out of a half-filled form.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/shared/components/Modal.js:15,51 || openproxy: web/src/shared/components/Modal.tsx:27,64 (no override at any of the 40 mount sites in either)
- **Parity fix**: Change the default in web/src/shared/components/Modal.tsx:27 from `closeOnOverlay = false` to `closeOnOverlay = true` so the overlay click handler at :64 is armed by default, matching 9router:15.

## P332-001 · P3 · Shared Select — new `searchable` prop adds a second, unlinked text input

- **9router**: `Select` renders exactly one focusable control: a native `<select>` (src/shared/components/Select.js:19-55). The provider picker on /dashboard/providers/new is that single control (:96-106), so the whole 'choose a provider' interaction is one tab stop navigated with the platform select keys.
- **OpenProxy**: `Select` gained a `searchable` prop that renders an extra filter `<input type="text" aria-label={searchPlaceholder}>` above the `<select>` (web/src/shared/components/Select.tsx:73-94). The filter only narrows the select's `<option>` list (:46-63) and has no onKeyDown: typing in it never moves focus or selection to the select, and Enter does nothing. It is enabled on exactly one call site — the provider picker at web/src/components/ProvidersNewPageClient.tsx:285.
- **Gap**: The shared Select grew an opt-in `searchable` mode that inserts a second text input which is not keyboard-linked to the native select it filters.
- **Why it matters**: On /dashboard/providers/new the provider picker is now two tab stops instead of one, and the first is a plain text field whose only effect is mutating a list the user has not yet focused. A keyboard user who tabs in expecting to pick a provider lands in a text box where Enter and the arrow keys do nothing; they must Tab again to reach the select. 9router's single native select needs no such extra stop.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/shared/components/Select.js:19,28,51 + .tmp/9router/src/app/(dashboard)/dashboard/providers/new/page.js:96 || openproxy: web/src/shared/components/Select.tsx:24,40,73,78,91,96 + web/src/components/ProvidersNewPageClient.tsx:285
- **Parity fix**: Drop the `searchable` prop from web/src/shared/components/Select.tsx (:24, :40, :47, :54, :59, :63, :73-94, :124-128) and remove `searchable` / `searchPlaceholder` from the provider Select at web/src/components/ProvidersNewPageClient.tsx:285-286, restoring the single native `<select>` that 9router renders. If the filter is kept, it at minimum needs an onKeyDown that forwards Enter/ArrowDown to the select.

## P333-001 · P3 · Sidebar active-section highlight on first paint

- **9router**: `src/shared/components/Sidebar.js:43` reads `const pathname = usePathname()` which Next.js resolves before the first client render, so `isActive` (`:71-76`) returns the correct section on the very first paint. `src/shared/components/Header.js:183` does the same for `getPageInfo`.
- **OpenProxy**: `web/src/shared/components/Sidebar.tsx:95-101` initialises `pathname` to `""` and only fills it in a `useEffect` after paint; `isActive` (`:134-139`) therefore matches nothing on the first frame. `web/src/shared/components/Header.tsx:196-205` has the identical `useState("")` + `useEffect` pattern, so the page title is also blank for one paint.
- **Gap**: The pathname used for nav highlighting and page-title resolution is empty until after the first paint.
- **Why it matters**: Because every `.astro` page mounts the shell with `client:only="react"`, the whole sidebar is client-rendered anyway; this adds a visible one-frame flash where no nav row is highlighted and the header has no title.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/src/shared/components/Sidebar.js:43, :71-76; .tmp/9router/src/shared/components/Header.js:183 | OpenProxy: web/src/shared/components/Sidebar.tsx:95-101, :134-139; web/src/shared/components/Header.tsx:196-205
- **Parity fix**: Derive the pathname lazily at state initialisation instead of in an effect — `useState(() => typeof window !== "undefined" ? window.location.pathname : "")` — in `web/src/shared/components/Sidebar.tsx:95` and `web/src/shared/components/Header.tsx:196`, so the first render already has the correct value.

## P334-001 · P3 · Zed capability-negotiation header values (open-sse/executors/zed.js → src/core/executor/zed.rs)

- **9router**: Sends `x-zed-client-supports-status-messages: "true"` and `x-zed-client-supports-stream-ended-request-completion-status: "true"` (executors/zed.js:254-255); the models fetch sends `x-zed-client-supports-x-ai: "true"` (src/app/api/v1/models/route.js, via `ZED_HEADERS.clientSupportsXai`).
- **OpenProxy**: Sends all three as `HeaderValue::from_static("1")` (zed.rs:368-375).
- **Gap**: The boolean capability headers use the string `"1"` on the OpenProxy side and the string `"true"` on the 9router side.
- **Why it matters**: These headers opt the client into Zed's status-message and stream-completion framing. If Zed parses them with a strict boolean parser, `"1"` reads as false and OpenProxy would not receive the terminal status frames it expects. The header names match exactly, so the divergence is confined to the encoded value; impact is server-dependent and unverified against the live API.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/executors/zed.js:254-255; .tmp/9router/open-sse/shared/zedAuth.js:19-28 (ZED_HEADERS). OpenProxy: src/core/executor/zed.rs:368-375.
- **Parity fix**: Change the three `HeaderValue::from_static("1")` values in `ZedExecutor::post_completions` to `HeaderValue::from_static("true")` to match 9router's encoding.

## P335-001 · P3 · open-sse/config/codexInstructions.js:90,109,115,116 vs src/core/executor/codex.rs:368

- **9router**: open-sse/config/codexInstructions.js:90 `...follow final‑answer formatting.`, :109 `...active voice; self‑contained; no ...`, :115 `...absolute, workspace‑relative, a/ or b/ diff prefixes...`, :116 `...line/column (1‑based)...`. All four use U+2011 NON-BREAKING HYPHEN. The exported `CODEX_DEFAULT_INSTRUCTIONS` value is 11,692 bytes.
- **OpenProxy**: src/core/executor/codex.rs:368 — `const DEFAULT_CODEX_INSTRUCTIONS: &'static str = \"...follow final-answer formatting... active voice; self-contained; ... workspace-relative, ... line/column (1-based)...\"`. All four are ASCII U+002D HYPHEN-MINUS; the file contains zero U+2011 bytes. This inline literal is the only value the executor ever uses (src/core/executor/codex.rs:409). A second, byte-identical-to-9Router copy DOES exist at src/core/config/codex_instructions.rs:7 (`include_str!("data/codex_default_instructions.txt")`) and its data file does carry the 4 U+2011 bytes — but that module is dead: the only references to it are its own `pub mod codex_instructions;` declaration (src/core/config/mod.rs:9) and a doc comment.
- **Gap**: OpenProxy holds two divergent copies of the same default-instructions constant: the one that is actually sent (ASCII hyphens) differs from 9Router in 4 characters, while the copy that is byte-identical to 9Router is never read.
- **Why it matters**: The bytes on the wire differ from the 9Router baseline, so a request-body diff or an upstream prompt cache keyed on `instructions` will never match across the two proxies, and the near-duplicate constant means any future re-sync of the prompt will land in the dead `codex_instructions.rs` copy and silently have no effect. The prompt semantics are unaffected — U+2011 and U+002D render identically and carry the same meaning to the model.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/config/codexInstructions.js:90,109,115,116 (U+2011). OpenProxy: src/core/executor/codex.rs:368 (U+002D, live) and src/core/config/data/codex_default_instructions.txt (U+2011, correct but unreferenced) via src/core/config/codex_instructions.rs:7. Verified by evaluating the JS export and the Rust literal and comparing: JS === data-file text (11,692 bytes, exact match) while JS !== codex.rs inline literal, differing on exactly those 4 lines; `grep -c $'‑'` returns 4 for the data file and 0 for codex.rs.
- **Parity fix**: Delete the duplicated inline literal at src/core/executor/codex.rs:368 and have the executor reference the existing verbatim copy instead — `use crate::core::config::codex_instructions::CODEX_DEFAULT_INSTRUCTIONS;` — then update the three test references (src/core/executor/codex.rs:1011,1183) to the imported name. That collapses the two copies into one, makes the live payload byte-identical to 9Router again, and puts future prompt re-syncs on the single source of truth. Alternatively, if the inline literal is deliberately kept, re-copy the four U+2011 characters into it.

## P336-001 · P3 · open-sse/executors/zed.js:253-259 vs src/core/executor/zed.rs:360-375

- **9router**: The `/completions` fetch sets `Accept: "application/x-ndjson, text/event-stream, */*"`, `User-Agent: "9router/zed"`, `X-Version` (as `x-zed-version: "0.200.0"`), plus the two client-capability headers (zed.js:253-259).
- **OpenProxy**: `post_completions` sets only `content-type`, `Authorization`, `x-zed-client-supports-status-messages`, `x-zed-client-supports-stream-ended-request-completion-status` and an extra `x-zed-client-supports-x-ai` (zed.rs:360-375). No `Accept`, no `User-Agent`, no `x-zed-version`.
- **Gap**: Three request headers present on every 9router Zed call are absent: the NDJSON `Accept`, the `User-Agent`, and `x-zed-version`.
- **Why it matters**: `Accept: application/x-ndjson, …` is the content negotiation that makes Zed answer with the NDJSON stream `line_to_sse` parses; without it the request falls back to reqwest's default `*/*` and Zed may pick a different representation, which would break the line parser. `x-zed-version` is part of the client fingerprint Zed validates.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/executors/zed.js:253-259. OpenProxy: src/core/executor/zed.rs:360-375.
- **Parity fix**: Add `Accept: application/x-ndjson, text/event-stream, */*`, `User-Agent: 9router/zed` and `x-zed-version: 0.200.0` (configurable) to the header map in `post_completions`.

## P337-001 · P3 · open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:238-242 vs src/server/api/chat.rs:1323 vs :1415-1423

- **9router**: The TTS tool-message strip runs BEFORE the token savers: `if (getModelType(alias, model) === "tts" && translatedBody.messages) { translatedBody.messages = translatedBody.messages.filter(msg => msg.role !== "tool"); delete translatedBody.tools; }` at open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:238-242, then compressMessages runs at :258 on the already-stripped body.
- **OpenProxy**: The same strip runs LAST, after every saver: compress_messages at src/server/api/chat.rs:1323, headroom at :1367, caveman/ponytail at :1390, and only then `if is_tts_request(...) { msgs.retain(...); obj.remove("tools"); }` at src/server/api/chat.rs:1415-1423.
- **Gap**: For TTS models OpenProxy compresses (and bills) tool content that it deletes two steps later, so the recorded compression stats for TTS requests are non-zero where 9Router records none.
- **Why it matters**: Only affects the compression metrics and log line for TTS-model requests (the tool messages are discarded either way), so the impact is observability rather than request content — but the saved-bytes figure reported to the user differs from 9Router.
- **Evidence**: 9router open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:238-242 vs :258; OpenProxy src/server/api/chat.rs:1323 (compress) vs :1415-1423 (strip)
- **Parity fix**: Move the is_tts_request strip above step 4 in src/server/api/chat.rs so the TTS tool-message removal precedes compress_messages, matching the order at open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:238-242 vs :258.

## P338-001 · P3 · open-sse/rtk/systemInject.js:75-79 and :223 vs src/core/rtk/mod.rs:400-413 and :299-305

- **9router**: Prompt-injection idempotency is exact and segment-scoped: `function hasPrompt(haystack, prompt) { if (haystack === prompt) return true; return haystack.split(SEP).includes(prompt); }` (open-sse/rtk/systemInject.js:75-79), and the Claude block check is equality: `if (sys.some(b => b && b.text === prompt)) return;` (open-sse/rtk/systemInject.js:223).
- **OpenProxy**: Both are substring tests: `if content.contains(prompt) { return false; }` in append_prompt_text (src/core/rtk/mod.rs:401) and `part_text(block).is_some_and(|text| text.contains(prompt))` in inject_claude_system_blocks (src/core/rtk/mod.rs:302); the same code is duplicated in src/core/translator/ponytail.rs.
- **Gap**: Prompt-injection idempotency is substring-based in OpenProxy but exact-segment-based in 9Router, so an existing system prompt that merely mentions the injection text suppresses re-injection in OpenProxy only.
- **Why it matters**: When a client or an earlier hop already put the caveman/ponytail text inside a larger sentence, 9Router still appends the canonical block while OpenProxy silently does nothing — the style directive reaches the model through one router and not the other.
- **Evidence**: 9router open-sse/rtk/systemInject.js:75-79 and :223; OpenProxy src/core/rtk/mod.rs:401 and :302, duplicated at src/core/translator/ponytail.rs
- **Parity fix**: Use the 9Router predicate: split on PROMPT_SEPARATOR and test segment equality (`content.split("\n\n").any(|s| s == prompt)`) instead of `contains`, and compare Claude block text with `==` rather than `.contains(...)`.

## P339-001 · P3 · open-sse/shared/qoder/attachments.js:275-292 vs src/core/executor/qoder.rs:1249-1284,993-998

- **9router**: `stripRemainingDataUris` — the over-budget safety net — replaces EVERY remaining data-URI in string content and in text blocks with `stubText({bytes: m.length, reason:"payload over Qoder size budget"})`, unconditionally (attachments.js:275-292). The `image_url` arm likewise always converts a `data:` URL to a stub (attachments.js:281-286).
- **OpenProxy**: The over-budget pass (qoder.rs:993-998) calls `strip_qoder_message_data_uris`, which routes string content and text blocks through `strip_qoder_data_uris` (qoder.rs:1256-1258, :1278-1284). That helper only replaces a data-URI whose decoded size exceeds `QODER_INLINE_FALLBACK_MAX_BYTES` (512 KB) and emits the reason "inlined data URI stripped from Qoder context" (qoder.rs:854-877). The `image_url` arm does match (qoder.rs:1264-1275).
- **Gap**: In the over-budget pass OpenProxy keeps every data-URI at or below 512 KB, whereas 9router strips them all.
- **Why it matters**: A payload that exceeded the 6 MB `QODER_MAX_PAYLOAD_BYTES` budget can still exceed it after OpenProxy's "strip" pass, so the request goes upstream and is rejected with 413 — the safety net does not fire for the common case of many medium images.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/shared/qoder/attachments.js:275-292. OpenProxy: src/core/executor/qoder.rs:993-998, :1249-1284, :854-877.
- **Parity fix**: Give `strip_qoder_message_data_uris` its own unconditional replacement path (reason "payload over Qoder size budget", `m.length()` as the byte count) rather than reusing the size-gated `strip_qoder_data_uris` helper.

## P340-001 · P3 · openproxy: src/core/executor/codex.rs:368 (const DEFAULT_CODEX_INSTRUCTIONS) | 9router: open-sse/config/codexInstructions.js:90, 109, 115, 116

- **9router**: codexInstructions.js contains four U+2011 NON-BREAKING HYPHEN characters in CODEX_DEFAULT_INSTRUCTIONS: line 90 "follow final‑answer formatting", line 109 "self‑contained", line 115 "workspace‑relative", line 116 "1‑based". These are literal source bytes.
- **OpenProxy**: The inlined DEFAULT_CODEX_INSTRUCTIONS at codex.rs:368 (the copy actually used by the executor) uses ASCII U+002D HYPHEN-MINUS in all four positions: "follow final-answer formatting", "self-contained", "workspace-relative", "1-based". Confirmed the string has zero U+2011 code points. Notably, the project's own data file src/core/config/data/codex_default_instructions.txt DOES contain the U+2011 characters and is byte-identical to the 9Router string (verified: both 11,692 chars, exact equality) — but that file is dead code, referenced only by src/core/config/codex_instructions.rs:7, which nothing imports; the live constant is a separate hand-maintained inline duplicate.
- **Gap**: The inlined default Codex instructions differ from 9Router's at four characters: OpenProxy uses ASCII hyphens where 9Router uses U+2011 non-breaking hyphens, even though an unused data file in the same repo already holds the byte-exact string.
- **Why it matters**: The injected instructions are a verbatim system prompt and participate in prompt-cache keying, so byte drift from canonical is a parity defect even at cosmetic weight. The substantive risk is maintenance rather than immediate user-visible breakage: the correct bytes exist in src/core/config/data/codex_default_instructions.txt but are dead, while the live constant is a hand-copied inline literal that has silently drifted — a second future edit is as likely to drift again. No client contract breaks today; the four characters are punctuation inside prose bullets.
- **Evidence**: 9router: open-sse/config/codexInstructions.js:90, 109, 115, 116 (U+2011 in source). openproxy: src/core/executor/codex.rs:368 (U+002D inline constant, actually used via :405-409); dead-but-correct copy at src/core/config/data/codex_default_instructions.txt (byte-identical to 9Router, verified by exact string comparison) exposed as src/core/config/codex_instructions.rs:7 with no importers. Verified by extracting both literals, decoding the Rust string literal, and enumerating all non-ASCII code points: 9Router has 4x U+2011 that the live Rust constant lacks.
- **Parity fix**: Eliminate the duplicate rather than hand-patching the literal. Change the inlined const at src/core/executor/codex.rs:368 to reference the existing byte-exact data file — e.g. `const DEFAULT_CODEX_INSTRUCTIONS: &str = include_str!("../../config/data/codex_default_instructions.txt");` — or repoint it at `crate::core::config::codex_instructions::CODEX_DEFAULT_INSTRUCTIONS`, which already wraps that file at src/core/config/codex_instructions.rs:7. Then delete the drifted inline literal so a single source of truth remains, and strengthen the existing test at codex.rs:1179-1194 (currently three substring/`starts_with` assertions that cannot catch this) with a length or checksum assertion so any future drift fails CI.

## P341-001 · P3 · src/server/api/chat.rs:2899-2926

- **9router**: The executor catch block special-cases aborts: `if (error.name === "AbortError") { streamController.handleError(error); return createErrorResult(499, "Request aborted"); }` (chatCore.js:394-397), and the request-detail log records 499 (chatCore.js:382, 389). 499 is absent from `ERROR_TYPES` so it renders as `{type:"invalid_request_error", code:""}`.
- **OpenProxy**: There is no 499 anywhere in `src/`. The single `Err(error)` arm hard-codes 502 in its fallback (`ComboAttemptError::new(502, message)`, chat.rs:2907) and uses `format!("{:?}", error)` (chat.rs:2900) for the message, so a cancelled/aborted upstream is indistinguishable from a gateway failure.
- **Gap**: HTTP 499 "Request aborted" is never produced; client-cancel and gateway-failure are conflated.
- **Why it matters**: Proxies, dashboards and the request-detail log cannot tell a user-cancelled request from a real upstream failure, and the client-facing message is a Rust `Debug` dump rather than the 9router "Request aborted" string.
- **Evidence**: 9router: .tmp/9router/open-sse/handlers/chatCore.js:382, 389, 394-397. OpenProxy: src/server/api/chat.rs:2899-2926
- **Parity fix**: In the `Err(error)` arm, detect cancellation (`tokio::select!` on a client-disconnect future, or `error.to_string().contains("canceled")`) and return `ComboAttemptError { status: 499, message: "Request aborted", .. }`; otherwise format the message as `format!("[502]: {error} (cause: {code})")` like `formatProviderError`.
